   > You remember seeing the colours inside things, the light every element gives off.
   > What: a hand-held scanner (Warbucks Core, made for this pack). Right-click to read the ore signal in the loaded chunks around you.
   > Chemistry: heated atoms give off light only at certain wavelengths, an emission spectrum unique to each element. That's how helium was found in the Sun before anyone found it on Earth.
   > How: amethyst prism, steel plates, copper wire and an analog circuit (check JEI). It lists each element with its strength, direction, distance and depth.
   > Tip: a "strong" signal is usually a rich deposit. Walk towards it and scan again.
40. **A Rich Lead Deposit**: 64x `modern_industrialization:lead_ore` *(after: Steel)*
   > What: rich ore deposits hold hundreds of ores in one chunk, at a metal-specific depth. Lead's sits around Y -24 to 16.
   > How: strip-mine at that depth and check ore with Jade. FTB Ultimine clears a vein in one go.
   > Tip: galena (lead sulfide, PbS) is the world's main lead ore, and it often carries silver.
41. **Osmium Hoard**: 32x `mekanism:ingot_osmium` *(after: Osmium)*
   > What: every Mekanism machine, casing and circuit costs osmium. A stockpile saves trips.
   > How: smelt or enrich raw osmium into ingots.
   > Tip: the Enrichment Chamber doubles osmium ore, so process it there once it's built.

### V. Electrochemistry and Acids

*Electricity splits compounds into elements. Acids dissolve what fire can't. Ore processing becomes chemistry.*

1. **Mekanism Gases**: `mekanism:electrolytic_separator` *(after: Mekanism Circuits)*
   > You remember pulling water apart with a word. A machine and a cable will have to do.
   > What: the Electrolytic Separator makes hydrogen and oxygen as gases you can pipe and store.
   > Chemistry: 2H₂O → 2H₂ + O₂. Current drives the reaction backwards from burning hydrogen.
   > How: feed it water and FE. Gases leave from the coloured sides (configure them with the Configurator).
2. **Pressurized Tubes**: 16x `mekanism:basic_pressurized_tube` *(after: Mekanism Gases)*
   > What: gases need sealed tubes. Item pipes and fluid pipes won't carry them.
   > How: craft basic pressurized tubes (see JEI) and run them from the separator's outputs.
   > Tip: vent what you don't need yet. A full output tank stops the whole separator.
3. **A Source of Sulfur**: 8x `modern_industrialization:sulfur_dust` *(after: Electric Macerator)*
   > What: sulfur is where sulfuric acid starts.
   > Chemistry: S is element 16. Volcanoes and fossil fuels are full of it; most industrial sulfur is stripped out of oil and gas.
   > How: macerate crushed lignite coal (about half the time you get sulfur dust), or centrifuge lava later. Press U on Sulfur Dust in JEI for all sources.
4. **Contact Process 1: SO₂**: `mekanism:chemical_oxidizer` *(after: Pressurized Tubes, A Source of Sulfur)*
   > What: the Chemical Oxidizer turns a solid into a gas. Sulfur dust becomes sulfur dioxide.
   > Chemistry: S + O₂ → SO₂. It's the sharp smell of a struck match.
   > How: one sulfur dust gives 100 mB of SO₂. Pipe it out with pressurized tubes.
5. **Contact Process 2: SO₃**: `mekanism:chemical_infuser` *(after: Contact Process 1: SO₂)*
   > What: the Chemical Infuser combines two gases. SO₂ plus oxygen from your separator gives sulfur trioxide.
   > Chemistry: 2SO₂ + O₂ → 2SO₃. Real plants pass the gases over a vanadium oxide (V₂O₅) catalyst at about 450 °C: the 'contact' in the name.
   > How: SO₂ in one side, O₂ in the other.
6. **Water Vapor**: `mekanism:rotary_condensentrator` *(after: Contact Process 2: SO₃)*
   > What: the Rotary Condensentrator converts fluids to gases and back. It turns water into water vapor.
   > How: set it to gas mode (the toggle in its GUI), pipe water in and pressurized tube out.
   > Tip: it also turns Mekanism gases into buckets, which is handy for storage and trading.
7. **Contact Process 3: H₂SO₄**: checkmark *(after: Contact Process 2: SO₃, Water Vapor)*
   > What: a second Chemical Infuser combines SO₃ with water vapor into sulfuric acid gas. Tick this when acid is flowing.
   > Chemistry: SO₃ + H₂O → H₂SO₄. Real plants dissolve SO₃ in existing acid first (oleum), because SO₃ and water alone make a corrosive mist.
   > Tip: store the acid in a chemical tank. The 5x ore line drinks it.
8. **Salt**: 16x `modern_industrialization:salt_dust` *(after: Electric Macerator)*
   > What: salt is the start of the chlor-alkali industry: chlorine, sodium and everything made from them.
   > Chemistry: NaCl is an ionic crystal: Na⁺ and Cl⁻ held together by charge. Split it and you get a soft, reactive metal and a toxic green gas.
   > How: MI salt ore is found underground and drops salt dust.
9. **Brine**: checkmark *(after: Salt, Contact Process 1: SO₂)*
   > What: brine is salty water, and Mekanism splits it into chlorine. Tick this when you have brine.
   > How: put salt dust in a Chemical Oxidizer (15 mB brine per dust), or build a Thermal Evaporation Plant (see the side quest).
   > Tip: a second oxidizer just for salt saves you from swapping inputs.
10. **Chlorine**: checkmark *(after: Brine, Mekanism Gases)*
   > What: the Electrolytic Separator also takes brine. Out come chlorine and sodium. Tick this when chlorine is flowing.
   > Chemistry: real brine electrolysis gives Cl₂ at one electrode and H₂ + NaOH at the other. Mekanism simplifies it to sodium + chlorine.
   > Tip: use a second separator so your hydrogen and oxygen line keeps running.
11. **Hydrogen Chloride**: checkmark *(after: Chlorine, Contact Process 2: SO₃)*
   > What: hydrogen chloride drives the 4x ore line. Tick this when HCl is flowing.
   > Chemistry: H₂ + Cl₂ → 2HCl. Dissolved in water it's hydrochloric acid, the same acid in your stomach.
   > How: hydrogen in one side of a Chemical Infuser, chlorine in the other.
12. **3x Ore: Oxygen**: `mekanism:purification_chamber` *(after: Mekanism Gases, Enrichment Chamber, Crusher)*
   > What: before acids, every route caps at 2x per ore. Purifying ore with oxygen gives 3x.
   > How: ore + O₂ → 3 clumps. Crusher: clumps → dirty dust. Enrichment Chamber: dirty dust → dust.
   > Tip: Mekanism shows each step in JEI. Chain them with pipes and side configuration.
13. **4x Ore: Hydrogen Chloride**: `mekanism:chemical_injection_chamber` *(after: 3x Ore: Oxygen, Hydrogen Chloride)*
   > What: injecting hydrogen chloride splits ore into shards: 4x.
   > How: ore + HCl → 4 shards. Purification Chamber: shards → clumps, then on through your 3x line.
   > Tip: your 3x machines are now the back half of the 4x line. Nothing is wasted.
14. **Dissolving Ore**: `mekanism:chemical_dissolution_chamber` *(after: 4x Ore: Hydrogen Chloride, Contact Process 3: H₂SO₄)*
   > What: the Dissolution Chamber dissolves ore in sulfuric acid, giving a dirty slurry.
   > Chemistry: acid leaching: H₂SO₄ turns metal minerals into soluble sulfates, leaving the rock behind.
   > How: feed it ore and sulfuric acid gas. The slurry comes out as a chemical.
15. **Washing Slurry**: `mekanism:chemical_washer` *(after: Dissolving Ore)*
   > What: the Chemical Washer rinses impurities out of the slurry.
   > Chemistry: washing removes the dissolved gangue (waste rock) so only the metal salt is left to crystallise.
   > How: slurry in, water in, clean slurry out. An MI electric water pump keeps the water coming.
16. **5x Ore: Dissolution**: `mekanism:chemical_dissolution_chamber`; `mekanism:chemical_washer`; `mekanism:chemical_crystallizer` *(after: Washing Slurry)*
   > Five from one. You used to call that a small miracle. Now you call it hydrometallurgy.
   > What: the Chemical Crystallizer turns clean slurry into crystals: 5x per ore.
   > Chemistry: this is hydrometallurgy, the way most of the world's copper is really refined.
   > How: crystals → Injection Chamber (shards) → Purification Chamber (clumps) → Crusher → Enrichment Chamber.
17. **Ore Byproducts**: `modern_industrialization:centrifuge`; 4x `modern_industrialization:silver_dust` *(after: 5x Ore: Dissolution, Electronic Circuit)*
   > What: real ores are never pure. Send Mekanism's dirty dust through MI's Centrifuge instead of the Enrichment Chamber for the same dust plus a chance at a bonus metal.
   > Chemistry: iron → nickel, copper → gold, gold → silver, lead → silver (galena), tin → tungsten, osmium → iridium, uranium → lead (decay).
   > How: the Centrifuge needs an electronic circuit. Route dirty dust to it, and rare metals arrive as a bonus.
18. **Bauxite**: 16x `modern_industrialization:bauxite_dust` *(after: Electric Blast Furnace)*
   > You remember a metal an emperor once served to his most honoured guests. It was never rare, only stubborn.
   > What: bauxite is the only practical aluminum ore. MI bauxite ore drops bauxite dust.
   > Chemistry: bauxite is mostly aluminium hydroxides, Al(OH)₃ and AlO(OH), stained red by iron oxide.
   > How: rich bauxite deposits sit around Y -16 to 24. No furnace will free the metal: it goes through the Bayer process first.
19. **Lye for the Digester**: `modern_industrialization:sodium_hydroxide_bucket` *(after: Electric Mixer, The Combiner)*
   > What: the Bayer process dissolves bauxite in hot lye, so you need sodium hydroxide solution first.
   > Chemistry: NaOH is a strong base: it fully splits into Na⁺ and OH⁻ in water, and OH⁻ is what pulls aluminium into solution.
   > How: combine Na + O + H in the Alchemistry Combiner (check JEI on Sodium Hydroxide), then Mixer: 1 crystal + 250 mB water → 250 mB lye.
   > Tip: the digester gives most of the lye back, so a few crystals go a long way. Later the Chemical Reactor makes it in bulk.
20. **Bayer Digestion**: `modern_industrialization:sodium_aluminate_solution_bucket` *(after: Bauxite, Lye for the Digester)*
   > What: the first step of every aluminium refinery since 1888: hot lye dissolves the aluminium out of bauxite.
   > Chemistry: Al(OH)₃ + NaOH → NaAl(OH)₄ (sodium aluminate). Iron and titanium oxides don't dissolve; they settle out as 'red mud'.
   > How: Mixer: 4 bauxite dust + 1000 mB lye → 1000 mB sodium aluminate solution + 2 tiny iron dust + 1 tiny titanium dust.
   > Tip: pipe the solution straight into the next Mixer; a bucket is only needed for this quest.
21. **Precipitation**: 8x `chemlib:aluminum_hydroxide_dust` *(after: Bayer Digestion)*
   > What: pure aluminium hydroxide, crystallised back out of the solution, with the lye recovered.
   > Chemistry: NaAl(OH)₄ → Al(OH)₃ + NaOH. Diluting the liquor makes the reaction run backwards; the impurities stayed behind in the red mud.
   > How: Mixer: 1000 mB sodium aluminate + 1000 mB water → 4 aluminium hydroxide dust + 900 mB lye.
   > Tip: loop the 900 mB of lye back into the digester. That loop is why the Bayer process is cheap.
22. **Alumina**: 4x `chemlib:aluminum_oxide_dust` *(after: Precipitation)*
   > What: alumina (aluminium oxide) is the white powder that actually goes into the smelter.
   > Chemistry: calcination: 2Al(OH)₃ → Al₂O₃ + 3H₂O at around 1000 °C. Alumina also makes sapphire and ruby, coloured by traces of iron, titanium or chromium.
   > How: Electric Blast Furnace: 2 aluminium hydroxide dust → 1 alumina dust.
23. **The Hall-Héroult Cell**: `modern_industrialization:hall_heroult_cell` *(after: Alumina)*
   > What: a multiblock smelter that splits alumina with electricity. It's built from steel and LV parts, because it's what makes your first aluminium.
   > Chemistry: alumina melts at 2072 °C, far too hot. Dissolved in molten cryolite it melts near 950 °C. Current then splits it: 2Al₂O₃ + 3C → 4Al + 3CO₂, burning the carbon anode away.
   > How: controller from steel large plates, analog circuits, cupronickel coils and an LV hull. Check JEI (press U on the controller) for the 3x3x3 shape: steel casings top and bottom, a ring of cupronickel coils with the controller in the middle layer.
   > {@pagebreak}
   > History: Charles Martin Hall (USA) and Paul Héroult (France) found the process independently in 1886, both in their early twenties. Within about ten years the price of aluminium fell by more than 90%.
   > Note: every primary aluminium smelter on Earth still uses it, and the cells draw a few percent of the world's electricity.
24. **Aluminum**: 8x `modern_industrialization:aluminum_ingot` *(after: The Hall-Héroult Cell)*
   > The metal comes away bright and light in your hand. Some part of you is pleased it still had to work for it.
   > What: aluminum is the metal of the MV tier: boards, rotors, casings, the Chemical Reactor and the Electrolyzer.
   > Chemistry: aluminium is the most common metal in the crust but clings to oxygen, so only electricity can pull it free.
   > How: Hall-Héroult Cell (LV power): 1 alumina + coke dust (the anode, used half the time) + a pinch of fluorite flux → 1 aluminum ingot.
   > Tip: 4 bauxite dust becomes 2 ingots. Alumina from the Combiner (Al + O) works in the cell too.
25. **The Alchemist's Alumina**: 2x `chemlib:aluminum_oxide` *(after: Alumina, The Combiner)*
   > What: the Combiner can build aluminium oxide straight from elements, and the cell accepts it like refined alumina.
   > Chemistry: 4Al + 3O₂ → 2Al₂O₃. The reaction is so eager it burns white-hot in thermite; you're running it backwards to get the metal.
   > How: check JEI on Aluminum Oxide for the Combiner recipe. For aluminium elements, check JEI (press U on Aluminum) to see what the Dissolver breaks into it.
26. **Electrum**: 4x `modern_industrialization:electrum_ingot` *(after: Electric Mixer)*
   > What: electrum cable and fine wire go into every MV component.
   > Chemistry: electrum is a natural gold-silver alloy. The first coins, in Lydia around 600 BC, were struck from it.
   > How: mix gold and silver dust, then smelt. Check JEI (press R on Silver Dust) for silver ore in this pack.
27. **Silicon**: 4x `modern_industrialization:silicon_ingot` *(after: Electric Blast Furnace, Nether Quartz)*
   > What: silicon is the semiconductor every transistor and diode is built on.
   > Chemistry: SiO₂ + 2C → Si + 2CO. Carbon pulls the oxygen off quartz at furnace heat: carbothermic reduction.
   > How: macerate quartz into dust, then EBF: quartz dust + coke dust → silicon dust. Smelt it into ingots.
28. **Electronic Circuit Board**: `modern_industrialization:electronic_circuit_board` *(after: Aluminum, Electrum)*
   > What: the base for MV circuits.
   > How: an analog circuit board with aluminum plates, electrum cable and a redstone battery (see JEI).
   > Tip: the Assembler can make these too, once you stock it.
29. **Transistors and Diodes**: 2x `modern_industrialization:transistor`; 2x `modern_industrialization:diode` *(after: Silicon, Electrum)*
   > What: diodes let current flow one way; transistors are switches controlled by current.
   > Chemistry: a pure silicon crystal barely conducts. Tiny amounts of other elements ('doping') make it conduct in controllable ways.
   > How: silicon plates, steel plates and electrum fine wire (diodes add glass).
30. **Electronic Circuit**: 2x `modern_industrialization:electronic_circuit` *(after: Electronic Circuit Board, Transistors and Diodes)*
   > What: the MV circuit. The Electrolyzer, Chemical Reactor and Centrifuge all need it, even though they sit on an LV hull.
   > How: electronic circuit board, 4 analog circuits, 2 diodes and 2 transistors.
   > Tip: set an Assembler to make these. You'll need dozens.
31. **Electrolysis**: `modern_industrialization:electrolyzer` *(after: Electronic Circuit)*
   > What: pass current through a compound and it splits. This is electrochemistry.
   > Chemistry: water → H₂ + O₂ (3000 mB water → 2000 mB H₂ + 1000 mB O₂). Salt → sodium + chlorine.
   > How: magnetic cupronickel wire, aluminum rods, an LV hull, an electronic circuit and a large pump.
32. **The Chemical Reactor**: `modern_industrialization:chemical_reactor` *(after: Electronic Circuit)*
   > What: combines items and fluids into new compounds. You'll use it constantly from here to the end.
   > How: glass, a large pump, aluminum rotors, an LV hull, electronic circuits and electrum cable.
   > Tip: MI fluids go in through pipes. A Configurable Tank holds spare H₂ and O₂.
33. **Sodium**: 4x `modern_industrialization:sodium_dust` *(after: Electrolysis, Salt)*
   > What: sodium goes into the HV tier's sodium battery. The chlorine is a bonus.
   > Chemistry: 2NaCl → 2Na + Cl₂. Real sodium is made this way from molten salt (the Downs cell). It reacts violently with water.
   > How: Electrolyzer: 2 salt dust → 1 sodium dust + 125 mB chlorine.
34. **Sulfuric Acid**: `modern_industrialization:sulfuric_acid_bucket` *(after: The Chemical Reactor, A Source of Sulfur)*
   > Acid opens what fire cannot. You remember that lesson; you remember learning it the hard way.
   > What: sulfuric acid, as an MI fluid you can bucket. The Undergarden portal and many MI recipes need it.
   > Chemistry: S + O₂ + H₂O → H₂SO₄, the whole contact process in one vessel. Industry makes more of it than any other chemical.
   > How: Chemical Reactor: 1 sulfur dust + 200 mB O₂ + 200 mB water → 500 mB sulfuric acid.
35. **Silicon Battery**: 2x `modern_industrialization:silicon_battery` *(after: Silicon, Electrum)*
   > What: the MV battery. Every MV hull holds two.
   > How: battery alloy plates, electrum cable and silicon dust.
   > Tip: make four. The Advanced (MV) hull uses two, and you'll want a second hull soon.
36. **MV: Aluminum**: `modern_industrialization:electronic_circuit`; `modern_industrialization:advanced_machine_hull` *(after: Electronic Circuit, Silicon Battery)*
   > The circuits are getting smaller and the power larger. So are you.
   > What: the MV hull is the base for every MV machine, and Powah, Hostile Neural Networks and Extended Crafting all want MV circuits.
   > How: advanced machine casing (an LV hull wrapped in aluminum plates) + electronic circuit + 2 silicon batteries + electrum cable.
37. **Uranium**: 4x `modern_industrialization:uranium_dust` *(after: Electric Macerator)*
   > What: uranium is the fuel of Powah's reactors and, much later, fission.
   > Chemistry: U is element 92, the heaviest element found in quantity in nature. It slowly decays into lead.
   > How: rich uranium deposits sit deep, around Y -56 to -24. Macerate the ore into dust.
38. **Uranium Dioxide**: `powah:energizing_orb`; 4x `powah:uraninite` *(after: MV: Aluminum, The Chemical Reactor, Uranium)*
   > What: uraninite fuels Powah's reactors. Powah's Energizing Orb needs an MV circuit.
   > Chemistry: uraninite is UO₂, the main ore of uranium in nature. Here you make it: U + O₂ → UO₂.
   > How: Chemical Reactor: uranium dust + 500 mB oxygen → uraninite. Powah's own uraninite ore is turned off.
39. **The Undergarden**: `undergarden:catalyst`; visit `undergarden:undergarden` *(after: Sulfuric Acid)*
   > What: a strange underground dimension. Its portal catalyst needs a bucket of MI sulfuric acid.
   > How: build a stone-brick portal frame and right-click it with the catalyst.
   > Tip: bring torches and food. It is dark down there.
40. **Thermal Evaporation**: `mekanism:thermal_evaporation_controller`; `mekanism:thermal_evaporation_valve`; 16x `mekanism:thermal_evaporation_block` *(after: Mekanism Gases)*
   > What: a tall multiblock that boils water down into brine, and brine down into lithium.
   > Chemistry: evaporation concentrates dissolved salts. Salt flats and lithium ponds in Chile work the same way.
   > How: see Just Enough Mekanism Multiblocks in JEI for the layout. Taller towers run faster; heat speeds it up.
41. **Bottled Gases**: 3x `mekanism:basic_chemical_tank` *(after: Pressurized Tubes)*
   > What: chemical tanks store gases and keep them when broken, so you can carry them around.
   > How: fill one tank each with hydrogen, oxygen and chlorine.
   > Tip: a tank of hydrogen also fuels the Gas-Burning Generator (Power chapter).
42. **Fun Fact: Oleum**: checkmark *(after: Contact Process 3: H₂SO₄)*
   > What: SO₃ hitting water directly forms a fine acid mist that's hard to capture.
   > Chemistry: plants absorb SO₃ into concentrated H₂SO₄ to make oleum (H₂S₂O₇), then dilute: H₂S₂O₇ + H₂O → 2H₂SO₄.
   > Tip: you've now run, in miniature, a process that makes over 250 million tonnes a year.
43. **Five for Everything**: checkmark *(after: 5x Ore: Dissolution)*
   > What: a challenge: put at least five different ores through the full 5x line.
   > How: one Dissolution Chamber handles every metal, but each slurry needs its own washer and crystallizer pass. Tick this when five ores are done.
   > Tip: Mekanism factories (upgraded machines) run several ores at once.
44. **A Red Mountain**: 128x `modern_industrialization:bauxite_dust` *(after: Bauxite)*
   > What: rich deposits hold hundreds of ores in one chunk. Find bauxite's and the MV tier stops feeling slow.
   > How: strip-mine around Y -16 to 24 and look for red ore with Jade. FTB Ultimine helps a lot.
   > Tip: Fortune works on bauxite ore, since it drops dust.
45. **A Hint of Titanium**: 9x `modern_industrialization:titanium_tiny_dust` *(after: Bayer Digestion)*
   > What: Bayer digestion leaves a little titanium behind with the iron. Save it for much later.
   > Chemistry: bauxite carries titanium dioxide (TiO₂) as an impurity. It doesn't dissolve in lye, so it ends up in the 'red mud'.
   > How: every Bayer digestion (4 bauxite dust) gives 1 tiny titanium dust. Nine of them pack into a full dust.
46. **Hydrochloric Acid, MI Style**: `modern_industrialization:hydrochloric_acid_bucket` *(after: The Chemical Reactor, Sodium)*
   > What: MI makes hydrochloric acid as a fluid, the alternative to Mekanism's HCl gas.
   > Chemistry: H₂ + Cl₂ → 2HCl. 1000 mB of each gas gives 2000 mB of acid.
   > How: chlorine from salt electrolysis, hydrogen from water electrolysis, both into the Chemical Reactor.
47. **Lye**: `modern_industrialization:sodium_hydroxide_bucket` *(after: The Chemical Reactor, Sodium)*
   > What: sodium hydroxide (NaOH, lye) is the strong base to sulfuric acid's strong acid. Soap is made with it.
   > Chemistry: acid + base → salt + water. H₂SO₄ + 2NaOH → Na₂SO₄ + 2H₂O.
   > How: Chemical Reactor: sodium dust + 1000 mB oxygen + 1000 mB hydrogen → 2000 mB sodium hydroxide.
   > Tip: this is the bulk lye supply for the Bayer digester, so you can stop feeding it Combiner crystals.

### VI. Petrochemistry

*Crude oil is a mixture. Separate it by boiling point, then build polymers from the pieces.*

1. **Black Gold**: `modern_industrialization:oil_drilling_rig`; `modern_industrialization:crude_oil_bucket` *(after: MV: Aluminum)*
   > You remember the world before people: forests that grew, died and sank, pressed into oil over ages you once watched pass.
   > What: crude oil is a soup of hydrocarbons, from tiny gas molecules to long waxy chains.
   > Chemistry: hydrocarbons are just carbon and hydrogen (CₙH₂ₙ₊₂ and friends). Chain length decides whether one is a gas, a fuel or a tar.
   > How: put the Oil Drilling Rig in an MI Quarry over an oil-rich area (check JEI). It slowly wears out aluminum drills.
   > Tip: pipe the crude straight into a tank. You'll need a lot of it.
2. **Fractional Distillation**: `modern_industrialization:distillation_tower` *(after: Black Gold)*
   > What: the Distillation Tower is a multiblock that splits one fluid into several fractions at once.
   > Chemistry: heat the crude and each fraction boils off at its own temperature. Light molecules rise to the top, heavy ones stay low.
   > How: the 3D layout is in JEI and the MI guidebook. Each extra layer adds one more output.
   > Tip: every other mod gets its fuel and plastic from here, so build it with room to grow.
3. **Sour Fractions**: `modern_industrialization:sulfuric_light_fuel_bucket`; `modern_industrialization:sulfuric_heavy_fuel_bucket`; `modern_industrialization:sulfuric_naphtha_bucket` *(after: Fractional Distillation)*
   > What: 1000 mB of crude gives 500 light fuel, 200 heavy fuel and 300 naphtha, but all three are 'sour'.
   > Chemistry: natural oil carries sulfur compounds. Burn them and you get SO₂, the gas behind acid rain.
   > How: run crude through the Distillation Tower and catch each output in its own tank or bucket.
4. **Sweetening the Oil**: `modern_industrialization:light_fuel_bucket`; `modern_industrialization:heavy_fuel_bucket`; `modern_industrialization:naphtha_bucket` *(after: Sour Fractions)*
   > What: hydrotreating removes the sulfur and gives clean light fuel, heavy fuel and naphtha.
   > Chemistry: hydrogen reacts with sulfur compounds to form H₂S. Real refineries turn that into sulfur and then acid; MI hands you sulfuric acid directly.
   > How: Chemical Reactor: 12 B of a sour fraction + 2 B hydrogen → 12 B clean fraction + 2 B H₂SO₄.
   > Tip: feed the acid back into your ore line. Nothing is wasted.
5. **Diesel Power**: `modern_industrialization:diesel_bucket`; `modern_industrialization:lv_diesel_generator` *(after: Sweetening the Oil)*
   > What: diesel runs MI generators, tools and, later, your rockets (Ad Astra burns MI diesel).
   > Chemistry: diesel is a blend of C₁₀ to C₂₀ hydrocarbons: mostly light fuel, with heavy fuel for energy density.
   > How: Mixer: 5 B light fuel + 1 B heavy fuel → 6 B diesel.
   > Tip: a Large Diesel Generator is far more efficient than a row of small ones.
6. **Steam Cracking**: `modern_industrialization:steam_cracked_naphtha_bucket` *(after: Sweetening the Oil)*
   > What: naphtha is too big to make plastic from. Cracking breaks it into the small building blocks chemists actually want.
   > Chemistry: at high temperature with steam, long chains snap apart into short, reactive molecules with double bonds (alkenes).
   > How: Chemical Reactor: 1000 mB naphtha + 100 mB steam → 1000 mB steam-cracked naphtha.
7. **Ethylene**: `modern_industrialization:ethylene_bucket` *(after: Steam Cracking)*
   > What: distilling cracked naphtha gives eight products: methane, acetylene, ethylene, propene, butadiene, benzene, toluene and ethylbenzene.
   > Chemistry: ethylene's C=C double bond can open up and link to its neighbours. That is how plastics are born.
   > How: send steam-cracked naphtha through the Distillation Tower (more layers give more outputs; all eight need the full tower).
   > Tip: store every fraction. Each one has a use later in this chapter.
8. **Polymerisation**: `modern_industrialization:polyethylene_bucket` *(after: Ethylene)*
   > What: polyethylene, (C₂H₄)ₙ, is the plastic in bags and bottles, and the laminate on HV circuit boards.
   > Chemistry: a metal catalyst opens each double bond so the molecules join head to tail. Real plants use chromium (Phillips) or titanium catalysts.
   > How: Chemical Reactor: 500 mB ethylene + 4 tiny lead or chromium dust → 300 mB polyethylene.
9. **HDPE**: 8x `mekanism:hdpe_pellet` *(after: Polymerisation)*
   > What: high-density polyethylene pellets become Mekanism's plastic sheets, rods and later the MekaSuit.
   > Chemistry: HDPE has straight chains that pack tightly, so it's stiffer than ordinary polyethylene.
   > How: Pressurized Reaction Chamber with ethylene, water and substrate (check JEI).
10. **Chromium and Manganese**: 4x `modern_industrialization:chromium_dust`; 4x `modern_industrialization:manganese_dust` *(after: MV: Aluminum)*
   > What: stainless steel needs chromium, nickel and manganese on top of iron.
   > Chemistry: chromium is leached with acid: crushed chromium + HCl → chromium chloride solution, then electrolysis splits out the metal plus H₂ and Cl₂.
   > How: Chemical Reactor (chromium crushed dust + 9 B HCl), then Electrolyzer. Check JEI for manganese.
11. **Stainless Steel Dust**: 9x `modern_industrialization:stainless_steel_dust` *(after: Chromium and Manganese)*
   > What: the mixed powder that becomes stainless steel.
   > Chemistry: at 10.5% chromium or more, the surface forms an invisible Cr₂O₃ film that stops rust spreading.
   > How: Mixer: 6 iron dust + 1 chromium + 1 nickel + 1 manganese → 9 stainless steel dust.
12. **Hot Ingots**: `modern_industrialization:vacuum_freezer`; `modern_industrialization:stainless_steel_hot_ingot` *(after: Stainless Steel Dust)*
   > What: the Electric Blast Furnace melts the dust into a hot ingot that must be cooled before use.
   > How: EBF turns 1 stainless steel dust into 1 hot ingot. Build the Vacuum Freezer multiblock (layout in JEI) to cool it.
   > Tip: later, the Heat Exchanger cools hot ingots too and gives argon and helium back.
13. **Stainless Steel**: 8x `modern_industrialization:stainless_steel_ingot` *(after: Hot Ingots, Electric Blast Furnace)*
   > What: stainless steel resists corrosion, so it can hold acids and hot fluids.
   > Chemistry: if the surface is scratched, chromium re-forms the oxide film at once. The steel heals itself.
   > How: cool hot ingots in the Vacuum Freezer. HV circuit boards need 4 stainless plates each.
   > Tip: automate the whole line: mixer → EBF → freezer → compressor.
14. **Digital Circuit Board**: `modern_industrialization:digital_circuit_board` *(after: Polymerisation, Stainless Steel)*
   > What: the base of every HV circuit.
   > Chemistry: polyethylene is an excellent insulator, so it separates the copper layers of the board.
   > How: Assembler: electronic circuit board + 4 stainless steel plates + silicon battery + 6 aluminum cable + 750 mB polyethylene.
15. **Sodium Battery**: 4x `modern_industrialization:sodium_dust`; 2x `modern_industrialization:sodium_battery` *(after: Salt)*
   > What: the HV hull needs two sodium batteries.
   > Chemistry: electrolysis splits salt: 2NaCl → 2Na + Cl₂. Sodium metal is so reactive it fizzes in water, which is what makes it a good battery material.
   > How: Electrolyzer: 2 salt dust → 1 sodium dust + chlorine. Then craft the battery (check JEI).
   > Tip: keep the chlorine. It's needed for HCl and, next chapter, PVC.
16. **HV: Digital Circuits**: `modern_industrialization:digital_circuit`; `modern_industrialization:turbo_machine_hull` *(after: Digital Circuit Board, Sodium Battery)*
   > Logic gates, clicking into place. You used to think this fast.
   > What: digital circuits unlock HV machines, AE2, the Angel Ring and elite jetpack coils.
   > How: Assembler: digital circuit board + 4 electronic circuits + AND, OR and NOT gates. The Turbo hull adds 2 sodium batteries and aluminum cable.
   > Tip: set up a dedicated assembler for circuits. You'll make hundreds.
17. **Fallen Stars**: `ae2:meteorite_compass`; `ae2:engineering_processor_press`; `ae2:silicon_press` *(after: HV: Digital Circuits)*
   > What: AE2's inscriber presses are only found in the core of meteorites.
   > How: craft a Meteorite Compass, follow it, and dig to the centre of the sky stone crater. Each meteorite's mysterious cube holds one press.
   > Tip: once you have one press, the Inscriber can copy it with an iron block.
18. **Fluix**: `ae2:charger`; 8x `ae2:fluix_crystal` *(after: HV: Digital Circuits)*
   > What: fluix crystals carry the ME network's signal. The ME Controller needs them.
   > How: charge certus quartz in the Charger, then drop charged certus, redstone and Nether quartz in water together (check JEI).
   > Tip: budding certus quartz grows new crystals, so certus is renewable once you find some.
19. **The Inscriber**: `ae2:inscriber`; 2x `ae2:engineering_processor` *(after: HV: Digital Circuits, Fallen Stars)*
   > What: every AE2 processor is printed in the Inscriber. In this pack it's built on a Turbo hull.
   > Chemistry: engineering processors print a circuit onto silicon, the same element your HV boards depend on.
   > How: press a diamond into a printed engineering circuit, silicon into printed silicon, then combine them with redstone.
20. **The God's Archive**: `ae2:inscriber`; `ae2:controller` *(after: The Inscriber, Fluix)*
   > Everything you ever owned, known at once. You remember having a mind like that.
   > What: Applied Energistics 2 stores items as data and crafts on demand.
   > How: the ME Controller needs a digital circuit, an engineering processor, fluix and smooth sky stone.
   > Tip: Tom's Simple Storage was your early network. AE2 takes over from here (see the Applied Energistics page in Engineering).
21. **First Storage Cells**: 2x `ae2:item_storage_cell_1k`; `ae2:fluid_storage_cell_1k` *(after: The God's Archive)*
   > What: storage cells go into an ME Drive. Fluid cells hold your acids and fuels as data too.
   > How: a 1k cell component needs a logic processor (gold + logic press). Check JEI for the housing.
   > Tip: a few fluid cells beat a wall of tanks for small stocks of rare chemicals.
22. **The Twilight Forest**: visit `twilightforest:twilight_forest` *(after: HV: Digital Circuits)*
   > What: a dimension of bosses, strange woods and unique metals (see the Twilight Forest page in Exploration).
   > How: dig a 2x2 pool of water ringed with flowers, stand in it, and throw in a diamond.
   > Tip: set a waystone near the portal before you explore.
23. **Benzene Rings**: `modern_industrialization:benzene_bucket`; `modern_industrialization:toluene_bucket` *(after: Steam Cracking)*
   > What: benzene (C₆H₆) and toluene (C₇H₈) come out of the cracked-naphtha distillation.
   > Chemistry: benzene is a flat ring of six carbons whose electrons are shared around the whole ring. That makes it unusually stable.
   > How: collect them from the Distillation Tower's benzene and toluene outputs.
   > Tip: benzene also makes dyes and, via caprolactam, nylon.
24. **Styrene**: `modern_industrialization:ethylbenzene_bucket`; `modern_industrialization:styrene_bucket` *(after: Benzene Rings)*
   > Chemistry: benzene + ethylene → ethylbenzene (acid catalyst). Steam over iron then pulls off H₂ to give styrene, C₈H₈, exactly as in real plants.
   > How: Chemical Reactor: benzene + ethylene + a little HCl → ethylbenzene. Then ethylbenzene + steam + iron dust → styrene + hydrogen.
   > Tip: the hydrogen by-product can go straight back into hydrotreating.
25. **Synthetic Rubber**: `modern_industrialization:styrene_butadiene_rubber_bucket` *(after: Styrene)*
   > What: styrene-butadiene rubber (SBR) is the rubber in most tyres. Here it makes rubber sheets by the stack.
   > Chemistry: styrene and butadiene polymerise together into one chain (a copolymer): stiff styrene parts, springy butadiene parts.
   > How: Chemical Reactor: styrene + butadiene → styrene-butadiene, then polymerise with tiny lead or chromium dust. The Vacuum Freezer turns 200 mB into 64 rubber sheets.
   > Tip: this replaces plant rubber for good and feeds the better cable recipes.
26. **Diesel Toys**: `modern_industrialization:diesel_mining_drill`; `modern_industrialization:diesel_jetpack` *(after: Diesel Power)*
   > What: MI's diesel drill and jetpack run on the fuel you just learned to blend.
   > How: check JEI for both recipes, then fill them from a tank.
   > Tip: the jetpack is loud and thirsty, but it flies. Real flight comes later.
27. **Fuel from Plants**: `modern_industrialization:biodiesel_bucket` *(after: Diesel Power)*
   > Chemistry: transesterification: plant oil + ethanol, with NaOH as catalyst, swaps the oil's glycerol backbone for ethyl groups. The result burns like diesel.
   > How: Chemical Reactor: plant oil + ethanol + sodium hydroxide → raw biodiesel, then wash it with steam (check JEI for plant oil).
   > Tip: Ad Astra rockets accept biodiesel too.
28. **Acrylic Glue**: `modern_industrialization:acrylic_acid_bucket`; `modern_industrialization:acrylic_glue_bucket` *(after: Steam Cracking)*
   > Chemistry: propene + oxygen → acrylic acid + water. Polymerised, it becomes a sticky acrylic: the same family as superabsorbent polymers.
   > How: Chemical Reactor for the acid, then polymerise with tiny lead or chromium dust.
   > Tip: the Chemical Reactor can turn acrylic glue into slime balls. No more slime farms.
29. **Nylon**: `modern_industrialization:caprolactam_bucket`; `modern_industrialization:nylon_bucket` *(after: Benzene Rings)*
   > Chemistry: benzene + hydrogen over nickel leads to caprolactam, a ring that opens up and polymerises into nylon-6.
   > How: Chemical Reactor: benzene + hydrogen + tiny nickel dust → caprolactam, then polymerise it.
   > Tip: 20 mB of nylon makes one string in the Chemical Reactor.
30. **A Mountain of Rubber**: 256x `modern_industrialization:rubber_sheet` *(after: Synthetic Rubber)*
   > How: keep the SBR line and Vacuum Freezer running until you have 256 sheets.
   > What: four full stacks of rubber sheets, made the modern way.
   > Tip: one bucket of SBR is five freezer runs, so a single bucket gets you there.

### VII. Materials and Nuclear

*Purity and precision: semiconductors, PVC, isotopes. Then transmutation: any element from any other.*

1. **Argon from Air**: `modern_industrialization:argon_bucket` *(after: HV: Digital Circuits)*
   > You once stirred the sky. Now you pour it, liquid, into a tank.
   > What: argon is an inert blanket gas. Growing a silicon crystal in air would ruin it.
   > Chemistry: air is 78% N₂, 21% O₂ and about 0.9% argon. Cool it until it liquefies, then separate the parts.
   > How: the Vacuum Freezer (or Pressurizer) with an Air Intake makes liquid air. The Centrifuge splits it into oxygen, nitrogen and a little argon.
   > Tip: the Heat Exchanger gives far more argon while it cools hot ingots.
2. **Semiconductor Silicon**: `modern_industrialization:monocrystalline_silicon`; 4x `modern_industrialization:silicon_wafer` *(after: Argon from Air)*
   > What: computer chips need silicon purer than 99.9999999%, grown as one single crystal.
   > Chemistry: a seed crystal is pulled slowly out of molten silicon (the Czochralski process). Every atom lines up with the one below it.
   > How: EBF: 32 silicon dust + 1 tiny iridium dust + argon → monocrystalline silicon. Cut it into wafers with lubricant.
   > Tip: iridium comes from osmium byproducts or the platinum line's residue (below).
3. **Chips**: 2x `modern_industrialization:random_access_memory`; `modern_industrialization:arithmetic_logic_unit`; `modern_industrialization:memory_management_unit` *(after: Semiconductor Silicon)*
   > What: a processing unit needs RAM, an ALU and an MMU.
   > Chemistry: a pinch of aluminum (p-type) or phosphorus (n-type) in silicon changes how it carries current. That's 'doping', the basis of every transistor.
   > How: Assembler. The ALU uses a diamond plate and logic gates, the MMU silicon wafers and platinum fine wire. Check JEI for RAM.
4. **Acetylene**: `modern_industrialization:acetylene_bucket` *(after: HV: Digital Circuits)*
   > What: acetylene (C₂H₂) has a triple bond, which makes it very reactive.
   > Chemistry: heat methane hard enough and pairs of molecules lose hydrogen and join: 2CH₄ → C₂H₂ + 3H₂.
   > How: Electric Blast Furnace: 200 mB methane → 200 mB acetylene. Cracked naphtha also gives a little.
5. **Vinyl Chloride**: `modern_industrialization:vinyl_chloride_bucket` *(after: Acetylene, Salt)*
   > Chemistry: C₂H₂ + HCl → C₂H₃Cl. HCl adds across the triple bond. This was the original industrial route to PVC.
   > How: Chemical Reactor: 1 B acetylene + 1 B hydrochloric acid → 1 B vinyl chloride.
   > Tip: your salt line's chlorine and hydrogen make the HCl.
6. **PVC**: `modern_industrialization:polyvinyl_chloride_bucket` *(after: Vinyl Chloride)*
   > What: polyvinyl chloride, (C₂H₃Cl)ₙ, is tough, fire-resistant and insulates EV circuit boards.
   > Chemistry: just like polyethylene, but every other carbon carries a chlorine atom. That's why PVC doesn't burn easily.
   > How: Chemical Reactor: 500 mB vinyl chloride + 4 tiny lead or chromium dust → 300 mB PVC.
7. **Cadmium Battery**: 4x `modern_industrialization:cadmium_dust`; 2x `modern_industrialization:cadmium_battery` *(after: HV: Digital Circuits)*
   > What: the EV hull and processing unit board each need 2 cadmium batteries.
   > Chemistry: a NiCd cell runs Cd + 2NiO(OH) + 2H₂O → Cd(OH)₂ + 2Ni(OH)₂, and it can be recharged by running it backwards.
   > How: MI centrifuges monazite dust into cadmium (in reality, it's mostly a by-product of zinc refining). Then craft the battery with annealed copper cable.
   > Tip: cadmium is toxic in real life. Here it just needs a lot of monazite.
8. **Processing Unit Board**: `modern_industrialization:processing_unit_board` *(after: PVC, Cadmium Battery)*
   > How: Assembler: digital circuit board + 4 platinum plates + 2 cadmium batteries + 8 annealed copper cable + 1500 mB PVC.
   > Chemistry: platinum never corrodes, so it makes contacts that last forever.
   > Tip: platinum comes from MI's ore processing or the Platinum Line below (check JEI).
9. **EV: Processing Units**: `modern_industrialization:processing_unit`; `modern_industrialization:highly_advanced_machine_hull` *(after: Processing Unit Board, Chips)*
   > A mind of silicon, insulated in salt and oil. It thinks the way you used to.
   > What: processing units unlock EV machines, the NASA Workbench, Flux Networks and Alchemistry's chambers.
   > How: Assembler: processing unit board + 4 digital circuits + 2 RAM + ALU + MMU. The EV hull adds 2 cadmium batteries.
10. **Yellowcake**: 4x `mekanism:yellow_cake_uranium` *(after: EV: Processing Units)*
   > What: yellowcake is concentrated uranium oxide powder, the raw material of nuclear fuel.
   > Chemistry: natural uranium is only 0.72% U-235, the isotope that splits. The rest is U-238.
   > How: Enrichment Chamber: 1 uranium ingot → 2 yellowcake. Then the Chemical Oxidizer turns it into uranium oxide gas.
   > Tip: a Geiger counter tells you when you're standing too close to anything radioactive.
11. **Hydrofluoric Acid**: 16x `mekanism:fluorite_gem` *(after: EV: Processing Units)*
   > Chemistry: CaF₂ + H₂SO₄ → CaSO₄ + 2HF. Hydrogen fluoride dissolves glass and is one of the most dangerous acids there is.
   > How: Chemical Dissolution Chamber: fluorite + sulfuric acid → hydrofluoric acid gas.
   > Tip: fluorite ore sits deep. Mystical Agriculture and Occultism can't make it in this pack.
12. **Uranium Hexafluoride**: `mekanism:chemical_infuser`; `mekanism:chemical_oxidizer` *(after: Yellowcake, Hydrofluoric Acid)*
   > What: UF₆ is the only uranium compound that becomes a gas at low temperature (it sublimes at 56 °C), so it can be spun.
   > Chemistry: real plants go UO₂ → UF₄ → UF₆ with HF and then fluorine. Mekanism combines the steps.
   > How: Chemical Infuser: uranium oxide + hydrofluoric acid → uranium hexafluoride.
13. **Isotope Separation**: `mekanism:isotopic_centrifuge` *(after: Uranium Hexafluoride)*
   > What: the two isotopes behave identically in every reaction, so chemistry can't separate them.
   > Chemistry: U-235 is about 1% lighter. Spin UF₆ fast enough and the heavier molecules drift outward.
   > How: Isotopic Centrifuge: uranium hexafluoride → fissile fuel.
14. **Reactor Parts**: 4x `mekanismgenerators:fission_fuel_assembly`; `mekanismgenerators:control_rod_assembly`; 4x `mekanismgenerators:fission_reactor_port` *(after: Isotope Separation)*
   > What: fuel assemblies hold the fuel, control rods absorb neutrons, and ports move fuel, coolant and waste.
   > Chemistry: each split releases 2 to 3 neutrons. Absorb too few and the chain reaction runs away.
   > How: check JEI for each part. Plan for water or sodium coolant and somewhere for the steam to go.
15. **Fission**: 16x `mekanismgenerators:fission_reactor_casing` *(after: Reactor Parts)*
   > What: split heavy nuclei and release enormous energy. Mind the temperature, and the waste.
   > Chemistry: U-235 + n → two lighter nuclei + 2-3 n + about 200 MeV, millions of times more than any chemical bond.
   > How: Just Enough Mekanism Multiblocks shows the layout in JEI. Pair it with a turbine (see Power).
   > Tip: wire a Logic Adapter to SCRAM the reactor if it overheats.
16. **Plutonium**: `mekanism:pellet_plutonium` *(after: Fission)*
   > Chemistry: U-238 captures a neutron and beta-decays twice into Pu-239, which is itself fissile.
   > How: Isotopic Centrifuge: 10 nuclear waste → 1 plutonium. Pressurized Reaction Chamber: plutonium + fluorite dust + water → pellet.
   > Tip: the pellet reaction also gives spent nuclear waste. Store it in a Radioactive Waste Barrel.
17. **Polonium**: `mekanism:solar_neutron_activator`; `mekanism:pellet_polonium` *(after: Fission)*
   > Chemistry: polonium-210 is made by neutron capture; it is intensely radioactive and gives off heat on its own.
   > How: Solar Neutron Activator (needs open sky and daylight): 10 nuclear waste → 1 polonium. Pellets come from the Pressurized Reaction Chamber, as with plutonium.
   > Tip: polonium is the input for the SPS and for Energy Cores.
18. **Transmutation by Neutron**: `mekanism:pellet_plutonium`; `mekanism:pellet_polonium` *(after: Plutonium, Polonium)*
   > The old alchemists wanted to change one element into another. You do it before breakfast.
   > What: reprocessing nuclear waste gives plutonium and polonium, two elements that barely exist in nature.
   > How: keep both pellet lines running. Plutonium feeds the quantum tier; polonium feeds antimatter.
   > Tip: spent waste still goes in a Radioactive Waste Barrel. Never dump it.
19. **Chamber Frame**: 16x `alchemistry:reactor_casing`; `alchemistry:reactor_energy`; `alchemistry:reactor_input`; `alchemistry:reactor_output` *(after: EV: Processing Units)*
   > What: Alchemistry's fission and fusion chambers share the same casing, energy, input and output blocks.
   > How: craft the frame blocks (check JEI) and build the chamber around its controller. The controllers themselves are crafted with a reactor casing.
   > Tip: build both chambers side by side so one can feed the other.
20. **Element Transmutation**: `alchemistry:fission_chamber_controller`; `alchemistry:fusion_chamber_controller` *(after: Chamber Frame)*
   > What: the fission chamber splits one element into two lighter ones; the fusion chamber merges two into a heavier one.
   > Chemistry: atomic numbers simply add or divide: fuse sodium (11) with chlorine (17) and you get nickel (28).
   > How: both controllers need a processing unit; fusion also needs a Nether Star.
   > Tip: with both, every element is within reach.
21. **The Complete Periodic Table**: 16x `chemlib:uranium`; `chemlib:oganesson` *(after: Element Transmutation)*
   > Oganesson. It barely exists in reality, but you made one.
   > What: the heaviest element on the table, made by fusing lighter ones.
   > Chemistry: real oganesson has been made only a handful of times, and each atom lasted under a millisecond.
   > How: fuse your way up in the Fusion Chamber (check JEI for which pairs reach 118).
   > Every block in this world can now be built from chemicals. You're close to what you once were.
22. **Wireless Power**: `fluxnetworks:flux_core` *(after: EV: Processing Units)*
   > What: Flux Networks moves energy anywhere in a dimension, or across them.
   > How: the Flux Core needs a processing unit in this pack. Add plugs at sources and points at machines.
   > Tip: set a password on your network if you share a server.
23. **Haber-Bosch**: `chemlib:ammonia_bucket` *(after: The Chemical Reactor, HV: Digital Circuits)*
   > Feeding a world used to take a harvest god. Now it takes a reactor and some hydrogen.
   > What: ammonia is the root of fertiliser, nitric acid and ammonium salts.
   > Chemistry: N₂ + 3H₂ → 2NH₃. Breaking nitrogen's triple bond is hard; this process feeds about half the world through fertiliser.
   > How: Chemical Reactor: 1 B nitrogen + 3 B hydrogen → 2 B ammonia. Fill a bucket from the output tank.
24. **Ostwald Process**: `chemlib:nitric_acid_bucket` *(after: Haber-Bosch)*
   > Chemistry: NH₃ + 2O₂ → HNO₃ + H₂O. Real plants burn ammonia over a platinum gauze, the very metal you're about to extract with it.
   > How: Chemical Reactor: 1 B ammonia + 2 B oxygen → 1 B nitric acid + 1 B water.
   > Tip: the water by-product can go back into your electrolysis line.
25. **Anode Sludge**: 4x `kubejs:pgm_sludge` *(after: Ostwald Process)*
   > What: refining nickel and copper electrically leaves a sludge rich in platinum-group metals.
   > Chemistry: noble metals don't dissolve at the anode, so they fall to the bottom of the cell.
   > How: Electrolyzer: 4 nickel or copper dust → 4 dust back + PGM sludge (25% chance).
   > Tip: it costs you nothing but power. Leave it running.
26. **Ammonium Chloride**: 4x `chemlib:ammonium_chloride_dust` *(after: Haber-Bosch)*
   > Chemistry: NH₃ + HCl → NH₄Cl. Open bottles of each near one another and a white smoke of this salt forms in the air.
   > How: Chemical Reactor: 1 B ammonia + 1 B hydrochloric acid → 1 ammonium chloride.
   > Tip: you need 2 per precipitation, so make plenty.
27. **Aqua Regia**: 2x `kubejs:chloroplatinic_acid`; `kubejs:pgm_residue` *(after: Anode Sludge)*
   > Chemistry: 3 HCl : 1 HNO₃. Nitric acid oxidises the platinum and chloride grabs the ions, forming chloroplatinic acid, H₂PtCl₆.
   > How: Chemical Reactor: 4 PGM sludge + 3 B HCl + 1 B nitric acid → 2 chloroplatinic acid + 1 PGM residue.
   > Tip: iridium, osmium, rhodium and ruthenium don't dissolve. Keep the residue.
28. **Precipitation**: 2x `kubejs:ammonium_hexachloroplatinate` *(after: Aqua Regia, Ammonium Chloride)*
   > Chemistry: ammonium chloride makes the platinum fall out as yellow ammonium hexachloroplatinate, (NH₄)₂PtCl₆.
   > How: Chemical Reactor: 1 chloroplatinic acid + 2 ammonium chloride → 1 ammonium hexachloroplatinate.
   > Tip: this step is why real platinum refining needs both the Haber-Bosch and Ostwald plants.
29. **The Platinum Line**: 4x `kubejs:pgm_sludge`; 2x `kubejs:chloroplatinic_acid`; 4x `modern_industrialization:platinum_dust` *(after: Precipitation)*
   > You remember metals that never tarnish. Now you know why: they refuse to react.
   > Chemistry: heating (calcining) drives off ammonia and HCl and leaves pure platinum metal.
   > How: Electric Blast Furnace: 2 ammonium hexachloroplatinate → 1 platinum dust.
   > Tip: processing unit boards need 4 platinum plates each. Automate this line.
30. **The Rare Four**: `modern_industrialization:iridium_dust`; `mekanism:dust_osmium`; `chemlib:rhodium_dust`; `chemlib:ruthenium_dust` *(after: The Platinum Line)*
   > What: the insoluble residue holds the rarest metals on Earth.
   > Chemistry: osmium is the densest element known, and iridium the most corrosion-resistant metal.
   > How: Centrifuge the PGM residue. Each output is a chance, so run plenty.
   > Tip: iridium is needed for monocrystalline silicon and, later, quantum circuit boards.
31. **Dress for the Job**: `mekanism:hazmat_mask`; `mekanism:hazmat_gown`; `mekanism:hazmat_pants`; `mekanism:hazmat_boots`; `mekanism:geiger_counter` *(after: Isotope Separation)*
   > How: check JEI for each piece.
   > What: a full hazmat suit blocks radiation; the Geiger counter shows how much is around you.
   > Tip: a leaking waste pipe can make an area dangerous for a long time. Check before you build.
32. **Closing the Fuel Cycle**: 4x `mekanism:reprocessed_fissile_fragment` *(after: Plutonium)*
   > Chemistry: plutonium-239 is fissile, so spent fuel can be recycled into new fuel, as some real reactors do.
   > How: Chemical Injection Chamber: plutonium pellet + hydrogen chloride → 4 fragments. Oxidize them into fissile fuel.
   > Tip: it means less waste and more fuel from every uranium ingot.
33. **The Unstable Ones**: `chemlib:technetium`; `chemlib:promethium`; `chemlib:astatine`; `chemlib:francium` *(after: Element Transmutation)*
   > What: four elements that real chemists almost never get to hold.
   > Chemistry: technetium (43) and promethium (61) are the only light elements with no stable isotope. Astatine and francium are the rarest natural elements: grams exist in the whole crust.
   > How: fuse or split your way to each atomic number (check JEI).
34. **Platinum Reserve**: 32x `modern_industrialization:platinum_ingot` *(after: The Platinum Line)*
   > How: keep the Platinum Line running, then smelt the dust.
   > What: half a stack of platinum ingots, all pulled out of sludge.
   > Tip: every processing unit board eats 4 platinum plates. You'll be glad of the reserve.

### VIII. Creation

*Leave the cradle. Make matter from energy. Seed a new world.*

1. **Leaving the Cradle**: `ad_astra:nasa_workbench` *(after: EV: Processing Units)*
   > You remember the stars up close. Time to stop looking at them from the ground.
   > What: the NASA Workbench assembles every Ad Astra rocket.
   > How: in this pack it needs an EV (Highly Advanced) hull, so processing units come first.
2. **Rocket Parts**: `ad_astra:rocket_nose_cone`; 4x `ad_astra:rocket_fin`; 2x `ad_astra:steel_tank`; `ad_astra:steel_engine` *(after: Leaving the Cradle)*
   > What: a Tier 1 rocket needs a nose cone, 4 fins, 2 steel tanks, a steel engine and 6 steel blocks.
   > Chemistry: a rocket carries its own oxidiser as well as fuel: there's no air to burn in once you're up.
   > How: all parts are crafted from steel plates and rods (check JEI). The nose cone uses a lightning rod.
3. **Breathe**: `ad_astra:space_helmet`; `ad_astra:oxygen_loader` *(after: Leaving the Cradle)*
   > What: the Oxygen Loader fills your space suit's tank.
   > Chemistry: it accepts plain water, but 100 mB of water gives only 4 mB of oxygen. Pure O₂ from your electrolysis line (2H₂O → 2H₂ + O₂) goes in 1:1.
   > How: pipe MI or Mekanism oxygen into the loader, power it, and put the suit in the slot.
4. **The Full Suit**: `ad_astra:space_suit`; `ad_astra:space_pants`; `ad_astra:space_boots` *(after: Breathe)*
   > What: the full set keeps you alive in vacuum and in the cold of the Moon.
   > How: check JEI for each piece. Wear all four before launch.
   > Tip: the chest piece holds the oxygen. Watch the gauge on your HUD.
5. **Launch Pad**: `ad_astra:launch_pad` *(after: Rocket Parts)*
   > What: rockets are placed on a launch pad, not on the bare ground.
   > How: craft it (check JEI) and place it in the open, with clear sky above.
   > Tip: build a pad on each world you visit so you always have somewhere to land and relaunch.
6. **Rocket Fuel**: 3x `modern_industrialization:diesel_bucket` *(after: Leaving the Cradle, Diesel Power)*
   > What: in this pack rockets run on MI diesel (biodiesel works too). Ad Astra's own oil refining is removed.
   > Chemistry: a kilogram of diesel releases about 45 MJ when it burns: more than ten times a good battery.
   > How: fill the rocket from buckets or pipes (check its GUI for how much it holds).
   > Tip: bring spare fuel for the trip home.
7. **One Small Step**: `ad_astra:tier_1_rocket`; visit `ad_astra:moon` *(after: Launch Pad, Rocket Fuel, The Full Suit)*
   > The Moon looks smaller than you remember. Or perhaps you were larger.
   > How: build the rocket in the NASA Workbench, place it on the pad, fuel it, climb in and launch. Pick the Moon on the planet screen.
   > Tip: place a bed or waystone near your landing site before exploring.
8. **Desh**: 8x `ad_astra:raw_desh`; 4x `ad_astra:desh_plate` *(after: One Small Step)*
   > What: desh is Ad Astra's lunar metal, needed for the Tier 2 rocket and oxygen equipment.
   > How: mine desh ore on the Moon, smelt it, and press the ingots into plates in Ad Astra's Compressor.
   > Tip: the Moon also has cheese ore. Really.
9. **A Breathable Room**: `ad_astra:oxygen_distributor` *(after: Desh)*
   > What: the Oxygen Distributor fills a sealed room with air, so you can take off your helmet.
   > How: built from an Oxygen Loader, a large gas tank, fans, an oxygen gear and desh plates. Feed it oxygen and power.
   > Tip: any gap in the walls lets the air out. Seal the room completely, doors included.
10. **Supercritical Phase Shifter**: 2x `mekanism:sps_port`; `mekanism:supercharged_coil` *(after: Transmutation by Neutron)*
   > What: the SPS is Mekanism's largest machine. It needs huge amounts of power.
   > How: Just Enough Mekanism Multiblocks shows the full layout in JEI. Ports feed polonium in and power the supercharged coil.
   > Tip: feed it from a fission reactor and turbine, or several Flux Networks plugs.
11. **Bottling Antimatter**: `mekanism:chemical_crystallizer` *(after: Supercritical Phase Shifter)*
   > What: the SPS outputs antimatter as a gas. The Chemical Crystallizer packs 1000 mB of it into one pellet.
   > How: pipe antimatter from the SPS output port into the crystallizer.
   > Tip: pipe the SPS straight into the crystallizer so the antimatter never sits in a tank.
12. **Antimatter**: 4x `mekanism:sps_casing`; `mekanism:pellet_antimatter` *(after: One Small Step, Bottling Antimatter)*
   > Matter from nothing but energy. This is the first thing you've made that you once made too.
   > Chemistry: when matter meets antimatter both vanish into pure energy, E = mc². One gram of each releases about 1.8 × 10¹⁴ J.
   > How: SPS → antimatter gas → Chemical Crystallizer → antimatter pellet.
   > Tip: it's the most energy-dense substance there is. Antimatter Cores need four pellets each.
13. **Empty Energy Core**: `kubejs:energy_core_empty` *(after: Transmutation by Neutron)*
   > What: the vessel for the most concentrated energy you can hold.
   > Chemistry: polonium gives off so much heat by decay that a gram of it glows. Here it seeds the core.
   > How: craft: 4 stainless steel plates + 4 polonium pellets + a processing unit in the centre.
   > Tip: the Complete Periodic Table quest rewards one empty core.
14. **Charge the Core**: `kubejs:energy_core` *(after: Empty Energy Core)*
   > What: an empty core holds nothing until you charge it with raw electrical energy.
   > How: MI Assembler at 512 EU/t for 6000 ticks: about 3 million EU per core.
   > Tip: give it its own power line. A charging core will starve every other machine on the network.
15. **Antimatter Core**: `kubejs:energy_core`; `kubejs:antimatter_core` *(after: Charge the Core, Antimatter)*
   > You feel it humming in your hand. You remember being this.
   > What: a charged core, upgraded with antimatter and a quantum circuit.
   > How: MI Assembler at 2048 EU/t for 6000 ticks: charged Energy Core + 4 antimatter pellets + 1 quantum circuit.
   > Tip: the Seed needs eight. Start a production line, not a one-off.
16. **Made of Cheese**: `ad_astra:cheese` *(after: One Small Step)*
   > What: the old rumour is partly true: the Moon has cheese ore.
   > How: mine moon cheese ore and smelt it (or macerate it in MI).
   > Tip: it's food. Lunar picnic, anyone?
17. **Low Earth Orbit**: visit `ad_astra:earth_orbit` *(after: One Small Step)*
   > What: every planet has an orbit dimension above it, a good place for a space station.
   > How: pick Earth Orbit on the rocket's planet screen.
   > Tip: bring building blocks and a way home.
18. **Grand Tour**: visit `ad_astra:moon`; visit `ad_astra:mars`; visit `ad_astra:venus`; visit `ad_astra:mercury`; visit `ad_astra:glacio` *(after: One Small Step)*
   > What: the Moon (Tier 1 rocket), Mars (Tier 2), Venus and Mercury (Tier 3) and Glacio (Tier 4).
   > How: a dimension task completes on arrival. Plan fuel and oxygen for each trip.
   > Tip: Venus and Mercury are hot enough to cook an ordinary space suit. Bring the netherite one.
19. **Heat-Proof Suit**: `ad_astra:netherite_space_helmet`; `ad_astra:netherite_space_suit` *(after: The Full Suit, Mars)*
   > What: the netherite space suit survives the heat of Venus and Mercury.
   > What: the netherite space suit survives the heat of Venus and Mercury.
   > How: the chest piece is a netherite chestplate with ostrum plates (from Mars), gas tanks and an oxygen gear. Check JEI for the rest.
20. **Glacio**: `ad_astra:tier_4_rocket`; visit `ad_astra:glacio` *(after: Venus)*
   > What: Glacio orbits another star and needs a Tier 4 rocket built from calorite.
   > How: calorite comes from Venus. The tanks, engine and blocks are all calorite (check JEI).
   > Tip: it's the only planet outside our solar system. Take a look at the sky.
21. **Antiprotonic Nucleosynthesizer**: `mekanism:antiprotonic_nucleosynthesizer` *(after: Antimatter)*
   > What: Mekanism's late-game machine uses antimatter to rebuild one item into another.
   > How: check JEI for the recipe and for what it can transmute.
   > Tip: it uses antimatter as fuel, so it needs your SPS running.

### The Seed of a New World

*Every tier in one 9x9 grid. The act of making a world.*

1. **The Last Recipe**: checkmark *(after: Leaving the Cradle)*
   > You remember now what all of this was for. Not machines. A world.
   > What: the Seed of a New World is the pack's final craft: a 9x9 Ultimate Table recipe that uses something from every tier you've climbed.
   > How: each line on this page gathers one ingredient. The checklist quests next to the centre hold the exact counts.
   > Tip: you can start the cheap lines (coke, water, acid, plastic) right away. The Antimatter Cores at the bottom take the longest.
   > {@pagebreak}
   > The full recipe: 4 coke, 8 ChemLib water, 4 ChemLib hydrogen, 8 sulfuric acid buckets, 16 polyethylene buckets, 8 processing units, 4 stainless steel plates, 8 oganesson, 4 plutonium pellets, 4 polonium pellets, 4 Nether Stars, 8 Antimatter Cores and 1 Dragon Egg. 81 slots, every one filled.
   > Why these: read them in order and they retell the climb: fire (coke), the elements (water, hydrogen), the Acid Age, the Oil Age, the machine ages, the atomic age, and the energy you learned to bottle at the end.
   > Where: the Extended Crafting Ultimate Table (9x9). JEI shows the layout; press U on the Seed.
2. **Coke**: 4x `modern_industrialization:coke` *(after: The Last Recipe)*
   > What: the four corners of the Seed are coke, the first thing your coke oven ever made.
   > Chemistry: coke is coal baked without air. Heat drives off water, tar and coal gas, leaving a hard, porous lump that is mostly carbon.
   > How: any coke oven or MI Coke Oven. Four pieces.
   > Tip: it's fitting that the last recipe starts with the first fuel.
   > {@pagebreak}
   > Deep dive: why coke, not coal?
   > Raw coal is full of volatile hydrocarbons and sulfur. In a blast furnace they would boil off, foul the iron and choke the burden.
   > Coking (about 1000 °C, no oxygen) cooks them out. What's left is strong enough to carry the weight of ore above it and burns hot and clean.
   > In the furnace, coke does two jobs: it burns for heat, and its carbon monoxide strips oxygen from iron oxide: Fe₂O₃ + 3CO → 2Fe + 3CO₂.
3. **Hydrogen, Element 1**: 12x `chemlib:hydrogen` *(after: The Last Recipe)*
   > What: the Seed needs 4 hydrogen on its own, and 8 water needs 16 more hydrogen to build. Start with a dozen and keep the Dissolver running.
   > Chemistry: one proton and one electron. Hydrogen makes up about three quarters of all the ordinary matter in the universe by mass.
   > How: the Alchemistry Dissolver breaks ChemLib water into 2 hydrogen + 1 oxygen. Other compounds give hydrogen too (check JEI).
   > Tip: it's the same element you used for the Potion of Flying back in Chapter II.
   > {@pagebreak}
   > Deep dive: the first element
   > Almost all the hydrogen in existence was made in the first few minutes after the Big Bang. Every other element was built from it later, inside stars.
   > Stars fuse hydrogen into helium. Bigger stars go on to carbon, oxygen and up to iron. Heavier elements need supernovae and neutron-star collisions.
   > So hydrogen is the right place to start a new world: it's where the old one started too.
4. **Synthesized Water**: 8x `chemlib:water` *(after: Hydrogen, Element 1)*
   > What: 8 ChemLib water, made from elements rather than scooped from a lake.
   > Chemistry: 2H₂ + O₂ → 2H₂O. The reaction releases a lot of energy: it's what powers a hydrogen fuel cell.
   > How: Alchemistry Combiner: 2 hydrogen + 1 oxygen → 1 water.
   > Tip: the Dissolver gives you the oxygen along with the hydrogen, so nothing is wasted.
   > {@pagebreak}
   > Deep dive: why build water at all?
   > You could pour a bucket. The point of the Seed is that every part of a world can be made from its elements, and water is the most important compound of all.
   > Water's bent shape (104.5° between the two O-H bonds) makes it polar. That polarity is why it dissolves salts, why ice floats, and why it holds so much heat.
   > No water, no life. It's on the Seed eight times.
5. **Sulfur**: 16x `modern_industrialization:sulfur_dust` *(after: The Last Recipe)*
   > What: eight buckets of acid need 16 sulfur dust.
   > Chemistry: sulfur is a yellow non-metal. Most of the world's supply comes from scrubbing it out of oil and gas, much as you do in petrochemistry.
   > How: sulfuric purification of crude oil and fuels in the MI Chemical Reactor, or from ore processing byproducts (check JEI).
6. **The King of Chemicals**: `modern_industrialization:sulfuric_acid_bucket` *(after: Sulfur)*
   > What: the acid that unlocked 5x ore processing. The Seed needs eight buckets.
   > Chemistry: S + O₂ → SO₂, then 2SO₂ + O₂ → 2SO₃, then SO₃ + H₂O → H₂SO₄. MI's Chemical Reactor does it in one step from sulfur, oxygen and water.
   > How: Chemical Reactor: 1 sulfur dust + 200 mB oxygen + 200 mB water → 500 mB sulfuric acid.
   > Tip: one reactor makes the full 8000 mB in a few minutes.
   > {@pagebreak}
   > Deep dive: the Contact Process
   > Industry makes sulfuric acid by the contact process. Burning sulfur gives SO₂; a vanadium(V) oxide catalyst at about 450 °C turns it into SO₃.
   > SO₃ isn't simply added to water: the reaction is so violent it makes a mist of acid. It's absorbed into concentrated acid first (making oleum, H₂S₂O₇), then diluted.
   > More sulfuric acid is made than any other chemical. A country's output of it was once used as a measure of its industrial strength.
7. **Ethylene**: `modern_industrialization:ethylene_bucket` *(after: The Last Recipe)*
   > What: the feedstock for polyethylene. 16 buckets of plastic need about 11.5 buckets of ethylene.
   > Chemistry: ethylene has a carbon-carbon double bond. That bond is what lets thousands of molecules link into a chain.
   > How: steam-crack naphtha or light fuel and distil the result, or dehydrate ethanol in the Chemical Reactor (check JEI).
8. **Polymerise**: `modern_industrialization:polyethylene_bucket` *(after: Ethylene)*
   > What: the Seed uses 16 buckets of polyethylene, more than any other ingredient.
   > Chemistry: n C₂H₄ → (–CH₂–CH₂–)ₙ. The double bond opens and each monomer joins the next.
   > How: Chemical Reactor: 500 mB ethylene + a tiny pile of chromium (or lead) dust → 700 mB polyethylene.
   > Tip: the chromium is a catalyst in real life too (see the next page).
   > {@pagebreak}
   > Deep dive: catalysts that make plastic
   > Most of the world's high-density polyethylene is made with chromium catalysts (the Phillips process) or titanium-aluminium ones (Ziegler-Natta).
   > The metal centre grabs an ethylene molecule and slots it into the growing chain, again and again. The result is a long, straight chain that packs tightly: strong, stiff plastic.
   > Without a catalyst you need huge pressures and get a branched, softer plastic (LDPE). Same formula, different shape, different material.
9. **Eight Processing Units**: 8x `modern_industrialization:processing_unit` *(after: The Last Recipe)*
   > What: processing units are MI's EV circuits. The Seed takes eight, and each Energy Core needs one more.
   > Chemistry: they're built on PVC boards. PVC is polyethylene with a chlorine atom on every other carbon, which makes it harder and flame-resistant.
   > How: MI Assembler (check JEI). Queue sixteen: eight for the Seed and eight for the cores.
   > Tip: processing units are slow. Start them first and let them run while you do the other lines.
10. **Stainless Steel**: 36x `modern_industrialization:stainless_steel_plate` *(after: The Last Recipe)*
   > What: 4 plates for the Seed itself and 4 for each of the 8 Energy Core shells: 36 in all.
   > Chemistry: stainless steel is iron with at least 10.5% chromium, often with nickel. The chromium forms a thin Cr₂O₃ film that heals itself when scratched.
   > How: plates from stainless steel ingots in the Compressor. Ingots from the Electric Blast Furnace (check JEI).
   > {@pagebreak}
   > Deep dive: passivation
   > Ordinary iron rusts because its oxide flakes off and exposes fresh metal. Chromium oxide does the opposite: it's a dense layer a few nanometres thick that sticks.
   > Scratch it and the exposed chromium reacts with oxygen in the air and seals the gap within moments. That's why stainless steel stays shiny.
   > Nickel keeps the steel in its austenite form at room temperature: tough, non-magnetic and easy to work.
11. **Oganesson ×8**: 8x `chemlib:oganesson` *(after: The Last Recipe, The Complete Periodic Table)*
   > What: the last element on the periodic table. You made one to finish the table; the Seed wants eight.
   > Chemistry: oganesson-294 has a half-life of under a millisecond. Only a handful of atoms have ever been made.
   > How: Alchemistry Fusion Chamber: fuse two elements whose atomic numbers add up to 118 (check JEI for the pairs).
   > Tip: automate the inputs. Eight by hand is a long afternoon.
   > {@pagebreak}
   > Deep dive: making element 118
   > In 2002 a team in Dubna, Russia fired calcium-48 ions (20 protons) at a californium-249 target (98 protons). 20 + 98 = 118.
   > Over months of beam time they saw about three atoms. Each decayed by alpha emission within a millisecond, into livermorium (116).
   > It sits in the noble gas column, but it may not behave like one: its electrons move so fast that relativity smears out its shells. Your Fusion Chamber does the same addition, rather more reliably.
12. **Plutonium Pellets**: 4x `mekanism:pellet_plutonium` *(after: The Last Recipe)*
   > What: four plutonium pellets for the Seed.
   > Chemistry: uranium-238 in a reactor captures a neutron and decays twice: U-238 → U-239 → Np-239 → Pu-239.
   > How: Mekanism: nuclear waste → Isotopic Centrifuge → plutonium → Chemical Crystallizer → pellet.
   > Tip: a running fission reactor makes waste whether you want it or not. This is a good use for it.
13. **Polonium Pellets**: 36x `mekanism:pellet_polonium` *(after: The Last Recipe)*
   > What: 4 pellets for the Seed and 4 for each of the 8 Energy Core shells: 36 in all. Polonium also feeds the SPS that makes antimatter.
   > Chemistry: Marie and Pierre Curie found polonium in 1898 and named it after Poland. Po-210 is so radioactive that a gram of it glows from its own heat.
   > How: Mekanism: nuclear waste → Solar Neutron Activator → polonium → Chemical Crystallizer → pellet.
   > Tip: activators only work in daylight. Build a row of them, not one.
14. **The Wither**: kill 1x `minecraft:wither` *(after: The Last Recipe)*
   > What: every Nether Star comes from a Wither. The Seed needs four.
   > How: soul sand T-shape, three wither skeleton skulls on top. Fight it somewhere you don't mind losing.
   > Tip: fight it underground or in a sealed box so it can't fly off, and bring milk for the wither effect.
15. **The Dragon**: kill 1x `minecraft:ender_dragon` *(after: The Last Recipe)*
   > What: the Dragon Egg is the Seed's centre slot.
   > How: kill the Ender Dragon. The egg appears on top of the exit portal. Push it off with a piston or knock it onto a torch; clicking it makes it teleport.
   > Tip: if someone on the server already took the egg, there's another way. Look into the Antiprotonic Nucleosynthesizer.
16. **Thirty-Two Antimatter Pellets**: 32x `mekanism:pellet_antimatter` *(after: The Last Recipe, Antimatter)*
   > What: each Antimatter Core takes 4 antimatter pellets, so eight cores need 32.
   > Chemistry: an antiproton has the proton's mass and the opposite charge. When one meets a proton both vanish into energy (and a spray of lighter particles).
   > How: SPS → antimatter gas → Chemical Crystallizer. Keep both running non-stop.
   > Tip: a second SPS halves the wait, if you can feed it the polonium and the power.
   > {@pagebreak}
   > Deep dive: how much energy?
   > E = mc². Annihilating 1 gram of antimatter with 1 gram of matter releases about 1.8 × 10¹⁴ J, roughly the energy of a 43-kiloton explosion.
   > Real labs (CERN's Antiproton Decelerator) have made only nanograms in decades, and keep them for minutes at most in magnetic traps.
   > Your SPS is far kinder than physics, but it still asks for a lot of power. That's the price of reversing every charge in an atom.
17. **Eight Quantum Circuits**: 8x `modern_industrialization:quantum_circuit` *(after: The Last Recipe)*
   > What: MI's top circuit. Each Antimatter Core needs one.
   > How: MI Assembler (check JEI). Quantum circuit boards come first.
   > Tip: quantum circuits also build the fusion reactor and the quantum armour in the Creation chapter. Make spares.
18. **Two Cores**: 2x `kubejs:antimatter_core` *(after: Antimatter Core, Thirty-Two Antimatter Pellets, Eight Quantum Circuits)*
   > What: you've made one Antimatter Core. Make it two.
   > How: Empty Energy Core (4 stainless plates, 4 polonium pellets, 1 processing unit) → MI Assembler at 512 EU/t to charge it → MI Assembler at 2048 EU/t with 4 antimatter pellets and 1 quantum circuit.
   > Tip: two assemblers, one for each step, let the cores flow without you.
19. **Four Cores**: 4x `kubejs:antimatter_core` *(after: Two Cores)*
   > What: four Antimatter Cores. The Seed's diamond of power is half built.
   > How: keep the line running. Each core is about 3 million EU to charge and 12 million more to upgrade.
   > Tip: if your network sags while the assemblers run, give them their own generator.
   > {@pagebreak}
   > Deep dive: the energy budget
   > Charging: 512 EU/t × 6000 ticks = about 3.1 million EU. Upgrading: 2048 EU/t × 6000 ticks = about 12.3 million EU.
   > That's about 15 million EU per core, or about 123 million EU for all eight, before counting the SPS that makes the antimatter.
   > A fission reactor with a turbine, or a Mekanism fusion reactor, makes this a matter of hours. A wall of solar panels makes it a matter of weeks.
20. **Fire, Water, Hydrogen**: 4x `modern_industrialization:coke`; 8x `chemlib:water`; 4x `chemlib:hydrogen` *(after: Coke, Synthesized Water)*
   > What: the Seed's corners and edges hold the first things you learned: coke from your coke oven, and water and hydrogen synthesized as chemicals.
   > How: 4 coke, 8 ChemLib water and 4 ChemLib hydrogen, all held at once.
   > Tip: all three are cheap now. Make spares.
21. **Eight Buckets of Acid**: 8x `modern_industrialization:sulfuric_acid_bucket` *(after: The King of Chemicals)*
   > What: 8 buckets of sulfuric acid, the chemical that unlocked 5x ore processing.
   > How: fill buckets from your sulfuric acid tanks.
   > Tip: buckets don't stack. Clear inventory space first, or keep them in a backpack.
22. **Sixteen Buckets of Plastic**: 16x `modern_industrialization:polyethylene_bucket` *(after: Polymerise)*
   > What: 16 buckets of polyethylene, the most of any ingredient.
   > How: polymerise ethylene in the Chemical Reactor, as in Petrochemistry.
   > Tip: fill them straight from your polymerisation reactor's output.
23. **Circuits and Steel**: 8x `modern_industrialization:processing_unit`; 4x `modern_industrialization:stainless_steel_plate` *(after: Eight Processing Units, Stainless Steel)*
   > What: 8 processing units and 4 stainless steel plates.
   > How: both come from lines you already run. Keep these apart from the ones meant for Energy Cores.
   > Tip: processing units are slow to make, so start these early.
24. **Heavy Elements**: 8x `chemlib:oganesson`; 4x `mekanism:pellet_plutonium`; 4x `mekanism:pellet_polonium` *(after: Antimatter Core, The Complete Periodic Table, Oganesson ×8, Plutonium Pellets, Polonium Pellets)*
   > What: 8 oganesson, 4 plutonium pellets and 4 polonium pellets.
   > How: oganesson from the Fusion Chamber; pellets from your reactor's waste.
   > Tip: keep 4 polonium pellets aside. The core shells will happily eat them all.
25. **Stars and an Egg**: 4x `minecraft:nether_star`; `minecraft:dragon_egg` *(after: The Wither, The Dragon)*
   > What: 4 Nether Stars, and the Dragon Egg at the very centre.
   > How: defeat the Wither four times; kill the Ender Dragon for the egg.
   > Tip: on a server, agree who crafts the Seed before anyone grabs the egg.
26. **Eight Antimatter Cores**: 8x `kubejs:antimatter_core` *(after: Antimatter Core, Four Cores)*
   > What: eight Antimatter Cores ring the centre of the recipe.
   > How: that's 32 antimatter pellets, 32 polonium pellets, 32 stainless plates, 8 processing units and 8 quantum circuits in total.
   > Eight suns in your pack. You remember carrying more.
27. **Seed of a New World**: `kubejs:seed_of_a_new_world` *(after: Antimatter Core, The Complete Periodic Table, 9x9: Ultimate Table, Fire, Water, Hydrogen, Eight Buckets of Acid, Sixteen Buckets of Plastic, Eight Antimatter Cores, Circuits and Steel, Heavy Elements, Stars and an Egg)*
   > Every lesson, in one 9x9 grid. You hold it, and it holds a world.
   > What: a recipe on the Ultimate Table that needs something from every tier.
   > How: 4 coke, 8 water, 4 hydrogen, 8 sulfuric acid, 16 polyethylene, 8 processing units, 4 stainless plates, 8 oganesson, 4 plutonium, 4 polonium, 4 Nether Stars, 8 Antimatter Cores, and the Dragon Egg at the centre.
   > Tip: check JEI for the exact layout before you fill the table. One wrong slot and nothing happens.
   > {@pagebreak}
   > Deep dive: reading the grid
   > The pattern is symmetrical in every direction. Coke holds the four corners. Water and acid line the outer ring. Plastic forms crosses on all four sides.
   > Stainless steel and processing units make an inner square. Oganesson, plutonium and Nether Stars sit closer in. Eight Antimatter Cores form a diamond around the centre.
   > And in the middle, one Dragon Egg: the only thing in the old world that was already a seed of something new.
28. **Ultimate Auto Table**: `extendedcrafting:ultimate_auto_table` *(after: The Last Recipe, 9x9: Ultimate Table)*
   > What: an auto-crafting version of the Ultimate Table. Set the recipe once, then pipe the ingredients in.
   > How: check JEI for the recipe. It needs power to craft.
   > Tip: useful if your team wants a second Seed, or for the other 9x9 recipes in the pack.
29. **The Bill of Materials**: checkmark *(after: The Last Recipe)*
   > What: a shopping list for the whole Seed, Energy Cores included.
   > How: read the next page, then tick this quest.
   > {@pagebreak}
   > Seed only: 4 coke, 8 water, 4 hydrogen, 8 acid buckets, 16 polyethylene buckets, 8 processing units, 4 stainless plates, 8 oganesson, 4 plutonium pellets, 4 polonium pellets, 4 Nether Stars, 1 Dragon Egg.
   > Eight cores: 32 stainless plates, 32 polonium pellets, 8 processing units, 32 antimatter pellets, 8 quantum circuits, about 123 million EU.
   > Grand total: 36 stainless plates, 36 polonium pellets, 16 processing units, 32 antimatter pellets, 8 quantum circuits, plus everything above.
   > In raw terms: 16 sulfur dust for the acid, 16 hydrogen and 8 oxygen for the water (plus 4 hydrogen), about 11.5 buckets of ethylene.
30. **Four Withers**: kill 4x `minecraft:wither` *(after: The Wither)*
   > What: kill the Wither four times, once for each Nether Star in the Seed.
   > How: a wither skeleton farm makes the skulls easy. Fight in a sealed bunker each time.
   > Tip: Looting on your sword raises the skull drop chance from wither skeletons.
31. **A Second Egg**: 2x `minecraft:dragon_egg` *(after: The Dragon)*
   > What: you found it: the Antiprotonic Nucleosynthesizer turns an ordinary egg into a Dragon Egg with a little antimatter.
   > Chemistry: nucleosynthesis means building heavier nuclei from lighter ones. Stars do it slowly. Mekanism does it with an egg and 4 mB of antimatter.
