25. **Tier Installers**: `mekanism:basic_tier_installer`; `mekanism:advanced_tier_installer` *(after: Basic Factory)*
   > What: right-click a placed machine with a Tier Installer to turn it into the next tier, keeping its contents and settings.
   > How: the basic installer needs a Basic Control Circuit; the advanced one needs an Advanced Control Circuit (MV).
   > Tip: a plain machine plus a basic installer becomes a basic factory.
26. **Precision Sawmill**: `mekanism:precision_sawmill` *(after: Mekanism Circuits)*
   > What: cuts logs into more planks than hand crafting, often with a bonus of sawdust, and breaks wooden items back down.
   > Chemistry: wood is about 40-50% cellulose (chains of glucose), with hemicellulose and lignin gluing the fibres together. Sawdust is the same polymers, finely divided.
   > How: iron, Infused Alloy, a Basic Control Circuit and a steel casing (check JEI).
   > Tip: sawdust feeds wood gasification in the PRC and the Cardboard Box.
27. **Factories for the Ore Line**: 1x any of `or(item(mekanism:basic_purifying_factory)item(mekanism:basic_injecting_factory)item(mekanism:advanced_purifying_factory)item(mekanism:advanced_injecting_factory)item(mekanism:elite_purifying_factory)item(mekanism:elite_injecting_factory)item(mekanism:ultimate_purifying_factory)item(mekanism:ultimate_injecting_factory))` *(after: Advanced Factory, 4x Ore: Hydrogen Chloride)*
   > What: a Purifying (O₂, 3x) or Injecting (HCl, 4x) Factory runs several ores at once. Ore processing is where factories pay off most.
   > Chemistry: the gas is consumed per operation: oxygen oxidises the ore surface into clumps, hydrogen chloride attacks it into shards. More slots means more gas, so size the separator to match.
   > How: build the factory from a Purification Chamber or Chemical Injection Chamber (both MV in this pack). Any tier and either type counts.
   > Tip: feed the gas into the factory's gas side with pressurized tubes, the same as the single machine.
28. **Combiner**: `mekanism:combiner` *(after: HV: Digital Circuits)*
   > What: the Combiner presses a main input together with a second block, for example flint + cobblestone into gravel, or 4 obsidian dust + cobbled deepslate back into obsidian.
   > Chemistry: pressing powders into a solid below their melting point is sintering; it's how ceramics and many metal parts are made.
   > How: Reinforced Alloy, Elite Control Circuits (HV) and a steel casing (check JEI). Press U on it in JEI for every recipe.
29. **Speed and Energy Upgrades**: 2x `mekanism:upgrade_speed`; 2x `mekanism:upgrade_energy` *(after: Basic Factory)*
   > What: slot them into any Mekanism machine. Speed upgrades make it work faster; energy upgrades cut its power use and enlarge its buffer.
   > How: Infused Alloy and glass around osmium dust (speed) or gold dust (energy). A machine takes up to 8 of each.
   > Tip: speed upgrades raise power use faster than they raise speed. Balance every speed upgrade with an energy upgrade.
30. **The Other Upgrades**: `mekanism:upgrade_filter`; `mekanism:upgrade_chemical`; `mekanism:upgrade_muffling`; `mekanism:upgrade_anchor`; `mekanism:upgrade_stone_generator` *(after: Speed and Energy Upgrades)*
   > What: the Filter upgrade lets the Electric Pump pick heavy water; Chemical cuts gas use in gas-fed machines; Muffling quiets machines; Anchor keeps a machine's chunk loaded.
   > Chemistry: the Stone Generator upgrade does what a cobble generator does: lava cooled by water solidifies into stone.
   > How: Infused Alloy and glass around tin dust, iron dust or diamond dust; wool for muffling; water and lava buckets for stone (check JEI).
   > Tip: a machine's tooltip lists which upgrades it accepts.
31. **Moving Day**: `mekanism:cardboard_box` *(after: Precision Sawmill)*
   > What: a Cardboard Box made from sawdust. Right-click almost any block (even a full chest or a spawner) to pack it up and carry it.
   > Chemistry: cardboard is pressed cellulose fibre, the same polymer that makes up most of the sawdust.
   > Tip: boxes can't pick up everything; blocks on the mod's blacklist won't pack.
32. **Challenge: Every Stage of Iron**: `mekanism:crystal_iron`; `mekanism:shard_iron`; `mekanism:clump_iron`; `mekanism:dirty_dust_iron` *(after: Factories for the Ore Line, 5x Ore: Dissolution)*
   > What: the full 5x chain, one stage of each: crystal (5x) → shard (4x) → clump (3x) → dirty dust → dust.
   > Chemistry: sulfuric acid dissolves the iron oxide into iron sulfate (Fe₂O₃ + 3H₂SO₄ → Fe₂(SO₄)₃ + 3H₂O). Washing rinses out insoluble rock, and crystallising pulls pure salt back out of solution.
   > How: dissolve, wash and crystallise, then inject crystals with HCl, purify shards with O₂, crush clumps. Dirty dust goes to the MI Centrifuge for byproducts.
   > Tip: each stage can also be fed raw ore directly, so a half-built line still gives 3x or 4x.
33. **Heating the Plant**: `mekanism:resistive_heater`; `mekanism:fuelwood_heater`; 8x `mekanism:basic_thermodynamic_conductor` *(after: Evaporation Ponds)*
   > What: the Resistive Heater turns FE into heat; the Fuelwood Heater burns furnace fuel. Thermodynamic Conductors carry the heat to where it's needed, such as a Thermal Evaporation Plant.
   > Chemistry: evaporation speeds up with temperature because more water molecules have enough energy to escape the surface. The conductors are copper in steel: copper conducts heat about 8 times better than steel.
   > How: heaters need a steel casing and a circuit or Energy Tablet; conductors are steel and copper (check JEI). Watch the plant's temperature in its GUI.
   > Tip: a hot plant in a desert biome runs much faster than a cold one in the snow.
34. **Salt and Sodium**: 16x `mekanism:salt` *(after: Heating the Plant, Mekanism Gases)*
   > What: brine from the evaporation plant crystallises into salt, and splits into sodium and chlorine in the Electrolytic Separator.
   > Chemistry: real sodium is made by electrolysing molten NaCl (2NaCl → 2Na + Cl₂, the Downs cell). Salt is also a classic HCl source: NaCl + H₂SO₄ → NaHSO₄ + HCl.
   > How: Chemical Crystallizer: 15 mB brine → 1 salt. Electrolytic Separator: brine → sodium + chlorine. Salt also counts as a hydrogen chloride source in an HCl machine's chemical item slot (press U on Salt in JEI).
   > Tip: sodium is the coolant for the best fission setups (see the generator band).
35. **Dynamic Tank**: 16x `mekanism:dynamic_tank`; 2x `mekanism:dynamic_valve`; 4x `mekanism:structural_glass` *(after: Mekanism Circuits)*
   > What: a hollow multiblock tank of any size from 3x3x3 up, for huge amounts of a fluid or a chemical.
   > How: dynamic tank blocks (steel and a bucket) for the frame, structural glass for walls, and valves to pipe in and out. The layout is in Just Enough Mekanism Multiblocks in JEI.
   > Tip: one dynamic tank block is also a part in the PRC and the Chemical Oxidizer.
36. **Pressurized Reaction Chamber**: `mekanism:pressurized_reaction_chamber` *(after: Mekanism Gases, Dynamic Tank)*
   > What: the PRC reacts an item, a fluid and a gas together under pressure. It makes substrate, HDPE, plutonium and polonium pellets, and gasifies coal and wood.
   > Chemistry: pressure pushes gas molecules closer together so they collide with the other reactants more often. Le Chatelier: a reaction that shrinks gas volume is favoured by high pressure.
   > How: Basic Control Circuits, Infused Alloy, steel, an Enrichment Chamber, a Dynamic Tank and chemical tanks (check JEI). Press U on it in JEI for every recipe.
   > Tip: it often makes two products at once (an item and a gas). Give each output side its own pipe.
37. **Substrate**: 16x `mekanism:substrate` *(after: Pressurized Reaction Chamber)*
   > What: substrate is Mekanism's polymer seed: a little of it, with ethene and oxygen, becomes HDPE.
   > Chemistry: plant matter can become ethylene: real bio-ethylene is made by dehydrating bioethanol (C₂H₅OH → C₂H₄ + H₂O). Polyethylene is then thousands of C₂H₄ units joined on a catalyst.
   > How: PRC: 2 bio fuel + water + hydrogen → substrate + ethene. Then substrate + water + ethene → 8 substrate (plus a little O₂). Check JEI for amounts.
   > Tip: MI ethylene is tagged as ethene in this pack, so your distillation tower can feed this line too.
38. **HDPE Parts**: 8x `mekanism:hdpe_sheet`; 2x `mekanism:hdpe_rod`; 4x `mekanism:hdpe_stick` *(after: Substrate, HDPE)*
   > What: HDPE pellets shaped into sheets, rods and sticks: parts for the Solar Neutron Activator, module bases, the MekaSuit and the Meka-Tool.
   > Chemistry: high-density polyethylene has straight chains with few branches, so they pack tightly. That makes it stiffer and stronger than the bendy LDPE in plastic bags.
   > How: PRC: substrate + ethene + oxygen → HDPE pellet (or use your MI line from the main path). Then shape pellets in the crafting grid (check JEI).
   > Tip: the MekaSuit alone eats dozens of sheets. Automate the pellets.
39. **Modules**: 4x `mekanism:module_base`; `mekanism:modification_station` *(after: HDPE Parts, EV: Processing Units, Polonium)*
   > What: module bases are the blank every MekaSuit and Meka-Tool module is built on. The Modification Station installs modules into your gear.
   > How: module base: an HDPE sheet in tin ingots and bronze nuggets (makes 2). Station: HDPE sheets, a chest, Ultimate Control Circuits (EV), a steel casing and a polonium pellet (check JEI).
   > Tip: press U on a Module Base in JEI to see every module and what each one needs.
40. **Meka-Tool**: `mekanism:meka_tool` *(after: Modules)*
   > What: the Atomic Disassembler grown up: one FE-powered tool that mines, digs, chops and fights, and takes modules for range, speed, silk touch, veinmining and more.
   > How: an Atomic Disassembler, a Configurator, HDPE sheets, Ultimate Control Circuits, polonium pellets and a Basic Induction Cell (check JEI).
   > Tip: it drinks power fast with big modules. Carry a charged energy cube or wear the MekaSuit.
41. **The Full MekaSuit**: `mekanism:mekasuit_pants`; `mekanism:mekasuit_boots` *(after: Modules, MekaSuit)*
   > Four pieces, one will. You remember standing in fire and feeling only warmth.
   > What: the MekaSuit legs and boots, completing the armour started on the Tools page. Legs take movement and locomotion modules; boots take jump and fall protection.
   > How: netherite leggings / boots in HDPE sheets, Ultimate Control Circuits, polonium pellets and a Basic Induction Cell (check JEI).
   > Tip: the full suit shares one energy pool through its modules. Charge it on a Chargepad or with a charging module.
42. **Pigments**: `mekanism:pigment_extractor`; `mekanism:painting_machine` *(after: Pressurized Reaction Chamber, MV: Aluminum)*
   > What: the Pigment Extractor pulls coloured pigment out of dyes; the Painting Machine applies it to blocks and items.
   > Chemistry: a pigment's colour comes from the light its molecules absorb: indigo absorbs orange-red light, so it looks blue.
   > How: the extractor needs Basic Circuits; the Painting Machine needs an Advanced Control Circuit (MV).
43. **Coal Gasification**: 16x `mekanism:dust_sulfur` *(after: Pressurized Reaction Chamber)*
   > What: the PRC can gasify coal: coal + water + oxygen → hydrogen gas, with the coal's sulfur left behind as sulfur dust.
   > Chemistry: the real water-gas reaction is C + H₂O → CO + H₂ at about 1,000 °C; a little oxygen burns part of the coal to keep it hot. Coal holds 0.5-5% sulfur, which refineries recover as elemental sulfur.
   > How: PRC with any coal or coal dust, water and O₂ (check JEI). Logs and sawdust gasify the same way into charcoal dust.
   > Tip: a handy extra sulfur source for the contact process.
44. **Challenge: The Full Chemical Plant**: `mekanism:chemical_infuser`; `mekanism:chemical_oxidizer`; `mekanism:electrolytic_separator`; `mekanism:pressurized_reaction_chamber`; `mekanism:chemical_washer`; `mekanism:chemical_crystallizer`; `mekanism:chemical_dissolution_chamber`; `mekanism:isotopic_centrifuge`; `mekanism:solar_neutron_activator`; `mekanism:sps_port` *(after: Pressurized Reaction Chamber, Supercritical Phase Shifter)*
   > What: one of each of Mekanism's ten chemical machines, from the Chemical Infuser to an SPS port.
   > Chemistry: together they cover the unit operations of a real chemical plant: reaction, oxidation, electrolysis, washing, crystallisation, dissolution and isotope separation.
   > How: most are on the main path (see the links on the left). The washer, crystallizer and dissolution chamber are MV in this pack; the centrifuge needs Ultimate Control Circuits (EV).
   > Tip: an optional collection. Keep spares in a chest as you build each one.
45. **Cables and Pipes**: 8x `mekanism:basic_universal_cable`; 8x `mekanism:basic_mechanical_pipe` *(after: Mekanism Circuits)*
   > What: Universal Cable carries FE, Mechanical Pipe carries fluids. With pressurized tubes (gases) and conductors (heat) they make Mekanism's four transmitter types.
   > How: steel with redstone (cable) or a bucket (pipe), 8 per craft. Higher tiers wrap 8 of the lower tier around an alloy.
   > Tip: transmitters of the same type join into one network. Colour them with a Configurator or paint to keep two networks apart.
46. **Logistical Transporters**: 16x `mekanism:basic_logistical_transporter` *(after: Cables and Pipes)*
   > What: item pipes that carry stacks to the nearest inventory that will take them, and show the items moving.
   > How: steel around a Basic Control Circuit, 8 per craft.
   > Tip: a transporter's colour (set with the Configurator) only delivers into sides of the same colour.
47. **Logistical Sorter**: `mekanism:logistical_sorter` *(after: Logistical Transporters)*
   > What: pulls items out of the inventory behind it and sends them down transporters by filter: item, tag, mod or material.
   > How: iron, a piston and a Basic Control Circuit. It is also a part of the Digital Miner.
   > Tip: give each filter a colour, and the items arrive only at inventories with that colour.
48. **Diversion and Restriction**: 2x `mekanism:diversion_transporter`; 2x `mekanism:restrictive_transporter` *(after: Logistical Transporters)*
   > What: a Diversion Transporter opens or closes with redstone; a Restrictive Transporter is only used when no other path works.
   > How: steel and iron bars, plus redstone for the diversion type (check JEI).
   > Tip: a restrictive segment in front of an overflow chest makes it fill last.
49. **Faster Transporters**: 8x `mekanism:elite_logistical_transporter`; 8x `mekanism:ultimate_logistical_transporter` *(after: Logistical Transporters)*
   > What: higher tiers move bigger stacks, faster.
   > How: 8 transporters of the tier below around Reinforced Alloy (elite) or Atomic Alloy (ultimate).
   > Tip: the line only needs to be as fast as its slowest machine. Upgrade the trunk, not every branch.
50. **Seismic Survey**: `mekanism:seismic_vibrator`; `mekanism:seismic_reader` *(after: Cables and Pipes)*
   > What: the Seismic Vibrator shakes the ground beneath it; the Seismic Reader then shows every block straight down to bedrock.
   > Chemistry: real prospectors use reflection seismology: sound waves bounce off layers of different density, and the echo times map oil and ore bodies.
   > How: vibrator: tin, lapis, Basic Control Circuits and a steel casing; reader: steel, lapis and an Energy Tablet (check JEI).
   > Tip: rich ore deposits sit at metal-specific depths. Survey before you dig.
51. **Formulaic Assemblicator**: `mekanism:formulaic_assemblicator` *(after: Logistical Transporters)*
   > What: saves a crafting recipe on a Crafting Formula and crafts it whenever ingredients arrive.
   > How: steel, a crafter, a chest, Basic Control Circuits and a steel casing (check JEI). Encode the recipe on a formula in its GUI.
   > Tip: handy for alloy blocks or circuits until your AE2 autocrafting is up.
52. **Nutritional Paste**: `mekanism:nutritional_liquifier`; `mekanism:canteen` *(after: Cables and Pipes)*
   > What: the Nutritional Liquifier turns food into nutritional paste; a Canteen carries it and feeds you when you drink.
   > Chemistry: food energy is chemical energy in carbohydrates, fats and proteins. Mashing food changes its form, not its calories.
   > How: liquifier: redstone, bowls, Basic Control Circuits and a steel casing. Canteen: tin and a bowl (check JEI). The MekaSuit has a module that drinks paste for you.
53. **Mine, Not Yours**: `mekanism:personal_chest`; `mekanism:security_desk` *(after: Logistical Transporters)*
   > What: a Personal Chest only opens for its owner. The Security Desk sets who may use your Mekanism blocks: you, a trusted list, or everyone.
   > How: personal chest: steel, glass, chests and a Basic Control Circuit. Security desk: steel, glass, a Network Reader and Elite Control Circuits (HV) (check JEI).
   > Tip: on a shared server, lock your reactor controls.
54. **Thermoelectric Boiler**: 16x `mekanism:boiler_casing`; 2x `mekanism:boiler_valve`; 4x `mekanism:superheating_element`; `mekanism:pressure_disperser` *(after: Fission)*
   > What: a multiblock boiler that turns water into steam, heated by Superheating Elements (power) or by hot coolant from a fission reactor.
   > Chemistry: boiling water takes 2,260 J per gram, far more than heating it. That latent heat is what the turbine later turns back into work.
   > How: the layout is in Just Enough Mekanism Multiblocks in JEI: casing walls, valves, a layer of pressure dispersers, and elements on the bottom.
   > Tip: a sodium-cooled fission reactor sends heated sodium here instead of boiling water itself.
55. **Sodium Coolant**: checkmark *(after: Thermoelectric Boiler)*
   > What: cool your fission reactor with sodium instead of water. It returns as superheated sodium, which the Thermoelectric Boiler uses to make steam before sending the sodium back.
   > Chemistry: sodium melts at 98 °C and boils at 883 °C, so it stays liquid at reactor temperatures without high pressure. Real fast reactors use it, and keep it away from water, which it reacts with violently.
   > How: sodium comes from brine in the Electrolytic Separator (see Salt and Sodium). Pipe it into the reactor, superheated sodium out to a boiler valve, and the cooled sodium back. Click to confirm once it runs.
   > Tip: one sodium loop feeds a much bigger boiler and turbine than water cooling does.
56. **Turbine Valves and Vents**: 2x `mekanismgenerators:turbine_valve`; 8x `mekanismgenerators:turbine_vent` *(after: Industrial Turbine)*
   > What: valves feed steam in and take power out; vents let the spent steam leave through the walls above the rotor.
   > Chemistry: a turbine extracts work as steam expands and cools. The more vent area, the more steam can pass per tick.
   > How: valve: turbine casings around an Advanced Control Circuit (MV). Vent: casings around iron bars (check JEI).
   > Tip: the turbine GUI shows whether vent flow or blade count is your limit.
57. **Turbine Internals**: 4x `mekanismgenerators:turbine_rotor`; 8x `mekanismgenerators:turbine_blade`; `mekanismgenerators:rotational_complex`; 2x `mekanismgenerators:electromagnetic_coil`; 4x `mekanismgenerators:saturating_condenser` *(after: Turbine Valves and Vents)*
   > What: the moving parts inside an Industrial Turbine. More blades and coils mean more power from the same steam.
   > Chemistry: condensers turn spent steam back into water, so the loop reuses the same water over and over.
   > How: stack rotors with two blades each on the centre column, a Rotational Complex on top, then coils and condensers above. See Just Enough Mekanism Multiblocks.
   > Tip: the turbine's GUI shows whether steam flow or blade count is the limit.
58. **Building the Fusion Reactor**: 16x `mekanismgenerators:fusion_reactor_frame`; 4x `mekanismgenerators:reactor_glass`; 2x `mekanismgenerators:fusion_reactor_port`; `mekanismgenerators:laser_focus_matrix` *(after: D-T Fuel)*
   > What: the shell of the Fusion Reactor: frames, reactor glass, ports for fuel and output, and a Laser Focus Matrix to let the ignition laser in.
   > How: the 3D layout is in Just Enough Mekanism Multiblocks in JEI. The controller sits on top in the middle.
   > Tip: ports switch between input and output with a Configurator.
59. **Ignition**: 3x `mekanism:laser`; `mekanism:laser_amplifier` *(after: Building the Fusion Reactor)*
   > What: lasers charge a Laser Amplifier, which fires a single huge pulse into the reactor to ignite it.
   > Chemistry: deuterium and tritium only fuse above about 100 million °C, when the nuclei move fast enough to beat their electric repulsion.
   > How: put a filled Hohlraum in the controller, aim the amplifier at the Laser Focus Matrix and fire it with a redstone signal. Then go to the Fusion quest.
   > Tip: once lit, the reactor sustains itself as long as fuel keeps coming.
60. **Challenge: Every Generator**: `mekanismgenerators:heat_generator`; `mekanismgenerators:solar_generator`; `mekanismgenerators:advanced_solar_generator`; `mekanismgenerators:wind_generator`; `mekanismgenerators:gas_burning_generator`; `mekanismgenerators:bio_generator` *(after: Heat Generator)*
   > What: one of each Mekanism generator: heat, solar, advanced solar, wind, gas-burning and bio.
   > Chemistry: between them: combustion, the photovoltaic effect, kinetic energy of air, and the energy stored in plant sugars by photosynthesis.
   > How: each one has its own quest on the Power page. The wind generator needs an Energy Tablet; the advanced solar is built from four basic solar generators.
   > Tip: none of these beats fission or fusion, but they never need fuel deliveries.
61. **Radioactive Waste Barrel**: 2x `mekanism:radioactive_waste_barrel` *(after: Fission)*
   > What: a barrel that stores spent nuclear waste and other radioactive chemicals, and lets them slowly decay.
   > Chemistry: radioactive decay can't be sped up by chemistry; you can only wait out the half-life. Real spent fuel spends years in cooling ponds, then decades in dry casks.
   > How: check JEI for the recipe. Pipe waste into it with pressurized tubes; never vent it or break a full tube.
   > Tip: reprocessing waste into plutonium and polonium (the T6 page) is better than storing it.
62. **Fusion Logic Adapter**: `mekanismgenerators:fusion_reactor_logic_adapter` *(after: Ignition)*
   > What: a reactor block that outputs redstone for things like ignition state, plasma temperature or low fuel.
   > How: a fusion reactor frame surrounded by redstone; swap one frame of the shell for it and pick the mode in its GUI.
   > Tip: wire "ready for ignition" to your laser amplifier to relight the reactor automatically.
63. **Osmium Armour**: `mekanismtools:osmium_helmet`; `mekanismtools:osmium_chestplate`; `mekanismtools:osmium_leggings`; `mekanismtools:osmium_boots` *(after: Mekanism: Metallurgic Infuser)*
   > What: Mekanism Tools adds a full armour set for each of its metals. Osmium is the first, and tougher than iron.
   > Chemistry: osmium is the densest natural element (22.6 g/cm³). Real osmium is brittle and its oxide, OsO₄, is volatile and very toxic, so nobody actually wears it.
   > How: osmium ingots in the usual armour shapes (check JEI).
   > Tip: bronze, steel and lapis sets exist too; see JEI for their stats.
64. **Refined Obsidian Armour**: `mekanismtools:refined_obsidian_helmet`; `mekanismtools:refined_obsidian_chestplate`; `mekanismtools:refined_obsidian_leggings`; `mekanismtools:refined_obsidian_boots` *(after: Osmium Armour, The Ultimate Paxel)*
   > What: the strongest Mekanism Tools armour: high protection and toughness, good until the MekaSuit.
   > Chemistry: obsidian is volcanic glass: lava cooled too fast for crystals to form. Infusing it with diamond and pressing it with osmium makes Mekanism's refined form.
   > How: refined obsidian ingots from the Osmium Compressor, in armour shapes (check JEI).
65. **Refined Glowstone Armour**: `mekanismtools:refined_glowstone_helmet`; `mekanismtools:refined_glowstone_chestplate`; `mekanismtools:refined_glowstone_leggings`; `mekanismtools:refined_glowstone_boots` *(after: Refined Obsidian Armour)*
   > What: the other Osmium Compressor metal as an armour set. Check JEI for how it compares with refined obsidian.
   > Chemistry: real glow-in-the-dark materials store energy in their crystal and release it as light (phosphorescence); glowstone is Minecraft's version.
   > How: Osmium Compressor: glowstone dust + osmium → refined glowstone ingots, then armour shapes.
66. **CNC Machines**: 1x any of `or(item(mekmm:cnc_stamper)item(mekmm:cnc_lathe)item(mekmm:cnc_rolling_mill))` *(after: Mekanism Circuits)*
   > What: Mekanism: More Machine adds CNC machines: the Stamper presses plates, the Lathe turns rods, the Rolling Mill rolls wire.
   > How: the CNC Stamper needs Basic Control Circuits, Basic Alloy, pistons and a steel casing. The Lathe also needs a Robit (check JEI). Any one counts.
   > Tip: MI's machines make most plates already. Use CNC machines for the mod recipes that list them in JEI (the stamper also prints AE2 processors).
67. **Recycler**: `mekmm:recycler`; 8x `mekmm:scrap` *(after: CNC Machines, MV: Aluminum)*
   > What: the Recycler grinds cheap blocks (stone, dirt, substrate) and sometimes leaves Scrap.
   > How: a Crusher, Advanced Control Circuits (MV), Infused Alloy and osmium (check JEI). About 1 stone in 6 gives a scrap.
   > Tip: 64 scrap compress into a Scrap Box, the first step towards UU-Matter. Start stockpiling early.
68. **Large Machines**: 1x any of `or(item(mekmm:large_heat_generator)item(mekmm:large_gas_burning_generator)item(mekmm:large_wind_generator)item(mekmm:large_chemical_infuser)item(mekmm:large_electrolytic_separator)item(mekmm:large_rotary_condensentrator)item(mekmm:large_solar_neutron_activator)item(mekmm:large_pigment_mixer))` *(after: CNC Machines, EV: Processing Units)*
   > What: More Machine's large machines do the work of many normal ones, such as the Large Electrolytic Separator or Large Chemical Infuser.
   > How: most need a Robit, steel blocks and ultimate-tier tanks or circuits (EV). Any one counts; check JEI for each.
   > Tip: the Large Solar Neutron Activator is a big help for polonium and tritium.
69. **UU-Matter**: `mekmm:replicator`; `mekmm:uu_matter` *(after: Recycler, Antimatter)*
   > Scrap, crystal, antimatter, and then anything. You remember when making matter was just a thought.
   > What: UU-Matter is More Machine's universal matter. The Replicator turns it back into items.
   > How: scrap boxes become Empty Crystals in the PRC (with fusion fuel and a special gas, check JEI). 64 crystals + antimatter in the Antiprotonic Nucleosynthesizer (an optional t7 quest) make one UU-Matter.
   > Tip: the Replicator needs Ultimate Control Circuits and Atomic Alloy. Check JEI for what it can copy.
70. **Presser**: `mekmm:presser` *(after: CNC Machines, HV: Digital Circuits)*
   > What: the Presser builds on the CNC Stamper and makes processors and other pressed parts (check JEI for its recipes).
   > How: a CNC Stamper with Elite Control Circuits (HV), Reinforced Alloy and pistons.
   > Tip: press U on the Presser in JEI to see what it makes.
71. **Planting Station**: `mekmm:planting_station` *(after: Recycler, HV: Digital Circuits)*
   > What: a machine that grows seeds and saplings indoors and outputs the harvest.
   > Chemistry: photosynthesis: 6CO₂ + 6H₂O → C₆H₁₂O₆ + 6O₂, here powered by electricity instead of sunlight.
   > How: Elite Control Circuits (HV), Reinforced Alloy, bio fuel and a steel casing (check JEI).
72. **A Small Tribute**: `mekmm:author_doll` *(after: UU-Matter)*
   > What: a decorative doll of More Machine's author, compressed out of 64 UU-Matter and a breath of antimatter in the Osmium Compressor.
   > Tip: the mod's modeller has a doll too, made from UU-Matter gas instead. Thank you, modders.
73. **Ambient Gas Collector**: `mekmm:ambient_gas_collector` *(after: Recycler, EV: Processing Units)*
   > What: a machine that collects gas from its surroundings (check JEI and its GUI for what it gathers).
   > Chemistry: real air is 78% N₂, 21% O₂ and 1% argon; industry separates it by cooling it into a liquid and distilling it.
   > How: Atomic Alloy, an osmium block, an ultimate chemical tank and ultimate tubes (EV).
74. **Radiance**: 4x `mekanism_extras:dust_radiance`; 2x `mekanism_extras:alloy_radiance` *(after: EV: Processing Units, Yellowcake)*
   > What: Mekanism Extras adds four tiers beyond ultimate: absolute, supreme, cosmic and infinite. Each needs a new alloy, starting with Radiance.
   > How: Osmium Compressor: glowstone dust + uranium oxide gas → Radiance Dust. Enrich it, then infuse Atomic Alloy with radiance (check JEI).
   > Tip: uranium oxide comes from oxidising yellowcake in the Chemical Oxidizer.
75. **Absolute Control Circuit**: 2x `mekanism_extras:absolute_control_circuit` *(after: Radiance)*
   > What: the first Extras circuit: an Ultimate Control Circuit between two Radiance Alloys.
   > How: craft it, or infuse an ultimate circuit with radiance in the Metallurgic Infuser.
   > Tip: absolute factories, cubes and tanks all start here.
76. **Supreme Control Circuit**: 2x `mekanism_extras:alloy_thermonuclear`; `mekanism_extras:supreme_control_circuit` *(after: Absolute Control Circuit)*
   > What: tier six. Its alloy is Thermonuclear: Radiance Alloy infused with thermonuclear material.
   > How: enriched radiance reacts with lava and molten thermonuclear in the PRC (check JEI for the full chain).
   > Tip: the circuit is an Absolute Control Circuit between two Thermonuclear Alloys.
77. **Cosmic Control Circuit**: 2x `mekanism_extras:alloy_shining`; `mekanism_extras:cosmic_control_circuit` *(after: Supreme Control Circuit, Antimatter)*
   > What: tier seven. Its alloy, Shining, needs enriched thermonuclear transmuted with antimatter.
   > How: Antiprotonic Nucleosynthesizer: enriched thermonuclear + antimatter → enriched shining. Then infuse Thermonuclear Alloy with it (check JEI).
   > Tip: the Nucleosynthesizer is slow; run it on fusion power.
78. **Infinite Control Circuit**: 2x `mekanism_extras:alloy_spectrum`; `mekanism_extras:infinite_control_circuit` *(after: Cosmic Control Circuit)*
   > Infinite, the circuit says. You remember when that word described you.
   > What: tier eight, the top of Mekanism Extras. Its alloy is Spectrum.
   > How: Osmium Compressor: enriched shining + antimatter → enriched spectrum; infuse Shining Alloy with it, then craft the circuit (check JEI).
79. **Absolute Factory**: 1x any of `or(item(mekanism_extras:absolute_smelting_factory)item(mekanism_extras:absolute_enriching_factory)item(mekanism_extras:absolute_crushing_factory)item(mekanism_extras:absolute_compressing_factory)item(mekanism_extras:absolute_combining_factory)item(mekanism_extras:absolute_purifying_factory)item(mekanism_extras:absolute_injecting_factory)item(mekanism_extras:absolute_infusing_factory)item(mekanism_extras:absolute_sawing_factory)item(mekanism_extras:absolute_oxidizing_factory)item(mekanism_extras:absolute_dissolving_factory)item(mekanism_extras:absolute_washing_factory)item(mekanism_extras:absolute_crystallizing_factory)item(mekanism_extras:absolute_centrifuging_factory))` *(after: Absolute Control Circuit)*
   > What: Extras factories have more slots than ultimate ones, and Extras adds factory types base Mekanism lacks (oxidizing, washing, crystallizing, centrifuging...).
   > How: upgrade an ultimate factory with Absolute Control Circuits and Radiance Alloy, or use an Absolute Tier Installer. Any absolute factory counts.
   > Tip: the extra factory types are the big win: a washing or crystallizing factory speeds up the whole 5x line.
80. **Naquadah**: 8x `mekanism_extras:raw_naquadah` *(after: Supreme Control Circuit)*
   > What: Naquadah ore, added by Mekanism Extras. It is the fuel for the Naquadah Reactor.
   > Chemistry: naquadah is not real; it comes from the Stargate films. Every other material on this page is real chemistry.
   > How: Naquadah ore generates underground and in the End. Jade shows the block name when you look at it.
   > Tip: it processes like other Mekanism ores: dissolve, wash and crystallise for the best yield.
81. **Naquadah Reactor**: `mekanism_extras:naquadah_reactor_controller`; 16x `mekanism_extras:naquadah_reactor_casing` *(after: Cosmic Control Circuit, Naquadah)*
   > What: a fusion-style power multiblock from Mekanism Extras that runs on naquadah-based fuel.
   > How: casings are lead blocks transmuted with antimatter in the Nucleosynthesizer; the controller needs Cosmic Control Circuits. Layout: Just Enough Mekanism Multiblocks.
   > Tip: the fuel chain starts with fluorinated naquadah-uranium fuel. Check JEI for each step.

## Engineering

### Power

*Energy is chemistry too: combustion, gas burning, fission and fusion.*

1. **First FE**: `generatorgalore:iron_generator` *(after: Forge Energy)*
   > You remember holding lightning. For now, a box that burns coal will do.
   > What: Generator Galore's generators burn fuel for FE (Forge Energy), the power most mods share.
   > Chemistry: burning is oxidation: C + O₂ → CO₂ releases the energy stored in carbon's bonds.
   > How: feed it coal or charcoal and connect a cable or put a machine next to it.
   > Tip: this chapter is a side path. Pick the power lines that suit your base.
2. **A Faster Burn**: `generatorgalore:gold_generator` *(after: First FE)*
   > What: the next Generator Galore tier: it burns fuel faster and makes more FE per tick.
   > How: upgrade from the iron generator (check JEI for the recipe).
   > Tip: a faster burn needs a steadier fuel supply. Pipe in charcoal from a tree farm.
3. **Generator Collector**: `generatorgalore:diamond_generator`; `generatorgalore:culinary_generator`; `generatorgalore:magmatic_generator`; `generatorgalore:halitosis_generator` *(after: A Faster Burn)*
   > What: a diamond-tier generator plus three that burn strange fuels.
   > How: each one's tooltip and JEI page show what it burns. The Halitosis Generator wants dragon's breath, so plan an End trip.
   > Tip: the Culinary Generator eats spare food. Hook it to a farm's overflow.
4. **Bio Fuel**: `mekanismgenerators:bio_generator` *(after: Heat Generator)*
   > What: Mekanism's Bio-Generator burns Bio Fuel.
   > Chemistry: plants store sunlight as sugars and cellulose. Burning them releases it, and the CO₂ is the same CO₂ the plant took in.
   > How: crush seeds and crops into Bio Fuel in a Mekanism Crusher.
5. **Burning Hydrogen**: `mekanismgenerators:gas_burning_generator` *(after: Mekanism Gases)*
   > What: the Gas-Burning Generator turns flammable gas into FE.
   > Chemistry: 2H₂ + O₂ → 2H₂O + energy. The only exhaust is water.
   > How: pipe hydrogen from your Electrolytic Separator in with pressurized tubes.
   > Tip: electrolysis costs more energy than burning hydrogen returns. Use it to store spare power, not to make it.
6. **Diesel Generators**: `modern_industrialization:mv_diesel_generator` *(after: Diesel Power)*
   > What: MI generators that burn the fuels from your distillation tower.
   > Chemistry: diesel is a mix of hydrocarbons around C₁₂ to C₂₀. Long chains pack lots of C-H bonds, and lots of energy.
   > How: pipe diesel into the generator and cable it to your machines.
7. **Large Diesel Generator**: `modern_industrialization:large_diesel_generator` *(after: Diesel Generators, HV: Digital Circuits)*
   > What: a multiblock diesel generator for real factories.
   > How: the 3D layout is in JEI and the MI guidebook. It needs fluid input and energy output hatches.
   > Tip: refined fuels (like boosted diesel) burn for more EU per bucket. Check JEI.
8. **Heat Generator**: `mekanismgenerators:heat_generator` *(after: First FE, Mekanism: Metallurgic Infuser)*
   > What: Mekanism's first generator. It burns fuel and turns heat into FE.
   > How: place lava next to it for a passive bonus, and feed it fuel for more.
   > Tip: lava from a pump makes this a decent passive generator early on.
9. **Store It**: `mekanism:basic_energy_cube` *(after: Heat Generator)*
   > What: energy cubes hold FE so short spikes don't stall your machines.
   > How: put one between your generators and machines. Configure its sides with the Configurator.
   > Tip: cubes upgrade in place: craft the next tier around the old one.
10. **Bigger Cubes**: `mekanism:advanced_energy_cube` *(after: Store It, MV: Aluminum)*
   > What: the Advanced Energy Cube holds and moves much more energy.
   > How: it needs an Advanced Control Circuit, which in this pack needs an MI electronic circuit (MV).
   > Tip: Elite and Ultimate cubes follow the HV and EV circuits.
11. **Evaporation Ponds**: `mekanism:thermal_evaporation_controller`; `mekanism:brine_bucket` *(after: Bigger Cubes)*
   > What: the Thermal Evaporation Plant boils water down into brine, and brine down into liquid lithium.
   > Chemistry: real lithium comes from salt flats: brine sits in ponds until the water evaporates and the salts concentrate.
   > How: a multiblock tower of Thermal Evaporation Blocks, Valves and one Controller. See Just Enough Mekanism Multiblocks in JEI.
   > Tip: taller towers and more heat run faster. Feed it plain water.
12. **Lithium**: 8x `mekanism:dust_lithium` *(after: Evaporation Ponds)*
   > What: lithium dust, for induction cells (and later, tritium).
   > Chemistry: lithium is the lightest metal and gives up its electron easily, which is why lithium-ion batteries store so much energy for their weight.
   > How: run brine through the plant again for liquid lithium. Turn it into gas in a Rotary Condensentrator, then crystallise it in a Chemical Crystallizer.
   > Tip: check JEI with R on Lithium Dust to confirm each step.
13. **Induction Matrix**: 16x `mekanism:induction_casing`; `mekanism:induction_port`; `mekanism:basic_induction_cell`; `mekanism:basic_induction_provider` *(after: Lithium)*
   > Power stored, waiting, obedient. This feels familiar.
   > What: a hollow multiblock battery. Cells inside set how much it stores; providers set how fast it charges and discharges.
   > How: casings form the shell (Structural Glass works for the walls), ports move energy in and out. Layout: Just Enough Mekanism Multiblocks.
   > Tip: switch one port to output with the Configurator. Start small; you can rebuild it larger later.
14. **Wireless Grid**: `fluxnetworks:basic_flux_storage`; `fluxnetworks:flux_plug`; `fluxnetworks:flux_point` *(after: Induction Matrix, EV: Processing Units)*
   > What: Flux Networks sends power through the air: Plugs take it in, Points give it out, Storage buffers it.
   > How: the Flux Core needs an MI processing unit (EV) in this pack. Link all devices to one network in their GUI.
   > Tip: put a Flux Plug on your induction matrix's output port and every Point on the network draws from it.
15. **EU Storage**: `modern_industrialization:lv_storage_unit` *(after: LV Machine Hull)*
   > What: MI's storage unit holds EU, Modern Industrialization's electricity.
   > How: built from redstone batteries, battery alloy plates and an LV hull. Put it between your turbine and your machines.
   > Tip: a buffer lets your steam turbine keep running while machines are idle.
16. **MV Steam Turbine**: `modern_industrialization:mv_steam_turbine` *(after: EU Storage, MV: Aluminum)*
   > What: a bigger steam turbine that outputs MV power.
   > Chemistry: a turbine converts the energy of hot, expanding steam into rotation, then a generator turns rotation into current.
   > How: assemble it from an MV hull, aluminum rotors and large motors (check JEI).
17. **Transformers**: `modern_industrialization:lv_mv_transformer` *(after: MV Steam Turbine)*
   > What: a transformer moves energy between two neighbouring voltage tiers (LV and MV here).
   > How: place it between an MV cable and your LV machines (or the other way round). Check which face is which in its tooltip.
   > Tip: real grids do the same: high voltage for transmission, low voltage at the socket.
18. **HV Steam Turbine**: `modern_industrialization:hv_steam_turbine` *(after: Transformers, HV: Digital Circuits)*
   > What: the HV steam turbine, for HV machine lines.
   > How: check JEI for the recipe. Feed it from a large boiler.
   > Tip: more steam than one turbine can eat? Add a second turbine on the same steam line.
19. **Large Steam Turbine**: `modern_industrialization:large_steam_turbine` *(after: HV Steam Turbine, EV: Processing Units)*
   > What: MI's multiblock steam turbine. It eats steam by the thousands of mB and makes serious EU.
   > How: the controller needs an HV steam turbine, a processing unit (EV) and a highly advanced hull. Layout: JEI or the MI guidebook.
   > Tip: pair it with a large boiler or a reactor's steam output.
20. **Energized Steel**: 8x `powah:steel_energized` *(after: Uranium Dioxide)*
   > What: Powah's basic alloy, made by the Energizing Orb.
   > How: place Energizing Rods aimed at the orb and power them. Put 1 iron + 1 gold in the orb for 2 Energized Steel.
   > Tip: more rods (and better rods) charge faster.
21. **Powah Energy Cells**: `powah:energy_cell_basic` *(after: Energized Steel)*
   > What: Powah's energy storage block, with tiers from Starter to Nitro.
   > How: iron, a dielectric casing and Powah capacitors. Higher tiers use better capacitors and crystals (check JEI).
   > Tip: cells pass power through each other, so a wall of them makes one big buffer.
22. **Thermo Generator**: `powah:thermo_generator_starter` *(after: Powah Energy Cells)*
   > What: a generator that needs a hot block underneath and a coolant fed in.
   > Chemistry: thermoelectric plates make current from a temperature difference (the Seebeck effect). Hotter below and colder inside means more power.
   > How: place it on lava or magma and pipe water in.
23. **Powah Reactor**: 36x `powah:reactor_basic` *(after: Thermo Generator)*
   > What: a multiblock reactor fuelled by the uraninite you oxidised yourself.
   > Chemistry: uraninite is UO₂. Its uranium releases heat as it fissions; the reactor turns that heat into FE.
   > How: place the reactor blocks in a stack and they form one reactor (check JEI). Feed it uraninite.
   > Tip: fuel, temperature and coolant all show in its GUI. Watch the temperature as you scale up.
24. **Photovoltaics**: `mekanismgenerators:solar_generator`; `powah:solar_panel_starter` *(after: MV: Aluminum)*
   > What: solar panels from Mekanism and Powah.
   > Chemistry: silicon solar cells use a p-n junction: a photon knocks an electron loose and the junction pushes it into a current.
   > How: place them under open sky. They stop at night and slow in rain.
   > Tip: pair solar with an energy cube to cover the night.
25. **Advanced Solar**: `mekanismgenerators:advanced_solar_generator` *(after: Photovoltaics)*
   > What: Mekanism's Advanced Solar Generator, a larger array with much more output.
   > How: check JEI for the recipe. Give it space: it's bigger than one block.
   > Tip: like the basic panel, it only works in daylight. Buffer it with a cube.
26. **Wind Power**: `mekanismgenerators:wind_generator` *(after: Photovoltaics)*
   > What: Mekanism's Wind Generator. It works day and night.
   > How: it makes more power the higher it's placed. Build it on a mountain or a tall tower.
   > Tip: it needs open space around it, so leave room.
27. **Reactor Safety**: 2x `mekanismgenerators:fission_reactor_logic_adapter` *(after: Fission)*
   > What: Logic Adapters let redstone read the reactor and switch it off.
   > Chemistry: a fission reactor makes heat by splitting heavy nuclei. If heat can't escape fast enough, the reactor takes damage; at 100% it melts down.
   > How: set one adapter to emit a signal on damage or high temperature (pick the mode in its GUI) and wire it to another in Activation mode.
   > Tip: start at a low burn rate (0.1 mB/t), keep coolant flowing, and drain waste and heated coolant constantly.
28. **Industrial Turbine**: 16x `mekanismgenerators:turbine_casing` *(after: Reactor Safety)*
   > What: Mekanism's multiblock turbine. Feed it steam from fission or a boiler and it makes huge amounts of FE.
   > How: casings, valves, vents, rotors with blades, a rotational complex, a pressure disperser and electromagnetic coils. Layout: Just Enough Mekanism Multiblocks.
   > Tip: a Saturating Condenser turns spent steam back into water, closing the loop.
29. **D-T Fuel**: `mekanism:solar_neutron_activator`; `mekanismgenerators:hohlraum` *(after: Industrial Turbine, Lithium)*
   > What: fusion fuel: deuterium and tritium, the two heavy isotopes of hydrogen.
   > Chemistry: deuterium comes from heavy water (D₂O). Tritium is bred from lithium by neutrons: ⁶Li + n → ³H + ⁴He.
   > How: electrolyse heavy water for deuterium; put lithium in the Solar Neutron Activator for tritium. Combine them into D-T fuel in the Chemical Infuser, then fill a Hohlraum with it (a chemical tank's item slot works; see its tooltip).
   > Tip: heavy water comes from a Mekanism Electric Pump with a Filter Upgrade.
30. **Fusion**: `mekanismgenerators:fusion_reactor_controller` *(after: D-T Fuel, Transmutation by Neutron)*
   > The power of a star, in a box you built. This is what you were.
   > What: Mekanism's fusion reactor, the top of the power tree.
   > Chemistry: D + T → ⁴He + n + 17.6 MeV, millions of times more energy per kilogram than any chemical reaction.
   > How: a filled Hohlraum in the controller, then a burst from a Laser through a Laser Amplifier to ignite it. Layout: Just Enough Mekanism Multiblocks.
31. **Upgrade in Place**: `generatorgalore:iron_to_gold_upgrade` *(after: A Faster Burn)*
   > What: Generators Galore upgrade kits turn a placed generator into the next tier, keeping its fuel and connections.
   > How: gold ingots, an item frame and a redstone block for iron → gold (check JEI for the others). Right-click the placed generator.
   > Tip: the tree branches: gold leads to diamond or to the food-burning Culinary Generator.
32. **Eight Times the Burn**: `generatorgalore:iron_generator_8x` *(after: A Faster Burn, An Ancient City)*
   > What: 8x generators burn fuel eight times as fast and make eight times the FE in one block.
   > How: eight generators of a tier around an echo shard (check JEI). Echo shards come from Ancient City chests.
   > Tip: a 64x version exists too, for when space matters more than echo shards.
33. **Burning a Star**: `generatorgalore:netherstar_generator` *(after: Generator Collector)*
   > You remember lighting stars. Burning one feels like a waste, and also like coming home.
   > What: the Nether Star Generator burns Nether Stars for an enormous amount of FE.
   > Chemistry: real stars run on fusion: hydrogen nuclei fuse into helium (4 ¹H → ⁴He) and a little mass becomes a lot of energy, E = mc².
34. **Blazing Crystals**: 8x `powah:crystal_blazing`; `powah:energy_cell_blazing` *(after: Powah Reactor, Blaze Rods)*
   > What: every Powah tier is built from a crystal made in the Energizing Orb. Blazing crystals unlock the blazing tier of cells, generators and cables.
   > How: energize a blaze rod (or four blaze powder) with 120,000 FE. Then craft blazing-tier parts (check JEI).
   > Tip: feed the energizing rods from your biggest power source; crystals charge faster with more rods.
35. **Niotic Crystals**: 4x `powah:crystal_niotic`; `powah:energy_cell_niotic` *(after: Blazing Crystals)*
   > What: the niotic tier. Reactors and generators get much stronger.
   > Chemistry: diamond is pure carbon in a rigid lattice where every atom bonds to four others. Pumping energy into it is Powah's magic, but diamond really does conduct heat better than any metal.
   > How: energize a diamond with 300,000 FE (check JEI).
36. **Spirited Crystals**: 4x `powah:crystal_spirited`; `powah:energy_cell_spirited` *(after: Niotic Crystals)*
   > What: the spirited tier, one step below the top.
   > Chemistry: emerald is the mineral beryl, Be₃Al₂Si₆O₁₈. A trace of chromium (Cr³⁺) replacing some aluminum gives it its green colour.
   > How: energize an emerald with 1,000,000 FE (check JEI). A villager trading hall pays off here.
37. **Nitro Crystals**: 16x `powah:crystal_nitro` *(after: Spirited Crystals)*
   > What: nitro crystals build the strongest Powah reactors and cells.
   > How: energize a Nether Star, two redstone blocks and a blazing crystal block with 20,000,000 FE. One craft makes sixteen.
   > Tip: save your Nether Stars for this; the Wither is the only reliable source (check JEI for a Hostile Neural Networks wither model).
38. **Ender Power**: `powah:ender_cell_basic`; `powah:player_transmitter_basic` *(after: Powah Energy Cells)*
   > What: Ender Cells share one energy pool across your base; the Player Transmitter charges the items in your inventory.
   > How: both need an Ender Core (an eye of ender, dielectric casing and a tiny capacitor, energized). Check JEI, and link them with a Binding Card.
   > Tip: a player transmitter keeps jetpacks and the MekaSuit charged while you work.
39. **Herculean Storage**: `fluxnetworks:herculean_flux_storage` *(after: Wireless Grid)*
   > What: Herculean (and then Gargantuan) Flux Storage holds far more energy in one block, shared with the whole flux network.
   > How: Basic Flux Storage blocks around glass panes (check JEI). Gargantuan is the same with Herculean blocks.
   > Tip: flux storage keeps its energy when broken, so you can carry charged batteries to a new base.
40. **Flux Controller**: `fluxnetworks:flux_controller`; `fluxnetworks:flux_configurator` *(after: Wireless Grid)*
   > What: the Flux Controller sends network energy wirelessly into players' inventories. The Configurator copies settings between flux devices.
   > How: flux blocks, a Flux Core and flux dust (check JEI). The Flux Core needs an MI processing unit in this pack.
   > Tip: turn charging off for your armor slots if it fights with other chargers.

### Powah!

*Powah: dielectric parts, the Energizing Orb, crystal tiers, generators, cells and wireless power. The starter tier is open early; the Orb needs an MI electronic circuit (MV).*

1. **Dielectric Paste**: 16x `powah:dielectric_paste` *(after: Forge Energy)*
   > Insulators and conductors. You remember that power is mostly about where it is not allowed to go.
   > What: the base of every Powah block: rods, casings, capacitors and cables.
   > Chemistry: a dielectric is an insulator that polarises in an electric field. Fired clay (aluminosilicates such as kaolinite, Alâ‚‚Siâ‚‚Oâ‚…(OH)â‚„) is the porcelain on real power-line insulators.
   > How: shapeless: 3 coal or charcoal + 2 clay balls + a lava bucket makes 24. Or 2 coal + 1 clay ball + 1 blaze powder for 16.
   > Tip: the lava recipe gives more per craft and keeps the bucket. Make a stack.
2. **Dielectric Rods**: 8x `powah:dielectric_rod`; 8x `powah:dielectric_rod_horizontal` *(after: Dielectric Paste)*
   > What: insulated rods, upright and horizontal. Both go into casings, the Orb, cables and cards.
   > How: 6 paste around 3 iron bars: a vertical line of bars for the upright rod, a horizontal line for the horizontal one. 8 per craft.
   > Tip: you'll need far more horizontal rods than upright ones; every cable craft eats six.
3. **Dielectric Casing**: 2x `powah:dielectric_casing` *(after: Dielectric Rods)*
   > What: the frame inside almost every Powah machine, cell and generator.
   > How: 4 iron ingots in the corners, horizontal rods top and bottom, upright rods in the middle row (check JEI).
4. **Capacitors**: 4x `powah:capacitor_basic`; 2x `powah:capacitor_basic_tiny` *(after: Dielectric Casing)*
   > What: capacitors set a block's tier. The tiny ones go into starter blocks, the basic ones into basic blocks.
   > Chemistry: a capacitor is two conductors separated by a dielectric. It stores E = Â½CVÂ², and a better dielectric raises the capacitance C.
   > How: iron ingots, paste and a redstone block make 4 basic capacitors. One basic capacitor splits into 2 tiny ones.
   > Tip: two basic capacitors combine into a large one, which the hardened tier needs later.
5. **Energy Cables**: 12x `powah:energy_cable_starter` *(after: Capacitors)*
   > What: Powah's cables. Each tier carries more FE per tick.
   > How: 6 horizontal rods, 2 iron nuggets and a tiny capacitor make 12 starter cables.
   > Tip: cables connect to any FE block, not just Powah ones. Use the Powah wrench to configure sides.
6. **Starter Energy Cell**: `powah:energy_cell_starter` *(after: Energy Cables)*
   > What: the smallest Powah cell. Enough to smooth out a furnator's output.
   > How: iron ingots in the corners, tiny capacitors on the sides, a dielectric casing in the middle.
   > Tip: the basic cell is the same recipe with basic capacitors. The bigger tiers come with energized steel and crystals.
7. **Powah Wrench**: `powah:wrench` *(after: Dielectric Paste)*
   > What: Powah's wrench. It has Rotate, Config and Link modes; the mode shows on the item.
   > How: iron ingots and dielectric paste (check JEI).
   > Tip: Config mode sets which sides of a cell or generator push or take power.
8. **Furnator**: `powah:furnator_starter` *(after: Capacitors)*
   > What: a Powah generator that burns furnace fuel for FE. It's an early way to run the Dissolver.
   > Chemistry: burning oxidises carbon. Coke burns cleaner and longer than coal because its volatile tars were already driven off.
   > How: paste, 2 tiny capacitors, a dielectric casing and a furnace.
   > Tip: feed it coke from your coke ovens for long runs.
9. **Magmator**: `powah:magmator_starter` *(after: Furnator)*
   > What: a generator that burns lava (and other hot fluids) instead of solid fuel.
   > Chemistry: lava is molten silicate rock at roughly 700 to 1,200 Â°C. The Magmator draws that stored heat off as FE.
   > How: like the Furnator, but with a bucket in place of the furnace. JEI's Magmatic Fluids list shows what it accepts.
   > Tip: a Nether lava lake and a pump give endless fuel.
10. **Basic Generators**: 1x any of `or(item(powah:furnator_basic)item(powah:magmator_basic)item(powah:thermo_generator_basic)item(powah:solar_panel_basic))` *(after: Furnator, Magmator)*
   > What: every Powah block upgrades by crafting the lower tier into the next one. Basic is the first step.
   > How: a starter generator in the bottom middle, iron ingots, 2 basic capacitors and a dielectric casing (check JEI).
   > Tip: upgrading keeps the old block's cost, so you never waste a starter.
11. **Player Aerial Pearl**: `powah:player_aerial_pearl` *(after: Dielectric Paste)*
   > What: the core of the Player Transmitter.
   > How: craft an Aerial Pearl (an ender pearl in iron bars and paste), then use it on a Zombie or a Husk.
   > Tip: trap a zombie near your base so you can make more later.
12. **Player Transmitter**: `powah:player_transmitter_starter`; `powah:binding_card` *(after: Player Aerial Pearl, Capacitors)*
   > What: a block that charges the FE items carried by the player bound to it.
   > How: Player Aerial Pearl on top, tiny capacitors and a casing in the middle, an upright rod below. The Binding Card is a blank card (2 horizontal rods) with redstone and rotten flesh; right-click to bind it to yourself.
   > Tip: power it from a cell and your jetpack never runs dry at home.
13. **Panes and Plates**: 3x `powah:photoelectric_pane`; 3x `powah:thermoelectric_plate` *(after: Capacitors)*
   > What: photoelectric panes go into Powah solar panels; thermo plates go into thermo generators (both in the Power chapter).
   > Chemistry: light can knock electrons out of a material: the photoelectric effect Einstein explained in 1905. Lapis gets its blue from trisulfur radical ions (Sâ‚ƒâ») trapped in the mineral lazurite.
   > How: pane: a glass pane in lapis and paste. Plate: a tiny capacitor in redstone and blaze powder.
   > Tip: blaze powder means a trip to the Nether first.
14. **Hopper and Discharger**: `powah:energy_hopper_starter`; `powah:energy_discharger_starter` *(after: Starter Energy Cell)*
   > What: the Energy Hopper charges FE items in the container it faces; the Energy Discharger pulls FE back out of items put in it.
   > How: both are a dielectric casing, tiny capacitors and paste; the hopper also takes a vanilla hopper (check JEI).
   > Tip: a hopper on a chest of tools makes a charging station.
15. **Batteries**: `powah:battery_starter` *(after: Starter Energy Cell)*
   > What: Powah batteries are items that hold FE. Higher tiers hold more.
   > How: basic capacitors, a redstone block and paste. Check its tooltip for how it charges your gear.
   > Tip: fill it from an Energy Hopper, empty it in a Discharger.
16. **Dimensional Binding Card**: `powah:binding_card_dim` *(after: Player Transmitter)*
   > What: the dimensional version of the Binding Card, for transmitters and other Powah blocks that take a card.
   > How: use a Binding Card on an Enderman or an Endermite.
   > Tip: read its tooltip to see what it does differently from the plain card.
17. **The Energizing Orb**: `powah:energizing_orb`; 2x `powah:energizing_rod_starter` *(after: Starter Energy Cell, Electronic Circuit)*
   > A glass sphere that fills with light. You remember charging things just by holding them.
   > What: the Orb turns FE into new materials: energized steel, crystals, ender cores. Every Powah tier above basic needs it.
   > How: in this pack: glass on top and sides, an MI electronic circuit on each side of a dielectric casing in the middle, and 3 horizontal rods on the bottom. Rods: quartz, tiny capacitors, a casing and an upright rod.
   > How: place the orb, aim Energizing Rods at it from nearby, feed the rods FE and put the ingredients in the orb. JEI's Energizing tab shows each recipe and its FE cost.
   > Tip: the first job is uraninite and energized steel: see the Main Path and Power chapter links.
18. **Charged Snowball**: 4x `powah:charged_snowball` *(after: The Energizing Orb)*
   > What: a snowball charged with 500,000 FE. Throw it and lightning strikes where it lands.
   > Chemistry: a lightning channel heats air to around 30,000 K, hotter than the Sun's surface. It splits Nâ‚‚ and Oâ‚‚, and the nitrogen oxides it makes fertilise the soil.
   > How: one snowball in the Energizing Orb.
   > Tip: lightning turns a pig into a zombified piglin and a villager into a witch. Aim carefully.
19. **Better Rods**: 2x `powah:energizing_rod_basic` *(after: The Energizing Orb)*
   > What: higher-tier rods pour FE into the orb faster. Several rods stack.
   > Chemistry: quartz is crystalline SiOâ‚‚ and is piezoelectric: squeeze it and it makes a voltage. Every quartz watch keeps time with a vibrating crystal.
   > How: a block of quartz on top, basic capacitors and a casing in the middle, a starter rod below.
   > Tip: crystals cost hundreds of thousands of FE. More and better rods matter more than anything else here.
20. **Hardened Rods**: 2x `powah:energizing_rod_hardened` *(after: Better Rods, Hardened Capacitors)*
   > What: the hardened rod, another step in charging speed.
   > How: a block of quartz, hardened capacitors, a casing and a basic rod below.
   > Tip: every rod tier is built from the one before, so keep upgrading the same rods.
21. **Hardened Capacitors**: 2x `powah:capacitor_basic_large`; 2x `powah:capacitor_hardened` *(after: Energized Steel)*
   > What: hardened capacitors unlock the hardened tier of everything.
   > How: 2 basic capacitors make a large one. A large capacitor in energized steel and paste makes 2 hardened capacitors.
   > Tip: large capacitors go into every crystal capacitor too. Craft spares.
22. **Hardened Cables**: 6x `powah:energy_cable_basic`; 12x `powah:energy_cable_hardened` *(after: Hardened Capacitors)*
   > What: faster cables for your growing grid.
   > How: basic: 6 rods, 2 starter cables, a basic capacitor. Hardened: 6 rods, 2 energized steel, a hardened capacitor makes 12 (check JEI).
   > Tip: a cable is only as fast as its slowest segment. Upgrade the trunk line first.
23. **Hardened Energy Cell**: `powah:energy_cell_hardened` *(after: Hardened Capacitors, Powah Energy Cells)*
   > What: the next storage step after the basic cell.
   > How: 2 basic cells, energized steel, hardened capacitors and a dielectric casing.
   > Tip: the crystal cells in the Power chapter each start from the cell below them.
24. **Hardened Generators**: 1x any of `or(item(powah:furnator_hardened)item(powah:magmator_hardened)item(powah:thermo_generator_hardened)item(powah:solar_panel_hardened))` *(after: Hardened Capacitors, Basic Generators)*
   > What: a hardened Furnator, Magmator, Thermo Generator or Solar Panel. Any one counts.
   > How: the basic generator plus energized steel, hardened capacitors and a casing (check JEI).
   > Tip: a hardened thermo generator on lava with water fed in is quiet, fuel-free power.
25. **Blazing Generators**: 1x any of `or(item(powah:furnator_blazing)item(powah:magmator_blazing)item(powah:thermo_generator_blazing)item(powah:solar_panel_blazing))` *(after: Hardened Generators, Blazing Gear)*
   > What: any blazing-tier generator. Output takes a big jump here.
   > How: the hardened generator plus blazing crystals, blazing capacitors and a casing.
   > Tip: feed the orb's rods from these and the next crystal tier comes quicker.
26. **Niotic Generators**: 1x any of `or(item(powah:furnator_niotic)item(powah:magmator_niotic)item(powah:thermo_generator_niotic)item(powah:solar_panel_niotic))` *(after: Blazing Generators, Niotic Gear)*
   > What: any niotic-tier generator. Spirited and nitro follow the same pattern.
   > How: the blazing generator plus niotic crystals, niotic capacitors and a casing (check JEI).
27. **Blazing Gear**: 2x `powah:capacitor_blazing`; `powah:energizing_rod_blazing` *(after: Blazing Crystals, Hardened Rods)*
   > What: the blazing capacitor is the key part of every blazing block; the blazing rod charges the orb faster still.
   > How: capacitor: a large capacitor in blazing crystals and paste (makes 2). Rod: quartz block, blazing capacitors, a casing and a hardened rod.
   > Tip: a blaze farm (or a Hostile Neural Networks blaze model) keeps the crystals coming.
28. **Niotic Gear**: 2x `powah:capacitor_niotic`; `powah:energizing_rod_niotic` *(after: Niotic Crystals, Blazing Gear)*
   > What: niotic capacitors and the niotic rod.
   > Chemistry: diamond is an excellent electrical insulator but conducts heat better than copper, because its stiff carbon lattice carries vibrations so well.
   > How: capacitor: a large capacitor in niotic crystals and paste. Rod: quartz block, niotic capacitors, a casing and a blazing rod.
   > Tip: niotic cables (6 rods, 2 niotic crystals, a niotic capacitor) carry far more FE.
29. **Spirited Gear**: 2x `powah:capacitor_spirited`; `powah:energizing_rod_spirited` *(after: Spirited Crystals, Niotic Gear)*
   > What: spirited capacitors and the spirited rod, one step below the top.
   > How: capacitor: a large capacitor in spirited crystals and paste. Rod: quartz block, spirited capacitors, a casing and a niotic rod.
   > Tip: each spirited crystal costs 1,000,000 FE. Run several spirited rods before you try for nitro.
30. **Nitro**: `powah:energizing_rod_nitro`; `powah:energy_cell_nitro` *(after: Nitro Crystals, Spirited Gear)*
   > Twenty million FE poured into a star. You remember power like this; it used to be yours.
   > What: the nitro rod and nitro energy cell, the strongest Powah blocks.
   > How: nitro capacitor: a large capacitor in nitro crystals and paste. Rod: quartz block, nitro capacitors, a casing and a spirited rod. Cell: nitro crystals, nitro capacitors, 2 spirited cells and a casing.
   > Tip: the nitro crystal recipe needs a block of 9 blazing crystals, so save some blazing crystals for it.
31. **Ender Core**: 2x `powah:ender_core` *(after: The Energizing Orb)*
   > What: the heart of Ender Cells and Ender Gates.
   > How: Energizing Orb: an eye of ender, a dielectric casing and a tiny capacitor, 50,000 FE.
   > Tip: one core per ender block, at every tier.
32. **Ender Gates**: 4x `powah:ender_gate_starter` *(after: Ender Core)*
   > What: ender gates connect to the same ender energy network as your Ender Cells, without storing much themselves.
   > How: obsidian, 2 starter cables and an ender core make 4. Set the channel in the GUI to match your cells.
   > Tip: put a gate next to each remote machine and one Ender Cell at your main bank.
33. **Dry Ice**: 4x `powah:dry_ice` *(after: Powah Reactor)*
   > What: a strong solid coolant for the Powah reactor. Cooler reactors waste less fuel.
   > Chemistry: real dry ice is solid COâ‚‚, which sublimes straight to gas at -78.5 Â°C. Powah's is made from energized blue ice, which is Hâ‚‚O, so take the name loosely.
   > How: Energizing Orb: 2 blue ice, 10,000 FE. JEI's Coolants and Solid Coolants tabs list every option and its strength.
   > Tip: the reactor also takes carbon and redstone to boost it. Check its GUI slots.
34. **Hardened Reactor**: 36x `powah:reactor_hardened` *(after: Dry Ice, Hardened Capacitors)*
   > What: a hardened reactor, much stronger than the basic one.
   > Chemistry: uraninite (UOâ‚‚) holds U-235, which splits when it absorbs a neutron and releases more neutrons and heat.
   > How: 4 basic reactor blocks, 4 hardened capacitors and 1 uraninite make 4 hardened blocks. Every block in the stack must be the same tier.
   > Tip: blazing, niotic, spirited and nitro reactors follow the same recipe with that tier's capacitor.

### Mob Data and Big Crafting

*Monifactory-inspired machinery: simulate mobs, craft on giant tables, and fold whole factories into a block.*

1. **Deep Learning**: `hostilenetworks:deep_learner`; `hostilenetworks:blank_data_model` *(after: Iron)*
   > You remember knowing every creature by name. You can learn them again, one fight at a time.
   > What: Hostile Neural Networks records mobs into data models. Trained models later make mob drops without the mob.
   > How: craft the Deep Learner and a Blank Data Model (check JEI). Put models in the Deep Learner and keep it in your inventory while you fight.
   > Tip: start with a mob whose drops you'll need in bulk, like blazes, slimes or endermen.
2. **Your First Model**: `hostilenetworks:data_model` *(after: Deep Learning)*
   > What: a data model attuned to one mob type. It starts at Faulty and levels up as you kill that mob.
   > How: right-click a mob with a Blank Data Model, or craft a specific model (check JEI). Then hunt with the Deep Learner.
   > Tip: tiers go Faulty, Basic, Advanced, Superior, Self Aware. Higher tiers are more accurate, so fewer simulations fail.
3. **Simulation**: `hostilenetworks:sim_chamber`; 4x `hostilenetworks:prediction_matrix` *(after: Your First Model, MV: Aluminum)*
   > What: the Simulation Chamber runs a trained model on FE and prediction matrices to make predictions and mob drops.
   > How: it needs an MV electronic circuit (aluminum chemistry). Insert a model of Basic tier or better, matrices and power.
   > Tip: pipe matrices in and drops out; one chamber per model keeps things simple.
4. **Loot Fabricator**: `hostilenetworks:loot_fabricator` *(after: Simulation)*
   > You remember making things appear from nothing. This is the mortal version: data, power and patience.
   > What: turns predictions into the specific drop you pick (blaze rods, ender pearls, slime balls and more).
   > How: also needs an MV electronic circuit. Feed it predictions from the chamber and select the output in its GUI.
   > Tip: simulated blazes are a cheap blaze-rod source for Silent Gear alloys and brewing.
5. **Self Aware**: checkmark *(after: Simulation)*
   > What: train one data model all the way to Self Aware, the top tier.
   > How: keep killing that mob with the model in your Deep Learner, or let the Simulation Chamber train it. Click the checkmark when you've done it.
   > Tip: a Self Aware model almost never fails a simulation.
6. **Twilight Predictions**: 4x `hostilenetworks:twilight_prediction` *(after: Simulation, The Twilight Forest)*
   > What: Twilight Forest mobs give their own prediction type.
   > How: train a model on a Twilight mob and run it in the Simulation Chamber.
   > Tip: the Loot Fabricator shows which drops each prediction can become.
7. **Black Iron Parts**: `extendedcrafting:black_iron_slate`; `extendedcrafting:basic_component`; `extendedcrafting:basic_catalyst` *(after: Steel)*
   > What: Extended Crafting tables are built from black iron slates plus a component and a catalyst of matching tier.
   > How: black iron ingot = iron ingot + black dye (shapeless). Two ingots make a slate. A component takes a slate, luminessence (glowstone, redstone, gunpowder) and iron; four components ring a black iron ingot for the catalyst.
   > Tip: make extra black iron; every table tier and the Crafting Core need more.
8. **Extended Crafting**: 8x `extendedcrafting:black_iron_ingot`; `extendedcrafting:basic_table` *(after: Black Iron Parts)*
   > What: the Basic Table is a 3x3 table for Extended Crafting recipes, and the base of every bigger table.
   > How: black iron slate, basic component and catalyst, an iron block and a crafting table.
   > Tip: each bigger table is crafted from the one before it, so don't use this one up as decoration.
9. **5x5: Advanced Table**: `extendedcrafting:advanced_table` *(after: Extended Crafting, MV: Aluminum)*
   > What: a 5x5 crafting grid for mid-game recipes.
   > Chemistry: its MI electronic circuit rests on aluminum, won by electrolysis of alumina: 2Al₂O₃ → 4Al + 3O₂.
   > How: the Basic Table, gold blocks, advanced component and catalyst, plus the electronic circuit (check JEI).
10. **7x7: Elite Table**: `extendedcrafting:elite_table` *(after: 5x5: Advanced Table, HV: Digital Circuits)*
   > What: a 7x7 grid. Crystaltine and many late parts are crafted here.
   > Chemistry: the MI digital circuit needs polyethylene, chains of ethylene (C₂H₄) from cracking oil.
   > How: the Advanced Table, diamond blocks, elite component and catalyst, plus the digital circuit.
11. **9x9: Ultimate Table**: `extendedcrafting:ultimate_table` *(after: 7x7: Elite Table, EV: Processing Units)*
   > You remember building worlds on a table far bigger than this. Nine by nine will do for now.
   > What: the endgame's biggest crafts happen here, including the Seed of a New World.
   > Chemistry: the MI processing unit needs PVC: polyvinyl chloride, (C₂H₃Cl)ₙ, made from ethylene and chlorine.
   > How: the Elite Table, emerald blocks, ultimate component and catalyst, plus the processing unit.
12. **Packaged Automation**: `packagedauto:packager`; `packagedauto:unpackager` *(after: The God's Archive, 5x5: Advanced Table)*
   > What: Packaged Auto lets AE2 autocraft with Extended Crafting tables, through the PackagedExCrafting addon.
   > How: encode recipes in the Encoder, then set up a Packager, a PackagedExCrafting crafter and an Unpackager by your ME network (tooltips and JEI explain each block).
   > Tip: one setup per table size handles every recipe of that size.
13. **Ender Crafting**: `extendedcrafting:ender_crafter`; 2x `extendedcrafting:ender_alternator` *(after: Black Iron Parts, Into the Fire)*
   > What: the Ender Crafter finishes its recipes over time, faster with more Ender Alternators nearby.
   > How: ender ingots (iron + ender pearl), eyes of ender and a crafting table (check JEI).
   > Tip: a ring of alternators around the crafter speeds it up a lot.
14. **Combination Crafting**: `extendedcrafting:crafting_core`; 4x `extendedcrafting:pedestal` *(after: 7x7: Elite Table)*
   > What: put the main item on the Crafting Core and the extras on Pedestals around it. The core uses FE to fuse them.
   > How: elite components and catalysts, a frame and black iron (check JEI). JEI shows every combination recipe.
   > Tip: pipe FE into the core; it won't start without power.
15. **Crystaltine**: 4x `extendedcrafting:crystaltine_ingot` *(after: 7x7: Elite Table)*
   > What: a late crafting metal made on the Elite Table.
   > Chemistry: lapis lazuli's blue comes from lazurite, a sodium aluminosilicate with trapped sulfur ions (S₃⁻).
   > How: diamonds, lapis, iron, gold and a Nether Star in a 7x4 pattern (check JEI).
16. **Compact Machines**: `compactmachines:personal_shrinking_device` *(after: LV Machine Hull)*
   > What: fold an entire factory into a single block. The Personal Shrinking Device lets you walk inside it.
   > How: shrinking and enlarging modules, an eye of ender, copper, iron and glass panes (check JEI).
   > Tip: it also shows you which machine room you're in and takes you back out.
17. **Machine Walls**: 16x `compactmachines:wall` *(after: Compact Machines)*
   > What: Compact Machine Walls frame every machine block.
   > How: crafted from polished deepslate (check JEI).
   > Tip: inside a room the walls are unbreakable; these crafted ones aren't.
18. **Atom Modules**: 2x `compactmachines:shrinking_module`; 2x `compactmachines:enlarging_module` *(after: Machine Walls)*
   > What: every Compact Machine needs one Atom Shrinking Module and one Atom Enlarging Module.
   > How: an eye of ender, a light pressure plate, a stone button and a piston (sticky for shrinking).
   > Tip: make spares; each new machine eats a pair.
19. **Step Inside**: visit `compactmachines:compact_world` *(after: Atom Modules)*
   > You remember holding a world in your palm. This one is smaller, but it's yours.
   > What: craft a Compact Machine, place it, and right-click it with the Personal Shrinking Device to enter its room.
   > How: walls + both modules + a metal sets the size: copper = tiny, iron = small, gold = normal, obsidian = giant, netherite = colossal (check JEI for the rest).
   > Tip: tunnels carry items, fluids and power through the walls; perfect for a compact chemical line.
20. **Hide the Cables**: `cable_facades:facade_wrench` *(after: LV Machine Hull)*
   > What: Cable Facades disguise cables and pipes as any block. Tidy factories, same throughput.
   > How: craft the Facade Wrench, then apply blocks to cables (check JEI).
   > Tip: facades on MI pipes make chemical lines much easier to look at.
21. **Teleporting Cake**: `telepastries:overworld_cake` *(after: Into the Fire)*
   > What: TelePastries cakes teleport you to their dimension when you eat a slice.
   > How: the Overworld Cake brings you home. Refill it with saplings; the Nether Cake takes obsidian.
   > Tip: place the Overworld Cake in your Nether hub for a quick ride home.
22. **Cake at the End**: `telepastries:end_cake` *(after: Teleporting Cake, The End)*
   > What: a cake that sends you to the End.
   > How: a cake surrounded by eyes of ender. Refill it with eyes of ender.
   > Tip: leave it in your base so the End is one bite away.
23. **Training Data: Blazes**: kill 30x `minecraft:blaze` *(after: Your First Model, Into the Fire)*
   > What: every kill with a matching model in your Deep Learner adds data. Blaze models are worth it: blaze rods feed brewing, Powah and Create's Blaze Burners.
   > How: put a blaze data model in the Deep Learner, keep it in your inventory, and hunt blazes at a Nether Fortress.
   > Tip: a blaze model at a high tier turns the Simulation Chamber into an endless rod farm.
24. **Training Data: Endermen**: kill 30x `minecraft:enderman` *(after: Your First Model)*
   > What: ender pearls go into Entangled blocks, AE2 seeds, Powah ender cells and teleporting. An enderman model makes them renewable.
   > How: load an enderman data model and hunt them at night, in warped forests, or in the End.
   > Tip: wear a carved pumpkin, or look at their feet, so they don't attack you first.
25. **Three Worlds of Data**: 4x `hostilenetworks:overworld_prediction`; 4x `hostilenetworks:nether_prediction`; 4x `hostilenetworks:end_prediction` *(after: Simulation, The End)*
   > What: each simulation gives a prediction for the mob's home dimension as well as the drop. Overworld, Nether and End predictions are used in recipes and Fabrication Directives.
   > How: run simulations of one mob from each dimension, such as a zombie, a blaze and an enderman.
   > Tip: predictions stack up fast; send them to storage with a pipe.
26. **The Data Center**: `hostilenetworks:data_center`; `hostilenetworks:data_center_io_port` *(after: Loot Fabricator)*
   > You remember watching a million lives at once. This is a pale copy, but a useful one.
   > What: the Data Center runs up to 25 Self Aware data models at the same time, each making one drop per cycle.
   > How: a Sim Chamber, a Loot Fabricator, a Nether Star, echo shards and obsidian. Build a 7x7x7 hollow shell: obsidian floor, black stained glass walls and ceiling, with the controller in a bottom wall facing out (JEI shows the rules).
   > Tip: IO ports in the shell let pipes feed inputs and pull the drops. A Fabrication Directive picks which drop each slot makes.
27. **Flux Crafting**: `extendedcrafting:flux_crafter`; 2x `extendedcrafting:flux_alternator` *(after: Extended Crafting)*
   > What: the Flux Crafter runs special recipes using FE delivered by nearby Flux Alternators, like the Flux Star.
   > How: gold and redstone ingots (check JEI). Place alternators around the crafter and power them.
   > Tip: JEI's Flux Crafter tab lists every recipe and the FE each one needs.
28. **Auto Tables**: `extendedcrafting:basic_auto_table` *(after: Crystaltine)*
   > What: Auto Tables remember a recipe and craft it with FE whenever the ingredients are inside. Each table size has its own.
   > How: a crafting table of the same size plus crystaltine components (check JEI).
   > Tip: the Ultimate Auto Table is also the heart of Packaged ExCrafting's Ultimate Crafter (see the Applied Energistics page).
29. **A Room for Every Line**: 4x `compactmachines:machine` *(after: Step Inside)*
   > What: one compact machine per chemical line keeps your base tidy: a sulfuric acid room, a chlorine room, a petrochemical room.
   > How: craft more machines (check JEI for the sizes) and enter each with the Personal Shrinking Device.
   > Tip: a room only runs while the chunk with its machine block is loaded, so claim and force-load your factory.

### Automation and Logic

*Make the factory run itself.*

1. **A Computer**: `computercraft:computer_normal` *(after: LV Machine Hull)*
   > You never did your own chores. You remember having things that did them for you.
   > What: CC: Tweaked computers run Lua programs: monitor tanks, balance chemical lines, build a base dashboard.
   > How: place it and right-click. Type help, or edit startup to write a program that runs on boot.
   > Tip: Mekanism machines can be read by computers directly. Put a computer next to one and try peripheral.getNames().
2. **Monitors**: 4x `computercraft:monitor_advanced` *(after: A Computer)*
   > What: monitors show program output on a wall. Placed together, they join into one big screen.
   > How: put monitors next to the computer and run monitor <side> <program> to send output to them.
   > Tip: advanced monitors show colour and accept clicks, so you can make touch buttons.
3. **Wired Networks**: 2x `computercraft:wired_modem`; 16x `computercraft:cable` *(after: Monitors)*
   > What: wired modems and networking cable connect a computer to machines that aren't touching it.
   > How: put a modem on the machine, run cable back to a modem on the computer, and right-click the machine's modem to attach it.
   > Tip: each attached peripheral gets a name. List them with peripheral.getNames().
4. **Ender Modem**: `computercraft:wireless_modem_advanced`; `computercraft:pocket_computer_advanced` *(after: Wired Networks)*
   > What: the Ender Modem sends messages with no range limit, even between dimensions. A pocket computer carries a terminal with you.
   > How: use rednet on both ends: one computer broadcasts tank levels, your pocket computer reads them.
