   > Tip: a remote alarm for your reactor is a very good idea.
5. **Turtles**: `computercraft:turtle_normal` *(after: A Computer)*
   > What: turtles are programmable robots that mine, build and farm.
   > How: craft one with a tool (a pickaxe makes a mining turtle) and fuel it with coal.
   > Tip: try the built-in excavate and tunnel programs first.
6. **Advanced Peripherals**: `advancedperipherals:energy_detector`; `advancedperipherals:me_bridge` *(after: Wired Networks, The God's Archive)*
   > What: extra peripherals: the Energy Detector measures power flow; the ME Bridge reads (and requests crafts from) your whole AE2 network.
   > How: place them on your wired network like any other peripheral.
   > Tip: an ME Bridge program can keep your stock of every intermediate topped up.
7. **Base Dashboard**: `advancedperipherals:fluid_detector`; 8x `computercraft:monitor_advanced` *(after: Advanced Peripherals)*
   > What: a wall screen that shows your chemical stocks, power and flow rates at a glance.
   > How: the Fluid Detector measures flow through a pipe. Combine it with tank readings and the energy detector in one program.
   > Tip: colour lines red when a tank drops below 20%. You'll spot problems from across the room.
8. **Buses**: `ae2:import_bus`; `ae2:export_bus`; `ae2:storage_bus` *(after: The God's Archive)*
   > What: an import bus pulls items out of a machine, an export bus pushes them in, and a storage bus lets ME see inside a chest or tank.
   > How: place them on cable facing the machine. Set filters in each bus's GUI.
   > Tip: Applied Mekanistics is in the pack, so buses and storage also handle Mekanism gases like hydrogen and SO₂.
9. **Crafting CPU**: 2x `ae2:crafting_unit`; `ae2:1k_crafting_storage` *(after: Buses)*
   > What: a crafting CPU is a multiblock that works out and tracks autocrafting jobs.
   > How: build a solid box of crafting units with at least one crafting storage, and connect it to your network.
   > Tip: more storage lets it plan bigger jobs; co-processing units run more steps at once.
10. **Encoding Patterns**: `ae2:pattern_encoding_terminal`; 8x `ae2:blank_pattern` *(after: Crafting CPU)*
   > What: a pattern is a recipe card: these inputs make that output.
   > How: in the Pattern Encoding Terminal, switch to Processing mode for machine recipes and fill in the inputs and outputs.
   > Tip: processing patterns work for any machine: MI, Mekanism or the Combiner.
11. **Autocraft a Chemical Line**: `ae2:pattern_provider`; `ae2:molecular_assembler` *(after: Encoding Patterns)*
   > What: pattern providers push ingredients into machines; molecular assemblers do crafting-table recipes.
   > How: put a provider against an MI or Mekanism machine, load a processing pattern, and send the output back into the network.
   > Tip: chain patterns (sulfur → SO₂ → SO₃ → H₂SO₄) and one request runs the whole line.
12. **Level Emitters**: `ae2:level_emitter` *(after: Base Dashboard, Buses)*
   > What: a level emitter gives a redstone signal when the network holds more (or less) than a number you set.
   > How: set the item and the amount, then wire the signal to a machine's redstone control.
   > Tip: stop the Electrolytic Separator when hydrogen hits your target and you never waste power on a full tank.
13. **Fully Automated Sulfuric Acid**: 16x `modern_industrialization:sulfuric_acid_bucket`; checkmark *(after: Autocraft a Chemical Line, Level Emitters)*
   > The factory hums without you. You remember this kind of quiet.
   > What: a line that makes sulfuric acid on its own and stops when the tanks are full.
   > Chemistry: 2SO₂ + O₂ → 2SO₃ (industry uses a vanadium oxide catalyst), then SO₃ + H₂O → H₂SO₄.
   > How: Separator (O₂) → Oxidizer (S → SO₂) → Infuser (SO₃) → Rotary Condensentrator (water vapour) → Infuser (H₂SO₄). Feed sulfur with a bus or LaserIO and gate it with a level emitter.
   > Tip: when it runs a full day with no help, fill 16 buckets and click the checkmark.
14. **Menril Resin**: 16x `integrateddynamics:crystalized_menril_chunk` *(after: LV Machine Hull)*
   > What: Integrated Dynamics builds everything from crystallised Menril, a resin from Menril trees.
   > Chemistry: drying resin is just evaporation: the liquid loses its solvent and hardens, like tree sap turning into amber.
   > How: squeeze Menril logs in a Squeezer for resin, then dry the resin in a Drying Basin.
   > Tip: Menril trees grow wild in the Overworld. Plant the saplings once you find one.
15. **Integrated Dynamics**: 8x `integrateddynamics:cable`; `integrateddynamics:logic_programmer`; 4x `integrateddynamics:variable` *(after: Menril Resin)*
   > What: read a value (tank level, item count), apply logic, and act on it, all with blocks and cards.
   > How: a Reader part (e.g. Fluid Reader) on a tank exposes values. Write one onto a Variable Card in the Logic Programmer.
   > Tip: a Redstone Writer can then turn that value into a signal: perfect for stopping a separator when its tank is full.
16. **Variable Store**: `integrateddynamics:variablestore`; `integrateddynamics:labeller` *(after: Integrated Dynamics)*
   > What: the Variable Store holds variable cards so the whole network can use them; the Labeller names them.
   > How: put cards in the store and reference them from any writer or display panel on the network.
   > Tip: name cards after what they measure ('H2 tank %'). You'll thank yourself later.
17. **Challenge: The Self-Stopping Separator**: checkmark *(after: Variable Store)*
   > What: make a Mekanism Electrolytic Separator turn itself off when its hydrogen tank is 90% full, and back on below 50%.
   > How: a Reader on the tank → a comparison in the Logic Programmer → a Redstone Writer on the separator (set it to redstone control in its GUI).
   > Tip: the gap between 50% and 90% (hysteresis) stops it flickering on and off.
   > Click the checkmark once it works.
18. **Super Factory Manager**: `sfm:manager`; 8x `sfm:cable`; `sfm:disk` *(after: Integrated Dynamics)*
   > What: one Manager block runs a short program that moves items, fluids and energy between everything on its cables.
   > How: put the disk in the Manager and write a program, e.g. EVERY 20 TICKS DO INPUT FROM a OUTPUT TO b END.
   > Tip: the in-game editor has examples. Start with one input and one output.
19. **Labels**: `sfm:labelgun`; `sfm:network_tool` *(after: Super Factory Manager)*
   > What: the Label Gun tags inventories with names (like a and b above) for your programs.
   > How: set a label in the gun, then right-click each machine. The Network Tool shows what's connected.
   > Tip: label by job ('sulfur_in', 'acid_out'), not by machine type.
20. **Logistics Network**: 2x `logisticsnetworks:logistics_node` *(after: Labels)*
   > What: Logistics Network nodes move items between inventories according to rules you set.
   > How: place nodes on the machines and set each node's direction and rules in its GUI (check JEI and the tooltips).
   > Tip: nodes are compact: good for tight spaces where cables won't fit.
21. **Logistics Filters**: `logisticsnetworks:tag_filter`; `logisticsnetworks:amount_filter` *(after: Logistics Network)*
   > What: filters narrow what a node moves: by tag, by amount and more.
   > How: put a filter in a node's slot and set it up in its GUI (check JEI for all the filter types).
   > Tip: a tag filter for 'c:dusts' sends every dust to one chest.
22. **LaserIO Cards**: 2x `laserio:card_item` *(after: LaserIO)*
   > What: cards tell each LaserIO node what to move and in which direction.
   > How: put a card in a node's side slot. Set it to Extract on the source and Insert on the destination.
   > Tip: right-click with a card in your hand to open its settings.
23. **Fluids and Gases by Laser**: 2x `laserio:card_fluid`; 2x `laserio:card_chemical` *(after: LaserIO Cards)*
   > What: fluid cards move liquids; chemical cards move Mekanism gases, like hydrogen and SO₂.
   > How: same as item cards: Extract on the tank, Insert on the machine.
   > Tip: one node can run several cards on one side. Hydrogen and oxygen out of one separator, two cards.
24. **LaserIO Filters**: `laserio:filter_basic`; `laserio:filter_tag`; `laserio:filter_count` *(after: Fluids and Gases by Laser)*
   > What: filters go inside cards. Basic matches a list, Tag matches a tag, Count keeps a set number in stock.
   > How: put the filter in a card's filter slot and set it up.
   > Tip: a count filter on an insert card keeps exactly 64 sulfur in the oxidizer and no more.
25. **Overclocking**: 2x `laserio:overclocker_card`; 2x `laserio:overclocker_node` *(after: LaserIO Filters)*
   > What: overclockers make LaserIO move more, faster.
   > How: overclocker cards go in a card's upgrade slot; overclocker nodes go in the node.
   > Tip: most lines don't need it. Add it when a machine starves.
26. **Printers and Disks**: `computercraft:printer`; `computercraft:disk_drive` *(after: A Computer)*
   > What: a Disk Drive lets you save programs on floppy disks and carry them between computers. A Printer prints text onto paper.
   > How: check JEI. Put a disk in the drive and it appears as disk/ in the computer's files.
   > Tip: print a list of your reactions and their inputs and bind the pages into a book: a real lab notebook.
27. **Advanced Turtle**: `computercraft:turtle_advanced` *(after: Turtles)*
   > What: advanced turtles have colour screens and can be clicked, which makes menus easy.
   > How: an advanced computer with gold instead of iron around it (check JEI). Give it a pickaxe to make a Mining Turtle.
   > Tip: run excavate 16 on a mining turtle and it digs a 16x16 hole down to bedrock, bringing everything back.
28. **Geo Scanner**: `advancedperipherals:geo_scanner` *(after: Advanced Peripherals)*
   > What: the Geo Scanner lists every block around it within a radius, including ores, for your computer to read.
   > Chemistry: real geologists find ore bodies by measuring gravity, magnetism and how rocks conduct electricity, rather than digging blind.
   > How: check JEI. Put it next to a computer (or in a pocket computer) and call scan(8).
   > Tip: write a program that counts ore blocks by type: it's a quick way to check a deposit before setting up a mine.
29. **Chat Box and Player Detector**: `advancedperipherals:chat_box`; `advancedperipherals:player_detector` *(after: Advanced Peripherals)*
   > What: the Chat Box lets a computer send chat messages; the Player Detector tells it who is nearby.
   > How: check JEI. Place them next to a computer.
   > Tip: have the base warn everyone in chat when a tank of hydrogen is nearly full or an induction matrix is running low.
30. **Inventory Manager**: `advancedperipherals:inventory_manager`; `advancedperipherals:memory_card` *(after: Advanced Peripherals)*
   > What: the Inventory Manager lets a computer move items into and out of your own inventory, once you bind it with a Memory Card.
   > How: right-click the Memory Card to bind it to yourself, then put it in the manager (check JEI).
   > Tip: a button that refills your hotbar with torches and food from storage is a classic first program.
31. **Integrated Tunnels**: checkmark *(after: Variable Store)*
   > What: Integrated Tunnels adds importer, exporter and interface parts for Integrated Dynamics cables: items, fluids and energy move along the network, controlled by variables.
   > How: craft an Item Importer and an Item Exporter (check JEI), put them on cables at a chest and a machine, and give the exporter a variable card that says what to move. Click the checkmark when it works.
   > Tip: a fluid exporter with a boolean variable from a tank reader only fills the reactor when it's below half.
32. **Integrated Terminals**: `integratedterminals:terminal_storage_portable` *(after: Integrated Tunnels)*
   > What: Integrated Terminals adds a Storage Terminal part that shows everything connected to an Integrated Dynamics network, plus a portable version.
   > How: a Storage Terminal part between two crystalized menril chunks makes the portable terminal (check JEI).
   > Tip: the Crafting Job Terminal shows what Integrated Crafting is working on.
33. **Integrated Crafting**: checkmark *(after: Integrated Tunnels)*
   > What: Integrated Crafting adds a Crafting Interface: give it recipe variables and it crafts on demand through the ID network, much like AE2 patterns.
   > How: craft a Crafting Interface (check JEI), write recipes into variable cards with the Logic Programmer, and put them in the interface. Click the checkmark when one craft works.
   > Tip: it's a cheaper autocrafter than AE2 for small chains, like rubber sheets or redstone batteries.
34. **Your First SFM Program**: checkmark *(after: Super Factory Manager)*
   > What: Super Factory Manager runs small text programs written on a disk. Labels name the blocks it talks to.
   > How: label a chest a and a furnace b with the Label Gun, then write this program on the disk and put it in the manager. Click the checkmark when items start moving.
   > {@pagebreak}
   > Program:
   > EVERY 20 TICKS DO
   >     INPUT FROM a
   >     OUTPUT TO b
   > END
   > Tip: add a side, like OUTPUT TO b TOP SIDE, to choose which face of the block is used. The manager's built-in examples cover fluids and energy too.

### The Mechanical Age

*Create: rotation, presses and mixers. An early mechanical age that runs alongside steam and feeds into Modern Industrialization.*

1. **Andesite Alloy**: 16x `create:andesite_alloy` *(after: Iron)*
   > Gears and shafts. You remember a world that ran on clockwork, and you liked it.
   > What: andesite alloy, Create's first building material.
   > How: craft andesite with iron nuggets (zinc nuggets work too). Later a Mechanical Mixer makes it in bulk.
   > Tip: hold W over almost any Create block to watch its Ponder tutorial.
2. **Andesite Casing**: 8x `create:andesite_casing` *(after: Andesite Alloy)*
   > What: casing blocks are the shell for gearboxes, deployers and more.
   > How: right-click a stripped log or stripped wood with andesite alloy. That's 'item application', and Create uses it a lot.
   > Tip: strip logs with an axe first. Any wood type works.
3. **Rotational Power**: 8x `create:shaft`; 8x `create:cogwheel`; `create:water_wheel` *(after: Andesite Casing)*
   > What: Create runs on rotation, not electricity. A water wheel on flowing water turns shafts and cogs.
   > How: water must flow across the wheel's blades. Shafts carry rotation straight; cogwheels pass it sideways.
   > Tip: every machine costs stress units (SU). If the network is over capacity, everything stops.
4. **Gear Ratios**: 4x `create:large_cogwheel` *(after: Rotational Power)*
   > What: a large cogwheel meshed with a small one doubles the speed (or halves it the other way).
   > Chemistry: physics, really: power is torque times speed. Gearing trades one for the other, and Create shows the trade as stress: a machine's SU cost grows with its speed.
   > How: a shaft and two planks make a large cogwheel. Place it diagonally next to a small one so the teeth mesh.
   > Tip: run slow machines (like mixers you don't need fast) at low speed to save stress.
5. **Windmills**: `create:windmill_bearing`; 8x `create:white_sail` *(after: Gear Ratios)*
   > What: a windmill bearing with sails attached spins without water.
   > How: place the bearing, build at least 8 sails onto the front of it (the Ponder shows how), and right-click the bearing.
   > Tip: more sails means more stress capacity but slower speed. Check the numbers with your goggles.
6. **Gearboxes and Belts**: 2x `create:gearbox`; 4x `create:belt_connector` *(after: Windmills)*
   > What: gearboxes turn rotation through corners; belts carry both rotation and items between two shafts.
   > How: belts are made from dried kelp. Click one shaft with a belt, then the other, to link them.
   > Tip: belts make great machine feeders: items ride past presses and fans on their own.
7. **Pressing and Mixing**: `create:mechanical_press`; `create:basin` *(after: Gearboxes and Belts)*
   > What: the Mechanical Press flattens ingots into plates and compacts items in a basin.
   > How: put the press over a depot, a belt or a basin, and power it.
   > Tip: plates are shared with MI in this pack, so pressed plates work in MI recipes too.
8. **Heated Mixing**: `create:mechanical_mixer`; `create:blaze_burner` *(after: Pressing and Mixing, Blaze Rods)*
   > What: a mixer over a basin, heated by a Blaze Burner, makes alloys and fluids.
   > Chemistry: heat speeds up reactions and melts metals so they can mix into alloys.
   > How: catch a blaze in an Empty Blaze Burner (iron plates around netherrack), put it under the basin, feed it fuel, and put the mixer above.
   > Tip: feed the burner with a deployer or a mechanical arm so it never goes cold.
9. **Brass**: 8x `create:zinc_ingot`; 8x `create:brass_ingot` *(after: Heated Mixing)*
   > A golden metal, mixed in a basin. The old skill comes back to your hands.
   > What: brass, the key metal of Create's second age.
   > Chemistry: brass is a copper-zinc alloy, usually about two-thirds copper. Zinc makes it harder and gives the gold colour.
   > How: 1 copper + 1 zinc in a heated mixer gives 2 brass. Zinc ore generates in the world.
10. **Brass Casing**: 8x `create:brass_casing` *(after: Brass)*
   > What: brass casing, for mechanical crafters, arms and brass tunnels.
   > How: right-click a stripped log or stripped wood with a brass ingot.
   > Tip: a deployer holding brass ingots can apply it automatically.
11. **Precision**: `create:precision_mechanism`; 4x `create:mechanical_crafter` *(after: Deployers, Brass Casing)*
   > Each step in its place, every time. You used to build worlds this way.
   > What: the precision mechanism, made by sequenced assembly: one item passes the same stations several times. Mechanical Crafters join into a big grid for recipes too large for a crafting table.
   > How: start with a gold plate, then deploy a cogwheel, a large cogwheel and an iron nugget onto it, 5 loops. JEI shows the sequence. Crafters are an electron tube, brass casing and crafting table.
   > Tip: some outputs are scrap. Sort them off the end of the belt and recycle them.
12. **Hand Crank**: `create:hand_crank` *(after: Andesite Casing)*
   > What: the simplest power source: you turn it yourself.
   > How: three planks over an andesite alloy. Hold right-click on it to spin whatever it's attached to.
   > Tip: handy for testing a contraption before you build a real generator for it.
13. **Goggles and Wrench**: `create:goggles`; `create:wrench` *(after: Rotational Power)*
   > What: Engineer's Goggles show speed and stress when you look at a machine; the wrench rotates and picks up Create blocks.
   > How: wear the goggles in your helmet slot and look at a shaft or generator.
   > Tip: sneak-right-click with the wrench to pick a block up instantly.
14. **Gauges**: `create:speedometer`; `create:stressometer` *(after: Gear Ratios)*
   > What: the Speedometer reads RPM; the Stressometer shows how much of your network's capacity is in use.
   > How: a compass on an andesite casing makes the speedometer (check JEI for the stressometer). Put them inline on a shaft.
   > Tip: both give a comparator signal, so a nearly full network can switch things off by redstone.
15. **Clutch and Gearshift**: `create:clutch`; `create:gearshift` *(after: Gear Ratios)*
   > What: a powered Clutch cuts the rotation passing through it; a powered Gearshift reverses it.
   > How: andesite casing + redstone, with a shaft (clutch) or a cogwheel (gearshift).
   > Tip: a gearshift on a timer makes a piston or gantry go back and forth by itself.
16. **Chain Drives**: 2x `create:encased_chain_drive` *(after: Clutch and Gearshift)*
   > What: encased chain drives pass rotation between each other in a line, in any direction, like a bike chain.
   > How: an andesite casing and three iron nuggets. The Adjustable Chain Gearshift version changes speed with a redstone signal (check JEI).
   > Tip: a neat way to bring power up a wall without shafts sticking out.
17. **Create Deco**: 8x `createdeco:brass_catwalk`; 8x `createdeco:andesite_sheet_metal` *(after: Andesite Casing)*
   > What: catwalks, sheet metal, cage lamps and other industrial building blocks.
   > How: check JEI for Create Deco recipes. Many use a press or the stonecutter.
   > Tip: catwalks over your machine lines make maintenance much easier.
18. **Create: Connected**: `create_connected:brass_gearbox`; `create_connected:kinetic_bridge` *(after: Windmills)*
   > What: Create: Connected adds small conveniences: compact gearboxes, a kinetic bridge that carries rotation across gaps, and more.
   > How: check JEI for everything the mod adds.
   > Tip: the kinetic bridge saves long runs of shafts.
19. **Kinetic Battery**: `create_connected:kinetic_battery` *(after: Create: Connected)*
   > What: the Kinetic Battery charges from rotation and gives it back later, so a windmill's power isn't wasted when nothing is running.
   > How: check JEI and its Ponder scene (hold W over it). Put it on a shaft line to charge it.
   > Tip: a charged battery can be picked up and moved, like a wind-up spring.
20. **Mechanical Bearing**: `create:mechanical_bearing` *(after: Rotational Power)*
   > What: a bearing turns the blocks attached to its face into a moving 'contraption': doors, rotors, fans, ferris wheels.
   > How: a wooden slab, an andesite casing and a shaft. Build onto its face, power it, and right-click to start.
   > Tip: moving blocks keep working: a spinning contraption can carry drills, harvesters and seats.
21. **Clockwork Bearing**: `create:clockwork_bearing` *(after: Mechanical Bearing, Electron Tubes)*
   > What: the Clockwork Bearing points its contraption at the current in-game time, like the hands of a clock.
   > How: a wooden slab, a brass casing and an electron tube. Build an hour hand on it, and a second layer for the minute hand.
   > Tip: a giant clock tower is a classic Create build.
22. **Glue and Chassis**: `create:super_glue`; 4x `create:linear_chassis` *(after: Mechanical Bearing)*
   > What: Super Glue sticks blocks together so they move as one; chassis blocks grab whole rows and layers at once.
   > How: super glue is slime, an iron plate and an iron nugget; drag it across a region to glue it. Linear chassis is alloy and logs.
   > Tip: a slimeball on a chassis face makes it sticky. Check the Ponder for range settings.
23. **Rope Pulley**: `create:rope_pulley` *(after: Glue and Chassis)*
   > What: the Rope Pulley lowers and raises a contraption on a rope, which makes a simple lift.
   > How: an andesite casing, wool and an iron plate. Glue a platform under it and power it.
   > Tip: the brass Elevator Pulley (check JEI) adds floor buttons and stops at contacts.
24. **Mechanical Piston**: `create:mechanical_piston`; 8x `create:piston_extension_pole` *(after: Glue and Chassis)*
   > What: a mechanical piston pushes and pulls glued blocks along a straight line, as far as its poles reach.
   > How: a wooden slab, an andesite casing and an extension pole. Put poles behind the piston head; each pole adds one block of travel.
   > Tip: rotation direction decides push or pull, so a gearshift reverses it.
25. **Drills and Harvesters**: `create:mechanical_drill`; `create:mechanical_harvester` *(after: Mechanical Piston)*
   > What: a moving Drill breaks blocks in front of it; a Harvester picks crops and leaves the plant to regrow.
   > How: both use andesite casing and alloy, with iron (drill) or iron plates (harvester). Mount them on a piston, bearing or gantry.
   > Tip: a slow bearing with harvesters sweeping a round field is a cheap automatic farm.
26. **Gantries**: `create:gantry_carriage`; 8x `create:gantry_shaft` *(after: Mechanical Piston)*
   > What: a Gantry Carriage runs along a powered gantry shaft, carrying its contraption with it, with no length limit.
   > How: gantry shafts are alloy and redstone; the carriage is a slab, a casing and a cogwheel. Power the shaft and the carriage moves.
   > Tip: gantries can carry other gantries, which makes an X-Y frame for a big drill or quarry.
27. **Minecart Contraptions**: 2x `create:cart_assembler` *(after: Glue and Chassis)*
   > What: a Cart Assembler on a rail turns a passing minecart and the blocks glued to it into a moving contraption.
   > How: andesite alloy, redstone and logs. Place it as a rail, build on top, then send a minecart through with the assembler powered.
   > Tip: a minecart contraption with a drill digs its own tunnel along the track.
28. **Millstone**: `create:millstone` *(after: Gearboxes and Belts)*
   > What: the Millstone grinds cobblestone to gravel, wheat to flour, flowers to dye, bones to bone meal and more.
   > Chemistry: grinding raises surface area: a powder reacts, dissolves and mixes much faster than a lump.
   > How: a cogwheel, an andesite casing and stone. Power it from the side or below and drop items in on top.
   > Tip: it doesn't take ores here. Ore crushing needs Crushing Wheels later.
29. **Mechanical Saw**: `create:mechanical_saw` *(after: Millstone)*
   > What: a still saw over a belt cuts logs into planks and acts as a stonecutter; on a contraption it fells whole trees.
   > How: iron plates, an iron ingot and an andesite casing. Set its target recipe with a filter if it has several.
   > Tip: a row of saws on a bearing makes a tree farm that never misses a log.
30. **Copper Casing**: 8x `create:copper_casing` *(after: Pressing and Mixing)*
   > What: copper casing is the base for spouts, item drains and hose pulleys.
   > How: right-click a stripped log or stripped wood with a copper ingot.
   > Tip: press a stack of copper sheets while you're at it. Pipes need them next.
31. **Pipes and Pumps**: 16x `create:fluid_pipe`; `create:mechanical_pump` *(after: Copper Casing)*
   > What: fluid pipes carry liquids, but only while a Mechanical Pump pushes them along.
   > Chemistry: a pump makes a pressure difference, and fluid always flows from high pressure to low.
   > How: copper sheets around a copper ingot make 4 pipes; a pipe and a cogwheel make a pump. The arrow on the pump shows the flow direction.
   > Tip: reverse the pump's rotation to reverse the flow.
32. **Hose Pulley**: `create:hose_pulley` *(after: Pipes and Pumps)*
   > What: the Hose Pulley lowers a hose into a pool and sucks the fluid up, or fills an area from your pipes.
   > How: a copper casing, a dried kelp block and a copper plate. Power it and pipe its output away with a pump.
   > Tip: a big enough body of water or lava counts as endless (check the Ponder), so it's a free fluid source.
33. **Bioethanol**: `createaddition:seed_oil_bucket`; `createaddition:bioethanol_bucket` *(after: Pipes and Pumps)*
   > What: Crafts & Additions makes liquid fuel from plants: seed oil, then biomass, then bioethanol, which burns in a Liquid Blaze Burner.
   > Chemistry: real bioethanol comes from yeast fermenting sugar: C₆H₁₂O₆ → 2 C₂H₅OH + 2 CO₂. Plant oils are triglycerides, long carbon chains that store a lot of energy.
   > How: compact seeds in a press and basin for seed oil; mix crops with seed oil over heat for biomass; mix biomass, sugar and cinder flour for bioethanol (check JEI).
   > Tip: it's a renewable fuel for Create steam engines that never touches your oil wells.
34. **Spout**: `create:spout` *(after: Pipes and Pumps)*
   > What: the Spout pours fluid into items below it on a depot or belt: water bottles, buckets, honey, lava cakes and more.
   > How: a copper casing over dried kelp. Pipe fluid into it and put a depot underneath.
   > Tip: spouts are a station in many sequenced assembly recipes, so you'll use them again.
35. **Item Drain**: `create:item_drain` *(after: Spout)*
   > What: the Item Drain empties fluid containers that roll over it and holds the fluid for your pipes.
   > How: iron bars over a copper casing. Put it in a belt line and pull the fluid out with a pump.
   > Tip: it's the easy way to turn buckets from another mod into piped fluid.
36. **Steam Engines**: 2x `create:steam_engine`; 4x `create:fluid_tank` *(after: Spout)*
   > What: Create's boiler: a fluid tank full of water, heated by Blaze Burners, drives steam engines on its sides.
   > Chemistry: water expands about 1,600 times when it boils. That pressure pushes the pistons.
   > How: pump water into the tank, put fuelled Blaze Burners underneath, and place engines on its sides with a shaft in front. Check the Ponder.
   > Tip: a bigger tank and more burners raise the boiler level. Goggles show what's limiting it.
37. **Blaze Cake**: 2x `create:blaze_cake` *(after: Spout)*
   > What: feeding a Blaze Cake to a burner makes it superheated for a while, hotter than any normal fuel.
   > How: compact an egg, sugar and cinder flour into a cake base, then spout 250 mB of lava into it.
   > Tip: some recipes need superheat (New Age's blank circuits, for one). Check the heat icon in JEI.
38. **Encased Fan**: 2x `create:encased_fan` *(after: Pressing and Mixing)*
   > What: an Encased Fan blows items along and processes them depending on what the air passes through.
   > How: a shaft, an andesite casing and a propeller (iron plates around alloy). Water in front washes, fire smokes, lava blasts, soul fire haunts.
   > Tip: items on a belt or depot in the air stream are processed; loose items on the ground are too.
39. **Smoking and Blasting**: `minecraft:campfire`; `minecraft:lava_bucket` *(after: Encased Fan)*
   > What: a fan blowing through fire or a campfire cooks food (smoking); through lava it smelts like a blast furnace (blasting).
   > Chemistry: hot air carries heat by convection, which is why a fan oven cooks faster than a still one.
   > How: put a lit campfire or a lava source block a block or two in front of the fan and send items through the stream.
   > Tip: one lava block never runs out, so bulk blasting costs nothing but rotation.
40. **Haunting**: `minecraft:soul_campfire`; 16x `minecraft:soul_sand` *(after: Smoking and Blasting)*
   > What: air blown through soul fire 'haunts' items: sand becomes soul sand, stone bricks infested, fungi swap Nether types.
   > How: a soul campfire or soul fire in front of a fan. Pass sand through it for soul sand. JEI lists every haunting recipe.
   > Tip: it's a renewable source of soul sand and soul soil, handy for Nether wart and soul torches.
41. **Fan Catalysts**: `create_connected:empty_fan_catalyst` *(after: Haunting)*
   > What: Create: Connected's fan catalysts hold the 'fire' or 'water' for a fan in one tidy block.
   > How: brass and iron bars make the empty catalyst; fill it to pick its processing type (check JEI).
   > Tip: no more flowing water flooding your belt line.
42. **Depots**: 2x `create:depot` *(after: Gearboxes and Belts)*
   > What: a Depot holds one stack of items for a press, fan, spout or deployer to work on.
   > How: andesite alloy and an andesite casing. Right-click to put items on it.
   > Tip: a depot under a press is the cheapest plate maker in the game.
43. **Funnels and Tunnels**: 4x `create:andesite_funnel`; 2x `create:andesite_tunnel` *(after: Depots)*
   > What: funnels pull items out of chests and machines onto belts, or insert items from a belt; tunnels split a belt line evenly.
   > How: andesite alloy over dried kelp makes 2 funnels. Place them on a chest face or at the end of a belt (check JEI for tunnels).
   > Tip: a redstone signal shuts a funnel off, which is an easy on/off switch.
44. **Weighted Ejector**: `create:weighted_ejector` *(after: Funnels and Tunnels)*
   > What: the Weighted Ejector flings items (or you) to a target block in an arc.
   > How: a gold plate, a depot and a cogwheel. Hold it and click the target before you place it.
   > Tip: stand on it for a free launch pad. The landing is your problem.
45. **Chutes**: 4x `create:chute` *(after: Funnels and Tunnels)*
   > What: chutes drop items straight down between machines and chests; a fan under or over one blows items upward.
   > How: iron plates and an iron ingot make 4 chutes.
   > Tip: a chute into a crushing wheel or basin is the simplest feeder there is.
46. **Item Vaults**: 4x `create:item_vault` *(after: Chutes)*
   > What: Item Vaults merge into one big storage block when placed together.
   > How: iron plates around a barrel. Place several side by side; funnels and pipes reach the whole vault.
   > Tip: vaults also go into frogports, so make a few extra.
47. **Smart Logistics**: 2x `create:brass_funnel`; 2x `create:brass_tunnel`; `create:smart_chute` *(after: Electron Tubes, Chutes)*
   > What: brass funnels, tunnels and smart chutes take a filter, so you choose exactly which items pass and how many.
   > How: each uses an electron tube and brass (check JEI). Put an item or a Create Filter in the slot on the block.
   > Tip: a brass tunnel set to 'synchronise' only lets items through when every lane has one: perfect for 1:1 recipes.
48. **Cardboard**: 8x `create:cardboard` *(after: Item Vaults, Heated Mixing)*
   > What: cardboard, for Create's package logistics.
   > Chemistry: paper and card are mats of cellulose fibres; water lets the fibres bond as it dries, which is why pulp holds together.
   > How: mix 4 pulpable items (like sticks or sugar cane; check JEI) with 250 mB of water for pulp, then press the pulp into cardboard.
49. **Packagers**: 2x `create:packager` *(after: Cardboard)*
   > What: a Packager boxes items from the inventory behind it into a package with an address, or unpacks packages addressed to it.
   > How: a cardboard block, iron ingots and redstone. Put a sign or name on it for its address (see the Ponder).
   > Tip: packages ride belts, chutes and chain conveyors like any other item.
50. **Frogports and Chains**: 2x `create:package_frogport`; 2x `create:chain_conveyor` *(after: Packagers)*
   > What: chain conveyors carry packages through the air; a Package Frogport tongues them off the chain at the right stop.
   > How: a frogport is a slimeball, an item vault and andesite alloy; a chain conveyor is a large cogwheel in andesite casings. Link chains with iron chains.
   > Tip: a chain network with frogports at each base is a whole-base mail system.
51. **Stock Keeping**: `create:stock_link`; `create:stock_ticker` *(after: Frogports and Chains)*
   > What: a Stock Link reports what a packager can reach; a Stock Ticker lets you browse that stock and order items to be packed and sent.
   > How: a transmitter (lightning rod, copper plates, redstone) on an item vault makes the link; add glass and gold for the ticker.
   > Tip: put the ticker by your workbench and order ingredients without walking to storage.
52. **Factory Gauges**: 2x `create:factory_gauge` *(after: Stock Keeping)*
   > What: Factory Gauges watch your stock and request ingredients for a recipe whenever a target amount runs low.
   > How: a stock link and a precision mechanism make 2. Place them on a packager and link them in their panel (see the Ponder).
   > Tip: chain gauges together and one order can run a whole production line.
53. **Rose Quartz**: 4x `create:rose_quartz`; 4x `create:polished_rose_quartz` *(after: Heated Mixing)*
   > What: rose quartz, for electron tubes.
   > How: craft 1 Nether quartz with 8 redstone, then polish it: hold sandpaper with the rose quartz in your off-hand and use it (a deployer can do this too).
   > Tip: washing crushed iron makes redstone as a byproduct, so a later ore line can feed this.
54. **Electron Tubes**: 4x `create:electron_tube` *(after: Rose Quartz)*
   > What: electron tubes go into deployers, mechanical crafters and other smart machines.
   > How: polished rose quartz on top of an iron plate.
   > Tip: make a stack. You'll need more than you think.
55. **Deployers**: `create:brass_hand`; 2x `create:deployer` *(after: Brass Casing, Electron Tubes)*
   > What: a deployer acts like a player's hand: it places, uses and applies items.
   > How: an andesite casing, an electron tube and a brass hand (pressed brass sheets).
   > Tip: deployers over a belt are how Create builds things in steps: that's sequenced assembly.
56. **Mechanical Arm**: `create:mechanical_arm` *(after: Precision)*
   > What: the mechanical arm moves items between depots, belts, basins and machines in its reach.
   > How: hold the arm and click inputs and outputs before you place it. Check the Ponder.
   > Tip: one arm can feed a whole ring of depots around it.
57. **Speed Controller**: `create:rotation_speed_controller` *(after: Precision)*
   > What: the Rotation Speed Controller sets the speed of the cogwheel on top of it to any RPM you choose.
   > How: a precision mechanism over a brass casing. Power it from below and set the speed on its face.
   > Tip: faster costs more stress, so only speed up what needs it.
58. **Crushing Wheels**: `create:millstone`; 2x `create:crushing_wheel` *(after: Precision)*
   > What: two Crushing Wheels spinning toward each other grind ore blocks into crushed ore, obsidian into powder, and stone into gravel and sand.
   > How: each wheel is a mechanical crafting recipe (check JEI). Place them side by side, spin them inward, and drop items in from above.
   > How: yields: an ore block gives 1 crushed ore plus a 75% chance of a second; deepslate and modded ores are capped at 2 in this pack. Raw ore gives just 1 crushed ore (no gain), plus an XP nugget chance.
   > Tip: crush ore blocks (silk touch), not raw ore. Even so it's the same 2x ceiling as every route before acids; Mekanism's acid chain is where 3x and up begins.
59. **Bulk Washing**: `create:encased_fan`; 8x `create:crushed_raw_iron` *(after: Crushing Wheels, Encased Fan)*
   > What: an encased fan blowing through water washes crushed ore into nuggets plus a byproduct.
   > How: put flowing water in front of the fan and pass items through the air stream (a belt works).
   > Tip: washed crushed iron gives 9 nuggets (one ingot's worth) and a 75% chance of redstone. The byproduct is the bonus, not extra iron.
60. **Sturdy Sheets**: 4x `create:powdered_obsidian`; 4x `create:sturdy_sheet` *(after: Crushing Wheels, Spout)*
   > What: sturdy sheets, the armour plate for train casings and schedules.
   > Chemistry: obsidian is volcanic glass: lava cooled so fast that its silica never formed crystals.
   > How: crush obsidian into powder, then a sequenced assembly: spout 500 mB of lava onto it, press, press. JEI shows the loop.
   > Tip: the crushing wheels give the obsidian back 75% of the time, so feed it in until it's all powder.
61. **Train Casing**: 8x `create:railway_casing` *(after: Sturdy Sheets)*
   > What: train casing is the base block for stations, controls, signals and bogeys.
   > How: right-click a brass casing with a sturdy sheet.
   > Tip: right-clicking a track with train casing places a bogey: the wheels your train sits on.
62. **Train Track**: 32x `create:track` *(after: Train Casing, Deployers)*
   > What: Create's train track, which can curve, slope and cross.
   > How: sequenced assembly on stone slabs (the 'sleepers'): deploy an iron or zinc nugget, deploy another, then press. Check JEI.
   > Tip: click one track end, then another, to lay a curve or slope between them.
63. **Stations and Controls**: 2x `create:track_station`; `create:controls` *(after: Train Track)*
   > What: a Train Station assembles a train from the casing, bogeys and blocks on the track beside it; Train Controls let you drive it.
   > How: a station is train casing and a compass; controls are a lever, train casing and a precision mechanism.
   > Tip: stand at the station's GUI and press assemble once the controls face the way you want to go.
64. **Your First Train**: 2x `create:schedule` *(after: Stations and Controls)*
   > A machine that carries itself across the world. You used to move whole mountains like this.
   > What: a Train Schedule tells a train where to stop and when to leave, so it runs by itself.
   > How: a sturdy sheet and paper make 4. Write stops in it and give it to the conductor (a mob in a seat behind the controls).
   > Tip: a train with portable storage interfaces at each station moves ore and goods on its own.
65. **Signals**: 4x `create:track_signal` *(after: Stations and Controls)*
   > What: signals split a line into sections so two trains never share one. Real railways use the same 'block' idea.
   > How: train casing and an electron tube make 4. Place them on the track at each junction.
   > Tip: check the Ponder for chain signals at crossings.
66. **Rotation to Electricity**: `createaddition:alternator` *(after: Precision, Analog Circuit)*
   > What: Crafts and Additions' Alternator turns rotation into FE.
   > Chemistry: a coil turning in a magnetic field pushes electrons along the wire: electromagnetic induction.
   > How: in this pack its core is an MI analog circuit (LV), so electricity from rotation waits for the LV age. Feed it fast rotation.
67. **Electricity to Rotation**: `createaddition:electric_motor` *(after: Rotation to Electricity)*
   > What: the Electric Motor turns FE back into rotation, with a speed you set.
   > How: give it FE from your MI or Mekanism grid and set the speed in its GUI.
   > Tip: motors free you from water wheels and windmills. Put a Create line anywhere.
68. **Wires and Connectors**: `createaddition:rolling_mill`; 4x `createaddition:connector`; `createaddition:copper_spool` *(after: Rotation to Electricity)*
   > What: the Rolling Mill turns ingots into rods and plates into wire. Spools of wire link connectors into power lines.
   > How: roll copper plates into wire, wind it onto a spool, then click two connectors with the spool to string a wire.
   > Tip: connectors carry power over long gaps without cables on the ground.
69. **Accumulators**: 2x `createaddition:capacitor`; `createaddition:modular_accumulator` *(after: Wires and Connectors)*
   > What: the Modular Accumulator stores FE, and several placed together merge into one bigger battery.
   > Chemistry: the capacitor here is zinc over copper, the same pair Volta used in the first battery in 1800.
   > How: needs brass casing, capacitors, copper rods and electrum wire. Electrum is gold + silver mixed hot.
   > Tip: put it between your alternators and your machines to smooth out spikes.
70. **Tesla Coil**: `createaddition:tesla_coil` *(after: Accumulators)*
   > What: the Tesla Coil charges FE items on belts or depots below it, and zaps anything that walks under it.
   > How: a mechanical crafting recipe with brass, a capacitor, electron tubes and a copper spool (check JEI). Power it with FE.
   > Tip: put a belt of jetpacks or gadgets under it for an automatic charging station.
71. **Create: New Age**: `create_new_age:generator_coil`; `create_new_age:basic_energiser` *(after: Rotation to Electricity)*
   > What: magnet-and-coil generators, and Energisers that charge items with electricity.
   > How: the Generator Coil's core is an MI analog circuit (LV) in this pack. Spin it among magnets; the Ponder shows the layout.
   > Tip: the Basic Energiser is just an andesite casing and a lightning rod.
72. **New Age Motors**: `create_new_age:basic_motor`; `create_new_age:carbon_brushes` *(after: Create: New Age)*
   > What: New Age's Basic Motor runs on FE and spins a shaft; carbon brushes and motor extensions tune it.
   > Chemistry: a motor is a generator run backwards: current in a coil inside a magnetic field feels a force and turns.
   > How: an andesite casing, a magnetite block, a shaft and iron nuggets (check JEI for magnetite). Brushes are alloy, coal and a shaft.
73. **Heat Engines**: `create_new_age:stirling_engine`; `create_new_age:basic_solar_heating_plate` *(after: Create: New Age)*
   > What: New Age moves heat through heat pipes. Solar heating plates collect it from sunlight; a Stirling Engine turns it into slow rotation, and a Boiler Heater uses it in place of a blaze burner.
   > Chemistry: a real Stirling engine heats and cools a sealed gas. Hot gas expands and pushes a piston; cooled gas contracts and pulls it back. No fuel burns inside it.
   > How: heat pipes, iron and glass for the plate; a copper block, heat pipes and a shaft for the engine (check JEI and the Ponder scenes).
74. **Charging Metal**: 4x `create_new_age:overcharged_iron` *(after: Create: New Age)*
   > What: overcharged iron, made by charging an iron ingot in an Energiser.
   > How: power the Energiser with FE and put iron underneath it (on a depot or belt). Each ingot takes 1,000 FE.
   > Tip: overcharged metals make better wire and magnets.
75. **New Age Circuits**: 4x `create_new_age:blank_circuit`; `create_new_age:copper_circuit` *(after: Charging Metal)*
   > What: New Age's own circuit boards, used in its better machines (check JEI for uses).
   > Chemistry: real boards are an insulating base with copper traces; here quartz, stone, iron and redstone fuse into the base.
   > How: compact stone, redstone, iron and quartz over a superheated burner (blaze cake) for 4 blanks, then deploy copper wire onto one.
76. **Layered Magnets**: 4x `create_new_age:layered_magnet` *(after: Charging Metal)*
   > What: stronger magnets for New Age generators.
   > How: overcharged iron and overcharged gold in layers (check JEI).
   > Tip: a stronger field induces more current in the same coil.
77. **Thorium Reactor**: 8x `create_new_age:thorium`; `create_new_age:reactor_rod` *(after: Charging Metal, EV: Processing Units)*
   > What: New Age's nuclear reactor, fuelled by thorium.
   > Chemistry: thorium-232 isn't fissile itself; it absorbs a neutron and becomes uranium-233, which is.
   > How: the rods need MI processing units (EV) in this pack, so it arrives alongside Mekanism fission. Check JEI and the Ponder for the build.
   > Tip: treat it like any reactor: cooling first, then power.
78. **Liquid Experience**: `create_enchantment_industry:mechanical_grindstone`; `create_enchantment_industry:experience_bucket` *(after: Precision)*
   > What: Enchantment Industry turns experience into a fluid you can store and pipe.
   > How: the Mechanical Grindstone strips enchantments off items; a Grindstone Drain collects the XP as fluid. Check JEI.
   > Tip: feed it the enchanted junk from your mob farm.
79. **Automated Enchanting**: `create_enchantment_industry:blaze_enchanter` *(after: Liquid Experience)*
   > What: the Blaze Enchanter spends liquid XP to enchant items automatically.
   > How: it's a smithing upgrade of a Blaze Burner with an enchanting table (check JEI). Give it an enchanting template and XP.
   > Tip: put it beside a belt and a deployer for a hands-free enchanting line.
80. **The Printer**: `create_enchantment_industry:printer` *(after: Automated Enchanting)*
   > What: the Printer copies written and enchanted books, paid for in liquid XP.
   > How: check JEI and the Ponder for the setup.
   > Tip: find a good enchanted book once, then print it as often as your XP lasts.
81. **Experience Lantern**: `create_enchantment_industry:experience_lantern` *(after: Liquid Experience)*
   > What: the Experience Lantern pulls nearby experience orbs in and stores them as liquid XP.
   > How: check JEI for the recipe. Hang it over a mob farm's kill zone and pipe its XP away.
   > Tip: pair it with the Blaze Enchanter and your farm pays for its own enchanting.

### Tools and Comfort

*Better tools from better materials, and a soft place to land.*

1. **Repair Kits**: `silentgear:crude_repair_kit` *(after: Tools from Parts)*
   > You remember tools that never wore out. These will, but you can mend them.
   > What: Silent Gear tools don't break for good; at zero durability they just stop working until repaired.
   > How: craft a Crude Repair Kit, load it with matching material, then combine it with the tool in a crafting grid.
   > Tip: a better kit (Sturdy, then Crimson and Azure) holds more material and repairs more per use.
2. **Blaze Gold**: 4x `silentgear:blaze_gold_ingot` *(after: Repair Kits, Into the Fire)*
   > What: Silent Gear's first magic alloy. It's a key ingredient of the Material Grader and the Starlight Charger.
   > Chemistry: gold (Au) is a noble metal: it barely reacts with anything, which is why it's found as native nuggets. Blaze gold is fictional; the blaze powder does the work.
   > How: gold ingot + 2 blaze powder in the Alloy Forge, or check JEI for the early crafting route.
   > Tip: keep a stack spare. Blaze gold blocks also go into the Starlight Charger.
3. **Material Grader**: `silentgear:material_grader` *(after: Blaze Gold)*
   > What: grades Silent Gear materials. A higher grade adds a stat bonus to every part made from that material.
   > How: quartz, iron, blaze gold and an Advanced Upgrade Base (check JEI). Feed it materials plus a catalyst (check JEI for which items count).
   > Tip: grade only the materials you'll use for heads and main parts; rods and bindings matter less.
4. **Salvager**: `silentgear:salvager` *(after: Material Grader)*
   > What: breaks old Silent Gear tools back into parts, so upgrading doesn't waste materials.
   > How: place it and drop in the old tool. Some material is lost on each salvage.
   > Tip: salvage before you throw anything in lava; the parts often have most of their value left.
5. **Crimson Iron**: 8x `silentgear:crimson_iron_ingot` *(after: Repair Kits, Into the Fire)*
   > What: crimson iron ore generates in Nether biomes. It's the base of crimson steel and the Alloy Forge.
   > How: mine crimson iron ore in the Nether (Jade shows the name), then smelt the raw ore.
   > Tip: run a few through the Alchemistry Dissolver and see which real elements it gives back.
6. **Crimson Steel**: 4x `silentgear:crimson_steel_ingot` *(after: Crimson Iron)*
   > What: a tough, heat-loving alloy for tool heads and the Alloy Forge frame.
   > Chemistry: real steel is iron with a little carbon (under about 2%); the carbon atoms jam the iron lattice so it can't slip. Crimson steel is the fictional cousin.
   > How: the first ingots are a shaped craft (crimson iron, blaze rods, magma cream); check JEI.
7. **The Alloy Forge**: `silentgear:alloy_forge` *(after: Crimson Steel)*
   > What: mixes metals into Silent Gear alloys: blaze gold, crimson steel (3 per batch), azure electrum and tyrian steel.
   > How: crimson steel ingots, blackstone and an iron block (check JEI). Fuel it like a furnace.
   > Tip: alloying in the forge gives more ingots per batch than the crafting-table route.
8. **Azure Electrum**: 4x `silentgear:azure_electrum_ingot` *(after: The Alloy Forge, The End)*
   > What: azure silver ore generates in the End. Alloyed with gold it becomes azure electrum, a fast, enchantable metal.
   > Chemistry: real electrum is a natural gold–silver alloy (Au–Ag); the ancient Lydians struck the first coins from it.
   > How: Alloy Forge: an azure silver block, 2 gold ingots and an ender pearl.
9. **Tyrian Steel**: 4x `silentgear:tyrian_steel_ingot` *(after: Azure Electrum)*
   > What: the top Silent Gear alloy: crimson steel, azure electrum, crushed shulker shell and netherite scrap.
   > Chemistry: Tyrian purple was a real dye made from sea snails, once worth its weight in silver. The steel just borrowed the colour.
   > How: Alloy Forge (check JEI). Crush shulker shells first.
10. **Bronze Paxel**: `mekanismtools:bronze_paxel` *(after: The Paxel, The First Alloy)*
   > What: a paxel is a pickaxe, axe and shovel in one tool. Bronze is tougher than iron.
   > Chemistry: bronze is copper with about 12% tin (Cu–Sn). Tin atoms stiffen copper's soft lattice.
   > How: craft a bronze pickaxe, axe and shovel, then combine them with a stick (check JEI).
11. **Osmium Paxel**: `mekanismtools:osmium_paxel` *(after: Bronze Paxel, Mekanism: Metallurgic Infuser)*
   > What: Mekanism's signature metal as a paxel: more durability and speed than bronze.
   > Chemistry: osmium (Os) is the densest natural element at about 22.6 g/cm³, twice as dense as lead.
   > How: osmium ore generates in the Overworld; smelt or process it, then craft the tools and combine.
12. **Diamond Paxel**: `mekanismtools:diamond_paxel` *(after: Osmium Paxel)*
   > What: the diamond paxel mines everything the diamond pickaxe can, in one slot.
   > Chemistry: diamond is pure carbon with every atom bonded to four others in a rigid 3D lattice. Graphite is the same carbon in flat sheets.
   > How: diamond pickaxe, axe and shovel plus a stick (check JEI).
13. **The Ultimate Paxel**: `mekanismtools:refined_obsidian_paxel` *(after: Diamond Paxel)*
   > You remember a hammer that could split mountains. This is a start.
   > What: refined obsidian is Mekanism's toughest material, and its paxel is the best of the line.
   > How: infuse obsidian dust with diamond in the Metallurgic Infuser, then press the refined obsidian dust in the Osmium Compressor (check JEI).
   > Tip: the same ingots make the Atomic Disassembler and much of Mekanism's late gear.
14. **Steel Paxel**: `mekanismtools:steel_paxel` *(after: Bronze Paxel, Steel)*
   > What: a sidegrade to bronze, made from the steel you already produce.
   > Chemistry: steel is iron with a little carbon from coke. Too much carbon (cast iron) makes it brittle.
   > How: steel pickaxe, axe and shovel plus a stick (check JEI).
15. **Atomic Disassembler**: `mekanism:atomic_disassembler` *(after: The Ultimate Paxel)*
   > What: an electric tool that mines, chops and digs, with vein and area modes. It runs on FE instead of durability.
   > How: refined obsidian, atomic alloy, infused alloy and an Energy Tablet (check JEI). Charge it in an energy cube.
   > Tip: sneak + right-click to switch modes.
16. **Bottled Oxygen**: `mekanism:scuba_mask`; `mekanism:scuba_tank` *(after: Mekanism: Metallurgic Infuser)*
   > What: the Scuba Mask and Tank let you breathe underwater for as long as the tank has oxygen.
   > Chemistry: the Electrolytic Separator splits water: 2H₂O → 2H₂ + O₂. The O₂ fills the tank; the H₂ is fuel.
   > How: steel, a control circuit and a chemical tank (check JEI). Fill the tank from a Chemical Tank or the separator's oxygen output.
   > Tip: great for ocean monuments and underwater prospecting.
17. **Free Runners**: `mekanism:free_runners` *(after: Bottled Oxygen)*
   > What: electric boots: auto step-up and no fall damage while charged.
   > How: infused alloy, a basic control circuit and an Energy Tablet (check JEI).
   > Tip: the armoured version adds protection; upgrade once you have more alloy.
18. **MekaSuit**: `mekanism:mekasuit_helmet`; `mekanism:mekasuit_bodyarmor` *(after: Free Runners, EV: Processing Units)*
   > You remember armour that was never armour, only certainty. This is the closest a mortal gets.
   > What: Mekanism's modular power armour. Add modules for flight, night vision, magnet, radiation shielding and more.
   > Chemistry: it needs HDPE sheets (polyethylene: long C₂H₄ chains) and polonium pellets. Real ²¹⁰Po is made by hitting bismuth-209 with neutrons; Mekanism makes it from nuclear waste in the Solar Neutron Activator.
   > How: netherite armour, HDPE sheets, ultimate control circuits, polonium pellets and induction cells (check JEI). Install modules in the Modification Station.
19. **Hazmat Gear**: `mekanism:hazmat_mask` *(after: Bottled Oxygen)*
   > What: the full hazmat suit stops Mekanism radiation from building up in you. Cheap and worth having before fission.
   > Chemistry: lead (Pb) is dense and has many electrons, so it absorbs gamma rays well.
   > How: lead ingots and orange dye for each piece (check JEI).
20. **Hydrogen Jetpack**: `mekanism:jetpack` *(after: Bottled Oxygen)*
   > What: Mekanism's jetpack burns hydrogen gas. Short hops, not true flight.
   > Chemistry: 2H₂ + O₂ → 2H₂O. Burning hydrogen gives only water and a lot of energy.
   > How: steel, tin, a basic control circuit and a chemical tank. Fill it with H₂ from the Electrolytic Separator.
21. **Jetpacks**: `ironjetpacks:basic_coil`; `ironjetpacks:jetpack` *(after: Analog Circuit)*
   > What: Iron Jetpacks run on FE. Every tier needs a coil, and in this pack the coils need MI circuits.
   > How: Basic coil = analog circuit (LV). Craft the coil, then cells, thrusters, strap and the jetpack for your metal (check JEI).
   > Tip: better metals fly faster and hold more FE. Charge it in any energy cube.
22. **Advanced Coil**: `ironjetpacks:advanced_coil` *(after: Jetpacks, MV: Aluminum)*
   > What: unlocks the middle jetpack tiers.
   > How: needs an MI electronic circuit (MV, aluminum chemistry). Check JEI for which jetpacks use it.
   > Tip: JEI shows which jetpacks use this coil.
23. **Elite Coil**: `ironjetpacks:elite_coil` *(after: Advanced Coil, HV: Digital Circuits)*
   > What: faster, longer flights.
   > How: needs an MI digital circuit (HV, polyethylene).
   > Tip: the digital circuit is your polyethylene line paying off; make a few extra.
24. **Ultimate Coil**: `ironjetpacks:ultimate_coil` *(after: Elite Coil, EV: Processing Units)*
   > What: the top jetpack tier.
   > How: needs an MI processing unit (EV, PVC chemistry).
   > Tip: by now the Angel Ring may suit you better; the jetpack still has the best speed.
25. **The Angel Ring**: `angelring:diamond_ring`; `angelring:angel_ring` *(after: Jetpacks, HV: Digital Circuits, The End)*
   > You remember the sky being yours. Now it is again.
   > What: portable creative flight, no fuel, from a Curios ring slot.
   > Chemistry: its MI digital circuit needs polyethylene: n C₂H₄ → (C₂H₄)ₙ, ethylene from your distillation tower.
   > How: Elytra, ghast tears, diamond blocks and Nether Stars plus the digital circuit. Craft the Diamond Ring first, then the Angel Ring.
26. **Hammock**: `comforts:hammock_white` *(after: Rest Anywhere)*
   > What: skips the day the way a sleeping bag skips the night, without moving your spawn.
   > How: white wool, string and sticks. Hang it between two Rope and Nails placed on facing walls (check JEI).
   > Tip: useful when you want night-only mobs, like the ones a Deep Learner needs.
27. **Warp Stone**: `waystones:warp_stone` *(after: Iron)*
   > What: Waystones (found in villages, or crafted) let you teleport between places you've visited. A Warp Stone works from anywhere.
   > How: amethyst, ender pearls and emeralds (check JEI). Activate a waystone first by right-clicking it.
   > Tip: it has a cooldown, so don't count on it in an emergency.
28. **Tool Belt**: `toolbelt:belt` *(after: Iron)*
   > What: a belt worn in a Curios slot that holds tools and swaps them into your hand with a key.
   > How: leather, string and an iron ingot. Add pouches for more slots.
   > Tip: set the belt key in Controls. Great for paxel, sword, hoe and wrench.
29. **Mining Gadget**: `mininggadgets:mininggadget_simple` *(after: LV Machine Hull)*
   > What: a mining laser that breaks blocks at range. Upgrades add 3x3 (and bigger) size, efficiency, magnet and void filters.
   > How: iron, gold, diamond and redstone (check JEI). Charge it with FE.
   > Tip: a void filter for stone and dirt keeps your inventory clean on long ore runs.
30. **Bindings and Grips**: `silentgear:binding`; `silentgear:grip` *(after: Tools from Parts)*
   > What: bindings and grips are optional Silent Gear parts. Each adds its material's traits and stats to the tool.
   > How: make the Binding and Grip blueprints, then craft the parts from string, vines, leather or wool (check JEI). Add them to a tool in a crafting grid.
   > Tip: the Material Book (or JEI) shows every material's traits. Hover a part to see what it adds.
31. **Coatings**: `silentgear:coating_smithing_template` *(after: Bindings and Grips, Material Grader)*
   > What: a coating covers a finished tool's main part in another material, like diamond or netherite, changing its stats and colour.
   > Chemistry: real tools are coated too: drill bits get a thin layer of titanium nitride (TiN) or diamond to stay sharp much longer.
   > How: craft the coating smithing template (check JEI), then apply the coating material at a smithing table.
32. **Silent Jewellery**: 1x any of `or(item(silentgear:ring)item(silentgear:bracelet)item(silentgear:necklace))` *(after: Bindings and Grips)*
   > What: Silent Gear jewellery carries material traits as curios: a ring of a lucky or magnetic material gives you that trait while worn.
   > How: a ring shank, bracelet band or necklace chain plus a gem setting, made with the matching blueprint (check JEI).
   > Tip: try different metals and gems. The traits list on each material shows what jewellery will do.
33. **Armour from Parts**: 4x any of `or(item(silentgear:helmet)item(silentgear:chestplate)item(silentgear:leggings)item(silentgear:boots))` *(after: Bindings and Grips, Crimson Steel)*
   > What: Silent Gear armour is built from plates of any material, so your best alloy becomes your best armour.
   > How: helmet, chestplate, legging and boot plates from their blueprints, then assemble each piece (check JEI). Any four pieces.
   > Tip: crimson steel and azure electrum plates have strong traits; check the Material Book before choosing.
34. **Primordial Goo**: `justdirethings:gooblock_tier1` *(after: Iron)*
   > You remember a slime that turned stone into anything you asked. This one is less clever, but it tries.
   > What: Just Dire Things goo converts the blocks it touches. Tier 1 goo turns an iron block into raw ferricore ore.
   > How: clay, sugar, rotten flesh and dirt (check JEI). Place an iron block against the goo and wait (JEI shows how long).
   > Tip: JEI's goo tab shows every conversion and which goo tier it needs.
35. **Ferricore**: 8x `justdirethings:ferricore_ingot`; `justdirethings:ferricore_wrench` *(after: Primordial Goo)*
   > What: ferricore is the first Just Dire Things metal: tools, the Pocket Generator, machines and upgrade blanks.
   > How: mine the raw ferricore ore the goo makes and smelt it (check JEI).
   > Tip: the Ferricore Wrench rotates and picks up JDT machines.
36. **Blazegold**: `justdirethings:gooblock_tier2`; 4x `justdirethings:blazegold_ingot` *(after: Ferricore, Into the Fire)*
   > What: tier 2 goo turns gold blocks into raw blazegold ore. Blazegold makes the next tier of JDT tools and machines.
   > How: upgrade tier 1 goo with blaze powder, redstone and Nether wart (check JEI), then feed it gold blocks.
   > Tip: goo only converts blocks touching it, so surround it with several gold blocks at once.
37. **Celestigem**: `justdirethings:gooblock_tier3`; 4x `justdirethings:celestigem` *(after: Blazegold, The Dragon's Breath)*
   > What: tier 3 goo turns diamond blocks into celestigem ore. Celestigem tools take powerful upgrades.
   > How: upgrade tier 2 goo with ender pearls, end stone and dragon's breath (check JEI).
   > Tip: JDT's flight upgrade needs End Crystals, so portable flight here comes after the End, as the pack intends.
38. **Eclipse Alloy**: `justdirethings:gooblock_tier4`; 2x `justdirethings:eclipsealloy_ingot` *(after: Celestigem, An Ancient City)*
   > What: tier 4 goo turns netherite blocks into eclipse alloy ore, JDT's top material.
   > How: upgrade tier 3 goo with sculk, a sculk shrieker and echo shards from an Ancient City (check JEI). Then sacrifice a netherite block.
   > Tip: it's expensive. Decide which eclipse alloy tool you want before you start.
39. **Pocket Generator**: `justdirethings:pocket_generator` *(after: Ferricore)*
   > What: the Pocket Generator burns fuel in your inventory and charges your FE items.
   > How: ferricore, a furnace, coal and a redstone block (check JEI).
   > Chemistry: burning coal is oxidation: C + O₂ → CO₂ releases about 394 kJ per mole of carbon as heat.
40. **Thinking with Portals**: `justdirethings:portalgun` *(after: Celestigem)*
   > You remember stepping between places without walking. You'd forgotten how much fun it was.
   > What: the Portal Gun shoots two linked portals onto walls. Walk in one, come out the other.
   > Tip: there is an upgraded Portal Gun too; JEI has its recipe.

### Just Dire Things

*Living goo that rewrites the blocks it touches: new metals, denser coal, refined fuels, upgradeable tools and small automation machines.*

1. **Feeding the Goo**: `justdirethings:gooblock_tier1`; 2x `minecraft:iron_block` *(after: Iron)*
   > You remember making things change just by touching them. The goo still can; you'll borrow the trick.
   > What: goo blocks slowly convert the blocks touching them. Tier 1 (Primogel) turns iron blocks into raw ferricore ore.
   > How: place the goo, put iron blocks against its sides and wait. JEI's Goo Spreading tab lists every conversion, its tier and its time.
   > Tip: after a conversion the goo can die (a 10% chance in this pack's config). Right-click exhausted Primogel with sugar or rotten flesh to wake it.
2. **Raw Ferricore**: 8x `justdirethings:raw_ferricore` *(after: Feeding the Goo)*
   > What: raw ferricore ore drops raw ferricore. Smelt or blast it into ferricore ingots, the base of everything on this page.
   > How: mine the converted block with a pickaxe. Silk Touch gives you the ore block itself instead.
   > Tip: one iron block (9 ingots) becomes one raw ferricore, so let a Create or MI line feed the goo once you need a lot.
3. **Upgrade Blanks**: 2x `justdirethings:upgrade_blank` *(after: Raw Ferricore)*
   > What: every Just Dire Things upgrade and smithing template starts from a blank.
   > How: four ferricore ingots in a diamond shape (check JEI).
   > Tip: keep a stack around; most upgrades are a blank plus a few vanilla items.
4. **Blazebloom and Blazegold**: `justdirethings:gooblock_tier2`; 4x `justdirethings:raw_blazegold` *(after: Raw Ferricore, Into the Fire)*
   > What: Blazebloom goo (tier 2) converts gold blocks into raw blazegold ore. Blazegold opens the second tier of tools and machines.
   > How: tier 1 goo ringed with blaze powder, redstone and Nether wart (check JEI). Smelt the raw blazegold.
   > Chemistry: real gold is famously unreactive: it doesn't tarnish in air because Au has a very high reduction potential (+1.50 V).
   > Tip: feed exhausted Blazebloom Nether wart or blaze powder.
5. **VoidShimmer and Celestigem**: `justdirethings:gooblock_tier3`; 4x `justdirethings:celestigem` *(after: Blazebloom and Blazegold, The Dragon's Breath)*
   > What: VoidShimmer goo (tier 3) turns diamond blocks into celestigem ore, which drops celestigem directly. Advanced machines and the third tool tier use it.
   > How: tier 2 goo ringed with ender pearls, end stone and dragon's breath (check JEI). A diamond conversion takes twice as long as iron or gold.
   > Chemistry: diamond is pure carbon: every atom bonds to four neighbours in a tetrahedral (sp³) lattice, which is why it's so hard.
   > Tip: VoidShimmer revives with chorus fruit or an ender pearl.
6. **Shadowpulse and Eclipse Alloy**: `justdirethings:gooblock_tier4`; 2x `justdirethings:raw_eclipsealloy` *(after: VoidShimmer and Celestigem, An Ancient City)*
   > The goo has learned to eat the hardest thing you own. You remember being that hungry.
   > What: Shadowpulse goo (tier 4) turns netherite blocks into raw eclipse alloy ore, Just Dire Things' top material.
   > How: tier 3 goo ringed with sculk, sculk shriekers and echo shards from an Ancient City (check JEI). Smelt the raw eclipse alloy.
   > Tip: one netherite block is a big price. Plan which eclipse alloy item you want first; Shadowpulse revives with sculk or a sculk catalyst.
7. **Primal Coal**: 8x `justdirethings:coal_t1` *(after: Feeding the Goo)*
   > What: tier 1 goo turns a coal block (or a charcoal block) into Primal Coal ore. Primal Coal burns for 4,800 ticks, three times a piece of coal.
   > Chemistry: real coal ranks up with heat and pressure: lignite (~65% carbon) → bituminous (~80%) → anthracite (>90%). More carbon, more energy per kilogram.
   > How: place coal or charcoal blocks against tier 1 goo and mine the ore (it drops several pieces).
   > Tip: nine Primal Coal make a block, and that block is the input for the next tier.
8. **Blaze Ember**: 8x `justdirethings:coal_t2` *(after: Primal Coal, Blazebloom and Blazegold)*
   > What: tier 2 goo turns a Primal Coal block into Blaze Ember ore. Blaze Ember burns 14,400 ticks: nine coal's worth.
   > How: craft a Primal Coal block, put it against Blazebloom goo and mine the ore for 3 to 4 embers.
   > Tip: each coal tier burns three times longer than the one before, so the climb pays off quickly.
9. **Voidflame Coal**: 8x `justdirethings:coal_t3` *(after: Blaze Ember, VoidShimmer and Celestigem)*
   > What: tier 3 goo turns a Blaze Ember block into Voidflame Coal ore: 43,200 ticks of burn per piece.
   > How: a block of Blaze Ember against VoidShimmer goo (check JEI for counts).
   > Tip: Voidflame Coal is also the catalyst for the tier 3 fluid fuel.
10. **Eclipse Ember**: 4x `justdirethings:coal_t4` *(after: Voidflame Coal, Shadowpulse and Eclipse Alloy)*
   > What: tier 4 goo turns a Voidflame Coal block into Eclipse Ember ore. One ember burns 129,600 ticks, as long as 81 pieces of coal.
   > How: a block of Voidflame Coal against Shadowpulse goo.
   > Chemistry: burning carbon, C + O₂ → CO₂, gives about 394 kJ per mole. Denser fuel just packs more carbon into the same slot.
11. **Charcoal Blocks**: 4x `justdirethings:charcoal` *(after: Feeding the Goo)*
   > What: Just Dire Things adds a charcoal block (nine charcoal). It burns for 16,000 ticks and tier 1 goo accepts it like a coal block.
   > Chemistry: charcoal is wood heated without air (pyrolysis): water and volatiles leave, mostly carbon stays.
   > Tip: a tree farm plus a furnace array makes the coal ladder fully renewable.
12. **Simple Coal Generator**: `justdirethings:generatort1` *(after: Primal Coal)*
   > What: burns solid fuel for Forge Energy: up to 1,000 FE/t into a 1,000,000 FE buffer.
   > How: ferricore, redstone, coal and a blast furnace (check JEI).
   > Tip: it burns fuel four times faster than a furnace, so the long-burning coal tiers are what make it worth having.
13. **Fuel Canister**: `justdirethings:fuel_canister` *(after: Primal Coal)*
   > What: a canister that soaks up furnace fuel and stores it as burn time. Its tooltip shows how much it holds.
   > How: ferricore around a piece of coal (check JEI).
   > Tip: pair it with the Pocket Generator from the Tools page so your FE tools recharge on long trips.
14. **Polymorphic Fluid**: 4x `justdirethings:polymorphic_catalyst`; `justdirethings:polymorphic_fluid_bucket` *(after: Blazebloom and Blazegold)*
   > What: Polymorphic Fluid is the base for every Just Dire Things fluid: fuels, portal fluid and time fluid.
   > How: blazegold + Nether wart makes four catalysts. Throw one into a water source block and it becomes Polymorphic Fluid. Bucket it up.
   > Chemistry: a real catalyst speeds a reaction and comes out unchanged. This one is used up, so chemists would call it a reagent.
   > Tip: JEI shows these 'fluid drop' recipes; each catalyst converts one source block.
15. **Blaze Ember Fuel**: `justdirethings:refined_t2_fluid_bucket` *(after: Polymorphic Fluid, Blaze Ember)*
   > What: liquid fuel for the Simple Fuel Generator. It comes from Polymorphic Fluid in two steps.
   > How: drop a Blaze Ember into a Polymorphic Fluid source to get Unrefined Blaze Ember Fuel, then let tier 2 goo touch that source to refine it.
   > Chemistry: real refineries separate crude oil by boiling point in a fractionating column; here the goo does the cleanup.
   > Tip: tier 3 and 4 fuels follow the same pattern: drop the next coal into the refined fuel below it, then refine with the next goo.
16. **Simple Fuel Generator**: `justdirethings:generatorfluidt1` *(after: Blaze Ember Fuel)*
   > What: burns refined fuel for up to 5,000 FE/t. Blaze Ember Fuel gives 450 FE per mB, Voidflame 1,300 and Eclipse Ember 4,000.
   > How: blazegold, redstone, buckets and a blast furnace (check JEI). Pipe or bucket refined fuel in.
   > Tip: one bucket of Eclipse Ember Fuel is 4,000,000 FE, nearly the generator's whole 5,000,000 FE buffer.
17. **Voidflame and Eclipse Fuels**: `justdirethings:refined_t3_fluid_bucket`; `justdirethings:refined_t4_fluid_bucket` *(after: Simple Fuel Generator, Eclipse Ember)*
   > What: the tier 3 and tier 4 liquid fuels, roughly 3x and 9x the energy of Blaze Ember Fuel per mB.
   > How: Voidflame Coal into refined Blaze Ember Fuel, then VoidShimmer goo; Eclipse Ember into refined Voidflame Fuel, then Shadowpulse goo.
   > Tip: one bucket of each. The refined fuel is consumed by the next step, so keep a source block of each tier going.
18. **Energy Transmitter**: 2x `justdirethings:energytransmitter` *(after: Simple Coal Generator, VoidShimmer and Celestigem)*
   > What: Energy Transmitters move FE without cables, up to 1,000 FE/t, losing a little for every block of distance (check the GUI).
   > How: celestigem, redstone, an ender pearl and blazegold (check JEI).
   > Chemistry: real power lines lose energy as heat, P = I²R, which is why grids use high voltage and low current.
19. **Time Crystals**: 4x `justdirethings:time_crystal` *(after: Shadowpulse and Eclipse Alloy)*
   > What: tier 4 goo turns budding amethyst into a Budding Time Crystal block, which grows time crystal clusters.
   > How: budding amethyst can't be moved, so take Shadowpulse goo to a geode and place it touching a budding block. The budding block has to mature in stages first; check JEI and its tooltip for what it needs.
   > Chemistry: amethyst is quartz, SiO₂, coloured purple by iron (Fe³⁺) impurities and natural radiation.
   > Tip: a Time Crystal Block converts back into a budding block, so one success is enough.
20. **The Time Wand**: `justdirethings:time_wand`; `justdirethings:time_fluid_bucket` *(after: Time Crystals, Polymorphic Fluid)*
   > What: the Time Wand makes a block tick faster (up to 256x), paid for in FE and Time Fluid.
   > How: Time Fluid: drop a time crystal into a Polymorphic Fluid source. The wand is blazegold, ferricore and a time crystal (check JEI).
   > Tip: try it on a slow crop, a furnace or a budding block.
21. **Paradox Machine**: `justdirethings:paradoxmachine` *(after: Time Crystals, The Time Wand)*
   > What: the Paradox Machine reverts blocks and entities in an area to an earlier state, paying FE and Time Fluid per block or entity.
   > How: eclipse alloy, time crystals, a time crystal block and a bucket of Time Fluid (check JEI).
   > Tip: it's powerful and expensive. Read its GUI before you aim it at your base.
22. **Ferricore Tools**: `justdirethings:ferricore_pickaxe`; `justdirethings:ferricore_axe`; `justdirethings:ferricore_shovel` *(after: Raw Ferricore)*
   > What: Just Dire Things tools work like iron ones but accept ability upgrades. Each tier can hold more abilities and break more blocks at once.
   > How: the usual tool shapes with ferricore ingots and sticks.
   > Tip: vein-style abilities are capped per tier: 64 blocks for ferricore, 128 blazegold, 192 celestigem, 256 eclipse alloy.
23. **Ore Miner**: `justdirethings:upgrade_oreminer` *(after: Ferricore Tools, Upgrade Blanks)*
   > What: Ore Miner harvests every connected ore when you break one.
   > How: a blank between two iron pickaxes, then combine the upgrade with your pickaxe in a smithing table (no template needed).
   > Tip: every ability goes on the same way. JEI shows which tools accept which upgrade.
24. **Blazegold Upgrade**: `justdirethings:template_blazegold`; `justdirethings:blazegold_pickaxe` *(after: Ferricore Tools, Blazebloom and Blazegold)*
   > What: tools climb tiers in the smithing table: template + old tool + new ingot.
   > How: a blazegold template is a blank ringed with blazegold. Smith it with a ferricore pickaxe and a blazegold ingot.
   > Tip: a template plus two ingots crafts two templates, so never spend your last one.
25. **Celestigem and the Paxel**: `justdirethings:celestigem_pickaxe`; `justdirethings:celestigem_paxel` *(after: Blazegold Upgrade, VoidShimmer and Celestigem)*
   > What: from celestigem up you can fuse a pickaxe, axe and shovel of the same tier into a paxel.
   > How: smith the blazegold tools up with a celestigem template, then put pickaxe, axe and shovel together in the smithing table (check JEI).
   > Tip: one paxel instead of three tools frees two hotbar slots.
26. **Eclipse Alloy Paxel**: `justdirethings:eclipsealloy_paxel` *(after: Celestigem and the Paxel, Shadowpulse and Eclipse Alloy)*
   > What: the top paxel. Eclipse alloy tools take the strongest abilities, such as Instant Break and Ore X-Ray.
   > How: smith celestigem tools with an eclipse alloy template, then fuse the three (check JEI).
   > Tip: Instant Break needs a Nether Star, a netherite pickaxe and time crystals.
27. **Dire Bows**: 1x any of `or(item(justdirethings:bow_ferricore)item(justdirethings:bow_blazegold)item(justdirethings:bow_celestigem)item(justdirethings:bow_eclipsealloy))` *(after: Ferricore Tools)*
   > What: Just Dire Things bows tier up like the tools and take arrow abilities: Potion Arrow, Splash, Lingering, Homing and more.
   > How: a ferricore bow first (check JEI); smith it up with templates.
   > Tip: Potion Arrow uses a Potion Canister (ferricore around a glass bottle) instead of tipped arrows.
28. **A Toolbox of Abilities**: 3x any of `or(item(justdirethings:upgrade_hammer)item(justdirethings:upgrade_treefeller)item(justdirethings:upgrade_smelter)item(justdirethings:upgrade_orescanner)item(justdirethings:upgrade_lawnmower)item(justdirethings:upgrade_leafbreaker)item(justdirethings:upgrade_skysweeper)item(justdirethings:upgrade_dropteleport))` *(after: Ore Miner)*
   > What: Hammer (3x3 up to 7x7 by tier), Treefeller, Smelter (auto-smelts drops), Ore Scanner, Lawnmower, Leafbreaker, Skysweeper and Drops Teleport.
   > How: each is a blank plus vanilla parts, e.g. Hammer = blank + two diamond pickaxes, Smelter = blank + blast furnace + blaze rods (check JEI).
   > Chemistry: smelting ore is reduction: carbon pulls the oxygen off the metal, e.g. Fe₂O₃ + 3CO → 2Fe + 3CO₂.
   > Tip: not every tier takes every upgrade; JEI's smithing recipes show the allowed pairs.
29. **Ferricore Armour**: `justdirethings:ferricore_helmet`; `justdirethings:ferricore_chestplate`; `justdirethings:ferricore_leggings`; `justdirethings:ferricore_boots` *(after: Raw Ferricore)*
   > What: Just Dire Things armour takes upgrades too: Step Assist and Jump Boost on boots, Walk and Run Speed on leggings, Mind Fog on helmets.
   > How: the usual armour shapes in ferricore. Smith them up with templates later.
   > Tip: higher tiers add Night Vision, Water Breathing, Negate Fall Damage and Lava Immunity.
30. **Built-in Elytra**: `justdirethings:upgrade_elytra`; `justdirethings:celestigem_chestplate` *(after: Celestigem and the Paxel)*
   > What: the Elytra upgrade builds an elytra into a celestigem or eclipse alloy chestplate, so you keep armour and glide.
   > How: a blank plus an elytra (from an End City), then smith it onto the chestplate.
   > Tip: it's gliding, not flight. True flight is the next step.
31. **Flight**: `justdirethings:upgrade_flight`; `justdirethings:eclipsealloy_chestplate` *(after: Shadowpulse and Eclipse Alloy, Upgrade Blanks)*
   > You remember the sky. The goo had to eat netherite before it would give it back.
   > What: the Flight upgrade gives creative-style flight. It only fits the Eclipse Alloy Chestplate, so it needs tier 4 goo and a netherite block.
   > How: a blank with phantom membranes, feathers and two End Crystals (check JEI), smithed onto an eclipse alloy chestplate.
   > Tip: this pack keeps the End Crystal recipe on purpose: JDT flight is a late reward, after the End and an Ancient City.
