32. **Chestplate Defences**: 2x any of `or(item(justdirethings:upgrade_invulnerability)item(justdirethings:upgrade_lavaimmunity)item(justdirethings:upgrade_extinguish)item(justdirethings:upgrade_deathprotection)item(justdirethings:upgrade_time_protection)item(justdirethings:upgrade_phase))` *(after: Flight)*
   > What: Invulnerability (a few seconds on demand), Lava Immunity, Extinguish, Death Protection (once every 5 minutes), Time Protection and Phase (walk through walls, on leggings).
   > How: check JEI for each; Death Protection takes a Totem of Undying and time crystals.
   > Tip: activated abilities sit on keybinds; look for Just Dire Things in Controls.
33. **Breaker and Placer**: `justdirethings:blockbreakert1`; `justdirethings:blockplacert1` *(after: Raw Ferricore)*
   > What: the Simple Block Breaker mines the block in front of it; the Simple Block Placer puts blocks down. Together they automate a goo farm.
   > How: ferricore, a diamond, lapis, redstone and an observer (breaker) or dispenser (placer). Check JEI.
   > Tip: a placer feeding iron blocks to the goo and a breaker harvesting the ore makes ferricore hands-free.
34. **Clicker and Sensor**: `justdirethings:clickert1`; `justdirethings:sensort1` *(after: Breaker and Placer)*
   > What: the Simple Clicker right- or left-clicks like a player; the Simple Sensor emits a redstone signal when it sees a chosen block or mob.
   > How: both use ferricore, lapis, redstone and an eye of ender (clicker: dispenser core, sensor: observer core).
   > Tip: a sensor watching for the converted ore can tell the breaker exactly when to fire.
35. **Fluid Collector and Placer**: `justdirethings:fluidcollectort1`; `justdirethings:fluidplacert1` *(after: Breaker and Placer)*
   > What: the fluid versions of the breaker and placer: collect source blocks into a tank, or set them down.
   > How: ferricore, lapis, redstone and a bucket around a dispenser or dropper (check JEI).
   > Tip: a placer and a collector around goo automate the refined fuel line.
36. **Item Collector and Dropper**: `justdirethings:itemcollector`; `justdirethings:droppert1` *(after: Raw Ferricore)*
   > What: the Item Collector pulls dropped items from an area into an attached inventory; the Simple Dropper drops items out like a configurable dropper.
   > How: Item Collector: ferricore, a hopper, a diamond and an ender pearl. Dropper: ferricore, lapis, redstone and a dropper.
   > Tip: the Machine Settings Copier (Primal Coal, paper, ferricore) copies one machine's setup to another.
37. **Advanced Machines**: 2x any of `or(item(justdirethings:blockbreakert2)item(justdirethings:blockplacert2)item(justdirethings:clickert2)item(justdirethings:sensort2)item(justdirethings:droppert2)item(justdirethings:fluidcollectort2)item(justdirethings:fluidplacert2))` *(after: Clicker and Sensor, VoidShimmer and Celestigem)*
   > What: the Advanced versions do the same jobs with more range and settings (check each GUI).
   > How: the Simple machine in the centre, ringed with celestigem, eyes of ender and redstone (check JEI).
   > Tip: one Advanced Breaker can harvest a whole wall of goo conversions.
38. **The Swapper**: `justdirethings:blockswappert1` *(after: Clicker and Sensor, Blazebloom and Blazegold)*
   > What: Swappers exchange the blocks (and optionally entities) between two linked spots. The Advanced Swapper uses eclipse alloy.
   > How: blazegold, lapis, redstone, an eye of ender and an observer (check JEI).
   > Tip: swap freshly converted ore out and a new iron block in, without breaking anything.
39. **Blazegold Utilities**: 2x any of `or(item(justdirethings:playeraccessor)item(justdirethings:inventory_holder)item(justdirethings:experienceholder))` *(after: Blazebloom and Blazegold)*
   > What: the Player Accessor lets pipes reach a bound player's inventory; the Inventory Holder stores and swaps a loadout; the Experience Holder banks XP.
   > How: all three are blazegold plus vanilla parts (armour stand, books, emerald, ender pearls; check JEI).
   > Tip: the Experience Holder is the tidy place to keep levels between anvil and enchanting sessions.
40. **Blazejet Wand**: `justdirethings:blazejet_wand` *(after: Blazebloom and Blazegold)*
   > What: Air Burst: the wand launches you in the direction you're looking.
   > How: a blazegold ingot over two ferricore ingots (check JEI).
   > Tip: it's the base of the Voidshift Wand, so don't throw it away.
41. **Voidshift Wand**: `justdirethings:voidshift_wand` *(after: Blazejet Wand, VoidShimmer and Celestigem)*
   > What: the Blazejet Wand upgraded with celestigem. It adds Void Shift (read its tooltip for the details).
   > How: celestigem, the Blazejet Wand and blazegold in a column.
   > Tip: it upgrades again into the Eclipsegate Wand.
42. **Eclipsegate Wand**: `justdirethings:eclipsegate_wand` *(after: Voidshift Wand, Shadowpulse and Eclipse Alloy)*
   > What: Eclipse Gate temporarily removes the blocks you click, then puts them back.
   > How: eclipse alloy, the Voidshift Wand and celestigem in a column.
   > Tip: perfect for stepping through your own base walls without breaking them.
43. **Polymorphic Wand**: `justdirethings:polymorphic_wand` *(after: Polymorphic Fluid)*
   > What: the wand polymorphs a mob into a random one (or, on the Advanced wand, a chosen target), using Polymorphic Fluid.
   > How: a fluid canister, a polymorphic catalyst and blazegold in a column. Fill it with Polymorphic Fluid.
   > Tip: the Advanced Polymorphic Wand adds eclipse alloy and celestigem and also costs FE.
44. **Catcher and Recall Totem**: `justdirethings:creaturecatcher`; `justdirethings:totem_of_death_recall` *(after: Blazebloom and Blazegold)*
   > What: the Creature Catcher captures a mob to carry and release elsewhere; the Totem of Death Recall is for getting back to where you died (read its tooltip).
   > How: both are blazegold and ender pearls (the totem adds diamonds and lapis). Check JEI.
   > Tip: it won't take the Ender Dragon, sadly.
45. **Portal Fluid**: `justdirethings:portal_fluid_bucket`; `justdirethings:portalgun_v2` *(after: Polymorphic Fluid, VoidShimmer and Celestigem)*
   > What: Portal Fluid powers the Advanced Portal Gun, the upgrade of the Portal Gun on the Tools page.
   > How: a Portal Fluid Catalyst (chorus fruit and lapis) dropped into Polymorphic Fluid makes Unstable Portal Fluid; tier 3 goo stabilises it. The gun adds celestigem and a fluid canister (check JEI).
   > Tip: the Advanced Portal Gun holds 1,000,000 FE; the basic one only 100,000.

### Storage and Logistics

*A god needs somewhere to keep things.*

1. **Sophisticated Barrel**: `sophisticatedstorage:barrel` *(after: A Backpack)*
   > You remember vaults that held the stars. A barrel is humbler, but it's a start.
   > What: Sophisticated Storage chests and barrels take upgrades, just like your backpack. Sophisticated Storage Plus raises their limits.
   > How: craft a wooden barrel (check JEI). Tier upgrades improve it in place later.
   > Tip: barrels keep their contents when broken, so moving a full one is easy.
2. **Tier Upgrades**: `sophisticatedstorage:basic_to_iron_tier_upgrade` *(after: Sophisticated Barrel)*
   > What: tier upgrades turn a placed chest or barrel into the next tier (copper, iron, gold, diamond, netherite) without moving items.
   > How: craft the upgrade (check JEI) and right-click it on the placed container.
   > Tip: you can skip tiers with the direct upgrades, like basic → iron.
3. **Compacting**: `sophisticatedstorage:compacting_upgrade` *(after: Tier Upgrades)*
   > What: the Compacting Upgrade squeezes 9 nuggets into an ingot and 9 ingots into a block as items arrive.
   > Chemistry: a block holds the same metal atoms as 9 ingots; only the packing changes, so no mass is lost either way.
   > How: craft it (check JEI) and put it in an upgrade slot. Add a filter so it only compacts what you choose.
4. **Storage Controller**: `sophisticatedstorage:controller`; 2x `sophisticatedstorage:storage_link` *(after: Compacting)*
   > What: the Controller joins touching Sophisticated containers into one inventory; Storage Links extend its reach.
   > How: place it next to your chests and barrels. Pipes into the controller reach every connected container.
   > Tip: one pipe into the controller is a simple sorting system: items go where matching items already are.
5. **Drawers**: `functionalstorage:oak_1` *(after: Sophisticated Barrel)*
   > What: Functional Storage drawers hold lots of a single item and show it on the front.
   > How: craft an oak drawer (1, 2 or 4 slots; check JEI). Right-click to insert, left-click to take.
   > Tip: upgrades (copper, gold, diamond, netherite) multiply drawer capacity.
6. **Compacting Drawer**: `functionalstorage:compacting_drawer` *(after: Drawers)*
   > What: a compacting drawer stores nuggets, ingots and blocks of one metal as a single pool, and hands out whichever you ask for.
   > How: craft it (check JEI), then insert the item you want to track.
   > Tip: perfect for copper and tin near your bronze setup.
7. **Drawer Controller**: `functionalstorage:storage_controller` *(after: Compacting Drawer)*
   > What: the Storage Controller links nearby drawers into one inventory you (and pipes) can use.
   > How: place it among your drawers. Use the Linking Tool to add drawers further away.
   > Tip: a Controller Extension gives you a second access point.
8. **An Early Network**: `toms_storage:storage_terminal`; `toms_storage:inventory_connector` *(after: Drawers)*
   > What: Tom's Simple Storage connects your chests into one searchable terminal. It's your network until AE2.
   > How: place an Inventory Connector touching your chests (or link them with Inventory Cables), and a Storage Terminal on it.
   > Tip: point it at a drawer controller and every drawer shows up in the terminal.
9. **Crafting Terminal**: `toms_storage:crafting_terminal` *(after: An Early Network)*
   > What: a terminal with a crafting grid that pulls ingredients from the network. JEI's + button fills it for you.
   > How: upgrade a Storage Terminal (check JEI).
   > Tip: a Wireless Terminal (also in JEI) lets you use it from across the base.
10. **Faster Furnaces**: `ironfurnaces:iron_furnace` *(after: Iron)*
   > What: Iron Furnaces are faster furnaces in metal tiers. A generator augment turns one into a small FE source.
   > Chemistry: smelting iron ore is a reduction: carbon pulls the oxygen off, Fe₂O₃ + 3CO → 2Fe + 3CO₂.
   > How: ring a furnace with iron (check JEI). Upgrade kits move it up a tier without breaking it.
11. **Pipez**: 8x `pipez:item_pipe` *(after: Iron)*
   > What: simple pipes for moving items, fluids and energy between non-MI machines.
   > How: place a line from source to target, then set the end touching the source to extract (the pipe tooltip explains how).
   > Tip: MI machines have their own pipes; Pipez is best for everything else.
12. **LaserIO**: 2x `laserio:laser_node` *(after: Pipez, LV Machine Hull)*
   > What: Laser Nodes move items, fluids and energy between machines without cables.
   > How: place nodes on machines, link them with the Laser Wrench, and add cards to set what moves (check JEI).
   > Tip: one node can serve all six faces of a block, great for compact chemical setups.
13. **Entangled**: checkmark *(after: Iron, Sophisticated Barrel)*
   > What: an Entangled Block is a window into another inventory: bind it to a chest or machine, and anything put in the Entangled Block goes into the bound block, even across dimensions.
   > How: ender pearls, obsidian and a wooden chest (check JEI). Right-click the target with the Entangled Binder (pearls, obsidian and a diamond), then right-click the Entangled Block. Click the checkmark when one is working.
   > Tip: put one next to a remote ore processor and pipe the output straight home. Both chunks must be loaded.
14. **Storage in Motion**: `sophisticatedstorageinmotion:storage_minecart` *(after: Tier Upgrades)*
   > What: Sophisticated Storage in Motion puts upgradeable chests and barrels on minecarts, boats and rafts, upgrades and all.
   > How: combine a minecart with a Sophisticated chest or barrel (check JEI). Boats and rafts work the same way.
   > Tip: a hopper upgrade on a storage minecart makes a simple ore train between your mine and base.
15. **Stack Upgrades**: `sophisticatedstorage:stack_upgrade_tier_1` *(after: Tier Upgrades)*
   > What: stack upgrades multiply how many items fit in each slot of a chest or barrel. Higher tiers multiply more.
   > How: an upgrade base and storage blocks (check JEI). Insert it in an upgrade slot.
   > Tip: a barrel with a few stack upgrades holds a whole mine's worth of cobblestone or raw ore.
16. **Drawer Upgrades**: `functionalstorage:diamond_upgrade` *(after: Drawer Controller)*
   > What: storage upgrades multiply a drawer's capacity. Each tier (copper, gold, diamond, netherite) holds more.
   > How: craft the upgrades in order (check JEI) and right-click the drawer to open its upgrade slots.
   > Tip: the Iron Downgrade does the reverse, limiting a drawer to a single stack per slot.
17. **Pullers and Pushers**: `functionalstorage:puller_upgrade`; `functionalstorage:pusher_upgrade` *(after: Drawer Upgrades)*
   > What: utility upgrades: the Puller pulls items from the block it faces, the Pusher pushes items into it. No pipes needed.
   > How: check JEI. Put one in a drawer's utility slot and set its direction in the drawer's menu.
   > Tip: a drawer with a Puller behind a macerator collects all the dust without a single pipe.
18. **Fluid Drawers**: `functionalstorage:fluid_1` *(after: Drawer Upgrades)*
   > What: fluid drawers store a fluid instead of items, and show it on the front.
   > Chemistry: handy for small stocks of acids: one drawer of H₂SO₄, one of HCl, one of HNO₃. A real lab keeps acids in separate, labelled containers too.
   > How: check JEI. Pipes and MI fluid pipes can fill and drain them; the storage upgrades work on them too.
19. **Ender Drawer**: `functionalstorage:ender_drawer` *(after: Drawer Upgrades)*
   > What: every Ender Drawer with the same frequency shares one inventory, wherever it is.
   > How: check JEI. Link drawers with the Linking Tool to give them the same frequency.
   > Tip: put one at your mine and one at your smelters to send ore home.
20. **Inventory Cables**: 16x `toms_storage:inventory_cable`; `toms_storage:inventory_cable_connector` *(after: An Early Network)*
   > What: inventory cables carry the Inventory Connector's reach to chests that aren't touching it.
   > How: run cables from the connector and put a Cable Connector on each chest or drawer (check JEI).
   > Tip: framed cables can wear any block's texture, so they hide in walls.
21. **Wireless Terminal**: `toms_storage:adv_wireless_terminal` *(after: Crafting Terminal)*
   > What: the Advanced Wireless Terminal opens your Tom's network from a long way off.
   > How: check JEI, then right-click it on a Storage Terminal to bind it.
   > Tip: it's the cheapest remote storage access until AE2.
22. **Sophisticated Chests**: `sophisticatedstorage:chest` *(after: Sophisticated Barrel)*
   > What: Sophisticated chests take the same tier upgrades and upgrade cards as the barrels.
   > How: craft a wooden chest version (check JEI). Tier upgrades work on it in place.
   > Tip: the Decoration Table re-skins chests and barrels with any wood or block, so your storage room can match your base.
23. **Sophisticated Shulker Box**: `sophisticatedstorage:shulker_box` *(after: Sophisticated Chests, The End)*
   > What: a shulker box that takes tier upgrades and upgrade cards, and keeps everything inside when you pick it up.
   > How: a chest, two shulker shells and a lever (check JEI). Shulkers live in End cities.
   > Tip: a shulker box with a compacting upgrade is a portable ore bank for long mining trips.
24. **Netherite Storage**: 1x any of `or(item(sophisticatedstorage:netherite_barrel)item(sophisticatedstorage:netherite_chest))` *(after: Stack Upgrades, Ancient Debris)*
   > What: the netherite tier has the most slots and upgrade slots of any Sophisticated container.
   > How: a diamond-to-netherite tier upgrade on a placed diamond chest or barrel (check JEI).
   > Tip: upgrade the containers you use most first; a netherite barrel full of stack upgrades holds a staggering amount.
25. **Limited Barrels**: `sophisticatedstorage:limited_barrel_1`; `sophisticatedstorage:limited_barrel_4` *(after: Sophisticated Barrel)*
   > What: limited barrels have one to four big slots and show their items on the front, like drawers, but take Sophisticated upgrades.
   > How: check JEI for each size. They use the same tier upgrades as normal barrels.
   > Tip: a four-slot limited barrel per metal (ore, dust, ingot, block) makes a tidy wall.
26. **Hopper Upgrade**: `sophisticatedstorage:hopper_upgrade` *(after: Tier Upgrades)*
   > What: the Hopper Upgrade pulls items from one side and pushes them out another, with filters.
   > How: an upgrade base, a hopper, iron and redstone (check JEI). Set directions and filters in the container's upgrade tab.
   > Tip: a barrel with a hopper upgrade under a machine's output collects everything without pipes.
27. **Compression Upgrade**: `sophisticatedstorage:compression_upgrade` *(after: Compacting)*
   > What: the Compression Upgrade links a few slots so nuggets, ingots and blocks of one material count as a single stock.
   > How: an upgrade base, pistons, iron and redstone (check JEI). Choose which slots it uses in the upgrade tab.
   > Tip: take out exactly the form you need: blocks for storage, ingots for crafting, nuggets for small recipes.
28. **Storage Input and Output**: `sophisticatedstorage:storage_input`; `sophisticatedstorage:storage_output` *(after: Storage Controller)*
   > What: Storage Input only accepts items into the controller's containers; Storage Output only lets them out. Pipes and hoppers use them like any inventory.
   > How: a wooden storage block, gold, planks and a repeater (check JEI). Place them touching the controller's group.
   > Tip: put an Input at the end of your ore line and an Output by your crafting area, so the two never mix.
29. **Storage Tool**: `sophisticatedstorage:storage_tool` *(after: Storage Input and Output)*
   > What: the Storage Tool links containers to a controller without touching it and toggles settings like locks and displays.
   > How: an ender pearl, iron, sticks and a redstone torch (check JEI). Its tooltip lists each mode.
   > Tip: locked slots keep their item type even when empty, so a sorting system never fills them with junk.
30. **Gold Backpack**: `sophisticatedbackpacks:gold_backpack` *(after: Iron Backpack)*
   > What: the gold backpack adds inventory rows and upgrade slots over the iron one.
   > How: surround an iron backpack with eight gold ingots. Everything inside stays put.
   > Tip: this pack's upgrade base is also sold in the Demigod Token shop (search "Demigod Token" in JEI).
31. **Diamond Backpack**: `sophisticatedbackpacks:diamond_backpack` *(after: Gold Backpack)*
   > What: the diamond backpack is the tier before netherite: even more rows and upgrade slots.
   > How: surround a gold backpack with eight diamonds. Netherite comes after it (see Netherite Backpack on the QoL page).
   > Tip: spend the extra slots on stack upgrades first; they multiply every slot at once.
32. **Everlasting**: `sophisticatedbackpacks:everlasting_upgrade` *(after: Diamond Backpack, The Wither, The End)*
   > What: with the Everlasting Upgrade, a dropped backpack can't burn, explode or despawn.
   > How: an upgrade base, nether stars and end crystals (check JEI). That means beating the Wither and visiting the End.
   > Tip: worth it before reactor work or deep Nether trips, where a lost pack hurts most.
33. **Crafting Upgrade**: `sophisticatedbackpacks:crafting_upgrade` *(after: Backpack Upgrades)*
   > What: adds a 3x3 crafting grid to the backpack screen that can use the backpack's own items.
   > How: an upgrade base, a crafting table, iron and a chest (check JEI).
   > Tip: JEI's + button fills the backpack grid straight from the backpack.
34. **Smelting on the Go**: `sophisticatedbackpacks:smelting_upgrade`; `sophisticatedbackpacks:auto_smelting_upgrade` *(after: Crafting Upgrade)*
   > What: the Smelting Upgrade is a furnace in the backpack screen. The Auto-Smelting Upgrade smelts matching items inside the backpack by itself, using fuel from the backpack.
   > Chemistry: smelting iron ore is a reduction: carbon monoxide strips the oxygen off the ore, Fe₂O₃ + 3CO → 2Fe + 3CO₂.
   > How: an upgrade base and a furnace for the first; the auto version adds a hopper, gold, redstone and a diamond (check JEI).
   > Tip: filter auto-smelting to raw ores only, or it will smelt your logs into charcoal too.
35. **Void Upgrade**: `sophisticatedbackpacks:void_upgrade` *(after: Smelting on the Go)*
   > What: the Void Upgrade destroys chosen items as they enter the backpack: cobblestone, dirt, gravel, rotten flesh.
   > Chemistry: real matter can't vanish (mass is conserved in every chemical reaction). In Minecraft, it can.
   > How: an upgrade base, ender pearls, obsidian and redstone (check JEI). Set the filter carefully.
   > Tip: keep a stack of cobblestone out of the filter. You'll want it for building and for the Matter Condenser later.
36. **Deposit and Restock**: `sophisticatedbackpacks:deposit_upgrade`; `sophisticatedbackpacks:restock_upgrade` *(after: Void Upgrade)*
   > What: with a Deposit Upgrade, using the backpack on a chest puts matching items into it. A Restock Upgrade does the reverse, topping up the backpack from the chest.
   > How: an upgrade base, a chest, iron, redstone and a piston (sticky for restock). Check JEI.
   > Tip: deposit ores into your processing chest, restock torches and food from your supply chest, all in two clicks.
37. **Tank Upgrade**: `sophisticatedbackpacks:tank_upgrade` *(after: Deposit and Restock)*
   > What: the Tank Upgrade stores fluid inside the backpack. Fill and empty it with buckets or tanks from its upgrade tab.
   > Chemistry: a real acid needs the right container: HF eats glass, so it's kept in PTFE; concentrated H₂SO₄ can sit in steel. A backpack tank isn't fussy.
   > How: an upgrade base surrounded by glass (check JEI).
   > Tip: a tank of water means you're never stuck in a lava cave without a way to cool it into obsidian.
38. **Speed and Fuel Augments**: `ironfurnaces:augment_speed`; `ironfurnaces:augment_fuel` *(after: Faster Furnaces)*
   > What: augments go in an Iron Furnace's augment slot. Speed cooks faster but burns more fuel; Fuel Efficiency does the opposite.
   > How: stone, redstone and paper around sugar (speed) or coal (fuel). Check JEI.
   > Tip: speed for ores you need now, fuel efficiency for slow bulk jobs like glass or charcoal.
39. **Furnace Generator**: `ironfurnaces:augment_generator` *(after: Speed and Fuel Augments)*
   > What: the Generator Augment turns an Iron Furnace into a small FE generator that burns furnace fuel.
   > Chemistry: burning carbon releases about 394 kJ per mole: C + O₂ → CO₂. Every generator, from a furnace to a power plant, turns that heat into work.
   > How: stone, redstone and paper around a repeater (check JEI). Put it in the augment slot and pull FE from the furnace.
   > Tip: a few generator furnaces on charcoal can run the Alchemistry Dissolver before MI's electric age.
40. **Factory and Blasting**: `ironfurnaces:augment_factory`; `ironfurnaces:augment_blasting` *(after: Furnace Generator)*
   > What: the Factory Augment gives a furnace several input slots that smelt side by side. The Blasting Augment makes it work like a blast furnace (the Smoking Augment like a smoker).
   > How: stone, redstone and paper around a piston (factory) or a blast furnace (blasting). Check JEI.
   > Tip: a factory furnace runs on FE, so put it next to your generator furnaces.
41. **Gold Furnace**: `ironfurnaces:gold_furnace` *(after: Faster Furnaces)*
   > What: each furnace tier cooks faster than the last: iron, gold, diamond, emerald, obsidian, netherite.
   > Chemistry: gold melts at 1064 °C, lower than iron (1538 °C). It's here for its glitter, not its heat resistance.
   > How: gold ingots and a gold block around an Iron Furnace (check JEI).
42. **Diamond Furnace**: `ironfurnaces:diamond_furnace` *(after: Gold Furnace)*
   > What: the diamond furnace is a big step up in speed.
   > Chemistry: diamond is pure carbon, and it burns: heated in air to about 900 °C it turns into CO₂, like coal does.
   > How: diamonds and glass around a Gold Furnace (check JEI).
43. **Emerald Furnace**: `ironfurnaces:emerald_furnace` *(after: Diamond Furnace)*
   > What: the emerald furnace is faster than diamond, and the base for the obsidian tier.
   > Chemistry: emerald is the mineral beryl, Be₃Al₂Si₆O₁₈, coloured green by a trace of chromium.
   > How: emeralds around a Diamond Furnace (check JEI). Villager trading is the easy source of emeralds.
44. **Obsidian Furnace**: `ironfurnaces:obsidian_furnace` *(after: Emerald Furnace, Blaze Rods)*
   > What: the obsidian furnace needs Nether blaze rods and is faster again.
   > Chemistry: obsidian is volcanic glass: lava, mostly SiO₂, cooled so fast that no crystals could form.
   > How: obsidian and blaze rods around an Emerald Furnace (check JEI).
45. **Netherite Furnace**: `ironfurnaces:netherite_furnace` *(after: Obsidian Furnace, Ancient Debris)*
   > You remember fire that answered to you. This one comes close.
   > What: the netherite furnace is the top of the normal Iron Furnaces line.
   > How: netherite ingots, magma cream and a soul fire block around an Obsidian Furnace (check JEI).
   > Tip: use upgrade kits on a placed furnace so you keep its augment and settings.
46. **Silver Furnace**: `ironfurnaces:silver_furnace` *(after: Faster Furnaces, Ore Byproducts)*
   > What: the silver furnace is a side tier between iron and gold, made with silver ingots.
   > Chemistry: silver (Ag) is the best conductor of heat and electricity of any element, better even than copper.
   > How: silver ingots and glass around an Iron Furnace (check JEI). Silver dust comes from ore byproducts in the MI Centrifuge.
47. **Upgrade Kits**: `ironfurnaces:upgrade_gold`; `ironfurnaces:upgrade_diamond` *(after: Gold Furnace)*
   > What: upgrade kits turn a placed furnace into the next tier and keep its contents, augment and settings.
   > How: each kit is the next tier's materials around a block (check JEI). Right-click the placed furnace with it.
   > Tip: kits are cheaper than crafting a new furnace when you have a whole row to upgrade.
48. **Logistics Nodes**: 2x `logisticsnetworks:logistics_node`; `logisticsnetworks:wrench` *(after: Blaze Rods)*
   > What: Logistics Network nodes sit on machines and chests and move items, fluids and energy between every node in the same named network, with no cables.
   > How: obsidian, a redstone block, hoppers, an eye of ender and a redstone torch for a node (check JEI). Open a node to create or join a network, then set what it imports and exports.
   > Tip: the Wrench has a copy-paste mode: set up one node, then paste its settings onto the rest.
49. **Node Upgrades**: `logisticsnetworks:iron_upgrade`; `logisticsnetworks:gold_upgrade` *(after: Logistics Nodes)*
   > What: upgrades raise how much a node moves per batch and how often. Iron, gold, diamond and netherite tiers each do more.
   > How: the tier's metal, hoppers, chests and smooth stone (check JEI). Put them in the node's upgrade slots.
   > Tip: the upgrade's tooltip lists its exact items, fluid and energy per batch.
50. **Moving Chemicals**: `logisticsnetworks:mekanism_chemical_upgrade` *(after: Node Upgrades, Bottled Gases)*
   > What: the Mekanism Chemical Upgrade lets nodes move Mekanism chemicals: H₂, O₂, Cl₂, SO₂ and the rest.
   > Chemistry: the contact process needs SO₂ and O₂ in the same place: 2SO₂ + O₂ → 2SO₃. Nodes can bring both to one Chemical Infuser.
   > How: basic chemical tanks, infused alloy, chests and an Iron Upgrade (check JEI).
   > Tip: one network for gases and another for items keeps the settings easy to read.
51. **Network Manager**: `logisticsnetworks:computer` *(after: Logistics Nodes)*
   > What: the Computer opens a Network Manager that lists your networks, their nodes and how much each channel moves.
   > How: iron, glass and a comparator (check JEI).
   > Tip: when a line stops, look here first: a channel with no flow shows where the jam is.

### Applied Energistics

*The archive of a god: every item as data, every recipe on demand.*

1. **Certus Quartz**: 16x `ae2:certus_quartz_crystal`; 8x `ae2:certus_quartz_dust` *(after: Fallen Stars)*
   > You remember crystals that sang when you touched them. These still hum, faintly.
   > What: certus quartz goes into almost every AE2 recipe: processors, glass, cells and cards.
   > Chemistry: certus is fictional, but it behaves like real quartz (SiO₂), which is piezoelectric: squeeze it and it makes a small voltage.
   > How: mine certus from meteorite craters and budding certus. Grind crystals into dust in the Inscriber with no press (or any crusher JEI lists).
   > Tip: charged certus plus certus dust dropped in water makes two new crystals. Grow it once you have a little.
2. **Sky Stone**: 16x `ae2:sky_stone_block`; 4x `ae2:smooth_sky_stone_block` *(after: Fallen Stars)*
   > What: sky stone is the rock of AE2 meteorites. The ME Controller in this pack needs smooth sky stone.
   > Chemistry: real stony meteorites are mostly silicate minerals like olivine and pyroxene; iron meteorites are an iron-nickel alloy.
   > How: mine the crater shell (it needs an iron pickaxe or better), then smelt sky stone into smooth sky stone.
   > Tip: the Inscriber grinds sky stone into sky dust, which ExtendedAE and AdvancedAE recipes ask for.
3. **All Four Presses**: `ae2:logic_processor_press`; `ae2:calculation_processor_press` *(after: Fallen Stars)*
   > What: the Inscriber has four presses: silicon, logic, calculation and engineering. You need all four for AE2's processors.
   > How: each meteorite's mysterious cube holds one press. Keep finding meteorites with the Meteorite Compass until you have the set.
   > Tip: never use your only copy in a long automation line. Make a spare in the Inscriber with an iron block first (check JEI).
4. **Printed Silicon**: 8x `ae2:silicon`; 4x `ae2:printed_silicon` *(after: Certus Quartz)*
   > What: printed silicon is the base layer of every AE2 processor.
   > Chemistry: industry makes silicon by heating sand with carbon to about 2000 °C: SiO₂ + 2C → Si + 2CO. Chips need it purer than 99.9999999%.
   > How: smelt certus quartz dust into silicon, then press it in the Inscriber with the Silicon Press.
   > Tip: JEI shows every silicon that fits the press. Check whether your MI silicon works before smelting stacks of certus.
5. **Logic Processors**: 4x `ae2:printed_logic_processor`; 4x `ae2:logic_processor` *(after: Printed Silicon, All Four Presses)*
   > What: logic processors go into cell components, cores, interfaces and much more. You'll want hundreds.
   > Chemistry: gold (Au) barely reacts with air or water, so it never corrodes. Real chips use gold for contacts and bond wires.
   > How: press a gold ingot with the Logic Press, then put printed logic on top, redstone in the middle and printed silicon at the bottom.
   > Tip: one Inscriber per processor type, each fed by pipes, keeps all three in stock.
6. **Calculation Processors**: 4x `ae2:printed_calculation_processor`; 4x `ae2:calculation_processor` *(after: Logic Processors)*
   > What: calculation processors go into crafting units, cards, bigger cell components and wireless parts.
   > Chemistry: a real quartz crystal vibrates at a precise frequency when a voltage drives it. Watches count 32,768 vibrations of quartz a second to keep time.
   > How: press a certus crystal with the Calculation Press, then combine it with redstone and printed silicon like a logic processor.
   > Tip: engineering processors use a diamond the same way. Diamond spreads heat better than any metal, which is why it's used to cool chips.
7. **Quartz Glass and Fiber**: 8x `ae2:quartz_glass`; 3x `ae2:quartz_fiber` *(after: Certus Quartz)*
   > What: quartz glass goes into cell housings and patterns. Quartz Fiber shares power between networks without sharing channels.
   > Chemistry: glass made from pure SiO₂ (fused silica) barely expands when heated. Optical fibre is the same glass, so pure that light travels kilometres inside it.
   > How: quartz dust and glass make quartz glass; glass and quartz dust make fibre (check JEI).
8. **Cell Housings**: 2x `ae2:item_cell_housing`; 2x `ae2:fluid_cell_housing` *(after: Quartz Glass and Fiber)*
   > What: a storage cell is a housing plus a component. Item housings take iron and copper; fluid housings take copper.
   > How: quartz glass, redstone and metal in a crafting table (check JEI). Combine a housing with any component to make the cell.
   > Tip: sneak-use an empty cell in the air to split it back into housing and component, for upgrading to a bigger one.
9. **Annihilation and Formation**: 2x `ae2:annihilation_core`; 2x `ae2:formation_core` *(after: Logic Processors)*
   > What: annihilation cores take things into the network; formation cores put things out. Interfaces, pattern providers, planes and the Molecular Assembler all need both.
   > How: nether quartz (annihilation) or certus (formation), plus fluix dust and a logic processor, two at a time. Grind fluix crystals in the Inscriber for the dust.
   > Tip: make a stack of each. Autocrafting eats them fast.
10. **Upgrade Cards**: 2x `ae2:basic_card`; 2x `ae2:advanced_card`; `ae2:speed_card`; `ae2:capacity_card` *(after: Calculation Processors)*
   > What: Acceleration Cards speed up buses, Inscribers and Molecular Assemblers. Capacity Cards add filter slots. Fuzzy, Inverter, Crafting and Redstone cards change what a part does.
   > How: basic cards from gold, iron, redstone and a calculation processor; advanced cards use a diamond instead. Add fluix for speed or certus for capacity (check JEI).
   > Tip: a Crafting Card on an export bus makes it order missing items from your autocrafting.
11. **Memory Card and Network Tool**: `ae2:memory_card`; `ae2:network_tool` *(after: Calculation Processors)*
   > What: the Memory Card copies a part's settings and filters to another; the Network Tool shows channels and holds spare upgrade cards.
   > How: both use a calculation processor (check JEI). Sneak-use the Memory Card on the source, then use it on the target.
   > Tip: the Memory Card is also how you link P2P tunnels.
12. **Cell Workbench**: `ae2:cell_workbench` *(after: Upgrade Cards, Cell Housings)*
   > What: the Cell Workbench sets which items a cell accepts (partitioning) and adds cards to cells.
   > How: wool, iron, a chest and a calculation processor (check JEI).
   > Tip: a cell partitioned to one dust, with a Void Card, keeps its stock without overflowing into your other cells.
13. **Dense Energy Cell**: `ae2:dense_energy_cell` *(after: Feeding the Network)*
   > What: a Dense Energy Cell stores eight times more AE than a normal cell.
   > How: eight Energy Cells around a calculation processor (check JEI). Put it anywhere on the network.
   > Tip: when a network starts flickering during autocrafting, more buffer is usually the cure, not more power input.
14. **Tunnels for Everything**: 2x `ae2:fe_p2p_tunnel`; 2x `ae2:fluid_p2p_tunnel` *(after: P2P Tunnels)*
   > What: P2P tunnels can carry FE, fluids, items, light or redstone instead of channels.
   > How: right-click an ME P2P tunnel with what it should carry, like a bucket or an FE item (check JEI for the full list).
   > Tip: FE tunnels move power through one dense cable instead of a bundle of power lines.
15. **Feeding the Network**: `ae2:energy_acceptor`; `ae2:energy_cell` *(after: AE2 Storage)*
   > What: the ME network turns FE into its own AE energy. The Energy Acceptor converts it; Energy Cells buffer it.
   > How: connect an Energy Acceptor to any FE source (an energy cube, a Powah cell, a Flux Point) and to your cables. Add an Energy Cell next to the controller (check JEI).
   > Tip: a big autocraft can drain the network fast. A Dense Energy Cell keeps it from browning out mid-job.
16. **Growing Certus**: 2x `ae2:growth_accelerator` *(after: AE2 Storage)*
   > What: Growth Accelerators speed up certus quartz buds growing on budding certus blocks.
   > Chemistry: real quartz (SiO₂) crystals grow from silica-rich water over thousands of years. Certus is fictional, but the idea of a seed crystal pulling dissolved material out of solution is real.
   > How: iron, fluix glass cable, quartz glass and a fluix block (check JEI). Place them touching budding certus and power them.
   > Tip: flawless budding certus never decays. Find it in meteorites and never break it with a normal pickaxe.
17. **P2P Tunnels**: 2x `ae2:me_p2p_tunnel` *(after: Cables and Channels)*
   > What: a pair of P2P tunnels carries 32 channels (or items, fluids, FE and redstone) through a single dense cable.
   > How: place both tunnels, then copy the input's settings to the output with a Memory Card (check JEI).
   > Tip: right-click a P2P tunnel with a bucket, a redstone dust or an FE item to change what it carries.
18. **The Matter Condenser**: `ae2:condenser`; `ae2:singularity` *(after: Feeding the Network)*
   > What: the Matter Condenser destroys items. With a 64k component inside, it squeezes them into Matter Balls or a Singularity.
   > How: craft it (check JEI), put a storage component in it, set it to Singularity and feed it cobblestone from a pipe. It takes a lot of items.
   > Tip: singularities unlock MEGA Cells' accumulation processor and every AdvancedAE machine, so start a cobblestone generator early.
19. **Quantum Bridge**: 8x `ae2:quantum_ring`; `ae2:quantum_link` *(after: The Matter Condenser, P2P Tunnels)*
   > What: two Quantum Network Bridges joined by entangled singularities act like one cable, even across dimensions.
   > How: build a 3x3 ring of 8 Quantum Rings around a Quantum Link Chamber at each end, then put one of a pair of entangled singularities in each chamber.
   > Tip: both ends must be chunk-loaded. Use FTB Chunks to force-load them.
20. **Spooky Action**: 2x `ae2:quantum_entangled_singularity` *(after: The Matter Condenser)*
   > You remember being in two places at once. It felt exactly like this.
   > What: you made a pair of Quantum Entangled Singularities by blowing up a singularity with ender pearl dust.
   > Chemistry: real entangled particles share one quantum state: measure one and you know the other, however far apart. Sadly it can't send messages faster than light; AE2 is kinder.
21. **AE2 Storage**: `ae2:drive`; `ae2:terminal` *(after: The God's Archive)*
   > You remember a mind that held every name of every thing. The archive is waking.
   > What: ME Drives hold storage cells; terminals let you see and search everything.
   > How: connect the drive and a terminal to your ME Controller with cables (check JEI).
   > Tip: keep power stable: an Energy Cell buffers the network.
22. **Cables and Channels**: 16x `ae2:fluix_smart_cable`; 4x `ae2:fluix_covered_dense_cable` *(after: AE2 Storage)*
   > What: each ME device uses one channel. Normal cables carry 8 channels, dense cables 32. The controller sends 32 out of each face.
   > How: craft fluix cables, then smart cables (they show channel use as lines) and dense cables (check JEI).
   > Tip: build a dense-cable backbone from the controller and branch normal cables off it. Smart cables show you where channels run out.
   > {@pagebreak}
   > Deep dive: if a device's light is off, it has no channel. Count devices on each normal cable (max 8). Drives, terminals, buses and interfaces all count. Plain cables, quartz fibre and energy cells don't.
   > How: the Network Tool (check JEI) shows the channel count of any cable when you right-click it.
23. **Storage Cells**: 2x `ae2:cell_component_1k`; `ae2:item_storage_cell_1k` *(after: AE2 Storage)*
   > What: cells are where the network actually stores things. Bigger components (4k, 16k, 64k, 256k) hold more.
   > Chemistry: certus and fluix are fictional, but the logic processors behind them echo real chips: doped silicon (Si).
   > How: craft components from processors and certus quartz, then put one in a cell housing (check JEI).
24. **Bigger Components**: `ae2:cell_component_16k`; `ae2:cell_component_64k` *(after: Storage Cells)*
   > What: each tier combines three of the last with a calculation processor and quartz glass, holding four times as many bytes.
   > How: 4k from 1k, 16k from 4k, 64k from 16k (check JEI). Put them in housings, or into the Matter Condenser.
   > Tip: a cell holds up to 63 different item types whatever its size, so use small cells for many odd items and big cells for bulk ores and dusts.
25. **Autocrafting**: `ae2:crafting_terminal` *(after: Storage Cells)*
   > What: pattern providers plus molecular assemblers turn whole chemical chains into one click.
   > How: a Crafting Terminal, some crafting storage, and patterns encoded in a Pattern Encoding Terminal (check JEI).
   > Tip: processing patterns can drive MI machines too: send the inputs, wait for the outputs. The Automation and Logic page walks through a full sulfuric acid line.
26. **Wireless Access**: `ae2:wireless_crafting_terminal` *(after: AE2 Storage)*
   > What: reach your ME network from anywhere in range of a Wireless Access Point.
   > How: check JEI. Link the terminal by putting it in the access point, and charge it in the Charger.
   > Tip: put the access point near the middle of your base for the best coverage.
27. **Universal Terminal**: `ae2wtlib:wireless_universal_terminal` *(after: Wireless Access)*
   > What: AE2 Wireless Terminals merges the crafting, pattern access and pattern encoding terminals into one Universal Terminal.
   > How: combine two wireless terminals in a crafting grid (check JEI); add more by crafting them in again.
   > Tip: a Magnet Card from the same mod pulls nearby drops straight into your network. A Quantum Bridge Card works across dimensions.
28. **Infinite Range**: `aeinfinitybooster:infinity_card` *(after: Wireless Access, Into the Fire)*
   > What: the Infinity Booster Card removes the Wireless Access Point's range limit within one dimension.
   > How: a Nether Star, wireless boosters, eyes of ender and netherite ingots (check JEI). The star means fighting the Wither first.
   > Tip: put it in the Wireless Access Point, not the terminal.
29. **Across Worlds**: `aeinfinitybooster:dimension_card` *(after: Infinite Range)*
   > What: the Dimension Card lets a Wireless Access Point reach you in any dimension: the Nether, the Twilight Forest, the Moon.
   > How: four Infinity Cards, four Nether Stars and an eye of ender (check JEI).
   > Tip: the access point's chunk must stay loaded while you're away.
30. **ME Requester**: `merequester:requester` *(after: Autocrafting)*
   > What: the ME Requester keeps a set amount of an item in stock by ordering crafts automatically.
   > How: check JEI. Set "keep 64 HDPE" and it never runs out.
   > Tip: it needs autocrafting patterns for whatever it keeps in stock.
31. **Requester Terminal**: `merequester:requester_terminal` *(after: ME Requester)*
   > What: the Requester Terminal lists every ME Requester on the network, so you can edit all your stock rules from one place.
   > How: an illuminated panel, a logic processor and a Requester, shapeless (check JEI).
   > Tip: keep a stock rule for each acid and solvent: H₂SO₄, HCl and HNO₃ are inputs to almost everything late game.
32. **Entro Crystals**: `extendedae:entro_seed`; 8x `extendedae:entro_crystal` *(after: Autocrafting)*
   > What: entro crystals feed every ExtendedAE machine. They grow like certus, from Budding Entroized Fluix.
   > How: craft Entro Seeds (sand, ender dust, redstone, glowstone, sky dust), then right-click a placed Fluix Block with a seed to make it budding. Harvest the clusters.
   > Tip: Growth Accelerators work on budding entro too.
33. **Crystal Assembler**: `extendedae:machine_frame`; `extendedae:crystal_assembler` *(after: Entro Crystals)*
   > What: the Crystal Assembler makes ExtendedAE's blocks from several items (and sometimes a fluid) at once.
   > How: crush entro to dust in the Inscriber, drop it in water with a gold ingot and lapis for an entro ingot, then build a Machine Frame and the Assembler (check JEI).
   > Tip: it accepts pipes and patterns, so the network can drive it like any other machine.
34. **ExtendedAE**: `extendedae:ex_pattern_provider`; `extendedae:ex_drive` *(after: Autocrafting, Crystal Assembler)*
   > What: ExtendedAE adds larger versions of AE2 blocks: the Extended Pattern Provider holds many more patterns, the Extended Drive many more cells.
   > How: made in the Crystal Assembler from the normal blocks plus concurrent processors (check JEI).
   > Tip: one extended provider can feed a whole row of chemical reactors.
35. **Wireless Connectors**: 2x `extendedae:wireless_connect` *(after: ExtendedAE)*
   > What: a pair of Wireless Connectors links two parts of your network without a cable between them.
   > How: made two at a time in the Crystal Assembler. Bind them with the Wireless Setup Kit (check JEI).
   > Tip: great for reaching a reactor room behind thick shielding.
36. **Assembler Matrix**: 8x `extendedae:assembler_matrix_frame`; `extendedae:assembler_matrix_crafter`; `extendedae:assembler_matrix_pattern` *(after: ExtendedAE)*
   > What: the Assembler Matrix is a multiblock that replaces walls of molecular assemblers: crafter blocks do the work, pattern blocks hold the patterns.
   > How: build a hollow box of frames and walls (or glass), with crafter, pattern and speed cores inside (check JEI for the rules).
   > Tip: it's for crafting-table recipes only. Processing patterns for MI machines still need pattern providers.
37. **Concurrent Processors**: 4x `extendedae:concurrent_processor` *(after: Crystal Assembler)*
   > What: concurrent processors go into nearly every Extended block: drives, providers, interfaces, inscribers and chargers.
   > How: build the Concurrent Press in the Crystal Assembler (eyes of ender, entro crystals and a Silicon Press), inscribe entro crystals with it, then assemble the prints with printed silicon and redstone (check JEI).
   > Tip: the Crystal Assembler can also make AE2's normal processors from their prints. One machine fed by pipes keeps all of them stocked.
38. **Extended Inscriber and Charger**: `extendedae:ex_inscriber`; `extendedae:ex_charger` *(after: Concurrent Processors)*
   > What: the Extended Inscriber and Extended Charger work on several items in parallel, so processors and charged certus stop being a bottleneck.
   > How: a normal Inscriber or Charger plus capacity cards and a concurrent processor, in the Crystal Assembler (check JEI).
   > Tip: the Circuit Slicer (also in JEI) cuts a whole storage block into nine prints at once: a gold block becomes nine printed logic circuits.
39. **Extended Interface and IO Port**: `extendedae:ex_interface`; `extendedae:ex_io_port` *(after: ExtendedAE)*
   > What: the Extended Interface stocks many more item types than a normal one. The Extended IO Port empties and fills cells far faster than AE2's IO Port.
   > How: both are made in the Crystal Assembler from the normal block plus cards and concurrent processors (check JEI).
   > Tip: an Extended Interface next to an MI machine can hold every input that machine needs, all at once.
40. **Extended Pattern Access**: `extendedae:ex_pattern_access_part`; `extendedae:wireless_ex_pat` *(after: ExtendedAE)*
   > What: the Extended Pattern Access Terminal lists every pattern provider on the network with search and filters. The wireless version works from your pocket.
   > How: a Pattern Access Terminal upgraded in the Crystal Assembler; add a wireless receiver and a dense energy cell for the wireless one (check JEI).
   > Tip: name your pattern providers in an anvil (Sulfuric Acid, Polyethylene) and the terminal groups them by name.
41. **Smarter Buses**: `extendedae:tag_storage_bus`; `extendedae:threshold_level_emitter` *(after: ExtendedAE)*
   > What: the Tag Storage Bus filters by item tag, so one bus can accept every dust or every ingot. The Threshold Level Emitter turns on at one amount and off at another.
   > How: a storage bus or level emitter plus processors, a book or redstone torches, in a crafting table (check JEI).
   > Tip: a threshold emitter on an MI Electrolyzer stops it flickering: on below 10,000 mB of hydrogen, off above 50,000 mB.
42. **MEGA Cells**: 4x `megacells:sky_steel_ingot`; `megacells:item_storage_cell_1m` *(after: Storage Cells)*
   > What: MEGA Cells adds 1M to 256M cells, bulk cells and bigger crafting storage.
   > Chemistry: sky steel is made by dropping charged certus, iron and sky stone into lava: heat drives the alloying, like a tiny smelter.
   > How: throw the ingredients into a lava pool for sky steel, then build the 1M component (check JEI).
43. **Sky Bronze and Sky Osmium**: 4x `megacells:sky_bronze_ingot`; 4x `megacells:sky_osmium_ingot` *(after: MEGA Cells)*
   > What: the same lava trick with copper gives sky bronze, and with osmium gives sky osmium. Both go into MEGA's larger parts.
   > Chemistry: bronze is really copper plus tin (Cu + Sn); here the sky stone plays the tin. Osmium (Os) is the densest natural element, 22.6 g/cm³.
   > How: drop charged certus, the ingot and a sky stone block into lava together. Each throw makes two ingots.
44. **Accumulation Processors**: 4x `megacells:accumulation_processor` *(after: MEGA Cells, The Matter Condenser)*
   > What: the accumulation processor is MEGA's fourth processor, needed for every 1M+ part.
   > How: inscribe a singularity between the calculation and engineering presses to make the Accumulation Press, then print sky steel with it (check JEI).
   > Tip: keep the press. The Inscriber can copy it with an iron block, like the other presses.
45. **MEGA Crafting CPU**: `megacells:1m_crafting_storage`; 4x `megacells:mega_crafting_unit` *(after: Accumulation Processors)*
   > What: MEGA crafting storage and units build a separate, much larger crafting CPU.
   > How: check JEI. MEGA blocks only join other MEGA blocks, so build it as its own multiblock.
   > Tip: the Seed of a New World chain is huge. One big CPU saves splitting the job by hand.
46. **MEGA Pattern Provider**: `megacells:mega_pattern_provider` *(after: MEGA Crafting CPU)*
   > What: a pattern provider with many more pattern slots than the normal one.
   > How: made as a cable part and turned into a block in the crafting grid (check JEI).
   > Tip: put one on each multi-input MI machine, like the Chemical Reactor or the Large Diesel Generator's fuel line.
47. **Bulk Cells**: `megacells:bulk_item_cell`; `megacells:compression_card` *(after: MEGA Crafting CPU)*
   > What: a Bulk Item Cell holds a single item type in near-unlimited amounts. The Compression Card stores nuggets, ingots and blocks as one pool.
   > How: the bulk component needs a 1M component and accumulation processors; the card is an advanced card plus a matter ball (check JEI).
   > Tip: perfect for cobblestone (condenser feed), salt dust or coal coke.
48. **Four Million Bytes**: `megacells:cell_component_4m`; `megacells:mega_item_cell_housing` *(after: Accumulation Processors)*
   > What: MEGA components go up to 256M. They need MEGA cell housings, not AE2's.
   > How: the 4M component is 1M components, accumulation processors, ender dust and vibrant quartz glass (check JEI for the bigger ones).
   > Tip: big cells pay off for bulk stocks like stone dusts and plastics. Keep many small cells for rare items instead.
49. **MEGA Energy**: `megacells:mega_energy_cell`; `megacells:greater_energy_card` *(after: Accumulation Processors, Dense Energy Cell)*
   > What: the MEGA Energy Cell is eight dense cells around an accumulation processor. The Greater Energy Card gives portable tools and terminals far more charge.
   > How: both in a crafting table (check JEI). The card is an advanced card plus a MEGA energy cell.
   > Tip: one MEGA cell is plenty for a Quantum Computer's burst of crafting.
50. **Gas in the Network**: `appmek:chemical_storage_cell_1k` *(after: Storage Cells, MV: Aluminum)*
   > What: Applied Mekanistics lets AE2 store and move Mekanism chemicals: H₂, O₂, Cl₂, HCl, SO₂ and the rest.
   > Chemistry: a 1k cell holds a lot of gas because the network stores amounts, not tanks. Real gases need pressure vessels.
   > How: a chemical cell housing (osmium, quartz glass, redstone) + a 1k component. Import buses on chemical tanks fill it.
   > Tip: processing patterns can pull stored SO₂ and O₂ straight from the cell to feed your sulfuric acid line.
51. **A Tank Farm in a Cell**: `appmek:chemical_storage_cell_64k` *(after: Gas in the Network, Bigger Components)*
   > What: a 64k chemical cell replaces a room full of chemical tanks.
   > Chemistry: one mole of any ideal gas fills about 22.4 L at 0 °C and 1 atm. Stored as data, it fills nothing at all.
   > How: chemical housing plus a 64k component, or upgrade a smaller cell in the crafting grid (check JEI).
52. **Shielded Storage**: `megacells:radioactive_chemical_cell` *(after: A Tank Farm in a Cell, MEGA Cells, Fission)*
   > What: normal chemical cells refuse radioactive chemicals. MEGA's Radioactive Chemical Cell holds one, like nuclear waste or plutonium.
   > Chemistry: spent fuel stays dangerous because unstable nuclei keep decaying, giving off α, β and γ radiation for years.
   > How: check JEI for the radioactive component and cell.
   > Tip: a cell never leaks, unlike a broken tank. It's the safest place to keep waste before you reprocess it.
53. **Energy in a Cell**: 4x `appflux:charged_redstone`; `appflux:fe_1k_cell` *(after: Storage Cells)*
   > What: Applied Flux stores FE in AE2 cells and moves it through the network with Flux Accessors.
   > How: redstone crystal (redstone block, fluix and glowstone dropped in water) → charge it in the AE2 Charger → FE cell core and housing (check JEI).
   > Tip: the housing needs insulating resin, which needs silicon: a little chemistry even here.
54. **Flux Accessor**: `appflux:flux_accessor`; `appflux:induction_card` *(after: Energy in a Cell)*
   > What: a Flux Accessor pushes FE from your FE cells into any machine it touches. An Induction Card lets an I/O bus or interface do the same.
   > How: an energy acceptor, energy processors (iron block charged into the Energy Press, then inscribed), copper and glowstone (check JEI).
   > Tip: one FE cell plus accessors can replace a tangle of power cables around a dense machine room.
55. **Reaction Chamber**: `advanced_ae:reaction_chamber`; 2x `advanced_ae:shattered_singularity` *(after: The Matter Condenser)*
   > What: AdvancedAE's Reaction Chamber combines items and a fluid with lots of energy. Its first job: shattering singularities.
   > How: a matter condenser, a vibration chamber, fluix dust, glowstone and a bucket (check JEI). Feed it a singularity, ender dust, sky dust and lava.
   > Tip: it uses a lot of power per craft. Put it on its own Energy Acceptor.
56. **Quantum Alloy**: 4x `advanced_ae:quantum_alloy` *(after: Reaction Chamber)*
   > What: quantum alloy is AdvancedAE's top material, for the quantum computer and gear.
   > How: press a shattered singularity into quantum-infused dust, react it with water into Quantum Infusion, then react that with copper ingots, singularities and shattered singularities (check JEI).
   > Chemistry: copper (Cu) is the base for a reason: after silver it's the best electrical conductor of any metal.
57. **Directional Providers**: `advanced_ae:adv_pattern_provider` *(after: ExtendedAE)*
   > What: the Advanced Pattern Provider lets a processing pattern send each input to a chosen side of the machine.
   > How: an Extended Pattern Provider, a logic processor, an ender pearl and redstone (check JEI). Encode patterns in the Advanced Pattern Encoder.
   > Tip: ideal for machines with separate input faces, like a Mekanism machine that takes gas on one side and items on another.
58. **Quantum Computer**: `advanced_ae:quantum_core`; 8x `advanced_ae:quantum_structure`; `advanced_ae:quantum_multi_threader` *(after: Quantum Alloy, MEGA Crafting CPU)*
   > You remember thinking a thousand thoughts at once. The machine remembers how, too.
   > What: the Quantum Computer is a multiblock crafting CPU: huge storage, many parallel jobs, and accelerators that multiply speed.
   > How: a Quantum Core inside a shell of Quantum Structure blocks, with multi-threaders, accelerators and storage blocks (check JEI for size limits).
   > Tip: build it next to your main pattern providers so every chemical line can run at once.
59. **Quantum Processors**: 4x `advanced_ae:quantum_processor` *(after: Quantum Alloy)*
   > What: quantum processors go into the Quantum Computer's parts, quantum gear and AdvancedAE's best buses.
   > How: press a shattered singularity between the logic and engineering presses for the Quantum Press. Inscribe quantum alloy with it, then add redstone and printed silicon (check JEI).
   > Tip: copy the Quantum Press before you use it in automation, like the other presses.
60. **Quantum Armour**: `advanced_ae:quantum_helmet`; `advanced_ae:quantum_chestplate`; `advanced_ae:quantum_leggings`; `advanced_ae:quantum_boots` *(after: Quantum Processors)*
   > What: quantum armour is netherite armour rebuilt around quantum processors, with slots for AdvancedAE's armour cards.
   > How: each piece needs its netherite piece, quantum alloy plates, quantum processors and a quantum storage component (check JEI).
   > Tip: cards like Magnet, Night Vision and Step Assist are built on the Quantum Upgrade Base. Flight in this pack comes from chemistry (pylons, jetpacks, the Angel Ring), not from AE2.
61. **Import and Export Cards**: `ae2importexportcard:import_card`; `ae2importexportcard:export_card` *(after: Wireless Access)*
   > What: these cards go in a wireless terminal. The Import Card pushes chosen items from your inventory into the network; the Export Card keeps chosen items stocked in your inventory.
   > How: an import (or export) bus, redstone blocks, an advanced card and processors (check JEI).
   > Tip: set the Import Card to cobblestone, dirt and raw ores, and mining trips never fill your inventory.
62. **Packaged Extended Crafting**: `packagedexcrafting:basic_crafter` *(after: Packaged Automation, Crystaltine)*
   > What: Packaged ExCrafting adds crafters that run Extended Crafting recipes from packages: one for each table size, plus combination and ender crafting.
   > How: an auto table of the right size, an ME package component, crystaltine components, black iron and a redstone catalyst (check JEI).
   > Tip: encode the recipe in the Packaged Auto encoder, just like normal packages.
63. **The Ultimate Crafter**: `packagedexcrafting:ultimate_crafter` *(after: Packaged Extended Crafting, 9x9: Ultimate Table)*
   > What: the Ultimate Crafter runs 9x9 Ultimate Table recipes automatically from the network.
   > How: same recipe as the basic crafter, with an Ultimate Auto Table in the middle (check JEI).
   > Tip: the Seed of a New World is a 9x9 recipe. Feed it from the network and the capstone crafts itself once its ingredients exist.
64. **Co-Processors**: 4x `ae2:crafting_accelerator`; `ae2:crafting_monitor` *(after: Crafting CPU)*
   > What: each Crafting Co-Processing Unit lets a CPU run one more step at the same time. The Crafting Monitor shows what the CPU is making.
   > How: a crafting unit plus an engineering processor makes a co-processor; a crafting unit plus a storage monitor makes the monitor (check JEI).
   > Tip: co-processors only help if the machines can keep up. Ten acid patterns on one Chemical Reactor still run one at a time.
65. **A Bigger Plan**: 2x `ae2:16k_crafting_storage`; `ae2:64k_crafting_storage` *(after: Co-Processors, Bigger Components)*
   > What: crafting storage limits how big a job a CPU can plan. Long chemical chains need far more than 1k.
   > How: a crafting unit plus a 16k or 64k cell component (check JEI). Add them to the CPU's solid box.
   > Tip: build two CPUs instead of one huge one, so a long job doesn't block a quick request.
66. **ME Interfaces**: 2x `ae2:interface` *(after: Crafting CPU)*
   > What: an interface keeps chosen items stocked in itself for pipes and machines to pull. A storage bus on an interface lets one network see into another.
   > How: iron, glass, an annihilation core and a formation core (check JEI). Set the items to keep in its top row.
   > Tip: a subnetwork for one chemical line, joined to the main network through an interface, keeps channels free.
67. **Pattern Access Terminal**: `ae2:pattern_access_terminal` *(after: ME Interfaces, Autocraft a Chemical Line)*
   > What: the Pattern Access Terminal shows the patterns in every pattern provider, so you can add or swap them without walking to each machine.
   > How: an illuminated panel, an engineering processor and a pattern provider, shapeless (check JEI).
   > Tip: one terminal next to your Pattern Encoding Terminal makes a tidy crafting desk.

### Building and Decoration

*A god's house should look like one.*

1. **Effortless Building**: `effortlessbuilding:randomizer_tool` *(after: A Workbench)*
   > You remember raising cities with a gesture. For now, a few clever tools will do.
   > What: Effortless Building adds mirror, array and line modes for big builds.
   > How: open its menu (check Controls) and pick a mode. The randomizer places a random mix of the blocks you load into it.
   > Tip: the randomizer with stone, andesite and cobblestone makes natural-looking walls.
2. **Construction Stick**: `constructionstick:wooden_stick` *(after: A Workbench)*
   > What: a Construction Stick places a whole row or face of blocks matching the one you click, using blocks from your inventory.
   > How: three sticks in a column (check JEI). Right-click the face of a wall to grow it by one layer.
   > Tip: the tooltip lists its modes, such as only extending in a straight line.
3. **Diamond Stick**: `constructionstick:diamond_stick` *(after: Construction Stick)*
   > What: better sticks place more blocks per click and last longer: copper, iron, diamond, then netherite.
   > Chemistry: diamond is pure carbon (C) in a tight 3D lattice, which makes it the hardest natural material.
   > How: a diamond on two sticks (check JEI). Templates applied at a smithing table add tricks like placing in mid-air.
4. **Rechiseled**: checkmark *(after: Iron)*
   > What: Rechiseled adds a chisel that turns stone, planks, metal blocks and more into decorative patterns: bricks, tiles, pillars, gears.
   > How: craft the chisel from an iron ingot on a stick, right-click to open it, put blocks in and pick a pattern. Click the checkmark when you've chiseled something.
   > Tip: many patterns connect to each other when placed side by side. Try the iron and copper block patterns for a machine room.
5. **Building Gadgets**: `buildinggadgets2:gadget_building` *(after: LV Machine Hull)*
   > What: FE-powered gadgets that place, swap, copy and remove blocks in bulk.
   > How: iron, redstone, lapis and a diamond (check JEI). Charge it in an energy cube.
   > Tip: link the gadget to a chest or backpack so it pulls blocks from storage.
6. **Copy and Paste**: `buildinggadgets2:gadget_copy_paste`; `buildinggadgets2:template_manager` *(after: Building Gadgets)*
   > What: copy an area and paste it elsewhere; the Template Manager saves copies to share.
   > How: check JEI. Great for repeating a chemical reactor cell or a row of machines.
   > Tip: templates can be shared with friends on the server.
7. **Exchanging Gadget**: `buildinggadgets2:gadget_exchanging` *(after: Building Gadgets)*
   > What: replaces blocks in an area with another block, keeping the shape.
   > How: check JEI. Perfect for turning a cobble shell into something pretty.
   > Tip: it uses the blocks in your inventory, so carry enough.
8. **Destruction Gadget**: `buildinggadgets2:gadget_destruction` *(after: Building Gadgets)*
   > What: removes a box of blocks in front of you, sized however you like. It can undo its last action.
   > How: check JEI. Set the depth, width and height in its menu.
   > Tip: perfect for hollowing out a hillside for a new factory floor.
9. **Framed Blocks**: 8x `framedblocks:framed_cube`; `framedblocks:framed_hammer` *(after: A Workbench)*
   > What: FramedBlocks are wooden frames that take the look of any block you right-click into them, in dozens of shapes.
   > How: framed cubes are planks and sticks (check JEI). Right-click a framed block with any full block to apply it as camouflage. The Framed Hammer takes it back off.
   > Tip: camouflage with glass, ores or even machine casings: the block keeps its shape and takes the texture.
10. **Slopes and Panels**: 6x any of `or(item(framedblocks:framed_slope)item(framedblocks:framed_corner_slope)item(framedblocks:framed_stairs)item(framedblocks:framed_slab)item(framedblocks:framed_panel)item(framedblocks:framed_pillar)item(framedblocks:framed_prism))` *(after: Framed Blocks)*
   > What: any six framed slopes, corner slopes, stairs, slabs, panels, pillars or prisms.
   > How: each shape is made from framed cubes (check JEI). Most come several at a time.
   > Tip: framed slopes camouflaged as stone make roofs and ramps that look hand-carved.
11. **Secret Storage**: `framedblocks:framed_secret_storage` *(after: Slopes and Panels)*
   > What: a full-block chest that wears any block's texture, so it disappears into a wall.
   > How: a framed chest surrounded by framed cubes (check JEI), then camouflage it.
   > Tip: hide your rarest elements in one. Nobody suspects the third stone brick from the left.
12. **Somewhere to Sit**: 2x any of `or(item(handcrafted:oak_chair)item(handcrafted:spruce_chair)item(handcrafted:birch_chair)item(handcrafted:jungle_chair)item(handcrafted:acacia_chair)item(handcrafted:dark_oak_chair)item(handcrafted:mangrove_chair)item(handcrafted:cherry_chair)item(handcrafted:bamboo_chair)item(handcrafted:crimson_chair)item(handcrafted:warped_chair))`; 1x any of `or(item(handcrafted:oak_table)item(handcrafted:spruce_table)item(handcrafted:birch_table)item(handcrafted:jungle_table)item(handcrafted:acacia_table)item(handcrafted:dark_oak_table)item(handcrafted:mangrove_table)item(handcrafted:cherry_table)item(handcrafted:bamboo_table)item(handcrafted:crimson_table)item(handcrafted:warped_table))` *(after: A Workbench)*
   > What: Handcrafted adds furniture you can actually sit on: chairs, couches, benches, tables, desks.
   > How: any wood works (check JEI). Right-click a chair to sit.
   > Tip: the Handcrafted Hammer changes the shape of trims and some other pieces (check its tooltip).
13. **A Proper Study**: 2x any of `or(item(handcrafted:oak_couch)item(handcrafted:spruce_couch)item(handcrafted:birch_couch)item(handcrafted:jungle_couch)item(handcrafted:acacia_couch)item(handcrafted:dark_oak_couch)item(handcrafted:mangrove_couch)item(handcrafted:cherry_couch)item(handcrafted:bamboo_couch)item(handcrafted:crimson_couch)item(handcrafted:warped_couch))`; 1x any of `or(item(handcrafted:oak_desk)item(handcrafted:spruce_desk)item(handcrafted:birch_desk)item(handcrafted:jungle_desk)item(handcrafted:acacia_desk)item(handcrafted:dark_oak_desk)item(handcrafted:mangrove_desk)item(handcrafted:cherry_desk)item(handcrafted:bamboo_desk)item(handcrafted:crimson_desk)item(handcrafted:warped_desk))` *(after: Somewhere to Sit)*
   > What: couches connect into long sofas; desks connect into long workbenches.
   > How: any wood (check JEI). Cushions and sheets add colour.
   > Tip: a desk by your Alchemistry machines makes a fine place to read the quest book.
14. **Macaw's Furniture**: 2x any of `or(item(mcwfurnitures:oak_wardrobe)item(mcwfurnitures:spruce_wardrobe)item(mcwfurnitures:birch_wardrobe)item(mcwfurnitures:jungle_wardrobe)item(mcwfurnitures:acacia_wardrobe)item(mcwfurnitures:dark_oak_wardrobe)item(mcwfurnitures:mangrove_wardrobe)item(mcwfurnitures:cherry_wardrobe)item(mcwfurnitures:crimson_wardrobe)item(mcwfurnitures:warped_wardrobe)item(mcwfurnitures:oak_drawer)item(mcwfurnitures:spruce_drawer)item(mcwfurnitures:birch_drawer)item(mcwfurnitures:jungle_drawer)item(mcwfurnitures:acacia_drawer)item(mcwfurnitures:dark_oak_drawer)item(mcwfurnitures:mangrove_drawer)item(mcwfurnitures:cherry_drawer)item(mcwfurnitures:crimson_drawer)item(mcwfurnitures:warped_drawer)item(mcwfurnitures:oak_kitchen_cabinet)item(mcwfurnitures:spruce_kitchen_cabinet)item(mcwfurnitures:birch_kitchen_cabinet)item(mcwfurnitures:jungle_kitchen_cabinet)item(mcwfurnitures:acacia_kitchen_cabinet)item(mcwfurnitures:dark_oak_kitchen_cabinet)item(mcwfurnitures:mangrove_kitchen_cabinet)item(mcwfurnitures:cherry_kitchen_cabinet)item(mcwfurnitures:crimson_kitchen_cabinet)item(mcwfurnitures:warped_kitchen_cabinet))` *(after: Somewhere to Sit)*
   > What: Macaw's Furniture adds kitchens, wardrobes, drawers, bookshelves and more. Many of them store items.
   > How: any two wardrobes, drawers or kitchen cabinets (check JEI for the full set).
   > Tip: drawers and cabinets open like chests, so a kitchen can double as food storage.
15. **Doors**: 2x any of `or(item(mcwdoors:oak_barn_door)item(mcwdoors:spruce_barn_door)item(mcwdoors:birch_barn_door)item(mcwdoors:jungle_barn_door)item(mcwdoors:acacia_barn_door)item(mcwdoors:dark_oak_barn_door)item(mcwdoors:mangrove_barn_door)item(mcwdoors:cherry_barn_door)item(mcwdoors:bamboo_barn_door)item(mcwdoors:crimson_barn_door)item(mcwdoors:warped_barn_door)item(mcwdoors:oak_modern_door)item(mcwdoors:spruce_modern_door)item(mcwdoors:birch_modern_door)item(mcwdoors:jungle_modern_door)item(mcwdoors:acacia_modern_door)item(mcwdoors:dark_oak_modern_door)item(mcwdoors:mangrove_modern_door)item(mcwdoors:cherry_modern_door)item(mcwdoors:bamboo_modern_door)item(mcwdoors:crimson_modern_door)item(mcwdoors:warped_modern_door)item(mcwdoors:oak_glass_door)item(mcwdoors:spruce_glass_door)item(mcwdoors:birch_glass_door)item(mcwdoors:jungle_glass_door)item(mcwdoors:acacia_glass_door)item(mcwdoors:dark_oak_glass_door)item(mcwdoors:mangrove_glass_door)item(mcwdoors:cherry_glass_door)item(mcwdoors:bamboo_glass_door)item(mcwdoors:crimson_glass_door)item(mcwdoors:warped_glass_door)item(mcwdoors:oak_cottage_door)item(mcwdoors:birch_cottage_door)item(mcwdoors:jungle_cottage_door)item(mcwdoors:acacia_cottage_door)item(mcwdoors:dark_oak_cottage_door)item(mcwdoors:mangrove_cottage_door)item(mcwdoors:cherry_cottage_door)item(mcwdoors:bamboo_cottage_door)item(mcwdoors:crimson_cottage_door)item(mcwdoors:warped_cottage_door))` *(after: A Workbench)*
   > What: Macaw's Doors adds barn, cottage, glass, modern and many more doors in every wood.
   > How: any two barn, modern, glass or cottage doors (check JEI for recipes).
   > Tip: glass doors let you see into a machine room without opening it.
16. **Trapdoors**: 4x any of `or(item(mcwtrpdoors:oak_barn_trapdoor)item(mcwtrpdoors:spruce_barn_trapdoor)item(mcwtrpdoors:birch_barn_trapdoor)item(mcwtrpdoors:jungle_barn_trapdoor)item(mcwtrpdoors:acacia_barn_trapdoor)item(mcwtrpdoors:dark_oak_barn_trapdoor)item(mcwtrpdoors:mangrove_barn_trapdoor)item(mcwtrpdoors:cherry_barn_trapdoor)item(mcwtrpdoors:bamboo_barn_trapdoor)item(mcwtrpdoors:crimson_barn_trapdoor)item(mcwtrpdoors:warped_barn_trapdoor)item(mcwtrpdoors:oak_glass_trapdoor)item(mcwtrpdoors:spruce_glass_trapdoor)item(mcwtrpdoors:birch_glass_trapdoor)item(mcwtrpdoors:jungle_glass_trapdoor)item(mcwtrpdoors:acacia_glass_trapdoor)item(mcwtrpdoors:dark_oak_glass_trapdoor)item(mcwtrpdoors:mangrove_glass_trapdoor)item(mcwtrpdoors:cherry_glass_trapdoor)item(mcwtrpdoors:bamboo_glass_trapdoor)item(mcwtrpdoors:crimson_glass_trapdoor)item(mcwtrpdoors:warped_glass_trapdoor)item(mcwtrpdoors:oak_classic_trapdoor)item(mcwtrpdoors:spruce_classic_trapdoor)item(mcwtrpdoors:birch_classic_trapdoor)item(mcwtrpdoors:jungle_classic_trapdoor)item(mcwtrpdoors:acacia_classic_trapdoor)item(mcwtrpdoors:dark_oak_classic_trapdoor)item(mcwtrpdoors:mangrove_classic_trapdoor)item(mcwtrpdoors:cherry_classic_trapdoor)item(mcwtrpdoors:bamboo_classic_trapdoor)item(mcwtrpdoors:crimson_classic_trapdoor)item(mcwtrpdoors:warped_classic_trapdoor))` *(after: Doors)*
   > What: decorative trapdoors that match the doors: barn, glass, classic and more.
   > How: any four barn, glass or classic trapdoors (check JEI).
   > Tip: trapdoors on walls make great shutters and cabinet fronts.
17. **Windows**: 4x any of `or(item(mcwwindows:oak_window)item(mcwwindows:spruce_window)item(mcwwindows:birch_window)item(mcwwindows:jungle_window)item(mcwwindows:acacia_window)item(mcwwindows:dark_oak_window)item(mcwwindows:mangrove_window)item(mcwwindows:cherry_window)item(mcwwindows:oak_pane_window)item(mcwwindows:spruce_pane_window)item(mcwwindows:birch_pane_window)item(mcwwindows:jungle_pane_window)item(mcwwindows:acacia_pane_window)item(mcwwindows:dark_oak_pane_window)item(mcwwindows:mangrove_pane_window)item(mcwwindows:cherry_pane_window)item(mcwwindows:crimson_pane_window)item(mcwwindows:warped_pane_window)item(mcwwindows:oak_four_window)item(mcwwindows:spruce_four_window)item(mcwwindows:birch_four_window)item(mcwwindows:jungle_four_window)item(mcwwindows:acacia_four_window)item(mcwwindows:dark_oak_four_window)item(mcwwindows:mangrove_four_window)item(mcwwindows:cherry_four_window))` *(after: Doors)*
   > What: Macaw's Windows adds framed windows, shutters, curtains, blinds and parapets.
   > How: any four framed windows (check JEI).
   > Chemistry: glass is mostly silica (SiO₂) melted with soda (Na₂CO₃) and lime (CaO), then cooled before it can crystallise.
18. **Roofs**: 16x any of `or(item(mcwroofs:oak_roof)item(mcwroofs:spruce_roof)item(mcwroofs:birch_roof)item(mcwroofs:jungle_roof)item(mcwroofs:acacia_roof)item(mcwroofs:dark_oak_roof)item(mcwroofs:mangrove_roof)item(mcwroofs:cherry_roof)item(mcwroofs:bamboo_roof)item(mcwroofs:crimson_roof)item(mcwroofs:warped_roof)item(mcwroofs:oak_steep_roof)item(mcwroofs:spruce_steep_roof)item(mcwroofs:birch_steep_roof)item(mcwroofs:jungle_steep_roof)item(mcwroofs:acacia_steep_roof)item(mcwroofs:dark_oak_steep_roof)item(mcwroofs:mangrove_steep_roof)item(mcwroofs:cherry_steep_roof)item(mcwroofs:bamboo_steep_roof)item(mcwroofs:crimson_steep_roof)item(mcwroofs:warped_steep_roof)item(mcwroofs:stone_roof)item(mcwroofs:cobblestone_roof)item(mcwroofs:blackstone_roof)item(mcwroofs:deepslate_roof)item(mcwroofs:andesite_roof))` *(after: Doors)*
   > What: Macaw's Roofs adds sloped, steep, attic and top roof pieces that join into proper roofs.
   > How: any sixteen roof pieces (check JEI). Wooden roofs come from logs and sticks.
   > Tip: place the lower and upper pieces together to make a gentler slope.
19. **Fences and Hedges**: 8x any of `or(item(mcwfences:oak_picket_fence)item(mcwfences:spruce_picket_fence)item(mcwfences:birch_picket_fence)item(mcwfences:jungle_picket_fence)item(mcwfences:acacia_picket_fence)item(mcwfences:dark_oak_picket_fence)item(mcwfences:mangrove_picket_fence)item(mcwfences:cherry_picket_fence)item(mcwfences:bamboo_picket_fence)item(mcwfences:crimson_picket_fence)item(mcwfences:warped_picket_fence)item(mcwfences:oak_stockade_fence)item(mcwfences:spruce_stockade_fence)item(mcwfences:birch_stockade_fence)item(mcwfences:jungle_stockade_fence)item(mcwfences:acacia_stockade_fence)item(mcwfences:dark_oak_stockade_fence)item(mcwfences:mangrove_stockade_fence)item(mcwfences:cherry_stockade_fence)item(mcwfences:bamboo_stockade_fence)item(mcwfences:crimson_stockade_fence)item(mcwfences:warped_stockade_fence)item(mcwfences:oak_wired_fence)item(mcwfences:spruce_wired_fence)item(mcwfences:birch_wired_fence)item(mcwfences:jungle_wired_fence)item(mcwfences:acacia_wired_fence)item(mcwfences:dark_oak_wired_fence)item(mcwfences:mangrove_wired_fence)item(mcwfences:cherry_wired_fence)item(mcwfences:bamboo_wired_fence)item(mcwfences:crimson_wired_fence)item(mcwfences:warped_wired_fence)item(mcwfences:oak_hedge)item(mcwfences:spruce_hedge)item(mcwfences:birch_hedge)item(mcwfences:jungle_hedge)item(mcwfences:acacia_hedge)item(mcwfences:dark_oak_hedge)item(mcwfences:mangrove_hedge)item(mcwfences:cherry_hedge))` *(after: Doors)*
   > What: Macaw's Fences adds picket and stockade fences, wired fences, hedges and gates.
   > How: any eight picket, stockade or wired fences or hedges (check JEI).
   > Tip: hedges are made from leaves, so you can keep a garden looking natural.
20. **Paths**: 16x any of `or(item(mcwpaths:cobblestone_basket_weave_paving)item(mcwpaths:andesite_basket_weave_paving)item(mcwpaths:brick_basket_weave_paving)item(mcwpaths:sandstone_basket_weave_paving)item(mcwpaths:cobblestone_honeycomb_paving)item(mcwpaths:andesite_honeycomb_paving)item(mcwpaths:brick_honeycomb_paving)item(mcwpaths:sandstone_honeycomb_paving)item(mcwpaths:cobblestone_diamond_paving)item(mcwpaths:andesite_diamond_paving)item(mcwpaths:brick_diamond_paving)item(mcwpaths:sandstone_diamond_paving)item(mcwpaths:stone_flagstone)item(mcwpaths:andesite_flagstone)item(mcwpaths:brick_flagstone)item(mcwpaths:sandstone_flagstone))` *(after: Stone Age)*
   > What: Macaw's Paths adds paving patterns (basket weave, honeycomb, flagstone) in stone, brick and sandstone.
   > How: any sixteen paving blocks (check JEI).
   > Tip: a path from your base to your waystone saves getting lost at night.
21. **Bridges**: 8x any of `or(item(mcwbridges:rope_oak_bridge)item(mcwbridges:rope_spruce_bridge)item(mcwbridges:rope_birch_bridge)item(mcwbridges:rope_jungle_bridge)item(mcwbridges:rope_acacia_bridge)item(mcwbridges:rope_dark_oak_bridge)item(mcwbridges:rope_mangrove_bridge)item(mcwbridges:rope_cherry_bridge)item(mcwbridges:rope_crimson_bridge)item(mcwbridges:rope_warped_bridge)item(mcwbridges:stone_brick_bridge)item(mcwbridges:cobblestone_bridge)item(mcwbridges:brick_bridge)item(mcwbridges:iron_bridge)item(mcwbridges:sandstone_bridge))` *(after: Paths)*
   > What: Macaw's Bridges adds bridge pieces that connect into rope, stone, brick and iron bridges, with piers and stairs.
   > How: any eight bridge pieces (check JEI).
   > Tip: an iron bridge over a lava lake makes the Nether commute much safer.
22. **Lamps and Lanterns**: 4x any of `or(item(mcwlights:classic_street_lamp)item(mcwlights:double_street_lamp)item(mcwlights:chain_lantern)item(mcwlights:bell_lantern)item(mcwlights:cross_lantern)item(mcwlights:tavern_lantern)item(mcwlights:garden_light)item(mcwlights:wall_lamp)item(mcwlights:iron_chandelier)item(mcwlights:golden_chandelier)item(mcwlights:copper_chandelier))` *(after: Light in the Dark)*
   > What: Macaw's Lights adds street lamps, lanterns, garden lights and chandeliers.
   > How: any four of them (check JEI).
   > Tip: street lamps along your paths keep mobs from spawning on the way home.
23. **Lava Lamp**: `mcwlights:lava_lamp` *(after: Lamps and Lanterns, Water)*
   > What: a lava lamp for your desk. This one really has lava in it.
   > Chemistry: a real lava lamp holds wax in water. The bulb heats the wax, it expands, its density drops below the water's and it rises; at the top it cools, gets denser and sinks.
   > How: iron nuggets, a lava bucket and an iron ingot (check JEI).
24. **Jars**: 2x `supplementaries:jar` *(after: A Workbench)*
   > What: Supplementaries jars hold items, liquids or even small mobs, and show what's inside.
   > How: glass and a wooden slab (check JEI). Right-click with an item or a bucket to fill.
   > Tip: a shelf of jars, one per element, makes a lovely periodic table display.
