25. **Rope and Sacks**: 16x `supplementaries:rope`; `supplementaries:sack` *(after: Jars)*
   > What: rope you can climb and pull things up with, and sacks that store items and can be carried.
   > How: grow flax (seeds from wild flax), then craft rope and sacks from it (check JEI).
   > Tip: ropes hanging down a mineshaft are a quick way up and down (check JEI for the pulley block).
26. **Soap**: 2x `supplementaries:soap` *(after: Jars)*
   > What: soap washes the dye off coloured blocks and items, and it powers the Bubble Blower.
   > Chemistry: saponification. Wood ash is rich in potash (K₂CO₃); in water it makes lye. Lye splits fat into glycerol and fatty-acid salts, which is soap. People made it this way for thousands of years.
   > How: a water bucket, four ash and a porkchop (check JEI). Ash is left behind when fire burns blocks away; check JEI for other sources.
27. **Bubbles!**: `supplementaries:bubble_blower` *(after: Soap)*
   > You remember making nebulae. These are smaller, but they're very pretty.
   > What: the Bubble Blower uses soap to blow bubbles; it can also make Bubble Blocks (check its tooltip).
   > Chemistry: a soap film is a thin water layer between two layers of soap molecules. The colours come from light reflecting off its front and back surfaces and interfering.
28. **Create Deco Lamps**: 4x any of `or(item(createdeco:yellow_andesite_lamp)item(createdeco:red_andesite_lamp)item(createdeco:green_andesite_lamp)item(createdeco:blue_andesite_lamp)item(createdeco:yellow_brass_lamp)item(createdeco:red_brass_lamp)item(createdeco:green_brass_lamp)item(createdeco:blue_brass_lamp)item(createdeco:yellow_copper_lamp)item(createdeco:red_copper_lamp)item(createdeco:green_copper_lamp)item(createdeco:blue_copper_lamp)item(createdeco:yellow_iron_lamp)item(createdeco:red_iron_lamp)item(createdeco:green_iron_lamp)item(createdeco:blue_iron_lamp)item(createdeco:yellow_zinc_lamp)item(createdeco:red_zinc_lamp)item(createdeco:green_zinc_lamp)item(createdeco:blue_zinc_lamp))` *(after: Create Deco)*
   > What: Create Deco lamps in four colours and five metals light up a factory floor properly.
   > How: a nugget, a torch and a plate of the metal (check JEI). Any four lamps.
   > Tip: lamps can be switched off with redstone: red lamps as warning lights next to a reactor are a nice touch.
29. **Hulls and Supports**: 4x any of `or(item(createdeco:andesite_hull)item(createdeco:brass_hull)item(createdeco:copper_hull)item(createdeco:iron_hull)item(createdeco:zinc_hull)item(createdeco:industrial_iron_hull))`; 4x any of `or(item(createdeco:andesite_support)item(createdeco:brass_support)item(createdeco:copper_support)item(createdeco:iron_support)item(createdeco:zinc_support)item(createdeco:industrial_iron_support))` *(after: Create Deco Lamps)*
   > What: hulls are riveted metal walls; supports are girders for roofs and catwalks.
   > How: any four hulls and four supports (check JEI).
   > Tip: copper hulls weather like copper blocks do. Wax them with honeycomb to keep the shine.
30. **Copycats**: 2x `copycats:copycat_block`; 4x any of `or(item(copycats:copycat_slope)item(copycats:copycat_stairs)item(copycats:copycat_board)item(copycats:copycat_beam)item(copycats:copycat_slice)item(copycats:copycat_vertical_slope))` *(after: Andesite Casing)*
   > What: Copycats+ adds many more Create copycat shapes: slopes, boards, beams, slices. Each takes the look of the block you apply to it.
   > How: cut zinc ingots in a stonecutter (zinc ore is found underground; check JEI), then right-click a placed copycat with a block.
   > Tip: copycat boards cover pipes and shafts while leaving them working.
31. **Laboratory Blocks**: 16x `laboratoryblocks:laboratory_block` *(after: Nether Quartz)*
   > You remember a laboratory of white stone and light. You can build it again.
   > What: Artemis' Laboratory Blocks adds clean lab walls, tiles, glass, screens, vents and fans.
   > Chemistry: the recipe uses Nether quartz, which is silicon dioxide (SiO₂), the same compound as sand and glass.
   > How: eight stone around one Nether quartz makes eight laboratory blocks (check JEI). Stonecut them into tiles, pillars and doors.
32. **Glowing Panels**: 8x `laboratoryblocks:glowstone_particles`; 8x `laboratoryblocks:glowing_laboratory_block` *(after: Laboratory Blocks)*
   > What: glowing laboratory blocks give off light, so a lab can be bright with no torches.
   > How: one glowstone dust makes eight glowstone particles; one particle plus a laboratory block makes a glowing block (check JEI).
   > Chemistry: real glow-in-the-dark materials store energy from light and release it slowly (phosphorescence). Glowstone just glows; it's magic.
33. **Lab Screens**: 4x any of `or(item(laboratoryblocks:clear_laboratory_screen)item(laboratoryblocks:text_laboratory_screen)item(laboratoryblocks:wave_laboratory_screen)item(laboratoryblocks:quantum_laboratory_screen)item(laboratoryblocks:glowing_clear_laboratory_screen)item(laboratoryblocks:glowing_text_laboratory_screen)item(laboratoryblocks:glowing_wave_laboratory_screen)item(laboratoryblocks:glowing_quantum_laboratory_screen))` *(after: Laboratory Blocks)*
   > What: decorative screens: clear, text, wave and quantum patterns, plain or glowing.
   > How: clear screens from laboratory blocks, laboratory glass and iron screws; add a dye for the patterns (check JEI).
   > Tip: put a wave screen next to your reactor and a ComputerCraft monitor beside it for real numbers.
34. **Fans and Vents**: `laboratoryblocks:laboratory_fan`; 2x `laboratoryblocks:laboratory_vent` *(after: Laboratory Blocks)*
   > What: animated lab fans and vents. The redstone-controlled fan only spins when powered.
   > Chemistry: real labs work with HCl, NH₃ and Cl₂ under a fume hood, which pulls vapours away from your face. Breathing chlorine is a very bad idea.
   > How: laboratory blocks with iron nuggets and iron screws (check JEI).
35. **Lab Doors and Glass**: 2x any of `or(item(laboratoryblocks:laboratory_door)item(laboratoryblocks:glass_laboratory_door)item(laboratoryblocks:mesh_laboratory_door))`; 8x `laboratoryblocks:laboratory_glass` *(after: Laboratory Blocks)*
   > What: lab doors (solid, glass or mesh) and laboratory glass for viewing windows.
   > How: doors from laboratory blocks; laboratory glass is eight glass around a laboratory block (check JEI).
   > Tip: a glass wall around your Alchemistry room lets visitors admire it without touching anything.
36. **A Proper Laboratory**: checkmark *(after: Glowing Panels, Lab Screens, Fans and Vents, Lab Doors and Glass)*
   > White walls, humming fans, glass and light. You remember this place. It was always where you were happiest.
   > What: a challenge: build a real laboratory around your chemistry machines using these blocks.
   > How: include glowing panels, screens, a fume fan and a glass wall. Click the checkmark when you're proud of it.
   > Tip: share a screenshot with your server. Bragging rights are a demigod's privilege.

## Magic and Relics

### Ars Nouveau

*Spellcraft, Source, rituals and the Wilden: magic that runs on stored energy.*

1. **Novice Spell Book**: `ars_nouveau:novice_spell_book` *(after: Whispers of Magic)*
   > You remember speaking and the world listening. The words come back slowly.
   > What: the Novice Spell Book holds spells built from glyphs: a form (how it's cast), effects, then augments.
   > How: a book with an iron shovel, pickaxe, axe and sword (shapeless). Open it to combine glyphs, e.g. Projectile + Break to mine at range.
   > Tip: the Worn Notebook explains every glyph. Mana regenerates over time.
2. **Archwood Logs**: 16x any of `item_tag(c:logs/archwood)` *(after: Novice Spell Book)*
   > What: archwood is the wood Ars Nouveau builds with: the Scribe's Table, Source Jars, the Imbuement Chamber and every ritual tablet.
   > How: find the bright red, blue, green or purple trees in archwood forests and cut any 16 logs. Bring saplings home too.
   > Tip: ritual tablets ask for a specific colour (purple for Flight and Binding, green for farming rituals), so plant all four.
3. **Scribe's Table**: `ars_nouveau:scribes_table` *(after: Archwood Logs)*
   > What: the Scribe's Table crafts new glyphs, which unlock more spell parts.
   > How: archwood slabs, gold nuggets and archwood logs. Put your spell book on it and pick a glyph; each costs items and experience.
   > Tip: Light and Harvest are cheap early glyphs that pay off right away.
4. **Source**: `ars_nouveau:source_jar`; `ars_nouveau:imbuement_chamber` *(after: Scribe's Table)*
   > What: Source is Ars Nouveau's magical energy. Jars store it; the Imbuement Chamber spends it to make source gems and essences.
   > How: jars are glass and archwood slabs; the chamber is archwood planks and gold. Place jars near the chamber; they share Source within a few blocks.
   > Tip: empty jars do nothing. The next step is a Sourcelink to fill them.
5. **Volcanic Sourcelink**: `ars_nouveau:volcanic_sourcelink` *(after: Source)*
   > What: the Volcanic Sourcelink burns fuel and pushes the Source into nearby jars.
   > Chemistry: burning is fast oxidation: C + O₂ → CO₂ plus heat. The sourcelink claims that energy as Source.
   > How: two source gems, two gold ingots and a lava bucket. Feed it logs or coal with a hopper or pipe.
   > Tip: the other sourcelinks feed on crops (Agronomic), food (Mycelial), mob deaths (Vitalic) and potions (Alchemical).
6. **Source Gems**: 16x `ars_nouveau:source_gem` *(after: Volcanic Sourcelink)*
   > What: source gems are the currency of Ars Nouveau: sourcestone, pedestals, relays, essences and charms all need them.
   > Chemistry: amethyst is quartz (SiO₂) tinted purple by iron impurities; lapis owes its blue to lazurite, an aluminosilicate holding sulfur radicals.
   > How: put an amethyst shard or a lapis lazuli in the Imbuement Chamber with Source nearby. Each gem costs 500 Source.
   > Tip: a budding amethyst geode or an amethyst farm turns into an endless gem supply.
7. **Sourcestone**: 32x `ars_nouveau:sourcestone` *(after: Source Gems)*
   > What: sourcestone is the building block of the Arcane Core, the Arcane Pedestals and the Enchanting Apparatus.
   > How: eight stone around one source gem gives eight sourcestone (any c:stones work). A stonecutter turns it into bricks and tiles.
   > Tip: make extra; it's a handsome building stone for your magic room.
8. **Enchanting Apparatus**: `ars_nouveau:enchanting_apparatus`; `ars_nouveau:arcane_core`; 4x `ars_nouveau:arcane_pedestal` *(after: Sourcestone)*
   > What: the Apparatus sits on an Arcane Core and fuses items from surrounding Arcane Pedestals, using Source.
   > How: Core first, Apparatus on top, pedestals within a few blocks. Put the main item in the Apparatus and right-click (check JEI).
   > Tip: it also applies enchantments to gear: more ways to make divine equipment.
9. **Ritual Brazier**: `ars_nouveau:ritual_brazier` *(after: Enchanting Apparatus)*
   > What: rituals are area effects: sunrise, cloudshaping, overgrowth, flight and more. Each needs a brazier and a ritual tablet.
   > How: an Arcane Pedestal, a source gem block and three gold ingots (shapeless). Place it and right-click it with a ritual tablet.
   > Tip: most rituals draw Source from nearby jars, so build next to your source room.
10. **Ritual of Flight**: `ars_nouveau:ritual_flight` *(after: Ritual Brazier, Wilden Wings, Wings of a God)*
   > You already remember the sky. Now your home remembers it too.
   > What: gives creative flight to players near the brazier, paid for with Source. It's base flight, not world flight.
   > How: a purple archwood log, 3 Wilden Wings, 2 diamonds and an ender pearl. Put the tablet in the Ritual Brazier and keep the jars topped up.
   > Tip: this opens after the Infusion Pylon on purpose: real flight is a chemistry milestone (hydrogen from the Dissolver).
11. **Tier 1 Glyphs**: checkmark *(after: Scribe's Table)*
   > What: tier 1 glyphs are the basics: Projectile, Touch and Self (forms); Break, Harm, Ignite, Light, Harvest, Leap and Freeze (effects); Amplify, Delay and Randomize (augments).
   > How: scribe them at the Scribe's Table. Each costs a few common items plus experience; JEI shows the exact list.
   > Tip: a Touch + Break + Amplify spell is a magic pickaxe. Click the checkmark once you've written five glyphs.
12. **Apprentice Spell Book**: `ars_nouveau:apprentice_spell_book` *(after: Tier 1 Glyphs)*
   > What: the second spell book tier: more mana, more slots and access to tier 2 glyphs.
   > How: a crafting-grid upgrade: your Novice book with obsidian, 3 diamonds, 2 quartz blocks and 2 blaze rods (keeps your spells).
   > Tip: more mana means longer spells and more casts between rests.
13. **Tier 2 Glyphs**: checkmark *(after: Apprentice Spell Book)*
   > What: tier 2 adds the AOE, Pierce, Fortune, Extract, Extend Time and Dampen augments, and effects like Smelt, Crush, Grow, Exchange, Explosion, Heal and Slowfall.
   > How: scribe them with the Apprentice book. Several want an essence from the Imbuement Chamber (check JEI).
   > Tip: Crush + AOE grinds cobble to sand and gravel in bulk. Click the checkmark when you've written three.
14. **Explosion Glyph**: `ars_nouveau:fire_essence`; 3x `minecraft:tnt` *(after: Tier 2 Glyphs, Fire Essence)*
   > What: the Explosion glyph needs fire essence, three TNT and a fire charge. Scribe it and your spells can blast.
   > Chemistry: TNT is trinitrotoluene, C₇H₅N₃O₆: toluene nitrated with HNO₃ and H₂SO₄, the same acids your Ostwald and contact plants make.
   > How: gather the items, then write the glyph at the Scribe's Table.
   > Tip: Dampen makes the blast smaller and Amplify makes it bigger.
15. **Archmage Spell Book**: `ars_nouveau:archmage_spell_book` *(after: Apprentice Spell Book, The Wilden Chimera)*
   > You remember when every word you spoke was a spell. This book is a reminder.
   > What: the last book tier, with tier 3 glyphs and the most mana.
   > How: crafting grid: the Apprentice book with 3 ender pearls, 2 emeralds, a Totem of Undying, a Nether Star and a Wilden Tribute.
   > Tip: the Wither and the Wilden Chimera both stand between you and this book.
16. **Tier 3 Glyphs**: checkmark *(after: Archmage Spell Book)*
   > What: tier 3 glyphs are the strongest: Blink (teleport), Lightning, Fangs, Linger and Wall (lasting zones), Split, Glide and summons.
   > How: scribe them with the Archmage book; they cost the most experience (check JEI).
   > Tip: Glide works like an elytra, not like flight. Click the checkmark when you've written one.
17. **Other Sourcelinks**: 1x any of `or(item(ars_nouveau:agronomic_sourcelink)item(ars_nouveau:mycelial_sourcelink)item(ars_nouveau:vitalic_sourcelink)item(ars_nouveau:alchemical_sourcelink))` *(after: Volcanic Sourcelink)*
   > What: every sourcelink feeds on a different kind of energy. Agronomic eats crop growth, Mycelial eats food, Vitalic eats mob deaths, Alchemical eats potions.
   > Chemistry: all of them are the same idea in a costume: stored chemical energy (sugars, fats, potions) released and captured.
   > How: same frame as the Volcanic one (source gems and gold) around wheat, mushroom stew, a glistering melon or a brewing stand.
   > Tip: an Agronomic Sourcelink next to a crop farm is free Source forever.
18. **Source Relays**: 2x `ars_nouveau:relay` *(after: Source Gems)*
   > What: relays carry Source from one jar to another, so your sourcelinks don't have to sit next to the machines that spend it.
   > How: gold ingots around a source gem block. Link them with a Dominion Wand (the Worn Notebook shows how). A Relay Splitter shares one input between many outputs.
   > Tip: put noisy sourcelink farms out of sight and relay the Source in.
19. **Ender Source Jar**: `ars_additions:ender_source_jar` *(after: Enchanting Apparatus)*
   > What: an Ars Additions jar whose Source is shared with your other Ender Source Jars, wherever they are.
   > How: a Source Jar in the Apparatus with 4 ender pearls and 4 popped chorus fruit.
   > Tip: one at home and one at an outpost makes a Source network without relays.
20. **Storage Lectern**: `ars_nouveau:storage_lectern`; 2x `ars_nouveau:repository` *(after: Enchanting Apparatus)*
   > What: the Storage Lectern shows the contents of linked Repositories and chests in one searchable screen.
   > How: a lectern with 4 chests in the Apparatus; Repositories are archwood logs and gold nuggets. Link them with a Dominion Wand.
   > Tip: it's small and cheap; AE2 or Occultism storage scale further.
21. **Spell Turret**: `ars_nouveau:spell_turret` *(after: Enchanting Apparatus)*
   > What: a turret casts the spell you inscribe into it whenever it gets a redstone signal, using Source from nearby jars.
   > How: the Basic Spell Turret is source gems, gold and a redstone block; upgrade it in the Apparatus with a source gem block and 2 blaze rods.
   > Tip: a turret with Break or Harvest on a clock is a simple farm.
22. **Ars Additions Charms**: 1x any of `or(item(ars_additions:undying_charm)item(ars_additions:fall_prevention_charm)item(ars_additions:fire_resistance_charm)item(ars_additions:night_vision_charm)item(ars_additions:water_breathing_charm)item(ars_additions:void_protection_charm))` *(after: Enchanting Apparatus)*
   > What: Ars Additions charms save you once from a specific danger: falling, fire, drowning, the void, or death itself.
   > How: made in the Enchanting Apparatus around a glass bottle (the Undying Charm takes a Totem of Undying, crying obsidian and glowstone). Recharge them in the Imbuement Chamber.
   > Tip: a Void Protection Charm (stable warp scroll and end stone bricks) is cheap insurance in the End.
23. **Handy Haversack**: `ars_additions:handy_haversack` *(after: Enchanting Apparatus)*
   > What: the Handy Haversack links to a container so items you pick up go straight there.
   > How: craft it (check JEI), then bind it to a chest or your storage as its tooltip says.
   > Tip: bind it to your AE2 or Occultism storage interface for a bottomless pocket.
24. **Ritual of Binding**: `ars_nouveau:ritual_binding` *(after: Ritual Brazier)*
   > What: the Ritual of Binding turns a creature charm into its familiar form, a companion that rides along and grants a bonus.
   > How: a purple archwood log, blank parchment, an ender pearl and 3 source gems. Run it in the brazier with the charm nearby.
   > Tip: the Worn Notebook lists what each familiar gives.
25. **A Familiar**: 1x any of `or(item(ars_nouveau:familiar_starbuncle)item(ars_nouveau:familiar_wixie)item(ars_nouveau:familiar_drygmy)item(ars_nouveau:familiar_whirlisprig)item(ars_nouveau:familiar_bookwyrm)item(ars_nouveau:familiar_amethyst_golem)item(ars_elemental:firenando_familiar)item(ars_elemental:flashjack_familiar)item(ars_elemental:siren_familiar))` *(after: Ritual of Binding)*
   > What: a familiar is a bound creature that rides along and gives a passive bonus: Starbuncles speed you up, Wixies boost potions, Bookwyrms cut spell costs.
   > How: run the Ritual of Binding with the creature's charm nearby to turn it into a familiar item. Use the item to call your familiar.
   > Tip: Ars Elemental adds elemental familiars (Firenando, Flashjack, Siren) that boost that school of magic.
26. **Sky Rituals**: 1x any of `or(item(ars_nouveau:ritual_sunrise)item(ars_nouveau:ritual_moonfall)item(ars_nouveau:ritual_cloudshaping))` *(after: Ritual Brazier)*
   > What: Sunrise skips to morning, Moonfall to night, and Cloudshaping clears or starts rain.
   > How: Sunrise is a red archwood log, 3 dandelions and a clock; Moonfall a blue log, an ink sac, a coal block and a clock; Cloudshaping a blue log, a feather and a source gem block.
   > Tip: Moonfall on demand is a quick way to hunt Wilden.
27. **Garden Rituals**: 1x any of `or(item(ars_nouveau:ritual_overgrowth)item(ars_nouveau:ritual_fertility)item(ars_nouveau:ritual_harvest)item(ars_nouveau:ritual_forestation)item(ars_nouveau:ritual_flowering))` *(after: Ritual Brazier, Earth and Water)*
   > What: green-log rituals tend the land: Overgrowth grows plants, Fertility breeds animals, Harvest reaps crops, Forestation plants trees.
   > How: each starts from a green archwood log; Harvest is the log, earth essence and an iron hoe. JEI has the rest.
   > Tip: a Harvest ritual next to an Agronomic Sourcelink feeds itself.
28. **Ritual of Scrying**: `ars_nouveau:ritual_scrying` *(after: Ritual Brazier)*
   > What: Scrying highlights a chosen block (usually an ore) through walls for a while.
   > How: a purple archwood log, 3 spider eyes, glowstone and a source gem block. The Worn Notebook shows how to pick which block it reveals.
   > Tip: combine it with the spectrometer and the pack's deposit depths to find rich veins faster.
29. **Ritual of Chunk Loading**: `ars_additions:ritual_chunk_loading` *(after: Ritual Brazier)*
   > What: an Ars Additions ritual that keeps its chunk loaded while it has Source.
   > How: craft the tablet (check JEI) and run it in a Ritual Brazier next to your Source Jars.
   > Tip: FTB Chunks can also force-load claimed chunks; use whichever you prefer.
30. **Warp Scrolls**: `ars_nouveau:stable_warp_scroll` *(after: Enchanting Apparatus)*
   > What: a Warp Scroll remembers a spot and takes you back once; the Stable Warp Scroll can be reused.
   > How: Warp Scroll: blank parchment, 4 lapis and 4 source gems. Stabilize it in the Apparatus with 4 blaze powder and 2 ender pearls.
   > Tip: a Ritual of Warping (purple log + warp scroll) does the same for creatures around the brazier.
31. **Fire Essence**: 2x `ars_nouveau:fire_essence` *(after: Source Gems)*
   > What: essences are source gems imbued with one idea. Fire essence goes into fire glyphs, threads and Ars Elemental gear.
   > Chemistry: gunpowder is about 75% saltpetre (KNO₃), 15% charcoal and 10% sulfur: the nitrate supplies the oxygen, so it burns without air.
   > How: Imbuement Chamber: a source gem, with flint and steel, a torch and gunpowder on nearby pedestals (2,000 Source).
   > Tip: Arcane Pedestals next to the chamber hold the extra ingredients.
32. **Earth and Water**: `ars_nouveau:earth_essence`; `ars_nouveau:water_essence` *(after: Source Gems)*
   > What: earth essence feeds growth rituals and earth spells; water essence makes Sanctuary, the Everfull Urn and water foci.
   > Chemistry: burned kelp was once Europe's source of soda ash (Na₂CO₃), and in 1811 the first iodine was isolated from seaweed ash.
   > How: earth: an iron ingot, seeds and dirt on pedestals. Water: a water bucket, a snow block and kelp. Both 2,000 Source.
   > Tip: keep a stack of source gems ready; essences go fast.
33. **Abjuration Essence**: `ars_nouveau:abjuration_essence` *(after: Source Gems)*
   > What: abjuration essence makes Potion Jars, the Potion Melder, Potion Flasks and Restoration.
   > How: Imbuement Chamber: a source gem, with a fermented spider eye, sugar and a milk bucket on pedestals (2,000 Source).
   > Tip: a Source Jar plus abjuration essence makes a Potion Jar, which stores potions by the litre.
34. **Manipulation Essence**: `ars_nouveau:manipulation_essence` *(after: Source Gems)*
   > What: manipulation essence goes into the Enchanter's Wand, Spell Bow, Repairing thread, runic chalk and Containment.
   > How: Imbuement Chamber: a source gem, with a stone button, redstone and a clock on pedestals (2,000 Source).
   > Tip: it also converts some blocks into others, such as stone into granite or tuff (check JEI).
35. **Anima Essence**: `ars_elemental:anima_essence` *(after: Source Gems)*
   > What: anima essence is the eighth essence, used for the Mark of Mastery and necromantic gear.
   > Chemistry: bone meal comes from bone, which is mostly hydroxyapatite, a calcium phosphate (Ca₅(PO₄)₃OH).
   > How: Imbuement Chamber: a source gem, with a wither skeleton skull, bone meal and a golden apple on pedestals (3,000 Source).
   > Tip: the skulls come from Nether fortresses, or Occultism's Wild Hunt.
36. **Air Essence**: `ars_nouveau:air_essence` *(after: Wilden Wings, Source Gems)*
   > What: air essence makes the Jump Ring, the Belt of Levitation, air foci and several threads.
   > How: Imbuement Chamber: a source gem, with a feather, a Wilden Wing and arrows on pedestals (2,000 Source).
   > Tip: save a few wings for the Ritual of Flight before spending them here.
37. **Conjuration Essence**: `ars_nouveau:conjuration_essence` *(after: Wilden Hunt, Source Gems)*
   > What: conjuration essence feeds summoning glyphs and can also make end stone or soul sand (check JEI).
   > How: Imbuement Chamber: a source gem, with a Wilden Horn, Starbuncle shards and a book on pedestals (2,000 Source).
   > Tip: the Mark of Mastery needs one of every essence, so make this one early.
38. **Wilden Hunt**: kill 5x `ars_nouveau:wilden_hunter` *(after: Novice Spell Book)*
   > What: Wilden are Ars Nouveau's wild magical beasts. Hunters charge, Stalkers fly, Guardians throw spikes.
   > How: they roam the Overworld at night, more often near archwood forests and Wilden dens. Bring a shield.
   > Tip: keep every horn, spike and wing they drop. Rituals need them.
39. **Wilden Wings**: 3x `ars_nouveau:wilden_wing` *(after: Wilden Hunt)*
   > What: Wilden Wings drop from Wilden Stalkers, the flying ones.
   > How: hunt Stalkers at night. The Ritual of Flight tablet needs three wings (purple archwood log, 3 wings, 2 diamonds, an ender pearl).
   > Tip: a Ritual of Wilden Summoning will need three more Wilden drops, so keep hunting.
40. **The Wilden Chimera**: kill 1x `ars_nouveau:wilden_boss` *(after: Wilden Wings, Ritual Brazier)*
   > You remember beasts that were many beasts at once. You remember winning.
   > What: the Wilden Chimera combines all three Wilden. It drops Wilden Tribute, used for the Archmage book and Ars Elemental's Mark of Mastery.
   > How: craft the Ritual of Wilden Summoning (purple archwood log, 3 Wilden drops, a lapis block), place it in a brazier away from your base, and fight.
   > Tip: it has phases and calls spikes from the ground. Keep moving and bring healing.
41. **Mark of Mastery**: `ars_elemental:mark_of_mastery` *(after: The Wilden Chimera, Fire Essence, Earth and Water, Abjuration Essence, Manipulation Essence, Anima Essence, Air Essence, Conjuration Essence)*
   > What: the Mark of Mastery upgrades elemental foci and turns Arcanist armor into elemental mage armor.
   > How: imbue a Wilden Tribute with all seven essences plus anima essence on the pedestals (check JEI). One tribute gives five marks.
   > Tip: five marks is a focus plus most of an armor set; plan before you spend them.
42. **Lesser Elemental Focus**: 1x any of `or(item(ars_elemental:lesser_fire_focus)item(ars_elemental:lesser_water_focus)item(ars_elemental:lesser_earth_focus)item(ars_elemental:lesser_air_focus))` *(after: Fire Essence, Earth and Water, Air Essence)*
   > What: an Ars Elemental focus is a curio that strengthens one school of spells (fire, water, earth or air) and lowers its cost.
   > Chemistry: the four classical elements were the first attempt at a periodic table. You know there are 118, but the spells don't.
   > How: in the Imbuement Chamber: an amethyst shard with 3 matching essences and 3 gold ingots on pedestals (5,000 Source).
   > Tip: choose the school your favourite spell uses most.
43. **Greater Elemental Focus**: 1x any of `or(item(ars_elemental:fire_focus)item(ars_elemental:water_focus)item(ars_elemental:earth_focus)item(ars_elemental:air_focus))` *(after: Lesser Elemental Focus, Mark of Mastery)*
   > What: the full focus gives a much bigger bonus to its school and protects you from matching hazards.
   > How: put the lesser focus in the Enchanting Apparatus with a Mark of Mastery on a pedestal (5,000 Source).
   > Tip: a fire focus pairs well with Ignite and Flare; a water focus with Watery Grave.
44. **Elemental Mage Armor**: 1x any of `or(item(ars_elemental:fire_hat)item(ars_elemental:aqua_hat)item(ars_elemental:earth_hat)item(ars_elemental:air_hat)item(ars_elemental:fire_robes)item(ars_elemental:aqua_robes)item(ars_elemental:earth_robes)item(ars_elemental:air_robes))` *(after: Mark of Mastery, Armor Tiers)*
   > What: Ars Elemental turns Arcanist armor into fire, water, earth or air armor, each with resistances and a spell bonus.
   > How: in the Enchanting Apparatus: an Arcanist piece plus a Mark of Mastery, a netherite ingot and two matching essences (7,000 Source).
   > Tip: a full set matching your focus makes that school very cheap to cast.
45. **Everfull Urn**: `ars_elemental:everfull_urn` *(after: Earth and Water, Enchanting Apparatus)*
   > What: the Everfull Urn pushes water into adjacent tanks and machines for free.
   > Chemistry: water is the feedstock for half your chemistry: electrolysis splits 2H₂O → 2H₂ + O₂.
   > How: a flower pot in the Apparatus with a source gem, 2 water essences and 2 prismarine shards.
   > Tip: one next to an electrolyzer saves a pump.
46. **Caster's Bag**: `ars_elemental:caster_bag` *(after: Magebloom, Manipulation Essence)*
   > What: the Curio Bag holds trinkets and charms; the Caster's Bag is its upgrade that also takes spell books and caster tomes.
   > How: Curio Bag: a chest in magebloom fiber. Then the Apparatus with 2 manipulation essences, 2 blaze powder and 2 gold blocks.
   > Tip: it frees hotbar slots for tools.
47. **Magebloom**: 8x `ars_nouveau:magebloom` *(after: Enchanting Apparatus)*
   > What: magebloom is a flower crop; each one gives 4 magebloom fiber for robes, threads, the Alteration Table and runic chalk.
   > How: Magebloom seeds come from the Apparatus (any seeds with 4 source gems). Plant them like wheat.
   > Tip: a small magebloom field early saves a lot of waiting later.
48. **Mage Armor**: 1x any of `or(item(ars_nouveau:sorcerer_robes)item(ars_nouveau:arcanist_robes)item(ars_nouveau:battlemage_robes))` *(after: Magebloom)*
   > What: Ars Nouveau armor comes in three styles (Sorcerer, Arcanist, Battlemage). Each piece adds mana and mana regeneration.
   > How: made in the Enchanting Apparatus around a golden armor piece with 4 magebloom fiber (check JEI for each style).
   > Tip: grow magebloom early; every piece needs fiber.
49. **Armor Tiers**: checkmark *(after: Mage Armor)*
   > What: mage armor has three tiers. Each tier raises mana and opens more thread slots.
   > How: put the armor in the Apparatus: tier 2 needs 2 blaze rods (2,500 Source), tier 3 needs 2 ender pearls and a chorus fruit (5,000 Source).
   > Tip: click the checkmark once you've upgraded a piece.
50. **Alteration Table**: `ars_nouveau:alteration_table`; 2x `ars_nouveau:blank_thread` *(after: Mage Armor)*
   > What: the Alteration Table holds a piece of mage armor and lets you put threads (perks) into its slots.
   > How: the table is a Scribe's Table with magebloom fiber; blank threads are magebloom fiber and gold nuggets.
   > Tip: right-click the table with armor, then with threads.
51. **Threads**: 1x any of `or(item(ars_nouveau:thread_spellpower)item(ars_nouveau:thread_magic_capacity)item(ars_nouveau:thread_repairing)item(ars_nouveau:thread_warding)item(ars_nouveau:thread_feather)item(ars_nouveau:thread_high_step))` *(after: Alteration Table)*
   > What: threads add perks: Spellpower, Magic Capacity, Repairing, Warding, Feather Falling, Step Height and more.
   > How: a blank thread in the Apparatus with the thread's ingredients (Repairing: an anvil and 2 manipulation essences; check JEI for the rest).
   > Tip: Spellpower takes one of every essence; it's worth it for a main spell set.
52. **Mana Amulet**: 1x any of `or(item(ars_nouveau:amulet_of_mana_boost)item(ars_nouveau:amulet_of_mana_regen))` *(after: Enchanting Apparatus)*
   > What: amulets are curios that raise your maximum mana or how fast it comes back.
   > How: a Dull Trinket (iron nuggets and a source gem) in the Apparatus with diamonds and source gems (Regen also wants gold).
   > Tip: Regen suits short, frequent spells; Boost suits big ones.
53. **Ring of Discount**: `ars_nouveau:ring_of_lesser_discount` *(after: Enchanting Apparatus)*
   > What: discount rings cut the mana cost of every spell you cast.
   > How: a Ring of Potential (iron nuggets and a source gem) in the Apparatus with 4 diamonds, 2 ender pearls and 2 source gems. The Greater ring swaps the pearls for blaze rods.
   > Tip: you have two ring slots; two rings stack.
54. **Enchanter's Arms**: 1x any of `or(item(ars_nouveau:enchanters_sword)item(ars_nouveau:spell_bow)item(ars_nouveau:enchanters_shield))` *(after: Enchanting Apparatus, Manipulation Essence)*
   > What: the Enchanter's Sword casts a spell on hit, the Spell Bow puts spells on arrows, and the Enchanter's Shield is a mana-powered shield.
   > How: the vanilla item in the Apparatus with source gem blocks and gold blocks (the bow also takes manipulation essence, the sword a diamond).
   > Tip: give them a spell at the Scribe's Table, like a spell book.
55. **Starbuncle**: `ars_nouveau:starbuncle_charm` *(after: Enchanting Apparatus)*
   > What: a Starbuncle carries items from one chest to another. Cute logistics.
   > How: befriend a wild Starbuncle for its shards (the Worn Notebook explains how), then put the shards in the Apparatus with 4 gold ingots.
   > Tip: use a Dominion Wand to set where it picks up and where it drops off.
56. **Too Cool for Logistics**: `ars_nouveau:starbuncle_shades` *(after: Starbuncle)*
   > You remember having servants who took themselves very seriously. This one does not.
   > What: Starbuncle Shades: tinted glass and a source gem. Give them to a Starbuncle.
   > Tip: they do nothing useful. That is the point.
57. **Wixie**: `ars_nouveau:wixie_charm` *(after: Enchanting Apparatus)*
   > What: a Wixie works a cauldron: it repeats a crafting recipe or brews potions from nearby chests.
   > How: cast a Dispel spell on a witch at half health or less for Wixie Shards, then the Apparatus with a sapling, an emerald, a crafting table and a brewing stand.
   > Tip: pair a brewing Wixie with a Potion Jar to keep your favourite potion stocked.
58. **Drygmy**: `ars_nouveau:drygmy_charm` *(after: Enchanting Apparatus)*
   > What: a Drygmy at its henge collects drops from nearby mobs without hurting them.
   > How: Drygmy shards (the Worn Notebook explains how to befriend one) in the Apparatus with fish, wheat, an apple, a carrot, seeds and 3 source gems.
   > Tip: the Worn Notebook shows the henge layout and what the Drygmy needs nearby.
59. **Whirlisprig**: `ars_nouveau:whirlisprig_charm` *(after: Enchanting Apparatus, Magebloom)*
   > What: a Whirlisprig speeds up plant growth around it and makes Source from a varied garden.
   > How: Whirlisprig shards in the Apparatus with a source gem, magebloom (seed and flower), a diamond, three saplings and wheat seeds.
   > Tip: the more different plants nearby, the happier it is.
60. **Two Roads to the Sky**: checkmark *(after: Ritual of Flight)*
   > What: a note on the Ritual of Flight and the pack's other flight sources.
   > How: read the second page, then click the checkmark.
   > {@pagebreak}
   > Ritual of Flight: the tablet is a purple archwood log, 3 Wilden Wings, 2 diamonds and an ender pearl. Ars Elemental adds a second recipe: a flashing (yellow) archwood log, 1 wing, 2 diamonds and a feather.
   > Range: the ritual only grants flight near its brazier and drains Source while you fly. It's base flight, not world flight.
   > Not flight: the Belt of Levitation, the Jump Ring and the Glide glyph help you move, but none of them is true flight.
   > Elsewhere: the Infusion Pylon with a Potion of Flying (hydrogen from the Dissolver), Iron Jetpacks (MI circuits) and the Angel Ring (digital circuit) all work anywhere. See Tools and Comfort.

### Apotheosis

*Affix loot, gems, enchanting beyond level 30, spawners and Gateways.*

1. **Gem Dust**: 4x `apotheosis:gem_dust` *(after: Iron)*
   > What: Apotheosis gear can roll affixes and sockets. Gems drop from mobs and chests; gem dust builds its tables.
   > How: crush spare gems into gem dust (JEI shows how).
   > Tip: keep good gems for sockets and crush the rest.
2. **Simple Reforging**: `apotheosis:simple_reforging_table` *(after: Gem Dust)*
   > What: the Simple Reforging Table rerolls affixes on gear of lower rarities.
   > How: an enchanting table, iron, smooth stone and gem dust.
   > Tip: reforging costs rarity materials; salvage junk gear to get them.
3. **Salvaging**: `apotheosis:salvaging_table` *(after: Simple Reforging)*
   > What: the Salvaging Table breaks affixed gear into materials of its rarity.
   > How: a smithing table, iron tools, copper, a lava bucket and gem dust (check JEI).
   > Tip: every affixed sword you don't want is a reforge for one you do.
4. **Gem Cutting**: `apotheosis:gem_cutting_table` *(after: Salvaging)*
   > What: the Gem Cutting Table upgrades gems to higher purity using matching gems and dust.
   > Chemistry: real gems are cut along cleavage planes, flat directions where the crystal's bonds are weakest.
   > How: planks, smooth stone, shears and gem dust.
5. **Divine Reforging**: `apotheosis:reforging_table` *(after: Gem Cutting, Into the Fire)*
   > You remember weapons that chose their wielders. These at least choose good affixes.
   > What: the full Reforging Table handles every rarity, up to mythic gear.
   > How: the Simple Reforging Table, netherite, nether bricks and arcane sands (check JEI).
6. **Better Bookshelves**: 4x `apothic_enchanting:hellshelf` *(after: Gem Dust, Into the Fire)*
   > What: Apothic Enchanting adds shelves that raise enchanting power, quanta and arcana. Hellshelves are the first step.
   > How: bookshelves, nether bricks and a blaze rod. Create: Enchantment Industry can automate enchanting later.
   > Tip: enchanting power above 30 unlocks enchantments beyond vanilla levels.
7. **Sigil of Socketing**: `apotheosis:sigil_of_socketing` *(after: Gem Cutting)*
   > What: adds a socket to gear in a smithing table, so you can slot in a gem.
   > How: gem-fused slate, amethyst, gunpowder and gem dust (check JEI).
   > Tip: a Sigil of Withdrawal pulls gems back out later.
8. **Augmenting**: `apotheosis:augmenting_table` *(after: Divine Reforging)*
   > What: the Augmenting Table improves or rerolls one affix at a time.
   > How: an enchanting table, a Nether Star, a godforged pearl and polished blackstone (check JEI).
   > Tip: save it for gear you plan to keep for the rest of the game.
9. **Bottled Monsters**: `minecraft:spawner` *(after: Iron)*
   > What: with Apothic Spawners a monster spawner can be picked up with a Silk Touch pickaxe and moved home.
   > How: find one in a dungeon or mineshaft, light it up, then mine it with Silk Touch. Check its tooltip after placing.
   > Tip: spawners are mob farms for drops you can't make chemically: bones, string, ender pearls, blaze rods.
10. **Spawner Runes**: 1x any of `or(item(apotheosis:spawner_rune)item(apotheosis:burning_spawner_rune)item(apotheosis:redstone_control_spawner_rune)item(apotheosis:ignore_players_spawner_rune)item(apotheosis:silent_spawner_rune)item(apotheosis:youthful_spawner_rune))` *(after: Bottled Monsters, Gem Dust)*
   > What: spawner runes change a spawner when you right-click it: spawn without players nearby, obey redstone, set mobs on fire, stay silent, and more.
   > How: a basic Spawner Rune is gem dust, gem-fused slate and spawner chains (check JEI for where chains come from). Each modifier rune adds one item, like campfires for Burning.
   > Tip: Redstone Control first, so you can switch the farm off when you don't need it.
11. **Elite Hunting**: kill 50x `minecraft:zombie` *(after: Iron)*
   > What: Apotheosis gives some monsters affixed gear and makes rare elites with a coloured name. Their drops can roll affixes and sockets.
   > How: kill 50 zombies. Pick up any named or glowing item they drop and read its tooltip.
   > Tip: elites hit hard. Gear up before poking one with a stick.
12. **Potion Charm**: `apotheosis:potion_charm` *(after: Gem Dust, The Brewer's Art)*
   > What: a Potion Charm holds a potion effect and applies it while switched on, using durability.
   > Chemistry: blaze powder is a reactive powder; here it fixes the potion so it lasts, a bit like a preservative.
   > How: three identical potions in the middle row and six blaze powder around them. Infusing it later makes it permanent (check JEI).
   > Tip: Night Vision or Haste charms are the most useful early.
13. **Rarity Materials**: 4x `apotheosis:luminous_crystal_shard` *(after: Salvaging)*
   > What: each rarity salvages into its own material: common scrap metal, uncommon timeworn fabric, rare luminous crystal, epic arcane sands, mythic godforged pearls.
   > How: salvage rare (blue) gear in the Salvaging Table until you have 4 luminous crystal shards.
   > Tip: reforging uses the material of that rarity, so sort your loot by colour before salvaging.
14. **Gate Pearl**: `gateways:gate_pearl` *(after: Into the Fire)*
   > What: a Gate Pearl opens a Gateway that releases waves of a chosen mob. Beat every wave for rewards.
   > How: the Blaze gate is five blaze rods, an ender pearl and three nether bricks. Apotheosis adds tiered gates (Frontier and up); JEI shows them all.
   > Tip: a gate is a controlled mob farm: blaze rods, ender pearls and XP on demand.
15. **Close a Gateway**: checkmark *(after: Gate Pearl)*
   > What: the gateway closes when every wave is defeated and pays out its reward.
   > How: throw the pearl in an open area, stay close (leaving fails the gate) and kill each wave. Click the checkmark when it closes.
   > Tip: tiered Apotheosis gates hand out affix gear and rarity materials.
16. **Sigil of Withdrawal**: `apotheosis:sigil_of_withdrawal` *(after: Sigil of Socketing)*
   > What: pulls every gem out of an item without destroying them, so a good gem can move to new gear.
   > How: gem-fused slate, blaze rods, ender pearls, a lava bucket and gem dust (makes 4).
   > Tip: withdraw gems before salvaging or reforging old gear.
17. **Sigil of Enhancement**: `apotheosis:sigil_of_enhancement` *(after: Divine Reforging)*
   > What: upgrades a socketed gem to the next rarity at the Reforging Table.
   > How: gem-fused slate, gem dust and a godforged pearl (makes 4). Godforged pearls come from salvaging mythic gear.
   > Tip: save these for gems you'll keep: a mythic gem in endgame gear is worth it.
18. **Infusion**: `apothic_enchanting:infused_hellshelf` *(after: Better Bookshelves)*
   > What: Apothic Enchanting can 'infuse' items: enchanting a hellshelf at the right power makes an Infused Hellshelf.
   > How: put a hellshelf in the enchanting table with enough shelves around it. JEI's infusion page shows the exact Eterna, Quanta and Arcana it needs.
   > Tip: the table's three stats are shown on the screen: power, randomness and how many extra enchantments you get.
19. **Enchantment Library**: `apothic_enchanting:library` *(after: Infusion)*
   > What: the Library absorbs enchanted books and stores their enchantments as points, then pulls out any level you've collected.
   > How: four ender chests, four infused shelves and an enchanting table.
   > Tip: it only gives back levels up to the highest single book you fed it, so feed it every spare book.
20. **Endshelves**: 4x `apothic_enchanting:endshelf` *(after: Infusion, The End)*
   > What: endshelves push enchanting power past what hellshelves and seashelves allow.
   > How: end stone bricks, a bookshelf, an ender pearl and Infused Breath (dragon's breath infused at the enchanting table).
   > Tip: collect dragon's breath in glass bottles during the Ender Dragon fight.
21. **Scrap Tome**: `apothic_enchanting:scrap_tome` *(after: Better Bookshelves)*
   > What: a Scrap Tome in an anvil with enchanted gear strips off some of its enchantments into a book (the gear is destroyed).
   > How: eight books around an anvil makes eight tomes.
   > Tip: the Extraction Tome (infused) keeps the item; check JEI's infusion page.

### Relics and Artifacts

*Found treasures and crafted curios: Relics, Artifacts and Reliquary.*

1. **Relics**: `reliquary:mob_charm_fragment` *(after: Iron)*
   > What: three mods of found and crafted treasures. Reliquary items are crafted from mob parts; Relics and Artifacts hide in chests and structures.
   > How: mob charm fragments drop from mobs. JEI shows the charm they build.
   > Tip: a Mob Charm stops that mob type attacking you.
2. **Alkahestry Tome**: `reliquary:alkahestry_tome` *(after: Relics)*
   > What: charged with redstone, the tome duplicates common items in a crafting grid.
   > Chemistry: alchemists searched for alkahest, a universal solvent. None exists (what would you keep it in?), but aqua regia, HCl + HNO₃, dissolves even gold.
   > How: check JEI for the tome and its recipes. Recharge it with redstone.
3. **Relic Hunter**: checkmark *(after: Relics)*
   > What: find any Relics or Artifacts item: a cloud in a bottle, running shoes, a leafy mantle, a mimic's treasure.
   > How: loot chests in dungeons, mineshafts and towers, and beware chests that bite. Click the checkmark when you have one.
   > Tip: Relics items level up as you use them; check their tooltips.
4. **Fortune Coin**: `reliquary:fortune_coin` *(after: Alkahestry Tome)*
   > What: pulls nearby items and XP toward you while active.
   > How: check JEI. Right-click to toggle it.
   > Tip: turn it off near your sorting system or it'll grab the items.
5. **A True Relic**: 1x any of `or(item(relics:chorus_staff)item(relics:experience_disperser)item(relics:ghostly_mantle)item(relics:glitchy_mantle)item(relics:hunting_belt)item(relics:jellyfish_necklace)item(relics:kinetic_belt)item(relics:leafy_mantle)item(relics:midnight_mantle)item(relics:pet_bone)item(relics:piglin_mask)item(relics:reflective_necklace)item(relics:rider_flute)item(relics:ring_of_the_seven_deadly_sins)item(relics:roller_skate)item(relics:shield_of_retaliation)item(relics:sphere_of_self_sacrifice)item(relics:springy_boot)item(relics:chef_hat)item(relics:clot_of_time)item(relics:cut_glass_boot))` *(after: Iron)*
   > You remember owning things no mortal smith made. A few of them seem to have survived.
   > What: Relics are found, never crafted: mantles, belts, necklaces, boots, a chef's hat, a ring of the seven deadly sins. Each has abilities that grow with use.
   > How: loot chests in dungeons, mineshafts, temples, villages and modded structures. Any one relic completes this quest.
   > Tip: read the tooltip: each relic says what it does and how it gains experience.
6. **Relic Growth**: checkmark *(after: A True Relic)*
   > What: relics earn experience when you use their abilities, and their levels make those abilities stronger.
   > How: keep the relic equipped and use it. Open its ability screen (the tooltip names the key) to spend levels. Click the checkmark after your first upgrade.
   > Tip: one well-levelled relic beats a drawer full of new ones.
7. **An Artifact**: 1x any of `or(item(artifacts:anglers_hat)item(artifacts:antidote_vessel)item(artifacts:aqua_dashers)item(artifacts:bunny_hoppers)item(artifacts:charm_of_shrinking)item(artifacts:charm_of_sinking)item(artifacts:chorus_totem)item(artifacts:cloud_in_a_bottle)item(artifacts:cowboy_hat)item(artifacts:cross_necklace)item(artifacts:crystal_heart)item(artifacts:digging_claws)item(artifacts:eternal_steak)item(artifacts:everlasting_beef)item(artifacts:feral_claws)item(artifacts:fire_gauntlet)item(artifacts:flame_pendant)item(artifacts:flippers)item(artifacts:golden_hook)item(artifacts:helium_flamingo)item(artifacts:kitty_slippers)item(artifacts:lucky_scarf)item(artifacts:night_vision_goggles)item(artifacts:novelty_drinking_hat)item(artifacts:obsidian_skull)item(artifacts:onion_ring)item(artifacts:panic_necklace)item(artifacts:pickaxe_heater)item(artifacts:plastic_drinking_hat)item(artifacts:pocket_piston)item(artifacts:power_glove)item(artifacts:rooted_boots)item(artifacts:running_shoes)item(artifacts:scarf_of_invisibility)item(artifacts:shock_pendant)item(artifacts:snorkel)item(artifacts:snowshoes)item(artifacts:steadfast_spikes)item(artifacts:strider_shoes)item(artifacts:superstitious_hat)item(artifacts:thorn_pendant)item(artifacts:umbrella)item(artifacts:universal_attractor)item(artifacts:vampiric_glove)item(artifacts:villager_hat)item(artifacts:warp_drive)item(artifacts:whoopee_cushion)item(artifacts:withered_bracelet))` *(after: Iron)*
   > What: Artifacts are simple, always-on curios: Running Shoes, Cloud in a Bottle, Digging Claws, a Lucky Scarf, Night Vision Goggles and many more.
   > How: found in chests underground and in structures, and dropped by Mimics. Any one artifact completes this quest.
   > Tip: a Cloud in a Bottle (double jump) and Running Shoes make early exploring much easier.
8. **That Chest Has Teeth**: kill 1x `artifacts:mimic` *(after: An Artifact)*
   > What: a Mimic looks exactly like a chest until you open it. It always drops an artifact.
   > How: they hide in mineshafts, dungeons and caves. If a chest stands somewhere odd, hit it before opening it.
   > Tip: Mimics are tough for their size; fight one with real armor, not a wooden sword.
9. **Divine Comedy**: `artifacts:whoopee_cushion` *(after: An Artifact)*
   > You remember the other gods laughing at something like this. You never admitted it was you.
   > What: the Whoopee Cushion. Equip it and crouch.
   > Tip: it does exactly what you think.
10. **Mob Ingredients**: 2x `reliquary:zombie_heart` *(after: Relics)*
   > What: Reliquary items are built from rare mob parts: zombie hearts, rib bones, catalyzing glands, slime pearls, molten cores and more.
   > How: they drop from their mobs (zombies drop zombie hearts). In this pack they can also be crafted: a zombie heart is rotten flesh in a ring of iron ingots.
   > Tip: a Looting sword makes the drops much more common than the iron recipe.
11. **Mob Charm Belt**: `reliquary:mob_charm_belt` *(after: Mob Ingredients)*
   > What: the belt holds Mob Charms, so you can be ignored by several mob types at once.
   > How: three leather and five mob charm fragments (check JEI). Each fragment is made from that mob's drops.
   > Tip: creeper and enderman charms are the most valuable around a base.
12. **Void Tear**: `reliquary:void_tear` *(after: Mob Ingredients, Ghast Tears)*
   > What: a Void Tear stores a huge stack of one item, and pulls more of it from your inventory.
   > How: a ghast tear, a nebulous heart, a slime pearl and lapis (shapeless).
   > Tip: it's also an ingredient for the Infernal Tear, Emperor's Chalice and Midas Touchstone.
13. **Lantern of Paranoia**: `reliquary:lantern_of_paranoia` *(after: Mob Ingredients)*
   > What: the lantern places torches from your inventory wherever it's dark enough for mobs to spawn.
   > How: iron ingots, glass, a slime pearl and a molten core (check JEI).
   > Tip: carry a stack of torches when caving and let it do the lighting.
14. **The Handgun**: `reliquary:handgun` *(after: Mob Ingredients)*
   > What: a pistol that fires magazines of special bullets: neutral, exorcism, blaze, ender, storm, seeker and more.
   > How: barrel, grip and hammer assemblies plus iron and a slime pearl. Bullets and magazines have their own recipes (check JEI).
   > Tip: Seeker bullets home in on targets; good against flying mobs.
15. **Phoenix Down**: `reliquary:phoenix_down` *(after: Mob Ingredients, Into the Fire)*
   > What: saves you from death, heals you, and gives you a few seconds of protection. It has several charges.
   > How: three Angelheart Vials and an Angelic Feather. Vials need milk, glass, fertile essence and an infernal claw (check JEI).
   > Tip: keep it in your inventory before boss fights and Nether trips.

### Occultism

*Spirits, pentacles, otherworld storage and miners that dig where you can't.*

1. **Demon's Dream**: 4x `occultism:datura` *(after: Iron)*
   > You remember seeing everything at once. One bite, and some of it comes back.
   > What: eating Demon's Dream Fruit gives the Third Eye: you see Otherworld blocks hidden behind normal ones.
   > How: break grass for Demon's Dream seeds, grow them, and eat a fruit. Seeds plus a book make the Dictionary of Spirits, which explains the rest.
   > Tip: with the Third Eye active, otherworld trees appear among normal oaks.
2. **Spiritfire**: 16x `occultism:otherstone` *(after: Demon's Dream)*
   > What: spiritfire doesn't hurt you and doesn't destroy items: things thrown into it come out converted.
   > Chemistry: andesite, the input for otherstone, is a grey volcanic rock made mostly of plagioclase feldspar.
   > How: throw a Demon's Dream Fruit on the ground and light it with flint and steel. Then throw andesite in for otherstone (diorite gives otherrock).
   > Tip: books, feathers and black dye also go in: they come out as taboo books, awakened feathers and purified ink.
3. **Spirit-Attuned Gems**: 4x `occultism:spirit_attuned_gem` *(after: Spiritfire)*
   > What: spirit-attuned gems are in almost every spirit tool: magic lamps, lenses, the infused pickaxe, stabilizers and crystals.
   > Chemistry: a diamond is pure carbon in a rigid tetrahedral lattice, which is why it survives spiritfire unchanged in substance.
   > How: throw diamonds into spiritfire. Four gems make a Spirit-Attuned Crystal, which the mineshaft and bigger pentacles need.
   > Tip: keep a few diamonds aside for this from the start.
4. **Silver**: 8x any of `item_tag(c:ingots/silver)` *(after: Demon's Dream)*
   > What: silver goes into magic lamps, lens frames, satchels, the infused pickaxe and storage remotes.
   > Chemistry: silver (Ag) is the best electrical conductor of all metals. It tarnishes black when traces of H₂S turn its surface into Ag₂S.
   > How: mine and smelt silver ore; any silver ingot counts (they're unified).
   > Tip: MI's ore processing gives silver as a byproduct too.
5. **Otherworld Trees**: 8x `occultism:otherworld_log`; 3x `occultism:otherworld_ashes` *(after: Spiritfire)*
   > What: otherworld trees look like oaks unless you have the Third Eye. Their logs burn in spiritfire to otherworld ashes, half of white chalk.
   > Chemistry: real wood ash is mostly calcium and potassium carbonates. Potash (K₂CO₃ leached from 'pot ash') gave potassium its name.
   > How: throw an oak sapling into spiritfire for an unstable otherworld sapling, grow it, and cut it while under the Third Eye. Burn logs for ashes.
   > Tip: a foliot sapling trader later sells stable saplings that don't need the Third Eye.
6. **Burnt Otherstone**: 3x `occultism:burnt_otherstone` *(after: Spiritfire)*
   > What: burnt otherstone counts as otherstone dust, the base of every chalk.
   > How: cut or craft otherstone into polished otherstone, then smelt it in a furnace.
   > Tip: make a stack; every new chalk colour starts from white impure chalk.
7. **Your First Pentacle**: `occultism:chalk_white`; `occultism:golden_sacrificial_bowl`; 4x `occultism:sacrificial_bowl` *(after: Otherworld Trees, Burnt Otherstone)*
   > What: rituals happen on chalk pentacles, with a Golden Sacrificial Bowl at the centre and Sacrificial Bowls holding the ingredients.
   > Chemistry: real chalk is calcium carbonate, CaCO₃: the shells of tiny sea creatures, pressed into rock.
   > How: burnt otherstone and otherworld ashes make impure white chalk; purify it in spiritfire. Bowls are otherstone (the golden one adds gold). The Dictionary shows each pentacle's layout.
   > Tip: ingredient bowls can stand anywhere within 8 blocks of the golden bowl.
8. **Bind a Foliot**: `occultism:book_of_binding_bound_foliot` *(after: Your First Pentacle)*
   > What: a bound Book of Binding holds a spirit's name and starts its rituals. Foliots are the weakest spirits.
   > How: craft the Book of Binding (taboo book, purified ink, awakened feather, blue dye), then bind it with a name as the Dictionary describes (check JEI).
   > Tip: Aviar's Circle, the foliot summoning pentacle, uses only white chalk.
9. **Foliot Crusher**: 4x any of `item_tag(c:dusts/gold)`; 2x any of `item_tag(c:dusts/obsidian)` *(after: Bind a Foliot)*
   > What: a crusher spirit picks up ores and gems and drops dusts. New chalks need gold, silver, iron, emerald, calcite and obsidian dust.
   > How: Summon Foliot Crusher: iron, gold, copper and silver ingots in the bowls, a white-chalk circle. Feed it items; any gold and obsidian dust counts for this quest.
   > Tip: a foliot gives 2 dusts per ore, the pack's normal cap. Bigger crushers exist; use the MI and Mekanism ore lines for real ore yield.
10. **Golden Chalk**: `occultism:chalk_gold` *(after: Foliot Crusher)*
   > What: golden (yellow) chalk turns a summoning into a possession: the spirit takes over a creature's body.
   > How: impure white chalk with glowstone dust and 2 gold dust, purified in spiritfire.
   > Tip: Hedyrin's Lure (white + gold) is the foliot possession pentacle.
11. **Possessed Endermite**: kill 1x `occultism:possessed_endermite` *(after: Golden Chalk)*
   > What: a possessed endermite has a high chance to drop end stone, which purple chalk needs.
   > How: foliot possession ritual with 2 dirt and 2 stone (the Dictionary shows the extra step). Kill what appears.
   > Tip: run it a few times; crushed end stone goes into every purple chalk.
12. **Possessed Skeleton**: 4x `minecraft:skeleton_skull` *(after: Golden Chalk)*
   > What: skeleton skulls raise a pentacle's calling power. Every djinni, afrit and marid pentacle places them.
   > How: foliot possession ritual with 4 bones and a chicken as the sacrifice. The possessed skeleton drops skulls.
   > Tip: check each pentacle in the Dictionary for how many skulls it wants.
13. **Purple Chalk**: `occultism:chalk_purple` *(after: Foliot Crusher, Possessed Endermite)*
   > What: purple chalk binds spirits into objects. Every crafting ritual (satchels, storage, miners) needs it.
   > Chemistry: obsidian is volcanic glass, about 70% SiO₂ that cooled too fast to crystallise.
   > How: impure white chalk with crushed end stone and 2 obsidian dust, purified in spiritfire.
   > Tip: Eziveus' Spectral Compulsion (white + purple) is the foliot crafting pentacle.
14. **Research Fragment Dust**: 2x `occultism:research_fragment_dust` *(after: Purple Chalk)*
   > What: research fragment dust is the special ingredient of lime chalk and knowledge tablets.
   > Chemistry: emerald is beryl, Be₃Al₂Si₆O₁₈, coloured green by traces of chromium.
   > How: foliot crafting ritual: emerald dust and 2 bottles o' enchanting.
   > Tip: crush emeralds with your crusher spirit for the dust.
15. **Lime Chalk**: `occultism:chalk_lime` *(after: Research Fragment Dust)*
   > What: lime chalk attracts spirits stronger than foliots. Every djinni pentacle and up uses it.
   > How: impure white chalk with research fragment dust, emerald dust and a slime ball, purified in spiritfire.
   > Tip: make several; afrit and marid pentacles still use lime.
16. **Light Gray Chalk**: `occultism:chalk_light_gray` *(after: Foliot Crusher, Silver)*
   > What: light gray chalk strengthens a pentacle's foundation; djinni pentacles need it.
   > Chemistry: calcite is CaCO₃, exactly the compound real chalk and limestone are made of.
   > How: impure white chalk with silver dust, iron dust and crushed calcite, purified in spiritfire.
   > Tip: calcite lines amethyst geodes, so collect it while you farm source gems.
17. **Bind a Djinni**: `occultism:book_of_binding_bound_djinni` *(after: Lime Chalk, Light Gray Chalk, Possessed Skeleton)*
   > You remember spirits who bowed when you passed. This one only negotiates.
   > What: djinn are the second rank of spirits. Their rituals make storage, soul gems, the infused pickaxe and better miners.
   > How: a Book of Binding with purple dye, bound with a name like the foliot one. Djinni pentacles add light gray and lime chalk and skeleton skulls.
   > Tip: the Dictionary's Pentacles section is a table: each tier upgrades the pentacle you drew before.
18. **Infused Pickaxe**: `occultism:infused_pickaxe` *(after: Bind a Djinni, Spirit-Attuned Gems)*
   > What: a djinni bound into a pickaxe lets you mine otherworld materials such as iesnium ore.
   > How: a spirit-attuned pickaxe head (3 gems) with 2 sticks and 2 silver ingots, in Strigeor's Higher Binding (the djinni crafting pentacle).
   > Tip: you only need it until you have iesnium; then make an iesnium pickaxe.
19. **Iesnium**: 2x `occultism:iesnium_ingot` *(after: Infused Pickaxe, Into the Fire)*
   > What: iesnium ore hides in the Nether and only shows up with the Third Eye. You need the infused pickaxe to mine it.
   > How: eat Demon's Dream (or wear goggles) in the Nether, mine the ore with the infused pickaxe and smelt it.
   > Tip: three ingots make an iesnium pickaxe, which every miner spirit needs.
20. **Spirit Miners**: `occultism:dimensional_mineshaft`; `occultism:miner_foliot_unspecialized` *(after: Iesnium)*
   > You remember the earth giving up its treasures when asked. Now a spirit asks for you.
   > What: the Dimensional Mineshaft holds a miner spirit that pulls blocks from the Otherworld over time.
   > How: mineshaft: djinni ritual with 4 otherstone, gold, an iesnium block and a spirit-attuned crystal. Foliot miner: a magic lamp (silver and an attuned gem), an iesnium pickaxe, iron and gravel.
   > Tip: in this pack miners can't produce chemistry-milestone ores: uranium, uraninite, osmium, fluorite, platinum, iridium or tungsten. Those still come from real processing.
21. **Djinni Miner**: `occultism:miner_djinni_ores` *(after: Spirit Miners)*
   > What: the djinni miner skips the stone and pulls only ores, much faster than a foliot.
   > How: a djinni ritual with the foliot miner, an iesnium pickaxe, gold, lapis and a spirit-attuned crystal.
   > Tip: the pack's ban list still applies: no uranium, uraninite, osmium, fluorite, platinum, iridium or tungsten.
22. **Afrit Deep Miner**: `occultism:miner_afrit_deeps` *(after: Djinni Miner, Red Chalk)*
   > What: the afrit miner brings deepslate ores as well as normal ones, faster again.
   > How: an afrit crafting ritual: the djinni miner, an iesnium pickaxe, a spirit-attuned crystal, afrit essence, an echo shard and crying obsidian.
   > Tip: miners wear out. Repair them with otherworld essence and an attuned gem in an afrit pentacle (check the Dictionary).
23. **Marid Master Miner**: `occultism:miner_marid_master` *(after: Afrit Deep Miner, Blue Chalk)*
   > You remember commanding the deep places. A marid remembers being commanded.
   > What: the marid miner is the fastest and also finds rare otherworld blocks like iesnium ore and ancient debris.
   > How: a marid crafting ritual: the afrit miner, an iesnium pickaxe, a spirit-attuned crystal, a netherite pickaxe, dragon's breath, a Totem of Undying, a Nether Star and marid essence.
   > Tip: even a marid can't reach the pack's chemistry milestones: no uranium, uraninite, osmium, fluorite, platinum, iridium or tungsten.
24. **Tallow and Candles**: `occultism:butcher_knife`; 4x `occultism:large_candle` *(after: Your First Pentacle)*
   > What: large candles placed in a pentacle steady it, so summoned spirits last longer before they fade.
   > Chemistry: tallow is rendered animal fat: triglycerides of stearic, palmitic and oleic acid, the stuff of the first candles.
   > How: kill pigs, cows or sheep with the Butcher Knife (iron and sticks) for tallow, then a string over tallow makes a candle.
   > Tip: the Dictionary's pentacle pages show where candles go.
25. **Foliot Workers**: 1x any of `or(item(occultism:book_of_calling_foliot_lumberjack)item(occultism:book_of_calling_foliot_farmer)item(occultism:book_of_calling_foliot_cleaner)item(occultism:book_of_calling_foliot_transport_items))` *(after: Bind a Foliot)*
   > What: summoned foliots can work: Lumberjack cuts trees, Farmer tends crops, Janitor picks up items, Transporter moves items between chests.
   > How: each is a foliot summoning ritual (Lumberjack: an otherworld sapling, 3 saplings and an axe). The Book of Calling it gives lets you set its work area.
   > Tip: a Transporter feeding a Crusher and a Janitor collecting the dust is a whole processing line.
26. **Surprisingly Large Bag**: `occultism:satchel` *(after: Purple Chalk, Silver)*
   > What: a satchel with a bound foliot inside that keeps it bigger on the inside.
   > How: a foliot crafting ritual: a chest, two leather, string and a silver ingot around the golden bowl, on a white and purple pentacle.
   > Tip: the djinni version, the Ender Satchel, opens your ender chest.
27. **Otherworld Goggles**: `occultism:otherworld_goggles` *(after: Purple Chalk, Spirit-Attuned Gems)*
   > What: the goggles give a permanent Third Eye, and show even more hidden blocks than Demon's Dream does.
   > How: lenses (glass panes and an attuned gem) become infused lenses in a foliot ritual with 2 silver and gold; add a lens frame (otherstone and silver) and leather.
   > Tip: goggles let you see, not harvest: you still need the infused or iesnium pickaxe.
28. **Divination Rod**: `occultism:divination_rod` *(after: Spirit-Attuned Gems)*
   > What: the rod points toward otherworld blocks such as otherstone and otherworld trees.
   > How: gold ingots, glass and a spirit-attuned gem. Use it as the Dictionary explains to pick a target.
   > Tip: handy for finding an otherworld grove before you have saplings.
29. **Machine Operator**: `occultism:book_of_calling_djinni_manage_machine` *(after: Bind a Djinni)*
   > What: the djinni Machine Operator keeps a linked machine fed from your Storage Actuator and returns the results.
   > How: djinni summoning ritual with a coal block, gold, iron and a furnace. Use its Book of Calling on the storage and the machine.
   > Tip: it's a light autocrafting option before AE2.
30. **Dimensional Matrix**: `occultism:dimensional_matrix` *(after: Bind a Djinni)*
   > What: the Dimensional Matrix binds a djinni to hold items in a pocket dimension.
   > Chemistry: the quartz it needs is SiO₂, the same crystal that keeps time in a real watch by vibrating at a fixed frequency.
   > How: a djinni crafting ritual with three quartz blocks and an ender pearl.
31. **Storage Actuator**: `occultism:storage_controller` *(after: Dimensional Matrix)*
   > What: the Dimensional Storage Actuator stores thousands of items and shows them in one searchable screen.
   > How: a foliot ritual makes the Storage Actuator Base (otherstone pedestal, 2 copper, gold). Put the matrix on top of the base in a crafting grid.
   > Tip: it's an alternative to AE2 for early storage. Stabilizers expand it.
32. **Storage Stabilizers**: 2x `occultism:storage_stabilizer_tier1` *(after: Storage Actuator)*
   > What: stabilizers pointed at the Actuator raise how many items and item types it holds.
   > How: tier 0 is an otherstone pedestal; tier 1 is a foliot ritual: the tier 0 stabilizer, a copper block, blaze dust and a spirit-attuned gem.
   > Tip: place them within a few blocks, facing the Actuator.
33. **Djinni Stabilizer**: `occultism:storage_stabilizer_tier2` *(after: Storage Stabilizers)*
   > What: the tier 2 stabilizer holds a djinni and adds much more room than tier 1.
   > How: djinni crafting ritual: a tier 1 stabilizer, a silver block, a ghast tear and 2 spirit-attuned gems.
   > Tip: upgrade your tier 1 stabilizers one at a time; they keep working in between.
34. **Greater Stabilizers**: 1x any of `or(item(occultism:storage_stabilizer_tier3)item(occultism:storage_stabilizer_tier4))` *(after: Djinni Stabilizer, Unbound Afrit)*
   > What: tier 3 binds an afrit, tier 4 a marid; each is a big jump in capacity.
   > How: tier 3: tier 2 stabilizer, a gold block, a Totem of Undying, a crystal and afrit essence. Tier 4 adds an iesnium block, a beacon and marid essence.
   > Tip: a few high-tier stabilizers beat many low ones.
35. **Storage Remote**: `occultism:storage_remote` *(after: Storage Actuator)*
   > What: opens your Storage Actuator from anywhere.
   > How: a djinni ritual with an inert remote (iesnium nuggets, an attuned gem, an otherstone tablet, silver), two ender pearls and quartz. Link it by clicking the Actuator.
   > Tip: one remote per player saves a lot of walking back to base.
36. **Stable Wormhole**: `occultism:stable_wormhole` *(after: Storage Actuator)*
   > What: a Stable Wormhole links to your Storage Actuator so you can open it from another room.
   > How: foliot crafting ritual: an otherstone frame, an ender pearl, 2 quartz and 2 gold ingots. Link it to the Actuator.
   > Tip: put one beside each crafting area.
37. **Soul Gem**: `occultism:soul_gem` *(after: Bind a Djinni)*
   > What: a Soul Gem traps a living creature so you can move it and release it elsewhere.
   > How: a djinni ritual: a diamond, copper, silver and gold ingots, a fragile soul gem and 3 soul sand or soil.
   > Tip: it's the easy way to move villagers or animals to your base.
38. **Familiar Ring**: `occultism:familiar_ring` *(after: Soul Gem)*
   > What: familiars are summoned with rituals (greedy, bat, beaver, deer, blacksmith...). The ring stores one so it travels with you.
   > How: a djinni ritual with a soul gem, two gold and two silver ingots. Capture your familiar with it.
   > Tip: the Greedy Familiar picks up items for you. The Dictionary lists what each familiar does.
39. **Bind an Afrit**: `occultism:book_of_binding_bound_afrit` *(after: Bind a Djinni)*
   > What: afrits are the third rank of spirits. They power stronger rituals and the deep-mining miner.
   > How: a Book of Binding with yellow dye, bound with the spirit's name (see the Dictionary).
   > Tip: afrit rituals need afrit essence, which only an unbound afrit drops.
40. **Gray Chalk**: `occultism:chalk_gray` *(after: Bind a Djinni)*
   > What: gray chalk is the foundation colour of afrit pentacles.
   > How: impure white chalk with gray paste (a djinni ritual: gunpowder, clay, a phantom membrane and gray dye), purified in spiritfire.
   > Tip: gray, light gray and black chalk can stand in for white in most pentacles.
41. **Orange Chalk**: `occultism:chalk_orange` *(after: Bind a Djinni)*
   > What: orange chalk lures afrits. It doesn't control them; that takes red chalk.
   > How: impure white chalk with cursed honey, glow berries and blaze dust. Cursed honey drops from a possessed bee (djinni possession with honey items and a chicken).
   > Tip: Ihagan's Enthrallment, the djinni possession pentacle, is white, light gray, lime and gold.
42. **Unbound Afrit**: kill 1x `occultism:afrit_wild` *(after: Bind an Afrit, Gray Chalk, Orange Chalk)*
   > You remember fire that obeyed. This one remembers being free.
   > What: the Summon Unbound Afrit ritual calls a hostile afrit. It drops afrit essence when killed.
   > How: Kandar's Open Conjure (white, gray, lime, orange, skulls and a spirit-attuned crystal). Netherrack, an iesnium ingot, flint and steel and gunpowder, plus a cow as the sacrifice.
   > Tip: fire resistance potions turn this from a disaster into a fair fight. Build far from anything flammable.
43. **Red Chalk**: `occultism:chalk_red` *(after: Unbound Afrit)*
   > What: red chalk is made from afrit essence and lets your pentacles bind afrits instead of just calling them.
   > How: impure white chalk with afrit essence, a torchflower and redstone dust, purified in spiritfire.
   > Tip: Sevira's Permanent Confinement (the afrit crafting pentacle) uses red, orange, lime, purple, gray and white.
44. **The Wild Hunt**: kill 5x `occultism:wild_hunt_wither_skeleton` *(after: Unbound Afrit, Into the Fire)*
   > What: the Wild Hunt ritual calls a pack of wither skeletons with a good chance of dropping wither skeleton skulls.
   > How: Osorin's Unbound Calling (green, light blue and pink chalk; check the Dictionary). Activated with a skeleton skull; copper, silver and gold blocks, a diamond, netherrack and soul sand, plus a sacrifice.
   > Tip: the easiest way to collect three skulls for the Wither, and the skulls marid pentacles need.
45. **Black Chalk**: `occultism:chalk_black` *(after: Red Chalk)*
   > What: black chalk is the strongest foundation chalk; marid pentacles need it.
   > Chemistry: real witherite is barium carbonate, BaCO₃, named after the 18th-century chemist William Withering. It has nothing to do with the Wither.
   > How: impure white chalk with 3 witherite dust. Witherite dust is an afrit ritual: netherite dust, a wither skeleton skull, blackstone dust and a wither rose.
   > Tip: netherite dust comes from crushing a netherite ingot, so save one.
46. **Bind a Marid**: `occultism:book_of_binding_bound_marid` *(after: Red Chalk)*
   > What: marids are the fourth and strongest rank of spirits.
   > How: a Book of Binding with green dye, bound with a name like the others.
   > Tip: like the afrit, its essence only comes from an unbound one.
47. **Unbound Marid**: kill 1x `occultism:marid_unbound` *(after: Black Chalk, Bind a Marid)*
   > You remember the sea answering you. This spirit answers to no one.
   > What: the unbound marid is hostile and very strong. It drops marid essence.
   > How: Tibira's Attraction (black, red, orange, lime and white chalk, wither skulls and a crystal). A conduit, prismarine crystals, a prismarine shard and a ghast tear; use a trident to start it.
   > Tip: bring your best armor and healing, and don't do it at home.
48. **Blue Chalk**: `occultism:chalk_blue` *(after: Unbound Marid)*
   > What: blue chalk, made from marid essence, lets pentacles control marids.
   > Chemistry: tube coral builds its skeleton from calcium carbonate (aragonite), the same CaCO₃ as your chalk.
   > How: impure white chalk with marid essence, lapis dust and tube coral, purified in spiritfire.
   > Tip: Uphyxes' Inverted Tower, the marid crafting pentacle, makes the master miner and tier 4 stabilizers.

### Equivalent Exchange

*A builder's toy for a demigod who has already mastered the elements.*

1. **The Philosopher's Stone**: `projecte:philosophers_stone` *(after: The Complete Periodic Table)*
   > You remember turning one thing into another with a thought. Now you know what it really costs.
   > What: the Philosopher's Stone transmutes blocks in the world and is the key to every ProjectE item.
   > Chemistry: alchemists spent centuries looking for a stone that turns lead into gold. Real transmutation needs a nuclear reaction, and you've already done that: it's how you made oganesson.
   > How: redstone dust and glowstone dust in a ring around one oganesson (this pack's recipe).
   > Tip: it isn't used up in crafting recipes, so one stone lasts forever.
2. **World Transmutation**: checkmark *(after: The Philosopher's Stone)*
   > What: right-click a block with the stone to turn it into a related block: stone and cobblestone, dirt, grass and sand, one wood or flower type into the next.
   > How: an outline shows what will change before you click; sneak-click for the reverse direction. ProjectE's keybinds change the area size and shape.
   > Tip: a whole hillside of stone into cobblestone (or back) takes seconds. Click the checkmark once you've tried it.
3. **Transmutation Table**: `projecte:transmutation_table` *(after: The Philosopher's Stone)*
   > What: the Transmutation Table turns items into EMC and EMC back into any item you've learned.
   > How: four obsidian in the corners, four stone on the sides, the Philosopher's Stone in the centre.
   > Tip: your EMC and your learned items belong to you, not the table: every table you open shows the same list.
4. **Burn to Learn**: checkmark *(after: Transmutation Table)*
   > What: placing an item in the table's burn slot destroys it, adds its EMC to your total and teaches you that item.
   > How: burn a stack of something cheap (cobblestone, logs, sand), then try to burn an iron ingot. The ingot has no EMC, so it can't be learned.
   > Tip: stone, wood, glass, terracotta, wool and other building materials all work. Click the checkmark when you understand the rule.
5. **Covalence Dust**: 8x `projecte:low_covalence_dust`; 8x `projecte:medium_covalence_dust`; 8x `projecte:high_covalence_dust` *(after: The Philosopher's Stone)*
   > What: three dusts used for ProjectE storage and repairs.
   > Chemistry: a covalent bond is two atoms sharing electrons. The name fits: these dusts bind items together.
   > How: low: charcoal and 8 cobblestone. Medium: an iron ingot and redstone. High: a diamond and coal. Each recipe makes 40.
   > Tip: covalence dust repairs tools in a crafting grid: the matching dust for the tool's material.
6. **Alchemical Chest**: `projecte:alchemical_chest` *(after: Covalence Dust)*
   > What: a large chest, and the base of the Alchemical Bag.
   > How: one of each covalence dust on top, stone, a diamond and stone in the middle, iron, a chest and iron on the bottom.
7. **Alchemical Bag**: 1x any of `or(item(projecte:white_alchemical_bag)item(projecte:orange_alchemical_bag)item(projecte:magenta_alchemical_bag)item(projecte:light_blue_alchemical_bag)item(projecte:yellow_alchemical_bag)item(projecte:lime_alchemical_bag)item(projecte:pink_alchemical_bag)item(projecte:gray_alchemical_bag)item(projecte:light_gray_alchemical_bag)item(projecte:cyan_alchemical_bag)item(projecte:purple_alchemical_bag)item(projecte:blue_alchemical_bag)item(projecte:brown_alchemical_bag)item(projecte:green_alchemical_bag)item(projecte:red_alchemical_bag)item(projecte:black_alchemical_bag))` *(after: Alchemical Chest)*
   > What: an Alchemical Bag shares its inventory with every bag of the same colour you own.
   > How: three high covalence dust on top, wool on each side of an Alchemical Chest, and three wool below. Dye the wool for a different colour.
   > Tip: keep one colour for building blocks so your supplies follow you between builds.
8. **Repair Talisman**: `projecte:repair_talisman` *(after: Covalence Dust)*
   > What: while it's in your inventory it slowly repairs your damaged tools and armor.
   > How: covalence dusts, string and paper (check JEI for the pattern).
9. **Transmutation Tablet**: `projecte:transmutation_tablet` *(after: Transmutation Table, Dark Matter)*
   > What: a portable Transmutation Table.
   > How: four Dark Matter blocks, four stone and a Transmutation Table. That's sixteen Dark Matter: a real project.
   > Tip: worth it for big builds far from home.
10. **The Law of Conservation**: checkmark *(after: The Philosopher's Stone)*
   > What: in this pack, EMC works on building blocks only. Metals, gems, dusts, ores and plates have no EMC.
   > How: read the second page, then click the checkmark.
   > {@pagebreak}
   > The rule: ProjectE normally gives every item an EMC value, so a pile of cobblestone can become diamonds. In a chemistry pack that would skip everything you've built.
   > Chemistry: matter isn't created or destroyed in a chemical reaction (Lavoisier, 1789). Iron comes from iron oxide, Fe₂O₃ + 3CO → 2Fe + 3CO₂. Copper comes from its ores. Gold comes from anode sludge and aqua regia. You can rearrange atoms; you can't conjure them.
   > So: ingots, nuggets, raw ores, dusts, gems, plates, gears, rods, wires and storage blocks are set to EMC 0. Nether Stars, the Dragon Egg and the Beacon are 0 too, because the Seed of a New World needs them.
   > Also removed: Energy Condensers, Collectors and Relays. They turn EMC into unlimited items or power.
   > What's left: a terraforming and building tool. Stone, wood, glass, sand, dirt and decoration are what a demigod reshapes the world with, and those still have EMC.
11. **Alchemical Coal**: 4x `projecte:alchemical_coal` *(after: The Philosopher's Stone)*
   > What: a dense fuel and the first step toward Dark Matter.
   > Chemistry: coal is mostly carbon. Packing more energy into less mass is what refining fuel is all about.
   > How: the Philosopher's Stone and four coal (shapeless). It must be real coal, not charcoal.
   > Tip: it also burns far longer than coal in a furnace.
