12. **Mobius Fuel**: 4x `projecte:mobius_fuel` *(after: Alchemical Coal)*
   > What: the next fuel tier.
   > How: the Philosopher's Stone and four alchemical coal. That's 16 coal per Mobius Fuel.
   > Tip: automate coal now (Create crushing, MI quarries or a coal farm); the next steps need a lot.
13. **Aeternalis Fuel**: 8x `projecte:aeternalis_fuel` *(after: Mobius Fuel)*
   > What: the top fuel, and the main ingredient of Dark Matter.
   > How: the Philosopher's Stone and four Mobius Fuel. Each one is 64 coal; this quest asks for eight (512 coal).
   > Tip: packaged autocrafting (PackagedAuto or AE2) can do the whole chain while you build.
14. **Dark Matter**: `projecte:dark_matter` *(after: Aeternalis Fuel)*
   > You remember materials no forge could make. This one you made from coal and patience.
   > What: Dark Matter is ProjectE's strongest crafting material: tools, armor and rings.
   > How: eight Aeternalis Fuel around a diamond block. The diamonds come from real mining or synthesis; EMC can't make them here.
   > Tip: four Dark Matter make a Dark Matter Block, used by the tablet and the Dark Matter Furnace.
15. **Dark Matter Tools**: 1x any of `or(item(projecte:dm_pick)item(projecte:dm_axe)item(projecte:dm_shovel)item(projecte:dm_sword)item(projecte:dm_hoe)item(projecte:dm_shears)item(projecte:dm_hammer))` *(after: Dark Matter)*
   > What: Dark Matter tools never break and dig very fast. Their charge key widens what they mine.
   > How: like diamond tools, but Dark Matter replaces the head and diamonds the handle (the pickaxe is 3 Dark Matter and 2 diamonds).
   > Tip: charge the Dark Matter Hammer to mine a wider area: great for clearing land.
16. **Dark Matter Armor**: `projecte:dm_helmet`; `projecte:dm_chestplate`; `projecte:dm_leggings`; `projecte:dm_boots` *(after: Dark Matter)*
   > What: very strong armor that doesn't wear out.
   > How: shaped like diamond armor, all Dark Matter: helmet 5, chestplate 8, leggings 7, boots 4.
   > Tip: that's 24 Dark Matter. Automate the fuel chain first.
17. **Black Hole Band**: `projecte:black_hole_band` *(after: Dark Matter)*
   > What: while active, it draws nearby items toward you.
   > How: an Iron Band (eight iron around a lava bucket) between two Dark Matter, with string above and below.
   > Tip: switch it off near your storage system.
18. **Red Matter**: `projecte:red_matter` *(after: Dark Matter)*
   > What: the strongest ProjectE material, for Red Matter tools and armor.
   > How: three Dark Matter in the middle row with three Aeternalis Fuel above and three below.
   > Tip: each one is three Dark Matter plus 384 coal of fuel. Only for the truly committed.
19. **Rapid Reorganisation**: `projecte:nova_catalyst` *(after: Mobius Fuel)*
   > You remember reshaping mountains. The quick way was never the tidy way.
   > What: a Nova Catalyst: TNT and Mobius Fuel. A much bigger blast.
   > Chemistry: TNT is trinitrotoluene, C₇H₅N₃O₆. It carries its own oxygen, which is why it burns so fast.
   > Tip: never near your base. Ever.

## Exploration

### Other Worlds

*The End, the Otherside and the Undergarden.*

1. **The End**: visit `minecraft:the_end` *(after: Into the Fire)*
   > You remember the edge of the world. It's closer than it used to be.
   > What: the End holds the dragon, End Cities, shulkers and azure silver ore. End Crystals power Just Dire Things' flight upgrade.
   > How: throw eyes of ender to find a stronghold, fill the portal frame and jump in.
   > Tip: bring blocks, a bow and slow-falling potions. YUNG's Better Strongholds make the hunt more interesting.
2. **Slay the Dragon**: kill 1x `minecraft:ender_dragon` *(after: The End)*
   > What: the Ender Dragon guards the End's central island. Beating it opens the gateways to the outer islands.
   > How: shoot or climb to the End Crystals on the obsidian pillars and destroy them first (the caged ones need a climb), then fight the dragon when it perches on the portal.
   > Tip: stay out of its purple breath and never look an enderman in the eye mid-fight. A bow and a stack of arrows do most of the work.
3. **The Dragon's Breath**: 4x `minecraft:dragon_breath` *(after: Slay the Dragon)*
   > What: the dragon's purple breath can be bottled and makes lingering potions.
   > How: catch the purple breath cloud in glass bottles during the fight, or respawn the dragon for another go.
   > Tip: crystals respawn the dragon when placed on the exit portal, if you need it again.
4. **An End City**: find structure `minecraft:end_city` *(after: Slay the Dragon)*
   > What: End Cities are tall purpur towers on the outer islands, guarded by shulkers. Some have an End Ship moored alongside.
   > How: throw an ender pearl into an End Gateway near the main island, then search the outer islands. The Explorer's Compass can find one.
   > Tip: shulker bullets make you float; carry milk or slow falling.
5. **End City Loot**: `minecraft:elytra`; 4x `minecraft:shulker_shell` *(after: The Dragon's Breath, An End City)*
   > What: End Cities hold elytra (on the end ships) and shulkers whose shells make shulker boxes.
   > How: take an End Gateway from the main island, then search the outer islands for purple towers.
   > Tip: the Angel Ring needs an elytra; shulker shells feed Silent Gear's tyrian steel.
6. **Rocket Boost**: 32x `minecraft:firework_rocket` *(after: End City Loot)*
   > What: firework rockets boost elytra flight. Thirty-two is a good start for a long trip.
   > Chemistry: gunpowder is black powder: about 75% potassium nitrate (KNO₃, the oxidiser), 15% charcoal and 10% sulfur. The nitrate supplies its own oxygen, so it burns fast even in a sealed tube.
   > How: paper + gunpowder (1–3 for longer flight) in the crafting grid. Creepers and the hunting page give gunpowder.
   > Tip: rockets with a firework star explode and hurt you; use plain ones for flying.
7. **The Egg**: `minecraft:dragon_egg` *(after: Slay the Dragon)*
   > One egg, one dragon, one world. You remember having a whole shelf of these.
   > What: the dragon leaves a single egg on top of the exit portal the first time it dies.
   > Tip: it teleports when you hit it. Push it off with a piston, or let it fall onto a torch to pick it up.
8. **An Ancient City**: checkmark *(after: Into the Fire)*
   > What: Ancient Cities sit deep below the Overworld in the deep dark. Each one's centre holds the frame of a portal to the Otherside.
   > How: the Explorer's Compass can find one. Sneak everywhere: shriekers summon the Warden. Click the checkmark once you're inside.
   > Tip: sneaking never triggers sculk sensors, and walking on wool doesn't either.
9. **The Warden**: kill 1x `minecraft:warden` *(after: An Ancient City)*
   > What: the Warden is the strongest regular mob in the game, and the key to the Otherside: with Deeper and Darker it drops the Heart of the Deep.
   > How: trigger shriekers three times to summon one (or let it find you). Fight from range; it's blind and tracks you by sound and smell.
   > Tip: its sonic boom goes through shields and armour. Break line of sight when it starts charging.
10. **The Otherside**: `deeperdarker:heart_of_the_deep`; visit `deeperdarker:otherside` *(after: The Warden)*
   > You remember a silence older than the world. You can hear it again.
   > What: beyond the Ancient City lies the Otherside, a dark dimension of sculk and echo trees.
   > How: defeat a Warden for its Heart of the Deep, then use it to light the portal frame in the city's centre.
   > Tip: the Warden is blind but hears everything. Fight from range and keep moving.
11. **Echoes of the Deep**: 4x `deeperdarker:reinforced_echo_shard` *(after: The Otherside)*
   > What: reinforced echo shards make Warden gear that rivals netherite.
   > How: check JEI for the recipe; echo shards come from Ancient City chests and Otherside loot.
   > Tip: Warden armour is a netherite smithing upgrade, so keep your netherite gear.
12. **The Ancient Temple**: find structure `deeperdarker:ancient_temple` *(after: The Otherside)*
   > What: the Otherside's great structure: a sculk-choked temple with traps and the best loot in the dimension.
   > How: the Explorer's Compass points the way. Step inside.
   > Tip: the temple is full of sculk; sneak, as you did in the Ancient City.
13. **Biomes of the Otherside**: find biome `deeperdarker:echoing_forest`; find biome `deeperdarker:overcast_columns`; find biome `deeperdarker:blooming_caverns` *(after: The Otherside)*
   > What: the Otherside has its own biomes: echo-tree forests, towering columns and caverns lit by blooming plants.
   > Chemistry: there's no sunlight down here; real cave and deep-sea ecosystems run on chemosynthesis, where microbes get energy by oxidising H₂S or CH₄ instead of from light.
   > How: Nature's Compass works in the Otherside. Visit all three.
14. **Soul Elytra**: `deeperdarker:soul_elytra` *(after: The Otherside, End City Loot)*
   > What: an upgraded elytra that boosts itself, no rockets needed.
   > How: an elytra, sculk bones (from Shattered), a soul crystal (from Stalkers) and soul dust (check JEI).
   > Tip: Stalkers and Shattered roam the Otherside; fight them at range.
15. **Warden Armour**: `deeperdarker:warden_chestplate` *(after: Echoes of the Deep)*
   > What: Warden armour is tougher than netherite.
   > How: netherite chestplate + reinforced echo shard + Warden upgrade template in a smithing table.
   > Tip: check JEI for where the Warden upgrade template comes from.
16. **Undergarden Metals**: 8x `undergarden:cloggrum_ingot`; 4x `undergarden:froststeel_ingot` *(after: The Undergarden)*
   > What: new ores beneath the world: cloggrum (tough) and froststeel (slows what it hits). Utherium comes much later.
   > How: mine them in depthrock and shiverstone, then smelt. Utherium only grows deep in tremblecrust and dreadrock, and its clusters need the Infuser: see the Undergarden page.
   > Tip: run them through the Alchemistry Dissolver: what are these strange metals made of?
17. **Regalium**: 4x `undergarden:regalium_crystal` *(after: Undergarden Metals)*
   > What: regalium is the Undergarden's treasure: a rare crystal in depthrock and shiverstone.
   > How: smelt regalium ore. It also turns up in catacomb chests.
   > Tip: catacombs are also where the Forgotten Guardian waits.
18. **The Catacombs**: find structure `undergarden:catacombs`; kill 1x `undergarden:forgotten_guardian` *(after: Regalium)*
   > What: the catacombs are a buried tomb of the Undergarden's old builders. The Forgotten Guardian, a hulking armoured giant, still stands watch.
   > How: the Explorer's Compass can find one. Clear the halls, then fight the guardian.
   > Tip: it's slow; hit it, step back out of its swing, and repeat.
19. **The Forgotten Ingot**: `undergarden:forgotten_ingot` *(after: The Catacombs)*
   > You remember the old guardians and the oaths they swore. This one has forgotten its own.
   > What: the Forgotten Guardian drops forgotten nuggets; nine make a Forgotten Ingot for the strongest Undergarden tools.
   > How: find a catacomb, beat the guardian, and craft the nuggets into an ingot. The smithing template is in catacomb chests.
   > Tip: it hits hard; armour up and keep your distance between swings.
20. **Ruins and Camps**: find structure `undergarden:forgotten_vestige`; find structure `undergarden:denizen_camp` *(after: Undergarden Metals)*
   > What: forgotten vestiges are ruins left by the Undergarden's builders; denizen camps belong to the hostile Denizens who live there now.
   > How: explore until you find one of each (the Explorer's Compass helps) and step inside.
   > Tip: Denizens fight in groups; draw them out one at a time.
21. **Biomes Below**: find biome `undergarden:forgotten_field`; find biome `undergarden:frostfields`; find biome `undergarden:smog_spires` *(after: Undergarden Metals)*
   > What: the Undergarden's biomes range from frozen fields to spires that belch smog.
   > Chemistry: a smog is a mix of smoke and fog; real industrial smog is soot plus sulfur dioxide (SO₂), which forms sulfuric acid droplets in damp air.
   > How: Nature's Compass works here too. Visit all three.

### The Undergarden

*A forgotten land under the world, opened with acid.*

1. **Depthrock**: 32x `undergarden:depthrock` *(after: The Undergarden)*
   > Acid opened the way, and the dark below remembers you. You were the one who sealed it.
   > What: depthrock is the Undergarden's stone, the way deepslate is the Overworld's. Its ores (cloggrum, froststeel, utherium, regalium) all sit in it or in icy shiverstone.
   > Chemistry: dissolve depthrock in the Alchemistry Dissolver and it gives silicon dioxide (SiO₂), iron and aluminium, with rare traces of zirconium, gallium, dysprosium and tungsten.
   > How: the catalyst is copper ingots, stone, an ender pearl and a bucket of MI sulfuric acid (this pack's recipe). Light the stone-brick frame and step through, then mine.
   > Tip: the Undergarden has no sun. Bring a stack of torches and mark your portal.
2. **Cloggrum Tools**: `undergarden:cloggrum_pickaxe`; `undergarden:cloggrum_sword` *(after: Depthrock)*
   > What: cloggrum is the first Undergarden metal: about as good as iron, and the base for every Forgotten tool later.
   > How: mine cloggrum ore (stone pickaxe or better), smelt the raw cloggrum, and craft tools as you would iron ones.
   > Tip: keep the cloggrum sword and pickaxe once you replace them: they're what the Forgotten template upgrades.
3. **Cloggrum Armour**: `undergarden:cloggrum_helmet`; `undergarden:cloggrum_chestplate`; `undergarden:cloggrum_leggings`; `undergarden:cloggrum_boots` *(after: Cloggrum Tools)*
   > What: a full set of cloggrum armour. The boots have a perk: scintling goo no longer slows you.
   > How: 24 cloggrum ingots for the set. Raw cloggrum also turns up in depleted mines.
   > Tip: scintlings leave sticky goo trails across whole caverns; the boots pay for themselves fast.
4. **Forgotten Tools**: `undergarden:forgotten_pickaxe`; `undergarden:forgotten_sword` *(after: Cloggrum Armour)*
   > What: Forgotten tools mine Undergarden blocks 1.5x as fast, Forgotten weapons hit non-boss Undergarden mobs 1.5x as hard. Only a Forgotten pickaxe can break dreadrock.
   > How: smithing table: Forgotten upgrade template + cloggrum tool + Forgotten Ingot. Ingots come from the Forgotten Guardian in the catacombs (see the Other Worlds page); templates from catacomb chests.
   > Tip: copy a template with 7 diamonds and a block of depthrock (makes 2). Do it before you use your last one.
5. **The Depths**: find biome `undergarden:depths` *(after: Forgotten Tools)*
   > Under the forgotten land there is an older one. You remember the floor you laid over it.
   > What: a layer of dreadrock seals the bottom of the Undergarden. Below it lie the Depths: infected caverns, puff mushroom forests and rogdorium groves.
   > How: dig down until you hit dreadrock, then break through it with a Forgotten pickaxe. Nothing else can mine it.
   > Tip: the Depths carry the Utheric Infection. Read the rogdorium quest before you stay long.
6. **Rogdorium**: 8x `undergarden:rogdorium` *(after: The Depths)*
   > What: rogdorium is a crystal found only in dreadrock. It's the key to the Infuser, and eating it holds back the Utheric Infection.
   > Chemistry: real poisoning by heavy metals is treated much the same way: a chelating agent such as EDTA binds the toxic metal ions so the body can flush them out.
   > How: mine dreadrock rogdorium ore with a Forgotten pickaxe (it drops rogdorium) or smelt the ore block.
   > Tip: carry a few rogdorium nuggets on every trip down; eat one when the infection meter climbs.
7. **The Infuser**: `undergarden:infuser` *(after: Rogdorium)*
   > What: the Undergarden's own processing block. With rogdorium fuel it purifies; with utherium it corrupts.
   > Chemistry: the idea is old: purification is often just a reaction that removes one impurity, the way a blast furnace strips oxygen from iron ore.
   > How: an utheric cluster, rogdorium and five dreadrock (check JEI). It also gives armour a Rogdorium Infusion.
   > Tip: JEI's Infusing category lists every conversion, including which fuel slot it needs.
8. **Purified Utherium**: 4x `undergarden:utherium_crystal` *(after: The Infuser)*
   > What: utherium crystal is the Undergarden's weapon against rotspawn. Mined utherium ore only gives raw utheric clusters.
   > How: mine or smelt utherium ore for clusters (9 utheric shards also make one), then purify each cluster in the Infuser with rogdorium.
   > Tip: rotbeasts and rotbelchers drop utheric shards, so hunting them feeds the line.
9. **Utherium Gear**: `undergarden:utherium_sword`; `undergarden:utherium_chestplate` *(after: Purified Utherium)*
   > Rot fears what is pure. You remember forging blades for exactly this.
   > What: utherium weapons deal 1.5x damage to rotspawn; the armour is the Undergarden's best plain set.
   > How: craft them from utherium crystals like diamond gear (check JEI).
   > Tip: a worn-out utherium tool smelts back into a crystal, so nothing is wasted.
10. **Slingshot**: `undergarden:slingshot`; 16x `undergarden:depthrock_pebble` *(after: Depthrock)*
   > What: a ranged weapon that fires pebbles, goo balls, gronglets or rotten blisterberries.
   > How: twistytwigs (two wigglewood planks make four) and sticks. Pebbles drop from mogs and stoneborn, or stonecut depthrock.
   > Tip: it takes its own enchantments: Ricochet bounces shots off walls, Self Sling launches you instead.
11. **Froststeel**: `undergarden:froststeel_sword`; `undergarden:froststeel_chestplate` *(after: Cloggrum Tools)*
   > What: froststeel weapons give targets the Chilly effect, which slows them.
   > Chemistry: real steel gets brittle in deep cold: below its ductile-to-brittle transition temperature it cracks instead of bending, which is why ships' hulls once split in icy seas.
   > How: froststeel ore only forms in shiverstone, in the frozen biomes. It needs an iron pickaxe; smelt the raw froststeel.
   > Tip: a froststeel sword is great against fast rotspawn; slowed foes can't close the gap.
12. **The Forgotten**: kill 5x `undergarden:forgotten`; `undergarden:cloggrum_battleaxe` *(after: Cloggrum Armour)*
   > What: the Forgotten are armoured undead that guard the catacombs and vestiges. Some carry a cloggrum battleaxe.
   > How: kill five Forgotten. The battleaxe can't be crafted: it only drops from a Forgotten carrying one.
   > Tip: they hit hard but slowly. Shield up, then strike.
13. **Forgotten Battleaxe**: `undergarden:forgotten_battleaxe` *(after: The Forgotten, Forgotten Tools)*
   > What: the cloggrum battleaxe, upgraded with Forgotten metal.
   > How: smithing table: Forgotten upgrade template + cloggrum battleaxe + Forgotten Ingot.
   > Tip: make all six Forgotten tools if you want the mod's own completion advancement.
14. **Rogdorium Spear**: `undergarden:spear` *(after: Rogdorium)*
   > What: a spear you can stab with or throw, like a trident without the water.
   > How: one rogdorium on top of two sticks.
   > Tip: retrieve it after every throw; it doesn't come back on its own.
15. **Two Kinds of Gronglet**: `undergarden:rogdoric_gronglet`; `undergarden:utheric_gronglet` *(after: The Infuser)*
   > What: gronglets are little glowing growths. The Infuser can purify one with rogdorium or corrupt one with utherium.
   > How: collect two wild gronglets, then run one through each Infuser mode (JEI shows both).
   > Tip: gronglets also work as slingshot ammo. The mod asks if that is okay.
16. **Collector's Shelf**: `undergarden:cloggrum_block`; `undergarden:froststeel_block`; `undergarden:utherium_block`; `undergarden:regalium_block`; `undergarden:rogdorium_block`; `undergarden:forgotten_block` *(after: Utherium Gear)*
   > What: one storage block each of cloggrum, froststeel, utherium, regalium, rogdorium and Forgotten metal.
   > Chemistry: feed spares to the Dissolver: the Undergarden's metals are fiction, but what they break down into tells you what the pack thinks they are made of.
   > How: nine of each ingot or crystal in a crafting grid.
   > Tip: a Forgotten block and a carved gloomgourd summon a Forgotten Minion.
17. **Woods Without Sun**: 8x `undergarden:smogstem_log`; 8x `undergarden:wigglewood_log`; 8x `undergarden:grongle_log` *(after: Depthrock)*
   > What: three kinds of tree that grow with no daylight at all.
   > Chemistry: real plants can't do that: photosynthesis (6CO₂ + 6H₂O → C₆H₁₂O₆ + 6O₂) needs light. Underground life runs on chemosynthesis or on food that falls from above.
   > How: smogstem forests, wigglewood forests and the gronglegrowth each have their own tree.
   > Tip: wigglewood planks make the twistytwigs for the slingshot.
18. **Shard Torches**: 8x `undergarden:shard_torch` *(after: Depthrock)*
   > What: a torch tipped with an utheric shard. It lights the dark and hurts rotspawn that come close.
   > How: one utheric shard on a stick. Shards drop from rotbeasts and rotbelchers and sit in catacomb chests.
   > Tip: ring your portal and your base with them.
19. **A Depleted Mine**: find structure `undergarden:depleted_mine` *(after: Depthrock)*
   > What: old mines left by the Undergarden's builders. The chests still hold raw cloggrum, raw froststeel and cloggrum tools.
   > How: explore depthrock caverns until you find timber supports and rails; step inside.
   > Tip: a quick way to an early cloggrum pickaxe.
20. **Mushroom Bogs**: find biome `undergarden:blood_mushroom_bog`; find biome `undergarden:ink_mushroom_bog`; find biome `undergarden:veil_mushroom_bog` *(after: Depthrock)*
   > What: three bogs named after the giant mushrooms that grow in them. Each mushroom makes its own stew and dye.
   > Chemistry: a fungus is closer to an animal than a plant: its cell walls are chitin, (C₈H₁₃O₅N)ₙ, the same polymer as insect shells.
   > How: Nature's Compass works down here. Visit all three.
21. **Into the Depths**: find biome `undergarden:infected_depths`; find biome `undergarden:puff_mushroom_forest`; find biome `undergarden:rogdorium_grove` *(after: The Depths)*
   > What: the rest of the Depths: the infected depths, the puff mushroom forest and the rogdorium grove.
   > How: with a Forgotten pickaxe, tunnel through dreadrock to each one.
   > Tip: the rogdorium grove is the richest place for rogdorium; carry plenty out.
22. **The Rotbelcher**: kill 3x `undergarden:rotbelcher`; 8x `undergarden:utheric_shard` *(after: Depthrock)*
   > What: a bloated rotspawn that hurls rot from a distance. Like rotbeasts, it drops utheric shards.
   > How: close the distance fast, or fight it with a bow or slingshot from cover.
   > Tip: a shard torch nearby softens it up while you fight.
23. **Brutes**: kill 3x `undergarden:brute`; 2x `undergarden:brute_tusk` *(after: Depthrock)*
   > What: brutes are tusked beasts that leave you alone until you hit them. Their tusks grind into bone meal.
   > Chemistry: tusks, teeth and bone are mostly hydroxyapatite, Ca₅(PO₄)₃OH, which is why bone meal feeds plants phosphorus.
   > How: one brute tusk crafts into four bone meal.
24. **The Denizens**: kill 5x `undergarden:denizen`; `undergarden:denizen_mask` *(after: Depthrock)*
   > What: Denizens live in camps around campfires. They drop mysterious masks, and their camp chests hold more.
   > How: find a denizen camp (see the Other Worlds page) and fight. Breaking their campfire angers the whole camp.
   > Tip: draw them out one at a time.
25. **You Monster**: kill 1x `undergarden:scintling` *(after: Depthrock)*
   > What: you killed a scintling, a harmless glowing snail. It dropped a goo ball.
   > Chemistry: real snail slime is a mix of glycoproteins and water that thickens under stress, letting the snail glue itself to walls.
   > Tip: goo balls make sticky pistons, so at least it wasn't for nothing.
26. **A Minion of Your Own**: kill 1x `undergarden:forgotten_guardian`; `undergarden:forgotten_block` *(after: Forgotten Tools)*
   > What: stack a carved gloomgourd on a block of Forgotten metal and it wakes as a Forgotten Minion that fights for you.
   > How: a block of Forgotten metal is nine Forgotten Ingots, so beat the guardian until you have enough nuggets.
   > Tip: the mod has an advancement for building one; the minion fires at your enemies.
27. **The Gloomper's Secret**: `undergarden:music_disc_gloomper_secret` *(after: Depthrock)*
   > Even down here someone wrote music. You remember humming it.
   > What: a hidden music disc from the Undergarden. The mod counts finding it as a challenge.
   > Tip: the Undergarden has four more discs; play them on a jukebox.
28. **Roasted Underbeans**: 8x `undergarden:roasted_underbeans` *(after: Depthrock)*
   > What: underbean bushes grow in the forests. The beans are edible raw, much better roasted.
   > Chemistry: roasting browns food by the Maillard reaction: amino acids and sugars combine into hundreds of new flavour molecules above about 140 °C.
   > How: pick a bush, then smelt or campfire-cook the beans.
   > Tip: underbeans on a stick steer a dweller you're riding.
29. **Blisterbombs**: 4x `undergarden:blisterbomb` *(after: Slingshot)*
   > What: a throwable bomb of rotten blisterberries that bursts into a harmful cloud.
   > Chemistry: real rotting fruit ferments: yeast turns its sugar into ethanol and carbon dioxide (C₆H₁₂O₆ → 2C₂H₅OH + 2CO₂), and sealed fruit can split from the gas.
   > How: a twistytwig over six rotten blisterberries (blisterberry bushes drop both kinds).
   > Tip: eight around a gloomgourd make a boomgourd: an explosive block.
30. **Hunter's Supper**: 4x `undergarden:dweller_steak`; 4x `undergarden:gloomper_leg` *(after: Depthrock)*
   > What: the best meats below: dwellers (big rideable grazers) and gloompers (hopping toads) both drop meat and leather.
   > Chemistry: heat denatures meat proteins: the coiled chains unfold and bond to each other, which is why meat firms up as it cooks.
   > How: cook the raw meat in a furnace, smoker or campfire.
31. **Gloomgourds**: 8x `undergarden:gloomgourd`; 4x `undergarden:gloomgourd_seeds` *(after: Depthrock)*
   > What: a dark gourd that grows like a pumpkin. Carve it for lanterns and minions, or bake it into pie.
   > How: find wild gloomgourds; one gourd crafts into four seeds. Plant them on deepsoil farmland.
   > Tip: a carved gloomgourd over a torch makes a gloom-o-lantern; over a shard torch, a shard-o-lantern.
32. **Four Stews**: `undergarden:bloody_stew`; `undergarden:indigo_stew`; `undergarden:inky_stew`; `undergarden:veiled_stew` *(after: Roasted Underbeans)*
   > What: each giant mushroom (blood, indigo, ink and veil) cooks into its own stew.
   > Chemistry: the Dissolver breaks Undergarden mushrooms into chitin and phosphoric acid (H₃PO₄); phosphate is how cells store energy, as ATP.
   > How: a bowl and the mushrooms (check JEI); the mushroom bogs have all four kinds nearby.
33. **Weird Fish**: `undergarden:gwibling_bucket`; 4x `undergarden:cooked_gwibling` *(after: Hunter's Supper)*
   > What: gwiblings are the small fish of the Undergarden seas. Catch one live, or cook them.
   > How: use a water bucket on a gwibling to catch it; kill others for raw gwibling and smelt it.
   > Tip: the full-grown gwib is not a friendly fish. Watch the water.
34. **Gloomgourd Pie**: 2x `undergarden:gloomgourd_pie` *(after: Gloomgourds)*
   > What: the Undergarden's best plain food.
   > How: a gloomgourd, any Undergarden mushroom and a glitterkelp from the seas, shapeless.
   > Tip: glitterkelp also smelts into ordinary dried kelp.

### The Twilight Forest

*A realm of permanent dusk, ruled by bosses in order.*

1. **Wood of Dusk**: 16x `twilightforest:twilight_oak_log` *(after: The Twilight Forest)*
   > The trees here are older than your forgetting. Their bark still knows your hand.
   > What: twilight oak is the forest's common wood. It makes mason jars, drying racks and tannin for leather.
   > How: chop any twilight oak. Canopy trees, mangroves and darkwood grow nearby too.
   > Tip: every side quest on this page branches from here; the boss line starts at the Naga's courtyard.
2. **Twilight Cake**: `telepastries:twilight_cake` *(after: The Twilight Forest)*
   > What: a TelePastries cake: eat a slice and you're taken to the Twilight Forest. A tidy alternative to the flower pool.
   > How: cake, poppies and dandelions, with an MI digital circuit (HV) in the bottom centre (this pack's recipe; check JEI).
   > Tip: keep the cake at your base and the portal pool stays optional.
3. **A Hollow Giant**: find structure `twilightforest:hollow_tree` *(after: Wood of Dusk)*
   > What: huge hollow trees tower over the forest. Their branches hide leafy caches with rare magic saplings.
   > How: find one (taller than everything around it) and climb inside. Search the leaf clusters for a chest.
   > Tip: bring ladders or scaffolding; the fall from the canopy is long.
4. **Magic Saplings**: 1x any of `or(item(twilightforest:time_sapling)item(twilightforest:transformation_sapling)item(twilightforest:mining_sapling)item(twilightforest:sorting_sapling))` *(after: A Hollow Giant)*
   > What: four magic trees: Time, Transformation, Mining and Sorting. Each grows with a working core inside its trunk.
   > How: loot one from a hollow tree's cache. Plant it where you want the tree; it can't be moved later.
   > Tip: breaking a core only drops a plain log, so choose the spot first.
5. **Tree of Time**: 4x `twilightforest:time_log` *(after: Magic Saplings)*
   > What: the Timewood Clock in the trunk makes blocks around the tree tick faster: crops, saplings and machines that tick.
   > How: grow a Tree of Time from its sapling and collect some of its logs (any log of the tree counts).
   > Tip: plant it in the middle of a farm. Check its tooltip in JEI for the range.
6. **Tree of Transformation**: 4x `twilightforest:transformation_log` *(after: Magic Saplings)*
   > What: the Heart of Transformation slowly rewrites the biome around the tree into a Twilight one.
   > How: grow a Tree of Transformation from its sapling and collect some of its logs.
   > Tip: don't plant it next to a base that needs a particular biome; the change spreads.
7. **Miner's Tree**: 4x `twilightforest:mining_log` *(after: Magic Saplings)*
   > What: the Minewood Core gathers ore from the ground around the tree (its tooltip in JEI explains the details).
   > Chemistry: real ore is just rock rich in a mineral, like haematite (Fe₂O₃) or chalcopyrite (CuFeS₂). The tree simply finds it for you.
   > How: grow a Miner's Tree from its sapling and collect some of its logs.
8. **Sorting Tree**: 4x `twilightforest:sorting_log` *(after: Magic Saplings)*
   > What: the Sortingwood Engine moves items from a chest by the tree into nearby chests that already hold the same item.
   > How: grow a Sorting Tree from its sapling and collect some of its logs.
   > Tip: a cheap sorting room for a forest outpost; at home your storage network does it better.
9. **Magic Map Focus**: `twilightforest:magic_map_focus` *(after: Wood of Dusk)*
   > What: the focus that turns paper into a Magic Map of the Twilight Forest.
   > How: a raven feather, torchberries and glowstone dust, shapeless. Ravens hop about the forest floor; torchberries hang from cave roofs (skeleton druids drop them too).
   > Tip: torchberries also make berry torches, which never need fuel.
10. **A Map of Dusk**: `twilightforest:filled_magic_map` *(after: Magic Map Focus)*
   > What: the Magic Map shows Twilight biomes and marks every boss structure with an icon.
   > How: eight paper around the focus makes a blank Magic Map. Use it inside the Twilight Forest to fill it in.
   > Tip: the Maze Map does the same job inside the Minoshroom's labyrinth; Nature's Compass finds biomes, Explorer's Compass finds structures.
11. **Hollow Hills**: find structure `twilightforest:small_hollow_hill` *(after: Wood of Dusk)*
   > What: hollow hills are mounds with a cave inside, lined with ore blocks, spawners and a treasure chest.
   > How: find a small hollow hill (the mounds in the plain forest) and step inside.
   > Tip: break the spawners first. The ore inside is free feedstock for your ore lines.
12. **Ore Magnet**: `twilightforest:ore_magnet` *(after: Hollow Hills)*
   > What: a hollow hill treasure that drags a vein of ore out of the rock towards you.
   > Chemistry: real mines do a version of this: magnetic separation pulls magnetite (Fe₃O₄) out of crushed rock, because it is strongly ferromagnetic.
   > How: loot it from a hollow hill chest (any size). Aim at stone and use it.
13. **The Great Hollow Hill**: find structure `twilightforest:large_hollow_hill` *(after: Ore Magnet)*
   > What: large hollow hills hold the most ore, the most spawners and the richest chest of the three sizes.
   > How: look for the biggest mounds in the forest and step inside.
   > Tip: light the cave up as you go; the spawners inside are what makes it dangerous.
14. **Hill Curios**: 1x any of `or(item(twilightforest:peacock_feather_fan)item(twilightforest:moonworm_queen)item(twilightforest:transformation_powder))` *(after: The Great Hollow Hill)*
   > What: odd treasures from hills and the Lich Tower: the Peacock Feather Fan blows a gust of wind, the Moonworm Queen fires glowing moonworms, and Transformation Powder turns some mobs into their Twilight cousins.
   > How: loot any one of them from hollow hill or tower chests.
   > Tip: the Moonworm Queen is a lighting tool that never runs out for good; its repair recipe is in JEI.
15. **Steeleaf**: 4x `twilightforest:steeleaf_ingot` *(after: Wood of Dusk)*
   > What: steeleaf is a metal-hard leaf found only as treasure: hollow hill, labyrinth, stronghold and Dark Tower chests.
   > Chemistry: a leaf of steel is fantasy. Real steel is iron with roughly 0.2 to 2% carbon dissolved in it, which is what makes it hard.
   > How: loot chests as you explore. Nine make a steeleaf block for storage.
16. **Steeleaf Gear**: 1x any of `or(item(twilightforest:steeleaf_pickaxe)item(twilightforest:steeleaf_sword)item(twilightforest:steeleaf_axe)item(twilightforest:steeleaf_helmet)item(twilightforest:steeleaf_chestplate)item(twilightforest:steeleaf_leggings)item(twilightforest:steeleaf_boots))` *(after: Steeleaf)*
   > What: steeleaf tools and armour come pre-enchanted. The pickaxe is crafted with Fortune II already on it.
   > How: craft any steeleaf piece (vanilla shapes with steeleaf; check JEI).
   > Tip: a Fortune II pickaxe this early doubles ore from your best veins.
17. **Ironwood**: 2x `twilightforest:liveroot`; 4x `twilightforest:ironwood_ingot` *(after: Wood of Dusk)*
   > What: ironwood is the forest's first metal: tougher than iron, and needed for the Twilight Metals milestone.
   > Chemistry: smelting raw iron is a reduction: carbon monoxide strips the oxygen, Fe₂O₃ + 3CO → 2Fe + 3CO₂. The liveroot and gold just make it Twilight.
   > How: liveroot + raw iron + a gold nugget makes 2 raw ironwood (shapeless). Smelt or blast it. Liveroot grows in the root tangles underground.
   > Tip: bring a stack of raw iron from home; liveroot is the only forest part.
18. **Ironwood Gear**: 1x any of `or(item(twilightforest:ironwood_pickaxe)item(twilightforest:ironwood_sword)item(twilightforest:ironwood_axe)item(twilightforest:ironwood_helmet)item(twilightforest:ironwood_chestplate)item(twilightforest:ironwood_leggings)item(twilightforest:ironwood_boots))` *(after: Ironwood)*
   > What: ironwood tools and armour come with enchantments built in (the pickaxe has Efficiency I).
   > How: craft any ironwood piece in the usual shapes (check JEI).
   > Tip: a full ironwood set is a solid start for the Naga and the Lich.
19. **Oreberries**: 8x any of `or(item(twilightforest:iron_berry)item(twilightforest:copper_berry)item(twilightforest:gold_berry))` *(after: Wood of Dusk)*
   > What: oreberry bushes grow underground in the forest and fruit iron, copper or gold berries. Smelt a berry for a nugget.
   > Chemistry: real plants can do this: hyperaccumulators such as Alyssum store nickel in their leaves, and phytomining burns them for metal-rich ash.
   > How: dig down a little under the forest floor to find the bushes (the enchanted forest grows them too). Collect 8 berries of any metal.
   > Tip: the bushes are thorny and hurt you when you walk through them. Harvest them, don't push past.
20. **Drying Rack**: `twilightforest:twilight_oak_drying_rack` *(after: Wood of Dusk)*
   > What: a rack that slowly dries what you hang on it: meat into jerky, mud into clay, treated leather into tanned leather.
   > How: three twilight oak slabs in a row. Every wood has its own rack; right-click items onto it.
   > Tip: drying takes minutes, not seconds. Build a row of racks.
21. **Tanning**: `twilightforest:tannin`; 4x `twilightforest:tanned_leather` *(after: Drying Rack)*
   > What: tanned leather is the material for the new traveller's gear.
   > Chemistry: real vegetable tanning works the same way: tannins (polyphenols from bark and leaves) bind to the collagen in hide and cross-link it, so it stops rotting and stays supple.
   > How: tannin = water bottle + twilight oak sapling + root strand + twilight oak leaves. Tannin + leather = treated leather; dry that on a rack to get tanned leather.
   > Tip: root strands come from the root blocks underground, near the liveroot.
22. **Traveller's Gear**: 1x any of `or(item(twilightforest:travellers_vest)item(twilightforest:travellers_belt)item(twilightforest:travellers_goggles)item(twilightforest:travellers_boots)item(twilightforest:travellers_gloves)item(twilightforest:travellers_wings))` *(after: Tanning)*
   > What: vest, belt, goggles, boots, gloves and wings made from tanned leather. Each piece takes modifiers (double jump, gliding, auto-repair and more).
   > How: craft any piece (check JEI). The modifier recipes are in JEI too; many use Twilight drops such as raven feathers and borer essence.
   > Tip: the wings need carminite, so they wait until the Dark Tower.
23. **Jerky**: 8x any of `or(item(twilightforest:meef_jerky)item(twilightforest:venison_jerky)item(twilightforest:beef_jerky)item(twilightforest:pork_jerky)item(twilightforest:chicken_jerky)item(twilightforest:mutton_jerky))` *(after: Drying Rack)*
   > What: dried meat from the drying rack: light, filling travel food.
   > Chemistry: drying lowers the water activity of meat below about 0.85, and most bacteria can't grow without free water. It's one of the oldest preservation methods there is.
   > How: hang raw meat (venison from forest deer works well) on a drying rack and wait. Collect 8 jerky.
24. **Charm of Life**: `twilightforest:charm_of_life_1` *(after: Wood of Dusk)*
   > What: carried in your inventory, the Charm of Life is used up to save you from a killing blow.
   > How: a chest find: fallen trunks, hollow hills, the Lich Tower, the stronghold and the Aurora Palace all have them.
   > Tip: carry one into every boss fight.
25. **Charm of Keeping**: `twilightforest:charm_of_keeping_1` *(after: Charm of Life)*
   > What: the Charm of Keeping is used up when you die and keeps some of your items with you. Higher tiers keep more.
   > How: found in many forest chests; maze slimes drop them too.
   > Tip: the forest's death caskets also hold what you drop, so you can come back for it.
26. **Stronger Charms**: 1x any of `or(item(twilightforest:charm_of_life_2)item(twilightforest:charm_of_keeping_2)item(twilightforest:charm_of_keeping_3))` *(after: Charm of Keeping)*
   > What: combine charms into a higher tier: Charm of Life II, or Charm of Keeping II and III.
   > How: four of a tier, shapeless, make one of the next tier (check JEI).
   > Tip: a Charm of Keeping III keeps your whole inventory, so it's worth saving sixteen of the first tier.
27. **The Green Twilight**: find biome `twilightforest:enchanted_forest`; find biome `twilightforest:firefly_forest`; find biome `twilightforest:mushroom_forest` *(after: Wood of Dusk)*
   > What: the outer Twilight biomes are open from the start: a glowing enchanted forest, a firefly forest and giant mushrooms.
   > Chemistry: fireflies (and their real cousins) glow by bioluminescence: the enzyme luciferase oxidises luciferin with O₂ and ATP, releasing light instead of heat.
   > How: Nature's Compass can point you to each biome. Just walk in.
28. **The Hedge Maze**: find structure `twilightforest:hedge_maze` *(after: The Green Twilight)*
   > What: a maze of hedges in the forest, with hedge spiders and chests at the dead ends.
   > How: find one in the forest and walk in.
   > Tip: bring shears or an axe and cut your own path when you're lost.
29. **The Questing Ram**: find structure `twilightforest:quest_grove` *(after: The Hedge Maze)*
   > What: a hidden grove where the Questing Ram lives. Feed it one wool of every colour and it rewards you.
   > Chemistry: wool dyes are real chemistry too: indigo, cochineal and lapis-blue (lazurite, a sulfur-bearing silicate) all coloured cloth in history.
   > Tip: the grove's ring of stones looks like a fairy ring in the enchanted forest.
30. **The Crumble Horn**: `twilightforest:crumble_horn` *(after: The Questing Ram)*
   > What: feed the Questing Ram all sixteen colours of wool and it pays out blocks of metal and gems, its trophy, and the Crumble Horn.
   > How: blow the horn at stone and it crumbles the blocks in front of you (check JEI for what turns into what).
   > Tip: it's a handy tunnelling tool for the labyrinth.
31. **The Naga's Courtyard**: find structure `twilightforest:naga_courtyard`; kill 1x `twilightforest:naga` *(after: The Twilight Forest)*
   > You remember this twilight. You walked here once, when the forest was young.
   > What: the first boss. Each boss lifts the curse on the next region (hunger, fire, darkness, frost or acid rain), so the order matters.
   > How: the courtyard is a walled stone garden in the plain forest (the Magic Map marks it). Fight inside it.
   > Tip: the Naga charges in straight lines; sidestep, then hit it while it turns.
32. **Naga Scale Armour**: `twilightforest:naga_chestplate`; `twilightforest:naga_leggings` *(after: The Naga's Courtyard)*
   > What: a tunic and leggings of naga scales. Both come pre-enchanted: Fire Protection III on the tunic, Protection III on the leggings.
   > How: eight scales for the tunic, seven for the leggings, in the usual armour shapes (check JEI).
   > Tip: the fire protection pays off later in the Hydra's fire swamp.
33. **The Naga**: 4x `twilightforest:naga_scale` *(after: The Naga's Courtyard)*
   > What: a great serpent coiled in a stone courtyard. Its scales unlock the path forward and make naga armour.
   > How: the Naga drops scales and its trophy when it dies. Hit it when it stops charging; stay out of its coils.
   > Tip: the Lich Tower is next. Its door won't open until the Naga is dead.
34. **The Lich Tower**: find structure `twilightforest:lich_tower`; kill 1x `twilightforest:lich` *(after: The Naga)*
   > What: the Lich waits at the top of a tall stone tower. Beating it opens the swamp, the dark forest and the snowy forest.
   > How: climb through the tower's rooms to the top. Break the shields orbiting it before you can hurt it.
   > Tip: the Lich's shadow clones are distractions; watch for the one that casts.
35. **Four Scepters**: `twilightforest:twilight_scepter`; `twilightforest:zombie_scepter`; `twilightforest:lifedrain_scepter`; `twilightforest:fortification_scepter` *(after: The Lich Tower)*
   > What: the Lich drops one scepter per kill: Twilight (fires bolts), Zombie (raises loyal zombies), Lifedrain (heals you as it hurts) and Fortification (a shield).
   > How: other Lich Towers have their own Lich. Beat it again to collect the set.
   > Tip: scepters recharge on a crafting grid with their own item (check JEI). The Renewal enchantment recharges them automatically.
36. **The Lich**: `twilightforest:lich_trophy` *(after: The Lich Tower)*
   > What: the Lich drops its trophy, one of its scepters and golden gear.
   > How: pick up the trophy from the top of the tower once it falls.
   > Tip: three ways open now: the labyrinth in the swamp, the stronghold in the dark forest, and the snowy forest below.
37. **The Labyrinth**: find structure `twilightforest:labyrinth`; kill 1x `twilightforest:minoshroom` *(after: The Lich)*
   > What: beneath the swamp lies a maze of mossy stone. The Minoshroom waits in a room at its heart.
   > How: enter through the stairs in the swamp. A Maze Map (made with a Maze Map Focus) shows the corridors.
   > Tip: mosquito swarms bite in the swamp; bring plenty of food.
38. **Mazebreaker**: `twilightforest:mazebreaker_pickaxe` *(after: The Labyrinth)*
   > What: a diamond pickaxe that chews through maze stone, found only in the labyrinth's best vault.
   > How: labyrinth vaults are sealed rooms off the corridors. The jackpot vault is rare; check every one.
   > Tip: a Maze Map shows unexplored rooms as gaps in the corridors.
39. **Maze Map Focus**: `twilightforest:maze_map_focus` *(after: The Labyrinth)*
   > What: the focus for Maze Maps, and the heart of the Uncrafting Table.
   > How: minotaurs in the labyrinth drop it. Kill them as you search for the Minoshroom.
   > Tip: keep a spare; the Uncrafting Table eats one.
40. **Minotaur Axe**: `twilightforest:diamond_minotaur_axe` *(after: The Labyrinth)*
   > What: a heavy diamond axe dropped by the Minoshroom.
   > How: beat the Minoshroom; the axe drops with its stroganoff.
   > Tip: it's an axe, so it still chops wood; keep it for both jobs.
41. **Maze Map**: `twilightforest:filled_maze_map` *(after: Maze Map Focus)*
   > What: a map that draws the tunnels around you at your own depth.
   > How: eight paper around a Maze Map Focus makes a blank Maze Map. Use it in the labyrinth to fill it in.
   > Tip: one map covers one level; make another for each floor.
42. **Ore Map**: `twilightforest:filled_ore_map` *(after: Maze Map)*
   > What: a Maze Map upgraded to also mark the ore around you.
   > How: a blank Maze Map + a diamond block + a gold block + an iron block, shapeless. Use it to fill it in.
   > Tip: expensive, but it finds rich pockets for your ore lines fast.
43. **Uncrafting Table**: `twilightforest:uncrafting_table` *(after: Maze Map Focus)*
   > What: the Twilight Forest's table that turns crafted items back into their ingredients, for XP.
   > Chemistry: the Alchemistry Dissolver goes further: it uncrafts all the way down to elements.
   > How: eight crafting tables around a Maze Map Focus (this pack leaves the recipe and the table unchanged).
   > Tip: the XP cost rises with the item's value, and some recipes can't be reversed; the table tells you.
44. **The Minoshroom**: `twilightforest:minoshroom_trophy` *(after: The Labyrinth)*
   > What: a mushroom minotaur in the swamp labyrinth. It drops its trophy, meef stroganoff and a mighty axe.
   > How: the Maze Map helps you find it in the maze. Sidestep its charges.
   > Tip: don't eat all the stroganoff at once; the next quest needs one.
45. **Mighty Stroganoff**: `twilightforest:meef_stroganoff` *(after: The Minoshroom)*
   > What: the fire swamp sets you alight until your body is used to its heat. Eating meef stroganoff does that.
   > How: eat one meef stroganoff from the Minoshroom, and keep another in your inventory for this quest.
   > Tip: it's also one of the best foods in the forest: a full bowl of hunger.
46. **The Hydra's Lair**: find structure `twilightforest:hydra_lair`; kill 1x `twilightforest:hydra` *(after: Mighty Stroganoff)*
   > What: the Hydra lives in a hill cave in the fire swamp, the hottest place in the forest.
   > Chemistry: swamps really do burn: rotting plants release methane (CH₄), which can ignite as marsh fire.
   > How: eat the stroganoff first or the swamp burns you. Strike the heads when they lunge.
   > Tip: the heads regrow if you're slow; pressure one at a time.
47. **The Wild Regions**: find biome `twilightforest:swamp`; find biome `twilightforest:fire_swamp`; find biome `twilightforest:dark_forest` *(after: The Hydra's Lair)*
   > What: the regions you fought through on the main line. Walk through each once you've earned it.
   > How: Nature's Compass points to each biome. The Lich lifts the swamp's hunger and the dark forest's darkness; the stroganoff lifts the fire swamp's burning.
   > Tip: a quick visit counts; you don't need to stay.
48. **The Hydra**: `twilightforest:hydra_trophy`; 2x `twilightforest:fiery_blood` *(after: The Hydra's Lair)*
   > What: the Hydra breathes fire from three heads. It drops its trophy, fiery blood and hydra chops.
   > How: fire resistance potions help. Hit the heads when they lunge at you; they regrow, so keep up the pressure.
   > Tip: a hydra chop is a huge meal that also heals; save them for boss fights.
49. **Fiery Metal**: 4x `twilightforest:fiery_ingot` *(after: The Hydra)*
   > What: fiery ingots: the hottest metal of the forest, for fire-proof tools and armour.
   > Chemistry: real blood is red because of the iron in haem, the part of haemoglobin that carries O₂. The Hydra's blood just burns as well.
   > How: a vial of fiery blood (Hydra) or fiery tears (Ur-Ghast) + an iron ingot, shapeless.
50. **Fiery Gear**: 1x any of `or(item(twilightforest:fiery_pickaxe)item(twilightforest:fiery_sword)item(twilightforest:fiery_helmet)item(twilightforest:fiery_chestplate)item(twilightforest:fiery_leggings)item(twilightforest:fiery_boots))` *(after: Fiery Metal)*
   > What: the Fiery Pickaxe smelts the ore it mines on the spot; the sword sets targets alight.
   > Chemistry: smelting is reduction: heat plus carbon pulls the oxygen off a metal oxide. The pickaxe does the furnace's job at the moment you mine.
   > How: fiery ingots with blaze rods for the tools, or alone for the armour (check JEI).
   > Tip: auto-smelting skips your ore doubling. Use it on things you'd only smelt anyway.
51. **Armour Shards**: 9x `twilightforest:armor_shard` *(after: The Hydra)*
   > What: shards of knightmetal, the stronghold's metal. Nine make a shard cluster.
   > How: goblin knights and block-and-chain goblins in the Goblin Knight Stronghold drop them; helmet crabs on the shore drop them too.
   > Tip: the dark forest has been safe to walk since the Lich; only its centre stays dark.
52. **Knightmetal**: `twilightforest:armor_shard_cluster`; 4x `twilightforest:knightmetal_ingot` *(after: Armour Shards)*
   > What: knightmetal ingots, for the heaviest armour and weapons in the forest.
   > Chemistry: remelting scrap is how most real steel is made today: an electric arc furnace melts recycled metal back into new ingots.
   > How: nine armour shards make a cluster (shapeless). Smelt or blast the cluster for one ingot.
53. **Twilight Metals**: 4x `twilightforest:ironwood_ingot`; 4x `twilightforest:knightmetal_ingot`; 2x `twilightforest:fiery_ingot` *(after: Fiery Metal, Knightmetal)*
   > What: the three alloys found nowhere else, for strong tools and armour.
   > How: ironwood from liveroot (see the forest), knightmetal from armour shards, fiery metal from iron and fiery blood or tears.
   > Tip: knightmetal armour and tools are great against the Knight Phantoms.
54. **Knight's Arms**: 1x any of `or(item(twilightforest:knightmetal_pickaxe)item(twilightforest:knightmetal_sword)item(twilightforest:knightmetal_axe)item(twilightforest:knightmetal_helmet)item(twilightforest:knightmetal_chestplate)item(twilightforest:knightmetal_leggings)item(twilightforest:knightmetal_boots)item(twilightforest:phantom_helmet)item(twilightforest:phantom_chestplate))` *(after: Knightmetal)*
   > What: knightmetal tools and armour, or the phantom helmet and chestplate the Knight Phantoms sometimes drop.
   > How: craft any knightmetal piece (check JEI), or take phantom armour from the phantoms' drops.
   > Tip: the phantoms also drop knightmetal weapons, so the fight can pay for itself.
55. **Block and Chain**: 1x any of `or(item(twilightforest:block_and_chain)item(twilightforest:knightmetal_shield))` *(after: Knight's Arms)*
   > What: the Block and Chain is a thrown flail that smashes blocks and mobs; the Knightmetal Shield blocks like a wall.
   > How: both start with a knightmetal ring (four ingots in a ring). Block and Chain: block + 3 ingots + ring. Shield: ingots, ring and towerwood (check JEI).
   > Tip: the shield needs towerwood from the Dark Tower.
56. **The Goblin Stronghold**: find structure `twilightforest:knight_stronghold`; kill 6x `twilightforest:knight_phantom` *(after: The Hydra)*
   > What: the Knight Phantoms haunt the deepest room of the Goblin Knight Stronghold, buried under the dark forest.
   > How: find the entrance in the dark forest (the Magic Map marks it). Inside, place a boss trophy on the Trophy Pedestal to wake the phantoms, then kill all six.
   > Tip: the stronghold opens after the Lich; this page sends you after the Hydra so you arrive with fiery and knightmetal gear.
57. **The Knight Phantoms**: `twilightforest:knight_phantom_trophy` *(after: The Goblin Stronghold)*
   > What: six phantom knights guard the Goblin Stronghold in the dark forest. Their trophy drops with the last one.
   > How: they wait deep inside the stronghold. Knightmetal armour helps.
   > Tip: beating them lifts the darkness on the dark forest's centre, where the Dark Tower stands.
58. **The Dark Tower**: find structure `twilightforest:dark_tower`; kill 1x `twilightforest:ur_ghast` *(after: The Knight Phantoms)*
   > What: a huge tower of towerwood in the centre of the dark forest, with the Ur-Ghast at the top.
   > How: climb through the tower's floors using its carminite contraptions; redstone opens the way in many rooms. A tower key from a chest opens the locked doors.
   > Tip: towerwood borers eat through the walls; kill them before they swarm.
59. **Carminite**: 4x `twilightforest:carminite` *(after: The Dark Tower)*
   > What: carminite powers the Dark Tower's devices and the traveller's wings.
   > Chemistry: its name echoes carmine, a real red dye: carminic acid (C₂₂H₂₀O₁₃), made by cochineal insects.
   > How: the Ur-Ghast drops some. Or craft it: 4 borer essence + 4 redstone + a ghast tear. Borer essence drops from towerwood borers.
60. **Tower Contraptions**: 1x any of `or(item(twilightforest:carminite_reactor)item(twilightforest:carminite_builder)item(twilightforest:reappearing_block)item(twilightforest:vanishing_block))` *(after: Carminite)*
   > What: the redstone gadgets of the Dark Tower: blocks that vanish and reappear, a builder that lays bridges, and the carminite reactor.
   > How: encased towerwood + carminite, with redstone, a dispenser or redstone ore depending on the device (check JEI).
   > Tip: vanishing blocks make secret doors for your base; a redstone pulse opens them.
61. **Experiment 115**: `twilightforest:experiment_115` *(after: The Dark Tower)*
   > What: a strange cake left in the Dark Tower's chests. Nobody knows who baked it.
   > How: found in Dark Tower chests. Place it and take a bite.
   > Tip: there's a rumour it can be refilled. Experiment.
62. **The Ur-Ghast**: `twilightforest:ur_ghast_trophy` *(after: The Dark Tower)*
   > You remember the last tower of the twilight. Its keeper remembers you too.
   > What: the great ghast at the top of the Dark Tower. Its fiery tears make more fiery metal.
   > How: climb the tower. Kill ghastlings near the ghast traps to charge them; charged traps pull the Ur-Ghast down so you can strike.
   > Tip: carminite from the tower makes redstone gadgets of the twilight.
63. **The Yeti Cave**: find structure `twilightforest:yeti_cave`; kill 1x `twilightforest:alpha_yeti` *(after: The Lich)*
   > What: the Alpha Yeti rules a cave in the snowy forest, surrounded by its smaller yetis.
   > Chemistry: cold kills by pulling heat out of you; still air is a poor conductor of heat, which is why fur and down keep you warm.
   > How: the snowy forest's frost lifts after the Lich. Find the cave and fight inside.
   > Tip: when it roars, icicles fall from the roof; keep moving.
64. **Arctic Fur**: 4x `twilightforest:arctic_fur`; 1x any of `or(item(twilightforest:arctic_helmet)item(twilightforest:arctic_chestplate)item(twilightforest:arctic_leggings)item(twilightforest:arctic_boots))` *(after: The Yeti Cave)*
   > What: ordinary yetis drop arctic fur, which makes light, warm armour you can dye.
   > Chemistry: fur traps a layer of still air, and air conducts heat about a thousand times worse than ice-cold water does.
   > How: kill yetis in the snowy forest. Arctic armour uses the usual shapes (check JEI).
65. **The Alpha Yeti**: `twilightforest:alpha_yeti_trophy` *(after: The Yeti Cave)*
   > What: the Alpha Yeti lives in a cave in the snowy forest. It drops its trophy, alpha yeti fur and ice bombs.
   > How: dodge the ice it throws and the icicles that fall from the cave roof.
   > Tip: carrying alpha yeti fur is what lets you bear the glacier's cold.
66. **Yeti Armour**: 1x any of `or(item(twilightforest:yeti_helmet)item(twilightforest:yeti_chestplate)item(twilightforest:yeti_leggings)item(twilightforest:yeti_boots))` *(after: The Alpha Yeti)*
   > What: armour of alpha yeti fur; every piece is crafted with Protection II already on it.
   > How: alpha yeti fur in the usual armour shapes (check JEI).
   > Tip: the Alpha Yeti's ice bombs freeze the ground where they land; throw them at a crowd.
67. **The Aurora Palace**: find structure `twilightforest:aurora_palace`; kill 1x `twilightforest:snow_queen` *(after: The Alpha Yeti)*
   > What: the Snow Queen's palace of packed ice and aurora blocks stands on the glacier.
   > Chemistry: real auroras are light from oxygen (green, red) and nitrogen (blue, purple) atoms excited by charged particles from the Sun.
   > How: the glacier opens after the Alpha Yeti. Climb the palace to the Queen's throne room.
   > Tip: break her ice shields first; she falls on you from above, so look up.
68. **Palace Armoury**: 1x any of `or(item(twilightforest:ice_sword)item(twilightforest:ice_bow)item(twilightforest:ender_bow))` *(after: The Aurora Palace)*
   > What: the Aurora Palace's chests hold the Ice Sword and Ice Bow, which slow what they hit, and the Ender Bow, which swaps you with your target.
   > How: loot the palace's rooms and caches on your way up.
   > Tip: the Ender Bow is a fine escape tool: shoot a mob far away and trade places.
69. **The Snow Queen**: `twilightforest:snow_queen_trophy` *(after: The Aurora Palace)*
   > What: the Snow Queen rules the Aurora Palace on the glacier.
   > How: climb the palace, then break her ice shields and avoid her drop attacks.
   > Tip: beating her finishes the snowy branch of the twilight.
70. **The Queen's Bows**: 1x any of `or(item(twilightforest:triple_bow)item(twilightforest:seeker_bow))` *(after: The Snow Queen)*
   > What: the Snow Queen drops one of two bows: the Tri-Bow fires three arrows at once, the Seeker Bow's arrows home in on targets.
   > How: beat the Snow Queen; one bow drops with her trophy.
   > Tip: the Seeker Bow is perfect against the flying Ur-Ghast's ghastlings.
71. **The Frozen Regions**: find biome `twilightforest:snowy_forest`; find biome `twilightforest:glacier` *(after: The Snow Queen)*
   > What: the cold half of the forest: the snowy forest and the glacier beyond it.
   > Chemistry: glacier ice is blue because water absorbs red light weakly; thick, bubble-free ice lets mostly blue through.
   > How: visit both. Warm armour stops the blizzard's chill.
72. **The Highlands**: find biome `twilightforest:highlands`; find biome `twilightforest:thornlands` *(after: The Ur-Ghast, The Snow Queen)*
   > Both branches beaten, the last barrier thins. You remember standing up here and looking down on all of it.
   > What: the highlands and the thornlands ring the forest's centre. Trolls and giants live up here.
   > How: with the Hydra, the Ur-Ghast and the Snow Queen beaten, the acid rain over the highlands stops. Step into the thornlands' edge too; their rain only stops once you hold the Lamp of Cinders.
   > Tip: giants are huge and hit hard; fight them from range.
73. **Trophy Wall**: `twilightforest:naga_trophy`; `twilightforest:lich_trophy`; `twilightforest:minoshroom_trophy`; `twilightforest:hydra_trophy`; `twilightforest:knight_phantom_trophy`; `twilightforest:ur_ghast_trophy`; `twilightforest:alpha_yeti_trophy`; `twilightforest:snow_queen_trophy` *(after: The Highlands)*
   > What: a trophy from every boss of the forest, in your inventory at the same time.
   > How: keep the trophies you already have. Bosses can be fought again in other structures of the same kind.
   > Tip: trophies make a fine wall in your base; place them with a right-click.
74. **Troll Caves**: find structure `twilightforest:troll_cave`; `twilightforest:lamp_of_cinders` *(after: The Highlands)*
   > What: caves under the highlands, full of trolls guarding vaults. The Lamp of Cinders hides in one.
   > How: find a troll cave in the highlands and loot its vaults. The lamp burns away the thorns that wall off the final plateau.
   > Tip: trolls throw rocks; fight them in the open, not in narrow tunnels.
75. **Magic Beans**: `twilightforest:magic_beans` *(after: Troll Caves)*
   > What: trolls drop magic beans. Planted on the glowing uberous soil beneath the clouds, they grow a beanstalk straight up.
   > Chemistry: real beans are legumes: bacteria in their root nodules fix N₂ from the air into ammonia (NH₃), the same job Haber-Bosch does with iron and heat.
   > How: kill trolls or loot their gardens (the gardens also hold uberous soil). Plant a bean on the glowing soil under the clouds.
76. **Giant's Tools**: 1x any of `or(item(twilightforest:giant_pickaxe)item(twilightforest:giant_sword))` *(after: Magic Beans)*
   > What: giant miners drop the Giant's Pickaxe, which breaks the oversized blocks of the cloud world; armoured giants drop the Giant's Sword.
   > How: climb the beanstalk to the clouds and beat a giant. Both can also be crafted from giant cobblestone and giant logs (check JEI).
   > Tip: giant blocks break down into normal ones: one giant log crafts into 64 oak logs.
77. **The Final Plateau**: find biome `twilightforest:final_plateau` *(after: Troll Caves)*
   > What: the highest ground in the forest, walled in by thorns and acid rain.
   > How: carry the Lamp of Cinders. Use it on the thorns to burn a path through the thornlands, then climb onto the plateau.
   > Tip: burnt thorns crumble away; don't stand in living ones, they hurt.
78. **The Unfinished Castle**: find structure `twilightforest:final_castle` *(after: The Final Plateau)*
   > A castle on the final plateau, waiting for a ruler. You remember planning to come back for it.
   > What: the Final Castle stands on the final plateau. Its last boss was never finished by the mod's authors, so this is the end of the road.
   > How: cross the plateau and step inside the castle.
   > Tip: the castle is still worth exploring for its architecture and its force-field doors.
79. **Castle Stone**: 16x `twilightforest:castle_brick` *(after: The Unfinished Castle)*
   > What: the Final Castle's pale brick, for building your own keep.
   > How: mine castle brick from the castle walls. Its cracked, mossy, worn and pillar variants are in JEI.
   > Tip: a stonecutter makes the stairs and pillars without waste.

### Deeper and Darker

*The Otherside: a silent world of sculk beneath the Ancient City.*

1. **Sculk Stone and Gloomslate**: 32x `deeperdarker:sculk_stone`; 32x `deeperdarker:gloomslate` *(after: The Otherside)*
   > You remember this silence. Every footstep here used to be yours.
   > What: the Otherside's two stones. Both carry their own versions of the usual ores: coal, iron, copper, gold, redstone, lapis, emerald and diamond.
   > Chemistry: real slate is a metamorphic rock: shale (mostly clay minerals) squeezed and heated until its grains line up, so it splits into flat sheets.
   > How: mine it and smelt the cobbled forms back into smooth stone.
   > Tip: these ores go through the Create crusher too, with the same pack caps as Overworld ore.
2. **Resonarium**: kill 5x `deeperdarker:sludge`; 8x `deeperdarker:resonarium` *(after: Sculk Stone and Gloomslate)*
   > What: resonarium is a vibration-tuned material that drops from sludges, oozing creatures of the Otherside.
   > How: hunt sludges in the Otherside's caves and collect the resonarium they drop.
   > Tip: a Looting sword raises the drop count.
3. **Resonarium Plate**: 2x `deeperdarker:resonarium_plate` *(after: Resonarium)*
   > What: the plate that upgrades diamond gear to resonarium.
   > Chemistry: turtle and armadillo scutes are built on keratin, the tough protein of hair, horn and hooves; its sulfur bridges (cysteine disulfides) make it hard.
   > How: four resonarium and four scutes (turtle or armadillo, any mix), shapeless.
4. **Resonarium Template**: `deeperdarker:resonarium_upgrade_smithing_template` *(after: Resonarium Plate)*
   > What: the smithing template that turns diamond gear into resonarium gear.
   > How: loot it from Ancient City chests, Trial Chamber reward chests, stronghold corridors or the Ancient Temple's top chest.
   > Tip: copy it with 7 diamonds and a resonarium (makes 2). Never use your last one.
5. **Resonarium Gear**: `deeperdarker:resonarium_pickaxe`; `deeperdarker:resonarium_chestplate` *(after: Resonarium Template)*
   > Stone that sings when struck. You remember tuning it to your own voice.
   > What: resonarium tools and armour: an upgrade over diamond that doesn't need netherite.
   > How: smithing table: resonarium template + diamond tool or armour piece + resonarium plate.
   > Tip: the rest of the set (axe, shovel, hoe, sword, helmet, leggings, boots) uses the same recipe.
6. **Warden Carapace**: 6x `deeperdarker:warden_carapace` *(after: The Warden)*
   > What: every Warden drops one to three carapace pieces (and its heart). The carapace is the core of reinforced echo shards.
   > How: kill Wardens, or loot Ancient City chests and the Ancient Temple.
   > Tip: one reinforced echo shard takes four carapace, four phantom membranes and an echo shard. Plan your Warden fights.
7. **Ancient Compass**: `deeperdarker:ancient_compass` *(after: Warden Carapace)*
   > What: a compass wrapped in Warden carapace. In the Otherside it points toward the nearest Ancient Temple.
   > Chemistry: an ordinary compass needle is magnetised iron or steel lining up with Earth's magnetic field.
   > How: a compass with four Warden carapace around it.
8. **Warden Template**: `deeperdarker:warden_upgrade_smithing_template` *(after: Warden Carapace)*
   > What: the template that upgrades netherite gear to Warden gear.
   > How: loot it from Ancient City chests (often) or the Ancient Temple's secret chest.
   > Tip: copy it with 7 diamonds and a sculk block (makes 2).
9. **Warden Tools**: `deeperdarker:warden_sword`; `deeperdarker:warden_pickaxe` *(after: Warden Template)*
   > What: the Otherside's best tools and weapons.
   > How: smithing table: Warden template + netherite tool + reinforced echo shard (see Echoes of the Deep on the Other Worlds page).
   > Tip: echo shards also come from the Demigod Token shop (check JEI) if Ancient City chests run dry.
10. **Cover Me with Sculk**: `deeperdarker:warden_helmet`; `deeperdarker:warden_leggings`; `deeperdarker:warden_boots` *(after: Warden Tools)*
   > The guardian's shell, worn by the one it guarded against. You remember that joke.
   > What: helmet, leggings and boots of Warden armour, to go with the chestplate.
   > How: the same smithing recipe on netherite armour: one template and one reinforced echo shard per piece.
   > Tip: that's three more Wardens' worth of carapace, at least.
11. **Sculk Transmitter**: `deeperdarker:sculk_transmitter` *(after: The Ancient Temple)*
   > What: a handheld sculk link. Link it to a container, and it opens that container from a distance.
   > How: found in the Ancient Temple's secret chest. Use it on a chest to link it, then use it anywhere to open that chest.
   > Tip: dye it to tell several apart; each colour is its own item.
12. **Soul Materials**: 8x `deeperdarker:soul_dust`; 2x `deeperdarker:soul_crystal`; 4x `deeperdarker:sculk_bone` *(after: Sculk Stone and Gloomslate)*
   > What: the Otherside's crafting drops: soul dust (snappers, leeches), soul crystals (stalkers) and sculk bones (shattered).
   > How: hunt in the Otherside; the Ancient Temple's chests hold some of each too.
   > Tip: the Monster Hunting page has quests for these mobs, so do both at once.
13. **Sonorous Staff**: `deeperdarker:sonorous_staff` *(after: Soul Materials)*
   > What: a staff that charges and releases a sonic boom, the Warden's own attack.
   > Chemistry: sound is a pressure wave: molecules bunch up and spread apart. A shock wave is a pressure jump so steep it travels faster than sound itself.
   > How: a Heart of the Deep, two soul crystals and two sculk bones (check JEI). Repair it with soul crystals.
   > Tip: the Volume and Reverberation enchantments raise its damage and range.
14. **Soundproof Glass**: 8x `deeperdarker:soundproof_glass` *(after: Soul Materials)*
   > What: glass that blocks vibrations, so sculk sensors (and Wardens) can't hear you through it.
   > Chemistry: sound needs a medium. Damping materials turn the wave's energy into heat; a vacuum stops it completely.
   > How: four soul dust around one glass makes two.
15. **Echo Trees**: 16x `deeperdarker:echo_log`; `deeperdarker:echo_sapling` *(after: Sculk Stone and Gloomslate)*
   > What: pale echo trees grow on echo soil in the echoing forest. Their wood is found nowhere else.
   > Chemistry: all wood is the same chemistry: cellulose fibres glued by lignin, an aromatic polymer that makes trees rigid.
   > How: cut a few trees; leaves drop saplings.
16. **Blooming Caverns**: 16x `deeperdarker:blooming_stem`; 8x `deeperdarker:bloom_berries` *(after: Sculk Stone and Gloomslate)*
   > What: the blooming caverns grow tall blooming stems (a second Otherside wood) and edible bloom berries.
   > How: cut the stems and pick the berries in the blooming caverns.
   > Tip: bloom berries are the easiest food to find down here.
17. **Light in the Dark**: 4x `deeperdarker:gloomslate_light` *(after: Sculk Stone and Gloomslate)*
   > What: sculk gleam is the Otherside's glowing block; set in gloomslate it makes a tidy lamp.
   > Chemistry: a glow stick makes cold light by chemiluminescence: hydrogen peroxide oxidises an oxalate ester, and the energy excites a dye that emits light.
   > How: one sculk gleam with four gloomslate around it.
18. **Crystallized Amber**: `deeperdarker:crystallized_amber` *(after: Sculk Stone and Gloomslate)*
   > What: amber blocks in the Otherside hold an item, or a live sculk leech. Break one to find out which.
   > Chemistry: real amber is fossilised tree resin: terpenes that slowly polymerised over millions of years, sometimes around an insect.
   > How: look in the Otherside's caves; the tooltip shows what's inside.
   > Tip: a leech pops out when you break the block. Be ready.
19. **Angler Fish**: kill 3x `deeperdarker:angler_fish`; 3x `deeperdarker:cooked_angler_fish` *(after: Sculk Stone and Gloomslate)*
   > What: the Otherside's waters hide angler fish, a good food once cooked.
   > Chemistry: a real anglerfish's lure glows because of symbiotic bacteria living in it, which produce light by bioluminescence.
   > How: catch or kill them in Otherside water, then smelt or campfire-cook.
20. **Leeches and Worms**: kill 5x `deeperdarker:sculk_leech`; kill 1x `deeperdarker:shriek_worm` *(after: Sculk Stone and Gloomslate)*
   > What: sculk leeches latch on in swarms; the shriek worm bursts from the ground.
   > How: leeches hide in crystallized amber and in caves; shriek worms burst out of the Otherside's ground.
   > Tip: a sweeping sword handles a leech swarm best.
21. **Gloomsherds**: 3x any of `or(item(deeperdarker:gloomsherd)item(deeperdarker:brittle_gloomsherd)item(deeperdarker:dark_heart_gloomsherd)item(deeperdarker:listener_gloomsherd)item(deeperdarker:snapper_gloomsherd)item(deeperdarker:temple_gloomsherd)item(deeperdarker:transmission_gloomsherd)item(deeperdarker:ward_gloomsherd)item(deeperdarker:wayfinder_gloomsherd))` *(after: Sculk Stone and Gloomslate)*
   > What: pottery sherds from the Otherside's makers. They decorate gloomslate pots, like vanilla sherds on decorated pots.
   > Chemistry: firing clay drives out water and partly melts its silicates into glass, which is why fired pottery survives for millennia.
   > How: collect any three. Amber blocks and the strange pots of the Otherside carry them.
22. **That Pot Moved**: kill 1x `deeperdarker:anger_pot` *(after: Sculk Stone and Gloomslate)*
   > What: some pots in the Otherside are alive: anger, sorrow and fear pots. You broke an angry one.
   > Tip: they drop gloomsherds, so a living pot is worth chasing.
23. **Grime Bricks**: 8x `deeperdarker:grime_brick` *(after: Sculk Stone and Gloomslate)*
   > What: grime balls fire into grime bricks, a building block made from the Otherside's mud.
   > Chemistry: brick-making is old chemistry: heating clay above about 900 °C sinters its particles together into a hard ceramic.
   > How: smelt grime balls (Ancient Temple chests hold them). Four grime balls also make a block of sculk grime.

### Space

*The Moon, the planets and the air you bring with you.*

1. **Desh on the Moon**: 8x `ad_astra:raw_desh` *(after: One Small Step)*
   > You remember the Moon from above. Now you're standing on it.
   > What: desh ore is found only on the Moon. Desh blocks, engines and tanks build the Tier 2 rocket.
   > Chemistry: the Moon has no atmosphere and its days swing from about +120 °C to −170 °C, so bring oxygen.
   > How: mine desh ore on the Moon and process it like any ore.
2. **Mars**: visit `ad_astra:mars`; `ad_astra:tier_2_rocket` *(after: Desh on the Moon)*
   > What: Mars holds ostrum ore, the metal for the Tier 3 rocket.
   > Chemistry: Mars is red because its dust is rich in iron(III) oxide, Fe₂O₃: rust. Its thin air is about 95% CO₂.
   > How: build the Tier 2 rocket from desh at the NASA Workbench, fuel it with diesel and launch.
3. **Ostrum**: 8x `ad_astra:raw_ostrum` *(after: Mars)*
   > What: ostrum ore is found only on Mars. It builds the Tier 3 rocket.
   > How: mine ostrum ore on Mars and process it.
   > Tip: grab some extra for the Tier 3 rocket's engine and tank.
4. **Heat Shielding**: `ad_astra:netherite_space_suit` *(after: Ostrum)*
   > What: Venus and Mercury are hot enough to kill in a normal space suit. The netherite space suit handles the heat.
   > How: check JEI for the netherite suit pieces. Fill it with oxygen at the Oxygen Loader.
   > Tip: the jet suit (later, check JEI) also handles the heat, and flies.
5. **Venus**: visit `ad_astra:venus`; `ad_astra:tier_3_rocket` *(after: Heat Shielding)*
   > What: Venus holds calorite ore, the metal for the Tier 4 rocket.
   > Chemistry: its atmosphere is about 96% CO₂ at roughly 92 times Earth's pressure, under clouds of sulfuric acid (H₂SO₄). A runaway greenhouse keeps it near 464 °C.
   > How: Tier 3 rocket from ostrum, plus your netherite space suit.
6. **Calorite**: 8x `ad_astra:raw_calorite` *(after: Venus)*
   > What: calorite ore is found on Venus. It builds the Tier 4 rocket, the only one that reaches another star.
   > How: mine calorite ore in the Venusian stone and process it like any ore.
   > Tip: Venus is hot and its rain is acid; keep the netherite suit on the whole time.
7. **Alien Metals**: 8x `ad_astra:desh_ingot`; 4x `ad_astra:ostrum_ingot`; 4x `ad_astra:calorite_ingot` *(after: Calorite)*
   > What: one metal from each world: desh (Moon), ostrum (Mars) and calorite (Venus). Each builds the next rocket tier.
   > How: process the raw ores; MI and Mekanism ore lines work on them too (check JEI).
   > Tip: the Dissolver can tell you what these metals break down into.
8. **Glacio**: visit `ad_astra:glacio`; `ad_astra:tier_4_rocket` *(after: Alien Metals)*
   > You remember stars that were yours to name. This one you'll have to visit first.
   > What: Glacio orbits Proxima Centauri, the nearest star to the Sun, about 4.2 light-years away. Unlike the others, it has breathable air.
   > How: build the Tier 4 rocket from calorite and launch.
9. **A Spare Breath**: `ad_astra:gas_tank` *(after: Breathe)*
   > What: a gas tank holds oxygen you can carry, to refill the suit far from your Oxygen Loader.
   > Chemistry: a resting adult breathes in roughly 550 litres of O₂ a day; your suit's tank is all that stands between you and vacuum.
   > How: iron plates and an iron rod (check JEI). Fill it in the Oxygen Loader like the suit.
   > Tip: the large gas tank holds much more and is a part in later machines.
10. **The Oxygen Can**: `ad_astra_giselle_addon:oxygen_can` *(after: A Spare Breath)*
   > What: the Giselle Addon's oxygen can stores oxygen and tops up the oxygen gear you're carrying.
   > How: iron plates around an iron rod (check JEI). Fill it with oxygen like a tank.
   > Tip: the addon also links Ad Astra to Mekanism and other mods; search its name in JEI to see what else it adds.
11. **Breathable Bases**: `ad_astra:oxygen_distributor` *(after: Mars)*
   > What: the Oxygen Distributor fills a sealed room with breathable air, so you can work without a helmet.
   > Chemistry: it makes oxygen from water with FE, the same idea as your Electrolytic Separator: 2H₂O → 2H₂ + O₂.
   > How: check JEI. Seal the room completely; any gap stops it working.
12. **Is There Air?**: `ad_astra:oxygen_sensor` *(after: Breathable Bases)*
   > What: the Oxygen Sensor gives a redstone signal depending on whether the space around it has oxygen.
   > How: check JEI for the recipe, place it inside your sealed base and wire it to a lamp or alarm.
   > Tip: a light that goes out when the air does is the cheapest life support alarm there is.
13. **Water for Oxygen**: `ad_astra:water_pump` *(after: A Spare Breath, Mekanism Gases)*
   > What: Ad Astra's powered water pump feeds water into your pipes, so a base on the Moon can make its own oxygen.
   > Chemistry: electrolysis splits water: 2H₂O → 2H₂ + O₂. Every 2 molecules of water give 1 of oxygen and 2 of hydrogen, which you can burn or store.
   > How: steel and desh plates, a gas tank and a hopper (check JEI). Pipe its water into a Mekanism Electrolytic Separator and the O₂ into an Oxygen Loader.
   > Tip: Ad Astra's ice shards are frozen water; the Moon and Mars both have ice shard ore.
14. **Netherite Oxygen Can**: `ad_astra_giselle_addon:netherite_oxygen_can` *(after: The Oxygen Can, Ostrum)*
   > What: the upgraded oxygen can holds much more oxygen than the iron one.
   > How: an oxygen can, a netherite ingot and ostrum plates in the crafting grid (check JEI). Its oxygen carries over.
   > Tip: on Venus every trip back to base costs time; carry more air instead.
15. **An Automated Launch Site**: `ad_astra_giselle_addon:fuel_loader`; `ad_astra_giselle_addon:rocket_sensor` *(after: One Small Step)*
   > What: the Fuel Loader takes piped fuel and fills any rocket or rover within a few blocks; the Rocket Sensor reports a nearby rocket's state to a comparator.
   > How: both are steel ingots and iron plates around a bucket or an observer (check JEI). Pipe diesel into the loader.
   > Tip: no more filling rockets by hand from buckets.
16. **Earth Gravity**: `ad_astra:gravity_normalizer` *(after: Desh on the Moon)*
   > What: the Gravity Normalizer gives an area Earth-like gravity, so your jumps behave in your Moon base.
   > Chemistry: the Moon's gravity is about 1/6 of Earth's, Mars about 3/8; you weigh less because they have less mass.
   > How: etrionic capacitors, a diamond block and desh plates (check JEI).
17. **The Rover**: `ad_astra:tier_1_rover` *(after: Desh on the Moon)*
   > What: a fuelled buggy for crossing big, airless plains quickly.
