   > How: desh engine, desh blocks and plates, a large gas tank, a radio and wheels (check JEI). Fuel it with diesel.
   > Tip: low gravity makes it bounce; slow down on hills.
18. **The Moon Is Cheese**: 4x `ad_astra:cheese` *(after: Desh on the Moon)*
   > What: Ad Astra hides cheese ore on the Moon.
   > Chemistry: the real Moon is mostly silicate rock: oxygen, silicon, magnesium, iron, calcium and aluminum.
   > How: mine moon cheese ore. Eat responsibly.
19. **Lunarians**: find structure `ad_astra:lunarian_village` *(after: One Small Step)*
   > What: Lunarians are the Moon's villagers. Their villages stand on the lunar wastelands, with towers nearby.
   > How: explore the Moon until you find one, then walk into it. They trade like villagers.
   > Tip: the Explorer's Compass works on other planets too.
20. **Moon Dungeon**: find structure `ad_astra:moon_dungeon` *(after: One Small Step)*
   > What: a dungeon hidden under the lunar wastelands, with loot and hostile guards.
   > How: the Explorer's Compass can find one. Step inside.
   > Tip: bring a spare gas tank: dungeons take longer than you think.
21. **Temples of Mars**: find structure `ad_astra:mars_temple`; find biome `ad_astra:martian_canyon_creek` *(after: Mars)*
   > What: temples stand in the Martian wastelands and canyon creeks.
   > Chemistry: Mars's canyons were carved by liquid water long ago; today its low pressure makes surface water boil or freeze almost at once.
   > How: visit a canyon creek and find a temple (the Explorer's Compass helps).
22. **The Polar Caps**: find biome `ad_astra:martian_polar_caps`; 8x `ad_astra:ice_shard` *(after: Temples of Mars)*
   > What: Mars's polar caps are frozen; ice shard ore gives ice shards.
   > Chemistry: the real caps are water ice with a seasonal layer of dry ice (solid CO₂), which turns straight to gas in spring (sublimation).
   > How: visit the polar caps biome and mine ice shard ore (it's on the Moon too).
23. **The Pygros**: find structure `ad_astra:pygro_village` *(after: Venus)*
   > What: Pygros are piglin-like natives of Venus. Their villages and towers stand in the Venus wastelands.
   > How: find a village (the Explorer's Compass helps) and step inside. Wear gold, just in case.
   > Tip: Pygro brutes guard the towers; they hit hard.
24. **Infernal Barrens**: find biome `ad_astra:infernal_venus_barrens` *(after: Venus)*
   > What: the harshest of the Venusian biomes.
   > Chemistry: Venus is hotter than Mercury despite being twice as far from the Sun: its thick CO₂ atmosphere traps heat.
   > How: visit it in the netherite space suit. Fire resistance helps.
25. **Mercury**: visit `ad_astra:mercury` *(after: Venus)*
   > What: a scorched, airless world reachable with the Tier 3 rocket.
   > Chemistry: with almost no atmosphere, Mercury can't hold heat: about 430 °C by day and −180 °C at night.
   > How: Tier 3 rocket plus the netherite space suit.
26. **Mercury Deltas**: find biome `ad_astra:mercury_deltas` *(after: Mercury)*
   > What: Mercury's deltas, a dark landscape of scorched stone.
   > Chemistry: despite the name, the planet has no mercury metal; the element was named after the same god for its quick, flowing liquid.
   > How: land on Mercury and walk into the deltas.
27. **Peaks of Glacio**: find biome `ad_astra:glacio_ice_peaks`; find biome `ad_astra:glacio_snowy_barrens` *(after: Glacio)*
   > Air you didn't bring, under a sun that isn't yours. You remember this feeling: arriving.
   > What: Glacio's frozen peaks and snowy barrens, with its own trees and woolly rams.
   > How: visit both biomes. You can take your helmet off here.
   > Tip: Glacio has ordinary ores too (coal, iron, copper, lapis) in its own stone.
28. **In Orbit**: visit `ad_astra:moon_orbit`; visit `ad_astra:mars_orbit` *(after: Mars)*
   > What: every planet has an orbit dimension above it, where space stations are built.
   > Chemistry: in orbit you're falling around the planet all the time, which is why everything floats.
   > How: choose the orbit on the rocket's planet screen instead of the surface.

### Exploration

*Go and see what the world has made without you.*

1. **Two Compasses**: `naturescompass:naturescompass`; `explorerscompass:explorerscompass` *(after: Iron)*
   > You remember knowing every corner of this world by heart. For now, a compass will do.
   > What: Nature's Compass points to any biome; Explorer's Compass points to any structure. Every quest on this page gets easier with them.
   > How: each is a compass ringed with a few common blocks (check JEI). Right-click to pick a target, then follow the needle.
   > Tip: the compasses work in other dimensions too: the Nether, the Undergarden, the Twilight Forest and even other planets.
2. **A Plains Village**: find structure `minecraft:village_plains` *(after: Iron)*
   > What: villagers trade emeralds for crops, tools and enchanted books, and their beds and fields are free for the taking (politely).
   > How: plains villages generate in plains and a few grassy modded biomes. Walk into one.
   > Tip: a cartographer villager sells maps to ocean monuments and woodland mansions.
3. **Towns and Towers**: checkmark *(after: A Swamp Village)*
   > What: Towns and Towers adds new village styles and pillager outposts across the biomes.
   > How: find a village or outpost you haven't seen before. Click the checkmark when done.
   > Tip: villages often have a waystone.
4. **A Forest Village**: find structure `towns_and_towers:village_forest` *(after: A Desert Village)*
   > What: Towns and Towers builds a village style for each biome. The forest village is timber and cobblestone.
   > How: find one in a forest biome (Explorer's Compass: Towns and Towers) and walk in.
   > Tip: each biome style has its own loot chests.
5. **A Meadow Village**: find structure `towns_and_towers:village_meadow` *(after: A Forest Village)*
   > What: high meadows below the peaks get their own village style.
   > How: find a meadow biome on a mountainside and the village in it.
   > Tip: meadows are where bees and wildflowers are thickest.
6. **A Snowy Taiga Village**: find structure `towns_and_towers:village_snowy_taiga` *(after: A Meadow Village)*
   > What: a village built for the cold, in the snowy taiga.
   > How: find a snowy taiga and the village in it.
   > Tip: bring leather boots for powder snow.
7. **A Swamp Village**: find structure `towns_and_towers:village_swamp` *(after: A Snowy Taiga Village)*
   > What: vanilla never put villages in swamps. Towns and Towers does.
   > How: find a swamp and the village in it.
   > Tip: witches love swamps too; keep a milk bucket handy.
8. **A Desert Village**: find structure `minecraft:village_desert` *(after: A Pillager Outpost)*
   > What: a sandstone village in the desert.
   > Chemistry: sandstone is sand grains (mostly quartz, SiO₂) cemented by minerals like calcite or iron oxides that precipitated from groundwater.
   > How: find a desert village (Explorer's Compass) and walk in.
9. **A Pillager Outpost**: find structure `towns_and_towers:pillager_outpost_forest` *(after: A Plains Village)*
   > What: pillagers build watchtowers. Towns and Towers gives each biome its own design.
   > How: find a forest outpost and walk in. Expect crossbow fire.
   > Tip: killing a pillager captain gives Bad Omen; in 1.21 you drink the ominous bottle it drops to start a raid.
10. **A Woodland Mansion**: find structure `minecraft:mansion` *(after: A Pillager Outpost)*
   > What: huge dark-oak mansions, very rare, deep in dark forests. Vindicators and evokers live inside.
   > How: a cartographer's woodland explorer map or the Explorer's Compass. Step inside.
   > Tip: evokers drop totems of undying; vindicators hit very hard.
11. **Better Dungeons**: checkmark *(after: Iron)*
   > What: YUNG's Better Dungeons adds catacombs, fortresses and spider caves underground.
   > How: find one (the Explorer's Compass lists them), clear it and loot it. Click the checkmark when done.
   > Tip: some dungeon chests hold Relics and Artifacts items.
12. **Zombie Catacombs**: find structure `betterdungeons:zombie_dungeon` *(after: Iron)*
   > What: YUNG's zombie dungeon: a tomb with spawners, coffins and loot.
   > How: find it with the Explorer's Compass (Better Dungeons) and step inside.
   > Tip: break or torch the spawners first; then loot at your leisure.
13. **Skeleton Fortress**: find structure `betterdungeons:skeleton_dungeon` *(after: Zombie Catacombs)*
   > What: YUNG's skeleton dungeon is a sprawling underground fort.
   > How: find it and step inside. Bring a shield.
   > Tip: torches on every spawner; skeletons will pick you off from the dark otherwise.
14. **Spider Caves**: find structure `betterdungeons:spider_dungeon` *(after: Skeleton Fortress)*
   > What: a natural-looking cave full of webs, cave spiders and their spawners.
   > Chemistry: real spider silk is a protein (spidroin) with crystalline β-sheets that make it, weight for weight, stronger than steel.
   > How: find one and step inside. A sword or shears cut webs fast.
   > Tip: cave spiders poison; carry milk.
15. **A Better Stronghold**: find structure `betterstrongholds:stronghold` *(after: Spider Caves)*
   > What: YUNG's Better Strongholds replaces the vanilla stronghold with a much larger, trapped maze. The End portal room is inside.
   > How: throw eyes of ender, or use the Explorer's Compass. Step inside.
   > Tip: mark your path with torches on one side; the halls all look alike.
16. **Better Mineshafts**: checkmark *(after: Iron)*
   > What: YUNG's Better Mineshafts are longer, with ore deposits, carts and loot.
   > How: explore one and loot a chest. Click the checkmark when done.
   > Tip: look for minecarts with chests along the tracks.
17. **An Old Mineshaft**: find structure `bettermineshafts:mineshaft_oak` *(after: Iron)*
   > What: YUNG's mineshafts come in styles for each biome. The oak style is the common one.
   > How: find one under a forest or plains (Explorer's Compass) and walk in.
   > Tip: the rails and planks are free building material.
18. **Mineshafts of the Caves**: find structure `bettermineshafts:mineshaft_dripstone`; find structure `bettermineshafts:mineshaft_lush` *(after: An Old Mineshaft)*
   > What: two mineshafts built into cave biomes: one among dripstone, one among moss and glow berries.
   > Chemistry: dripstone is calcium carbonate: water carrying dissolved Ca(HCO₃)₂ drips, loses CO₂, and leaves CaCO₃ behind, a millimetre at a time.
   > How: find a mineshaft under dripstone caves and one under lush caves.
19. **Trail Ruins**: find structure `minecraft:trail_ruins`; `minecraft:brush` *(after: Iron)*
   > What: half-buried ruins of an old people. Suspicious gravel hides pottery sherds and armour trims.
   > How: find the ruins (Explorer's Compass) and bring a brush (feather, copper ingot, stick).
   > Tip: brush slowly; breaking suspicious blocks destroys the loot.
20. **Trial Chambers**: find structure `minecraft:trial_chambers`; `minecraft:trial_key` *(after: A Better Stronghold)*
   > What: copper and tuff vaults deep underground. Trial spawners give keys; keys open vaults.
   > Chemistry: the halls show copper's patina: Cu weathers to green basic copper carbonate, Cu₂CO₃(OH)₂, unless you wax it.
   > How: find a chamber, beat a trial spawner's wave and take the key.
   > Tip: breezes deflect arrows; fight them up close.
21. **Temples**: find structure `minecraft:desert_pyramid`; find structure `minecraft:jungle_pyramid` *(after: Iron)*
   > What: a desert pyramid and a jungle temple, each with treasure and a trap.
   > Chemistry: the desert trap is TNT: trinitrotoluene, C₇H₅N₃O₆, which carries its oxygen inside the molecule.
   > How: find one of each. Don't stand on the pressure plate in the desert.
22. **Odd Little Houses**: find structure `minecraft:swamp_hut`; find structure `minecraft:igloo` *(after: Iron)*
   > What: a witch's hut in the swamp and an igloo on the snow. Some igloos hide a basement.
   > How: find one of each and step inside.
   > Tip: the igloo basement has a brewing stand and a golden apple for curing a zombie villager.
23. **A Ruined Portal**: find structure `minecraft:ruined_portal` *(after: Iron)*
   > What: broken Nether portals, with crying obsidian and a chest of gold.
   > Chemistry: obsidian forms when lava (molten silicate) cools so fast it can't crystallise: it's volcanic glass.
   > How: find one and stand inside it. Fill the missing blocks to repair the portal.
24. **A Shipwreck**: find structure `minecraft:shipwreck` *(after: Iron)*
   > What: sunken ships with supply, treasure and map chests. The map leads to buried treasure.
   > How: find a wreck (Explorer's Compass) and swim inside.
   > Tip: a potion of water breathing or a turtle shell helmet makes this easy.
25. **Buried Treasure**: find structure `minecraft:buried_treasure`; `minecraft:heart_of_the_sea` *(after: A Shipwreck)*
   > What: buried chests on beaches, each with a Heart of the Sea for a conduit.
   > How: follow a shipwreck's treasure map to the X and dig down.
   > Tip: the chest is usually right under the X, a few blocks down.
26. **Ocean Ruins**: find structure `minecraft:ocean_ruin_warm`; find structure `minecraft:ocean_ruin_cold` *(after: A Shipwreck)*
   > What: ruins on the sea floor: sandstone ones in warm water, stone bricks in cold.
   > Chemistry: warm-water coral reefs are calcium carbonate skeletons; more dissolved CO₂ makes seawater more acidic and dissolves them.
   > How: find one of each. Drowned guard them.
27. **Wreckage**: find structure `towns_and_towers:wreckage_ocean` *(after: A Shipwreck)*
   > What: Towns and Towers scatters its own wreckage across the oceans.
   > How: find it with the Explorer's Compass (Towns and Towers) and swim in.
   > Tip: boats are faster than swimming between ocean structures.
28. **An Ocean Monument**: find structure `minecraft:monument`; 4x `minecraft:wet_sponge` *(after: Buried Treasure)*
   > What: a prismarine temple guarded by guardians and three elder guardians. Its sponge rooms and gold block vault are the prize.
   > Chemistry: a sponge holds water by capillary action in its pores; dry it in a furnace to drive the water off.
   > How: find it with a cartographer's map or the Explorer's Compass. Bring water breathing and Respiration.
   > Tip: elder guardians give Mining Fatigue; kill all three to lift it (see the hunting page).
29. **A Nether Fortress**: find structure `minecraft:fortress` *(after: Into the Fire)*
   > What: dark nether-brick bridges and halls. Blazes give blaze rods; wither skeletons drop skulls for the Wither.
   > How: fortresses run in long lines across the Nether; the Explorer's Compass finds them quickly.
   > Tip: nether wart grows in the fortress stairwells; take some for brewing.
30. **A Bastion Remnant**: find structure `minecraft:bastion_remnant` *(after: A Nether Fortress)*
   > What: blackstone castles full of piglins and gold. Some hide netherite upgrade templates and ancient debris.
   > Chemistry: gold doesn't tarnish because it's the least reactive metal: it won't oxidise in air, even at high temperatures.
   > How: wear one piece of gold armour so piglins stay calm, then step inside.
   > Tip: opening chests angers piglins anyway; piglin brutes ignore gold entirely.
31. **Nether Fossils**: find structure `minecraft:nether_fossil` *(after: A Nether Fortress)*
   > What: giant bone fossils in the soul sand valleys.
   > Chemistry: bone is mostly hydroxyapatite, Ca₅(PO₄)₃(OH): a calcium phosphate mineral, the same family as phosphate rock for fertiliser.
   > How: find a soul sand valley and walk into one of the bone arches.
32. **A Nether Dungeon**: find structure `betterdungeons:small_nether_dungeon` *(after: A Nether Fortress)*
   > What: Better Dungeons adds small dungeons to the Nether too, with spawners and loot.
   > How: find one with the Explorer's Compass and step inside.
   > Tip: fire resistance first; everything here burns.
33. **Ruins in the Fire**: find structure `minecraft:ruined_portal_nether` *(after: A Bastion Remnant)*
   > What: ruined portals turn up in the Nether as well, with the same gold chest.
   > How: find one in the Nether and stand inside it.
   > Tip: crying obsidian makes respawn anchors, which let you respawn in the Nether.
34. **Erupting Inferno**: find biome `biomesoplenty:erupting_inferno` *(after: A Bastion Remnant)*
   > What: a Nether biome of lava, ash and erupting vents.
   > Chemistry: volcanic gas is mostly water vapour, CO₂ and SO₂; the SO₂ is what makes volcanic air sting.
   > How: Nature's Compass finds it. Fire resistance is a must.
35. **Cliffs of Chalk**: find biome `regions_unexplored:chalk_cliffs`; find biome `regions_unexplored:towering_cliffs` *(after: Iron)*
   > What: two Regions Unexplored cliff biomes: white chalk and towering stone.
   > Chemistry: chalk is calcium carbonate (CaCO₃) made of the shells of tiny sea plankton. Heat it and it gives quicklime: CaCO₃ → CaO + CO₂.
   > How: Nature's Compass finds each one. Visit both.
36. **Hot Ground**: find biome `biomesoplenty:hot_springs`; find biome `biomesoplenty:volcanic_plains` *(after: Cliffs of Chalk)*
   > What: two Biomes O' Plenty biomes warmed from below.
   > Chemistry: hot springs carry dissolved minerals; when the water cools at the surface, silica and carbonates precipitate as terraces.
   > How: visit both (Nature's Compass).
37. **Wetlands**: find biome `biomesoplenty:bog`; find biome `biomesoplenty:marsh`; find biome `regions_unexplored:fen` *(after: Hot Ground)*
   > What: three kinds of wetland from the two biome mods.
   > Chemistry: waterlogged peat has almost no oxygen, so it barely rots; microbes there make methane (CH₄), the original "marsh gas".
   > How: visit all three. Bring a boat.
38. **Caves That Glow**: find biome `regions_unexplored:redstone_caves`; find biome `regions_unexplored:bioshroom_caves`; find biome `biomesoplenty:glowing_grotto` *(after: Wetlands)*
   > What: cave biomes lit by redstone, giant glowing mushrooms and glowing fungi.
   > Chemistry: real glowing fungi use the same chemistry as fireflies: luciferin oxidised by O₂ with the help of an enzyme, luciferase.
   > How: these are underground; Nature's Compass gives the location, then dig down.
39. **Useful Forests**: find biome `biomesoplenty:maple_woods`; find biome `regions_unexplored:eucalyptus_forest`; find biome `biomesoplenty:orchard` *(after: Cliffs of Chalk)*
   > What: three forests whose real trees give us chemicals.
   > Chemistry: maple sap is about 2% sucrose (C₁₂H₂₂O₁₁), boiled down 40:1 into syrup. Eucalyptus oil is mostly eucalyptol (C₁₀H₁₈O).
   > How: visit all three.
40. **Dry Country**: find biome `regions_unexplored:joshua_desert`; find biome `regions_unexplored:saguaro_desert`; find biome `regions_unexplored:outback` *(after: Hot Ground)*
   > What: Joshua trees, saguaro cacti and the red outback.
   > Chemistry: red desert soil is coloured by a thin coat of iron(III) oxide, Fe₂O₃, on every grain: the same rust that colours Mars.
   > How: visit all three. Carry water.
41. **Cold Places**: find biome `biomesoplenty:tundra`; find biome `regions_unexplored:icy_heights`; find biome `biomesoplenty:snowblossom_grove` *(after: Wetlands)*
   > What: three cold biomes from the biome mods.
   > Chemistry: ice floats because water expands about 9% when it freezes: hydrogen bonds lock the molecules into an open hexagonal lattice.
   > How: visit all three. Warm clothes not required, but recommended.
42. **Colour Fields**: find biome `regions_unexplored:poppy_fields`; find biome `biomesoplenty:jacaranda_glade`; find biome `regions_unexplored:wisteria_grove` *(after: Caves That Glow)*
   > What: biomes full of flowers: poppies, jacarandas and wisteria.
   > Chemistry: most red, purple and blue petals owe their colour to anthocyanins, pigments that change colour with pH (red in acid, blue in base).
   > How: visit all three. Collect the flowers for dyes.
43. **Strange Heights**: find biome `regions_unexplored:spires`; find biome `biomesoplenty:crag` *(after: Caves That Glow)*
   > What: tall rock spires and broken crags: two of the most dramatic landscapes in the pack.
   > Chemistry: rock spires are what's left when softer rock around them is weathered away by water, frost and slow acid rain (H₂CO₃ from dissolved CO₂).
   > How: visit both. Bring a water bucket for the way down.
44. **Dead Lands**: find biome `biomesoplenty:wasteland`; find biome `biomesoplenty:dead_forest` *(after: Caves That Glow)*
   > What: cracked wasteland and grey dead forest.
   > Chemistry: soil dies when it loses organic matter and nitrogen; your Haber-Bosch ammonia (N₂ + 3H₂ → 2NH₃) is how real farms put nitrogen back.
   > How: visit both. A demigod might one day make them green again.
45. **The Origin Valley**: find biome `biomesoplenty:origin_valley` *(after: Strange Heights)*
   > Blocky trees, bright grass, a sky of old. You remember the world looking exactly like this, once.
   > What: a rare Biomes O' Plenty biome styled after Minecraft's earliest days.
   > Tip: there's a snowy version too, the wintry origin valley.

### Monster Hunting

*Every monster is just chemistry with bad manners.*

1. **Zombie Hunter**: kill 25x `minecraft:zombie` *(after: Shield)*
   > You remember when the dead stayed down out of respect. Time to remind them.
   > What: the first line of the hunting page. Each tier asks for more of the same mob and pays out a Hunter's Bounty.
   > How: zombies spawn in the dark and at night everywhere. A sword and shield are all you need.
   > Tip: zombies burn in daylight; fight at dawn and let the sun help.
2. **Zombie Slayer**: kill 100x `minecraft:zombie` *(after: Zombie Hunter)*
   > What: a hundred zombies. You'll get there just by playing at night.
   > Chemistry: rotten flesh really is rotten: bacteria break proteins into amines like putrescine and cadaverine, which is where the smell comes from.
   > Tip: a simple dark room over a spawner (YUNG's dungeons have plenty) speeds this up.
3. **Bane of the Dead**: kill 500x `minecraft:zombie` *(after: Zombie Slayer)*
   > What: five hundred. This one is for people who build mob farms.
   > How: a zombie spawner trap with a killing floor, or a Smite sword and a lot of nights.
   > Tip: Smite adds damage against all undead: zombies, skeletons, wither skeletons, phantoms and the Wither.
4. **Bone Collector**: kill 25x `minecraft:skeleton` *(after: Shield)*
   > What: skeletons drop bones and arrows, and sometimes their bow.
   > Chemistry: bone meal is crushed bone: calcium phosphate, a real phosphorus fertiliser for plants.
   > How: raise your shield when they draw, then close in between shots.
   > Tip: strafing sideways makes skeletons miss most of their arrows.
5. **Skeleton Slayer**: kill 100x `minecraft:skeleton` *(after: Bone Collector)*
   > What: a hundred skeletons, and a full stack of bones for bone meal.
   > How: keep killing them at night, or trap a skeleton spawner.
   > Tip: a skeleton spawner farm is also an arrow farm.
6. **Ossuary**: kill 500x `minecraft:skeleton` *(after: Skeleton Slayer)*
   > What: five hundred skeletons. Build a farm.
   > How: YUNG's skeleton dungeons have several spawners close together; trap them.
   > Tip: Looting raises bone and arrow drops.
7. **Undead of Every Climate**: kill 15x `minecraft:husk`; kill 15x `minecraft:stray`; kill 15x `minecraft:drowned` *(after: Zombie Hunter)*
   > What: the undead adapt to their biome: husks in deserts, strays in the snow, drowned in the water.
   > How: husks don't burn in daylight; strays shoot slowness arrows; drowned may carry tridents.
   > Tip: a drowned holding a trident can drop it. The trident is worth the hunt.
8. **Tide Breaker**: kill 100x `minecraft:drowned` *(after: Undead of Every Climate)*
   > What: a hundred drowned, for copper ingots, nautilus shells and the occasional trident.
   > Chemistry: fitting loot for the sea: copper resists seawater well, because it grows a protective skin of copper oxides and chlorides.
   > How: rivers and oceans at night. Channeling or Impaling help.
9. **Creeper Hunter**: kill 25x `minecraft:creeper` *(after: Shield)*
   > What: creepers drop gunpowder, the oldest chemical explosive.
   > Chemistry: gunpowder is potassium nitrate, charcoal and sulfur. The nitrate (KNO₃) supplies the oxygen: 2KNO₃ + S + 3C → K₂S + N₂ + 3CO₂.
   > How: hit and step back; a creeper resets its fuse when you leave its range.
   > Tip: a bow is the safest creeper tool there is.
10. **Demolition Expert**: kill 100x `minecraft:creeper` *(after: Creeper Hunter)*
   > What: a hundred creepers and a pile of gunpowder for TNT and rockets.
   > How: keep hunting at night; a Knockback sword keeps them away from you.
   > Tip: a creeper killed by a skeleton's arrow drops a music disc.
11. **Ssssilence**: kill 250x `minecraft:creeper` *(after: Demolition Expert)*
   > What: two hundred and fifty creepers. The world is quieter now.
   > How: a mob farm, or many careful nights.
   > Tip: cats scare creepers away from your base.
12. **Spider Hunter**: kill 25x `minecraft:spider` *(after: Shield)*
   > What: spiders drop string and spider eyes.
   > Chemistry: a fermented spider eye (eye + sugar + brown mushroom) turns potions into their opposites; real fermentation is microbes turning sugars into new compounds like ethanol and acids.
   > How: spiders climb walls; build with an overhang to keep them out.
   > Tip: spiders are neutral in daylight unless you hit them.
13. **Arachnophobe**: kill 100x `minecraft:spider` *(after: Spider Hunter)*
   > What: a hundred spiders, and enough string for every bow and wool block you need.
   > How: spiders spawn on the surface at night; Bane of Arthropods helps.
   > Tip: string from spiders makes wool: four string per block.
14. **Cave Spiders**: kill 25x `minecraft:cave_spider` *(after: Shield)*
   > What: small, poisonous spiders that only come from spawners in mineshafts and spider caves.
   > Chemistry: milk cures poison in Minecraft; in reality there's no general antidote, and venoms are treated with antivenom antibodies.
   > How: find a mineshaft or YUNG's spider cave and fight near the spawner. Carry milk.
   > Tip: torches on and around the spawner stop it.
15. **Pearl Diver**: kill 16x `minecraft:enderman` *(after: Shield)*
   > What: endermen drop ender pearls: teleportation, eyes of ender and many machines.
   > How: look at one to make it attack, then fight with your back to a 2-block-high gap; it can't follow under a low roof.
   > Tip: endermen hate water. A rain storm sends them teleporting away.
16. **Enderman Slayer**: kill 64x `minecraft:enderman` *(after: Pearl Diver)*
   > What: a stack of kills and, with luck, a stack of pearls.
   > How: warped forests in the Nether and the End are full of them.
   > Tip: a carved pumpkin on your head lets you look at endermen safely.
17. **Void Walker**: kill 256x `minecraft:enderman` *(after: Enderman Slayer)*
   > What: four stacks of endermen. You'll need an End farm or a lot of patience.
   > How: an enderman farm on the outer End islands is the classic way.
   > Tip: endermites make endermen aggressive; that's how most End farms lure them.
18. **Witch Hunt**: kill 10x `minecraft:witch` *(after: Shield)*
   > What: witches throw potions and drink their own to heal. They drop redstone, glowstone, sugar and bottles.
   > Chemistry: glowstone dust in-game is a brewing amplifier; real "glow" chemicals (like the zinc sulfide in glow paint) store light and release it slowly.
   > How: rush them; at range they out-potion you. Milk clears their effects.
   > Tip: swamp huts spawn witches over and over: a classic redstone farm.
19. **Coven Breaker**: kill 50x `minecraft:witch` *(after: Witch Hunt)*
   > What: fifty witches and a pile of redstone and glowstone.
   > How: a swamp hut farm, or raids (witches join them).
   > Tip: witches are immune to most potion damage; use a sword or bow.
20. **Slime Time**: kill 25x `minecraft:slime` *(after: Shield)*
   > What: slimes split when killed; the smallest drop slimeballs for sticky pistons and leads.
   > Chemistry: real slime is a polymer gel: long molecules cross-linked loosely enough to flow, like borax and PVA glue.
   > How: slimes spawn in swamps at night and in slime chunks underground.
   > Tip: every split counts as a kill. A big slime is many kills.
21. **Night Terrors**: kill 10x `minecraft:phantom` *(after: Shield)*
   > What: phantoms dive at players who haven't slept for three nights. Their membranes repair elytra.
   > How: stay up three nights, then fight with a bow on open ground at night.
   > Tip: once you're done, sleep: it resets the timer and phantoms stop coming.
22. **Skyclearer**: kill 50x `minecraft:phantom` *(after: Night Terrors)*
   > What: fifty phantoms, and enough membranes to keep an elytra flying for a long time.
   > How: a bow with Power, or a trident with Loyalty, and a sleepless week.
   > Tip: phantoms are undead; Smite works on them.
23. **Pest Control**: kill 50x `minecraft:silverfish` *(after: Cave Spiders)*
   > What: silverfish hide in infested stone in strongholds and mountains, and call their friends when hurt.
   > How: YUNG's strongholds are full of infested blocks; a single blow kills each one.
   > Tip: don't break blocks near a silverfish fight; you'll wake more.
24. **The Trials**: kill 10x `minecraft:breeze`; kill 10x `minecraft:bogged` *(after: Shield)*
   > What: two mobs from 1.21: breezes (wind spirits) and bogged (mossy skeletons with poison arrows), both from trial chambers.
   > How: find a trial chamber (see the Exploration page) and fight the trial spawners.
   > Tip: breeze rods make wind charges and the mace.
25. **Pillager Hunter**: kill 25x `minecraft:pillager` *(after: Shield)*
   > What: crossbow-armed illagers from outposts and patrols.
   > How: a shield blocks their bolts. Outposts respawn pillagers around the tower.
   > Tip: a captain (banner on its head) gives Bad Omen when killed: drink the ominous bottle to start a raid.
26. **Outpost Breaker**: kill 100x `minecraft:pillager` *(after: Pillager Hunter)*
   > What: a hundred pillagers.
   > How: camp an outpost or run raids. Raids bring them in waves.
   > Tip: a raid farm is the best emerald source in the game.
27. **Axes of Malice**: kill 10x `minecraft:vindicator` *(after: Pillager Hunter)*
   > What: axe-wielding illagers from raids and woodland mansions. They hit very hard.
   > How: keep your shield up; their axes can disable it, so back off after a block.
   > Tip: a vindicator named "Johnny" attacks every mob in sight.
28. **Evoker Bane**: kill 3x `minecraft:evoker` *(after: Axes of Malice)*
   > What: evokers summon fangs and vexes. Each one drops a Totem of Undying.
   > How: they come in later raid waves and live in woodland mansions. Kill the evoker first; its vexes die with it.
   > Tip: keep a totem in your off-hand when fighting raids.
29. **Beast of the Raid**: kill 3x `minecraft:ravager` *(after: Axes of Malice)*
   > What: ravagers are huge raid beasts that trample crops and charge.
   > How: they appear in raids from wave 3 on. Block the charge with a shield, then counterattack while it's stunned.
   > Tip: ravagers drop saddles.
30. **Guardian Hunter**: kill 25x `minecraft:guardian` *(after: Shield)*
   > What: the spiky fish of ocean monuments. They drop prismarine shards and crystals.
   > Chemistry: prismarine is in-game sea crystal; real sea creatures build shells and spines from calcium carbonate and silica pulled out of seawater.
   > How: find a monument (see the Exploration page). Break line of sight when their laser charges.
   > Tip: water breathing and Depth Strider make monuments much easier.
31. **Gold in the Rot**: kill 50x `minecraft:zombified_piglin` *(after: Into the Fire)*
   > What: zombified piglins drop gold nuggets and rotten flesh.
   > How: they're neutral until you hit one, then the whole crowd comes. Fight in a corridor.
   > Tip: gold farms over Nether-roof portals are a classic build.
32. **Blaze Hunter**: kill 25x `minecraft:blaze` *(after: Into the Fire)*
   > What: blazes drop blaze rods: brewing stands, blaze powder and eyes of ender.
   > Chemistry: blazes throw fireballs; real fireballs need fuel plus oxygen, which is why a fire triangle (heat, fuel, O₂) puts them out when you remove any side.
   > How: blaze spawners sit in Nether fortresses. Snowballs hurt them.
   > Tip: fire resistance makes you immune to their fireballs.
33. **Blaze Slayer**: kill 100x `minecraft:blaze` *(after: Blaze Hunter)*
   > What: a hundred blazes and a good supply of rods.
   > How: build a blaze farm around a fortress spawner.
   > Tip: blaze rods are a good furnace fuel: 12 items each.
34. **Ghastbuster**: kill 10x `minecraft:ghast` *(after: Into the Fire)*
   > What: ghasts drop ghast tears (regeneration potions, end crystals) and gunpowder.
   > How: hit their fireballs back at them with a sword or an arrow.
   > Tip: kill them over solid ground, not lava, or the tear is lost.
35. **Hot Jelly**: kill 50x `minecraft:magma_cube` *(after: Into the Fire)*
   > What: magma cubes split like slimes and drop magma cream for fire resistance.
   > How: basalt deltas are full of them.
   > Tip: every split counts as a kill.
36. **Skull Hunter**: kill 25x `minecraft:wither_skeleton` *(after: Into the Fire)*
   > What: tall black skeletons in Nether fortresses. They sometimes drop their skull; three summon the Wither.
   > How: fight in a 2-block-high tunnel; they're three blocks tall and can't follow.
   > Tip: Looting raises the skull drop chance.
37. **Bonebreaker**: kill 100x `minecraft:wither_skeleton` *(after: Skull Hunter)*
   > What: a hundred wither skeletons, and enough skulls for several Withers.
   > How: a wither skeleton farm on a fortress platform.
   > Tip: a Smite sword kills them quickly.
38. **Bastion Brawl**: kill 5x `minecraft:piglin_brute`; kill 10x `minecraft:hoglin` *(after: Into the Fire)*
   > What: the toughest regular Nether fighters: axe-armed piglin brutes in bastions and charging hoglins in crimson forests.
   > Chemistry: hoglins fear warped fungus; real animals avoid many plant compounds, which evolved as chemical defences.
   > How: brutes ignore gold armour; fight them in doorways. Hoglins drop pork and leather.
   > Tip: hoglins turn into zoglins in the Overworld, so don't bring one home.
39. **Shell Game**: kill 20x `minecraft:shulker` *(after: The End)*
   > What: shulkers hide in End Cities and drop shells for shulker boxes.
   > How: hit them when their shell opens. Their bullets make you levitate.
   > Tip: carry milk or slow falling against levitation.
40. **The Rotspawn**: kill 20x `undergarden:rotling`; kill 10x `undergarden:rotwalker`; kill 3x `undergarden:rotbeast` *(after: The Undergarden)*
   > What: the Undergarden's undead-like rotspawn: small rotlings, tall rotwalkers and the huge rotbeast.
   > How: utherium weapons deal extra damage to rotspawn.
   > Tip: rotbeasts hit hard and have a lot of health; fight them in the open.
41. **Things in the Dark**: kill 5x `undergarden:nargoyle`; kill 10x `undergarden:muncher` *(after: The Undergarden)*
   > What: more Undergarden predators: nargoyles and the hungry munchers.
   > How: explore the Undergarden at length; both turn up in its caverns.
   > Tip: cloggrum armour is the Undergarden's best early protection.
42. **Sculk Fauna**: kill 10x `deeperdarker:sculk_snapper`; kill 10x `deeperdarker:sculk_centipede` *(after: The Otherside)*
   > What: creatures of the Otherside, grown from sculk.
   > How: roam the Otherside's biomes. Sneak to approach without alerting the sculk.
   > Tip: carry food and a way home; the Otherside is a long way from your base.
43. **Shattered and Stalkers**: kill 10x `deeperdarker:shattered`; kill 3x `deeperdarker:stalker` *(after: Sculk Fauna)*
   > What: shattered drop sculk bones; stalkers drop soul crystals. Both go into the soul elytra.
   > How: both hunt by sound like the Warden. Fight at range.
   > Tip: sneak to keep your footsteps quiet.
44. **Martian Raptors**: kill 10x `ad_astra:martian_raptor` *(after: Mars)*
   > What: fast predators of the Martian surface.
   > Chemistry: anything living on Mars would need to cope with perchlorates (ClO₄⁻), reactive salts found in real Martian soil.
   > How: land on Mars and hunt in the wastelands. Keep your suit topped up.
45. **Twilight Pests**: kill 20x `twilightforest:redcap`; kill 20x `twilightforest:kobold`; kill 20x `twilightforest:swarm_spider` *(after: The Twilight Forest)*
   > What: the common hostile mobs of the Twilight Forest.
   > How: redcaps and kobolds roam the forest; swarm spiders spawn around hollow hills and spider-infested spots.
   > Tip: redcap sappers carry TNT; kill them before they light it.
46. **Maze and Swamp**: kill 10x `twilightforest:minotaur`; kill 10x `twilightforest:maze_slime`; kill 10x `twilightforest:skeleton_druid` *(after: The Lich)*
   > What: guardians of the labyrinth and the swamp beyond the Lich.
   > How: minotaurs and maze slimes fill the labyrinth; skeleton druids lurk in the forest at night.
   > Tip: maze slimes split like regular slimes.
47. **Goblins and Ghastguards**: kill 10x `twilightforest:lower_goblin_knight`; kill 5x `twilightforest:blockchain_goblin`; kill 10x `twilightforest:carminite_ghastguard` *(after: The Hydra)*
   > What: goblin knights and block-and-chain goblins hold the Goblin Stronghold; carminite ghastguards haunt the Dark Tower.
   > How: goblin knights ride in pairs; knock off the upper one, then finish the lower.
   > Tip: ghastguards' fireballs can be hit back like a ghast's.
48. **Snow Beasts**: kill 10x `twilightforest:yeti`; kill 10x `twilightforest:winter_wolf` *(after: The Lich)*
   > What: the snowy forest's hunters. Yetis drop arctic fur for warm armour.
   > How: yetis guard the Alpha Yeti's cave; winter wolves roam the snowy forest and breathe frost.
   > Tip: winter wolves come in packs; back against a tree.
49. **Trolls and Giants**: kill 5x `twilightforest:troll`; kill 3x `twilightforest:giant_miner` *(after: The Highlands)*
   > What: trolls in the troll caves and giant miners on the highlands.
   > How: trolls throw rocks; giants swing slowly but hard. Fight both from range.
   > Tip: giant miners drop giant pickaxes.
50. **Elder Guardians**: kill 3x `minecraft:elder_guardian` *(after: Guardian Hunter)*
   > What: each ocean monument holds three elder guardians. They cast Mining Fatigue until all three are dead.
   > How: the elders sit in the top room and two wing rooms. Water breathing, a good sword and patience.
   > Tip: elders drop wet sponges and, sometimes, a tide armour trim.
51. **The Wither**: kill 1x `minecraft:wither`; `minecraft:nether_star` *(after: Skull Hunter)*
   > You remember making stars. This one you'll have to take.
   > What: the Wither is the Nether's boss, summoned by the player. It drops a Nether Star for beacons and many late recipes.
   > How: four soul sand in a T with three wither skeleton skulls on top. Summon it deep underground or far from base: it destroys blocks.
   > Tip: below half health it gains armour that stops arrows; switch to a Smite sword.

## Farming and Food

### Farming and Food

*Grow what you need, cook it well, feed it back to the soil.*

1. **Knife and Board**: `farmersdelight:flint_knife`; `farmersdelight:cutting_board` *(after: Something to Eat)*
   > You remember feasts that lasted a week. Start with something smaller.
   > What: Farmer's Delight adds crops, meals and kitchen blocks. The knife harvests straw and cuts ingredients.
   > How: craft a Flint Knife and a Cutting Board. Place items on the board and use the knife on them.
   > Tip: a knife on tall grass gives straw; wild crops (tomatoes, onions, cabbage, rice) grow in the wild.
2. **Cooking Pot**: `farmersdelight:cooking_pot` *(after: Knife and Board)*
   > What: the Cooking Pot turns several ingredients into one meal. It needs heat underneath.
   > Chemistry: browning meat is the Maillard reaction: amino acids and sugars react above about 140 °C, making hundreds of flavour compounds.
   > How: iron, bricks, a wooden shovel and a water bucket (check JEI). Place it over a fire, campfire or Stove.
3. **A Proper Meal**: 2x `farmersdelight:vegetable_soup` *(after: Cooking Pot)*
   > What: Farmer's Delight meals restore a lot of hunger and give long effects like Nourishment (hunger stops dropping) and Comfort.
   > How: cook vegetable soup in the Cooking Pot (check JEI), then bowl it.
   > Tip: a Feeding Upgrade in your backpack eats these for you.
4. **Pasta with Meatballs**: 2x `farmersdelight:pasta_with_meatballs` *(after: A Proper Meal, The New Crops)*
   > What: a hearty meal that chains every kitchen tool: knife (minced beef), board (raw pasta from dough) and pot (tomato sauce).
   > Chemistry: dough is starch and gluten proteins; water lets gluten chains cross-link into the stretchy network that holds pasta together.
   > How: follow the tree in JEI: wheat dough → raw pasta, beef → minced beef, tomatoes → tomato sauce, then the pot.
   > Tip: press U on any ingredient in JEI to see every meal that uses it.
5. **A Varied Diet**: 6x any of `item_tag(farmersdelight:meals)` *(after: Pasta with Meatballs)*
   > What: collect six bowls or plates of any Farmer's Delight meal: stews, soups, rice dishes, ratatouille, you choose.
   > How: anything in the 'meals' tag counts. Mix and match, they don't have to be different.
   > Tip: meals stack to 16; keep a stack in the backpack for long mining trips.
6. **Rich Soil**: `farmersdelight:organic_compost`; 4x `farmersdelight:rich_soil` *(after: Knife and Board)*
   > What: organic compost slowly turns into Rich Soil, which grows crops faster and mushrooms into colonies.
   > Chemistry: composting is microbes breaking down organic matter with oxygen. They release CO₂ and heat, and leave humus rich in nitrogen.
   > How: craft compost from dirt, straw and bone meal plus rotten flesh or tree bark (check JEI), place it and wait.
   > Tip: till Rich Soil with a hoe to get rich farmland for crops.
7. **The Stove**: `farmersdelight:stove` *(after: Cooking Pot)*
   > What: the Stove heats a Cooking Pot or Skillet on top and can grill up to six items on its own surface.
   > How: iron ingots, bricks and a campfire (check JEI). Light it with flint and steel.
   > Tip: unlike a campfire it doesn't need logs, and it looks right in a kitchen.
8. **A Feast**: `farmersdelight:honey_glazed_ham_block` *(after: A Proper Meal)*
   > What: feasts are placed as blocks and served with a bowl, several portions each.
   > How: cook a Honey Glazed Ham (check JEI). Place it on a table and right-click with bowls.
   > Tip: a feast is the easiest way to feed a whole team before a boss fight.
9. **Banquet**: `farmersdelight:roast_chicken_block`; `farmersdelight:shepherds_pie_block` *(after: A Feast)*
   > What: two more feasts for the table: a whole Roast Chicken and a Shepherd's Pie.
   > How: both come from the Cooking Pot (check JEI). Each needs a container, often a bowl, to serve.
   > Tip: place feasts where everyone passes, like next to the team's waystone.
10. **Mushroom Colonies**: 2x any of `item_tag(farmersdelight:mushroom_colonies)` *(after: Rich Soil)*
   > What: a mushroom on Rich Soil grows into a colony that you can harvest again and again.
   > Chemistry: fungi don't photosynthesise. They digest organic matter with enzymes, which is why rich, dark soil suits them.
   > How: plant a brown or red mushroom on Rich Soil in low light, wait, then harvest it with shears; the colony stays and regrows.
   > Tip: mushroom colonies are the steady source of mushrooms for stews and rice dishes.
11. **Skillet**: `farmersdelight:skillet` *(after: The Stove)*
   > What: a frying pan that cooks one item at a time over heat, and doubles as a surprisingly good weapon.
   > How: iron ingots and a brick (check JEI). Place it on the Stove or a campfire, or check its tooltip for cooking in hand.
   > Tip: Fire Aspect on a skillet sounds silly; it works.
12. **Breakfast**: 4x `farmersdelight:fried_egg`; 4x `farmersdelight:cooked_bacon` *(after: Skillet)*
   > What: the start of most Farmer's Delight breakfasts: fried eggs and cooked bacon.
   > Chemistry: egg white turns opaque as heat unfolds its proteins (denaturation) and they tangle together; it can't go back.
   > How: cut raw porkchop into bacon on the Cutting Board, then fry it and the eggs on the Skillet or Stove.
   > Tip: combine them into Bacon and Eggs in the Cooking Pot (check JEI).
13. **The New Crops**: 4x `farmersdelight:tomato`; 4x `farmersdelight:onion`; 4x `farmersdelight:cabbage`; 4x `farmersdelight:rice` *(after: Knife and Board)*
   > What: Farmer's Delight crops are the base of nearly every meal.
   > How: find wild tomatoes, onions, cabbages and rice in the world (rice grows in shallow water). Break them for seeds and replant.
   > Tip: rice must be planted in water one block deep, like in a real paddy.
14. **Tomatoes on a Rope**: 16x `farmersdelight:rope` *(after: The New Crops)*
   > What: tomato vines climb rope, so a single plant grows several blocks tall and fruits all the way up.
   > How: craft rope from straw (check JEI), hang it above a tomato plant, and let the vine climb.
   > Tip: rope also lets you climb down mineshafts; right-click a placed rope to extend it downward.
15. **Harvest Festival**: 128x any of `item_tag(c:crops)` *(after: The New Crops)*
   > What: a full harvest: any 128 items from the shared crops tag (wheat, carrots, potatoes, tomatoes, rice and more).
   > How: a large field, Hopper Botany Pots, or an MI Farmer all count. Carry them in one go.
   > Tip: crop storage blocks (crates and bags) pack 9 crops each; you need the loose crops here.
16. **Hopper Botany Pots**: `botanypots:terracotta_hopper_botany_pot` *(after: Something to Eat)*
   > What: Hopper Botany Pots grow crops and push the harvest into the container below.
   > How: place one on a chest, add soil (dirt) and a seed.
   > Tip: Rich Soil or farmland grows faster than dirt, and Mystical Agriculture seeds work in pots too.
17. **Animal Husbandry**: 2x `minecraft:lead`; 16x `minecraft:wheat` *(after: Something to Eat)*
   > What: cows, sheep, pigs and chickens give food, leather, wool and eggs. Two adults fed their favourite food make a baby.
   > How: lead two animals into a pen and feed them. Just Enough Breeding adds a JEI page per animal: look up any mob egg or food to see what it eats and what it drops.
   > Tip: babies take about 20 minutes to grow; feeding them their breeding food speeds that up.
18. **Pet Food**: `farmersdelight:horse_feed`; `farmersdelight:dog_food` *(after: Animal Husbandry, Knife and Board)*
   > What: Horse Feed heals and speeds up horses; Dog Food heals tamed wolves and gives them buffs.
   > How: both are Farmer's Delight crafting recipes (check JEI).
   > Tip: a well-fed wolf is a decent bodyguard in the early Twilight Forest.
19. **N, P and K**: `chemlib:urea`; `chemlib:diammonium_phosphate`; `chemlib:potassium_nitrate` *(after: The Combiner)*
   > What: plants need nitrogen (N), phosphorus (P) and potassium (K) more than anything else from the soil. These three compounds deliver them.
   > Chemistry: urea CO(NH₂)₂ is 46% nitrogen; diammonium phosphate (NH₄)₂HPO₄ carries N and P; potassium nitrate KNO₃ carries K and N.
   > How: make all three in the Alchemistry Combiner (check JEI for the element recipe of each).
   > {@pagebreak}
   > Deep dive: why nitrogen is the hard one.
   > Air is 78% nitrogen, but N₂ has a triple bond that plants can't break. Only some soil bacteria (and lightning) can 'fix' it.
   > In 1909 Fritz Haber found how to force N₂ + 3H₂ → 2NH₃ with a metal catalyst, high pressure and heat; Carl Bosch scaled it up.
   > Today about half the nitrogen in your body passed through a Haber-Bosch plant. Ammonia is turned into urea, ammonium nitrate and phosphates.
   > You'll build that same reaction in the main quests (Haber-Bosch, HV tier). Phosphorus comes from mined phosphate rock, and potassium from potash salts like KCl.
20. **NPK Fertilizer**: `extended_industrialization:npk_fertilizer_bucket` *(after: N, P and K, The Chemical Reactor, Sulfuric Acid)*
   > What: Extended Industrialization's NPK Fertilizer is a fluid the MI Farmer can spread to grow crops faster.
   > Chemistry: bone meal is calcium phosphate; sulfuric acid frees phosphoric acid, H₃PO₄, just like the real 'wet process' for phosphate rock.
   > How: Chemical Reactor, three steps (check JEI): bone meal + H₂SO₄ → phosphoric acid; potato/carrot/beetroot + water + HCl → potassium chloride, then KCl + H₂ + O₂ → potassium hydroxide; finally nitrogen + phosphoric acid + KOH → NPK Fertilizer.
   > Tip: real NPK plants start from ammonia, not raw nitrogen. Here the recipe takes the shortcut.
21. **The Steam Farmer**: `extended_industrialization:steam_farmer` *(after: Analog Circuit, Electric Motor)*
   > What: Extended Industrialization's Farmer is a multiblock that tills, plants, harvests and (with fertiliser) speeds up a whole field.
   > How: analog circuits, a motor, bronze plated bricks, a steel combine and a bronze pipe casing (check JEI). Its layout is in JEI and the MI guidebook.
   > Tip: the Electric Farmer is the upgrade. JEI has a fertiliser page that shows which fluids speed up a Farmer.
22. **Food Fight**: 8x `farmersdelight:rotten_tomato` *(after: The New Crops)*
   > What: you found a use for spoiled tomatoes: rotten tomatoes can be thrown.
   > Chemistry: rotting is bacteria and fungi breaking sugars into acids, alcohols and gases. Don't eat it.
   > Tip: they're also good compost material. Probably better than throwing them at the team.

### Mystical Agriculture

*Resource crops, within the rules of chemistry.*

1. **Mystical Agriculture**: `mysticalagriculture:infusion_altar`; 16x `mysticalagriculture:inferium_essence` *(after: Iron)*
   > You remember coaxing metal out of the ground like wheat. Some of that still works.
   > What: resource crops grow essence that crafts into real materials. Seeds are made on the Infusion Altar with Infusion Pedestals around it.
   > How: inferium ore gives inferium essence (mobs drop some too). The altar is gold, stone and red wool; pedestals are in JEI.
   > Tip: Tier 3/4/5 seeds need MI LV/MV/HV circuits, and crops for chemistry milestones can't be grown at all. Farming is a convenience here, not a shortcut.
2. **Prosperity**: 8x `mysticalagriculture:prosperity_shard`; `mysticalagriculture:prosperity_seed_base` *(after: Mystical Agriculture)*
   > What: prosperity shards come from prosperity ore. They make seed bases (the core of every resource seed) and infusion crystals.
   > How: mine prosperity ore; 4 shards around wheat seeds make a Prosperity Seed Base.
   > Tip: Ultimine the veins; you'll want a lot of shards.
3. **Infusion Crystal**: `mysticalagriculture:infusion_crystal` *(after: Prosperity)*
   > What: a crystal that combines lower essence into higher tiers. It wears out with use.
   > How: a diamond, prosperity shards and inferium essence (check JEI).
   > Tip: the Master Infusion Crystal never wears out; it comes much later, from supremium.
4. **Prudentium**: 8x `mysticalagriculture:prudentium_essence` *(after: Infusion Crystal)*
   > What: the second essence tier, for tier 2 seeds (JEI lists every crop and its tier).
   > How: 4 inferium essence around an infusion crystal make 1 prudentium (check JEI).
   > Tip: it uncrafts back to inferium if you overshoot (check JEI).
5. **Tertium: LV Seeds**: 8x `mysticalagriculture:tertium_essence` *(after: Prudentium, Analog Circuit)*
   > What: tier 3 seeds (iron, copper, redstone, nether quartz, tin, lead and more) need Tertium essence and an MI analog circuit.
   > Chemistry: the analog circuit stands on steel, and steel stands on coke: coal baked without air until only carbon is left.
   > How: 4 prudentium around an infusion crystal make 1 tertium. Each seed type costs its circuit once; planted seeds multiply.
6. **Imperium: MV Seeds**: 8x `mysticalagriculture:imperium_essence` *(after: Tertium: LV Seeds, MV: Aluminum)*
   > What: tier 4 seeds (gold, lapis, nickel, blaze, enderman and more) need Imperium essence and an MI electronic circuit.
   > Chemistry: the electronic circuit needs aluminum, which only electrolysis can free from its oxide: 2Al₂O₃ → 4Al + 3O₂.
   > How: 4 tertium around an infusion crystal make 1 imperium.
7. **Supremium: HV Seeds**: 8x `mysticalagriculture:supremium_essence` *(after: Imperium: MV Seeds, HV: Digital Circuits)*
   > You remember gardens that grew gold. These come close, and they still answer to chemistry.
   > What: tier 5 seeds (diamond, emerald, netherite and more) need Supremium essence and an MI digital circuit.
   > Chemistry: the digital circuit needs polyethylene: thousands of ethylene units, C₂H₄, linked into one chain.
   > How: 4 imperium around an infusion crystal make 1 supremium.
8. **Growth Accelerators**: 4x `mysticalagriculture:inferium_growth_accelerator` *(after: Mystical Agriculture)*
   > What: growth accelerators placed below farmland speed up the crops above them. Stack several for more effect.
   > How: check JEI for each tier's recipe.
   > Tip: put them under the floor of your crop room so they're out of the way.
9. **Awakening**: `mysticalagriculture:awakening_altar`; 4x `mysticalagriculture:awakened_supremium_essence` *(after: Supremium: HV Seeds)*
   > What: the Awakening Altar turns supremium into awakened supremium for the strongest Mystical Agriculture gear.
   > How: JEI shows the altar, its Awakening Pedestals, the Essence Vessels and the awakening recipe.
   > Tip: awakened supremium makes the best Mystical Agriculture tools and armour.
10. **Master Infusion Crystal**: `mysticalagriculture:master_infusion_crystal` *(after: Supremium: HV Seeds)*
   > What: an infusion crystal with no durability, so essence upgrades stop costing crystals.
   > How: a supremium gemstone, supremium essence and prosperity shards (check JEI).
   > Tip: worth it once you are mass-producing tertium and imperium for seeds.
11. **Inferium Seeds**: 4x any of `item_tag(mysticalagriculture:seeds)` *(after: Mystical Agriculture)*
   > What: Inferium Seeds grow inferium essence, the fuel for every higher tier. Any 4 Mystical Agriculture seeds complete this.
   > How: 8 inferium essence around wheat seeds in a crafting grid (no altar needed). Plant them on farmland and replant the extra seeds.
   > Tip: every other resource seed starts from a seed base on the altar, so build a big inferium field first.
12. **Essence Farmland**: 8x `mysticalagriculture:inferium_farmland` *(after: Inferium Seeds)*
   > What: farmland infused with essence. Inferium crops drop more essence on higher-tier farmland.
   > How: one inferium essence plus one farmland, shapeless (check JEI). Higher tiers use their own essence.
   > Tip: upgrade the farmland under your inferium field as each new essence tier arrives.
13. **Mystical Fertilizer**: 4x `mysticalagriculture:mystical_fertilizer` *(after: Inferium Seeds)*
   > What: instantly grows a crop or sapling, including resource crops that bone meal can't push.
   > How: bone meal, inferium essence and a diamond make 4 (check JEI).
   > Tip: save it for slow, expensive crops rather than wheat.
14. **Agglomeratio**: `mysticalagriculture:nature_agglomeratio`; `mysticalagriculture:earth_agglomeratio` *(after: Prosperity)*
   > What: some crops (nature, the four elements, dye, nether, coral and others) don't map to one item. Their seed recipes use an agglomeratio instead.
   > How: Nature Agglomeratio is pumpkin, potato, cactus and sugar cane; JEI shows the rest.
   > Tip: the elemental crops (air, earth, water, fire) feed many other recipes, so they're good early picks.
15. **Why Chemistry Won't Grow**: checkmark *(after: Tertium: LV Seeds)*
   > What: seeds for uranium, uraninite, osmium, fluorite, silicon, steel, aluminum, graphite, platinum, iridium, refined obsidian and refined glowstone don't exist in this pack. Click the checkmark when you've read why.
   > How: if JEI shows no altar recipe for a crop, it's one of these. Get them from ore processing and the reactors in the main quests.
   > Tip: iron, copper, tin, lead, gold and other everyday metals are still farmable, behind their circuit gates.
   > {@pagebreak}
   > Deep dive: why these metals?
   > Each blocked resource is the reason a tier exists. Steel needs carbon from coke and an oxygen-controlled furnace; aluminum needs electrolysis of molten alumina (Hall-Héroult, 2Al₂O₃ → 4Al + 3O₂).
   > Silicon is reduced from quartz with carbon at about 2000 °C (SiO₂ + 2C → Si + 2CO). Fluorite is the fluorine source for uranium enrichment; uranium and uraninite are the nuclear chain.
   > Platinum and iridium come from the PGM line: anode sludge, aqua regia, ammonium hexachloroplatinate, calcining. Graphite and osmium gate Mekanism and MI steps.
   > Growing them on a plant would skip the chemistry those tiers teach. Everything else stays farmable, so farming saves time without replacing a single reaction.
16. **Watering Can**: `mysticalagriculture:watering_can` *(after: Inferium Seeds)*
   > What: right-click to water crops in an area, which makes them grow faster while you hold it.
   > How: iron ingots, a bowl and bone meal (check JEI). Fill it from any water source.
   > Tip: stand in the middle of the field and hold right-click.
17. **Inferium Ingots**: 4x `mysticalagriculture:inferium_ingot` *(after: Prosperity)*
   > What: essence-infused ingots for tools, armour and the better watering cans.
   > How: a prosperity ingot plus 2 inferium essence (check JEI for the prosperity ingot).
   > Tip: each essence tier has its own ingot; JEI shows the upgrade path.
18. **Inferium Watering Can**: `mysticalagriculture:inferium_watering_can` *(after: Watering Can, Inferium Ingots, Mystical Fertilizer)*
   > What: a tiered watering can covers a larger area and works faster.
   > How: your watering can, inferium ingots and mystical fertilizer (check JEI). The higher tiers repeat the pattern.
   > Tip: the can keeps its contents when upgraded.
19. **A Lucky Harvest**: `mysticalagriculture:fertilized_essence` *(after: Inferium Seeds)*
   > What: crops sometimes drop Fertilized Essence. You found one.
   > How: it crafts into mystical fertilizer (check JEI).
   > Tip: more crops means more chances; a big inferium field pays for itself.
20. **Soulstone**: 4x `mysticalagriculture:soulium_dust`; 8x `mysticalagriculture:soulstone` *(after: Mystical Agriculture, Into the Fire)*
   > What: soulstone clusters generate in the Nether. A few of their blocks are soulium ore, the source of soulium dust.
   > How: mine soulstone in the Nether and watch for soulium ore (check JEI for its drops).
   > Tip: a soulium ingot is a prosperity ingot plus 2 soulium dust; soul dust (for soul glass) comes from smelting smooth soulstone.
21. **Soulium Dagger**: `mysticalagriculture:soulium_dagger` *(after: Soulstone)*
   > What: kills with this dagger capture the mob's soul into a Soul Jar in your inventory.
   > How: a golden sword, soulium ingots and soulium dust (check JEI).
   > Tip: JEI's soul extraction page shows how many souls each mob gives.
22. **Soul Jars**: 2x `mysticalagriculture:soul_jar` *(after: Soulium Dagger)*
   > What: each jar stores souls of one mob type. Full jars are the material for mob seeds.
   > How: soul glass (soul dust around glass) and a soulium ingot (check JEI).
   > Tip: carry one empty jar per mob type you're hunting.
23. **Fill a Jar**: kill 20x `minecraft:zombie` *(after: Soul Jars)*
   > What: fill a Soul Jar with zombie souls for zombie seeds (rotten flesh).
   > How: keep an empty Soul Jar in your inventory and kill zombies with the Soulium Dagger.
   > Tip: the Soul Extractor can also pull souls out of mob drops like rotten flesh (check JEI).
24. **Mob Seeds**: `mysticalagriculture:soulium_seed_base` *(after: Fill a Jar, Tertium: LV Seeds)*
   > What: mob seeds start from a Soulium Seed Base and grow drops like rotten flesh, bones and string.
   > How: a prosperity seed base plus 2 soulium dust. On the altar, put it in the middle and the full jars and essence on the pedestals (check JEI).
   > Tip: zombie, skeleton, spider and creeper are tier 3, so they also need an analog circuit.
25. **Machine Frame**: `mysticalagriculture:machine_frame` *(after: Soulstone)*
   > What: the shared frame for the Harvester, Soul Extractor, Seed Reprocessor and other MA machines.
   > How: iron, redstone and stone (check JEI).
   > Tip: MA machines run on Forge Energy, so they plug into any FE cable.
26. **Harvester**: `mysticalagriculture:harvester` *(after: Machine Frame)*
   > What: the Harvester picks mature crops in an area in front of it and replants them, pushing drops into its inventory.
   > How: machine frame, soulium ingots, iron and two diamond scythes (check JEI). Power it with FE.
   > Tip: pipe its output into storage and the field runs itself.
27. **Soul Extractor**: `mysticalagriculture:soul_extractor` *(after: Machine Frame, Soulium Dagger)*
   > What: fills Soul Jars from mob drops (rotten flesh, bones, string and more) instead of kills.
   > How: machine frame, soulium ingots, iron and a soulium dagger (check JEI). Power it with FE.
   > Tip: feed it drops from an HNN Simulation Chamber for a fully passive soul supply.
28. **A Pot Farm**: 8x `botanypots:terracotta_hopper_botany_pot`; 8x any of `item_tag(mysticalagriculture:seeds)` *(after: Hopper Botany Pots, Inferium Seeds)*
   > What: Mystical Agriculture seeds grow in Hopper Botany Pots, which harvest by themselves into a chest below.
   > How: put each pot on a hopper, pipe or chest, add dirt or farmland and a seed. Hold any MA seed in JEI to see its pot growth time.
   > Tip: a column of pots over one shared item pipe is a compact essence farm for every tier.

## Collections

### Quality of Life

*Small things that make a big difference.*

1. **One Book to Rule Them All**: `akashictome:tome` *(after: A Workbench)*
   > You remember a library that held everything. A single book will have to do.
   > What: the Akashic Tome merges every mod's guidebook into one item.
   > How: craft it, then right-click it with other guidebooks to add them.
   > Tip: right-click the tome to choose which book to open.
2. **Find Anything**: `naturescompass:naturescompass`; `explorerscompass:explorerscompass` *(after: Iron)*
   > What: Nature's Compass finds biomes; Explorer's Compass finds structures (Nether Fortresses, Ancient Cities, villages).
   > How: craft both (check JEI), right-click to pick a target, then follow the needle.
   > Tip: the Explorer's Compass is the fastest way to find an Ancient City for the Deeper and Darker quests.
3. **No More Mob Spawns**: `torchmaster:megatorch` *(after: Iron)*
   > What: the Mega Torch stops hostile mobs spawning naturally in a huge radius.
   > How: check JEI for the recipe and place it near the middle of your base.
   > Tip: it blocks natural spawns only. Spawners and Simulation Chambers still work.
4. **Graves**: checkmark *(after: Iron)*
   > What: if you die, Corail Tombstone keeps your items in a grave where you fell.
   > How: walk back and open the grave to recover everything. Click the checkmark once you've read this.
   > Tip: JourneyMap drops a waypoint where you died, so the grave is easy to find.
5. **Backups**: checkmark *(after: Iron)*
   > What: Simple Backups saves your world automatically.
   > How: on a server, backups live in the server's backups folder. In single player, look in your instance folder.
   > Tip: copy a backup somewhere else before trying risky reactor builds.
6. **Dread Lamp**: `torchmaster:dreadlamp` *(after: No More Mob Spawns, Into the Fire)*
   > What: the Dread Lamp blocks Nether mob spawns in a large radius, the way the Mega Torch does for Overworld mobs.
   > How: check JEI. Place it at your Nether base or portal hub.
   > Tip: place it before building in the Nether so your base stays quiet.
7. **Crafting on a Stick**: `crafting_on_a_stick:crafting_table` *(after: A Workbench)*
   > What: a crafting table you carry around. Right-click it to open a 3x3 grid anywhere: in a mine, out exploring, mid-build.
   > How: craft it from a crafting table and a stick (check JEI). The same mod makes pocket versions of the stonecutter, smithing table, anvil, grindstone, loom and cartography table.
   > Tip: it saves a hotbar slot over placing and breaking tables. The pocket stonecutter is great for building with Create Deco and Rechiseled blocks.
8. **A Whole Workshop**: `crafting_on_a_stick:stonecutter`; `crafting_on_a_stick:smithing_table` *(after: Crafting on a Stick)*
   > What: optional: the pocket stonecutter and smithing table.
   > How: each one is its block plus a stick (check JEI).
   > Tip: the smithing table is how netherite and Apotheosis upgrades are applied, so it's handy to carry.
9. **Backpack Upgrades**: `sophisticatedbackpacks:pickup_upgrade`; `sophisticatedbackpacks:magnet_upgrade` *(after: A Backpack)*
   > What: pickup and magnet upgrades pull items straight into your backpack.
   > How: craft an Upgrade Base, then the upgrades (check JEI). Put them in the backpack's upgrade slots.
   > Tip: set a filter on the pickup upgrade so it only grabs ores and drops you actually want.
10. **Iron Backpack**: `sophisticatedbackpacks:iron_backpack` *(after: A Backpack)*
   > What: each backpack tier adds inventory rows and upgrade slots: iron, gold, diamond, netherite.
   > How: surround your backpack with iron ingots (check JEI). It keeps everything inside.
   > Tip: stack upgrades multiply how many items fit in each slot.
11. **Feeding Upgrade**: `sophisticatedbackpacks:feeding_upgrade` *(after: Backpack Upgrades)*
   > What: eats food from the backpack automatically when you're hungry.
   > How: check JEI. Farmer's Delight meals restore a lot per bite.
   > Tip: set a filter so it doesn't eat your golden apples.
12. **Netherite Backpack**: `sophisticatedbackpacks:netherite_backpack` *(after: Iron Backpack)*
   > What: the top tier: the most inventory slots and upgrade space.
   > How: ring the backpack with gold, then diamonds, then smith in a netherite ingot with a netherite upgrade template.
   > Tip: a netherite backpack has room for several stack upgrades at once.
13. **Waystones**: `waystones:waystone` *(after: Iron)*
   > What: waystones are teleport points. Activate one by right-clicking it, then warp between any you've activated.
   > How: found in villages, or craft one (check JEI). Place them at your base, mines and portals.
   > Tip: warping costs a little XP depending on distance; check the config if it seems steep.
14. **Warp Scrolls**: 4x `waystones:warp_scroll` *(after: Waystones)*
   > What: a single-use warp to any activated waystone, handy before you can afford a Warp Stone.
   > How: paper, an ender pearl, gold nuggets and an ink sac (check JEI).
   > Tip: keep one in your backpack for emergencies.
15. **Maps**: checkmark *(after: A Workbench)*
   > What: JourneyMap draws a minimap and a full world map as you explore, with waypoints.
   > How: press J for the full JourneyMap. FTB Chunks has its own map for claims (check Controls for its key). Click the checkmark when you've tried them.
   > Tip: drop a waypoint at every rich ore deposit you find. Deposits are rare, so note the depth too.
16. **Claim Your Land**: checkmark *(after: Maps)*
   > You remember a whole world that answered to you. Start with a few chunks.
   > What: claimed chunks are protected from other players and explosions (per your team settings). Force-loaded chunks keep machines running while you're away.
   > How: open the FTB Chunks map and drag across chunks to claim them (the map's help shows the controls). Click the checkmark when done.
   > Tip: force-load your main factory so chemical lines don't stall when you travel.
17. **Inventory Tricks**: checkmark *(after: A Workbench)*
   > What: small mods that save hours: Mouse Tweaks (drag and scroll to move stacks), Inventory Tweaks (sorting) and TrashSlot (a trash can in your inventory).
   > How: try a scroll-wheel drag over stacks, the sort button, and the trash slot by your hotbar. Click the checkmark when done.
   > Tip: Polymorph shows a button when two recipes share a grid; click it to pick the one you want.
18. **Feral Flare Lantern**: `torchmaster:feral_flare_lantern` *(after: No More Mob Spawns, Into the Fire)*
   > What: the lantern places invisible light sources in the dark spots around it, so a cave or a big hall lights up without a single torch.
   > How: gold ingots, glass and glowstone dust (check JEI). Place it in the middle of the room.
   > Tip: great for a clean-looking factory floor. It still lets mobs spawn in spots it can't reach.
19. **Grave Tricks**: checkmark *(after: Graves)*
   > What: Corail Tombstone does more than store your items: it adds scrolls, tablets and the Ankh of Prayer, plus a book that makes items soulbound.
   > How: search 'tombstone' in JEI to browse them. Click the checkmark when you've had a look.
   > Tip: by default only you can open your grave, so a death on a server doesn't mean losing your gear to someone else.
20. **Loot for Everyone**: checkmark *(after: Iron)*
   > What: with Lootr, structure chests (dungeons, temples, villages, mineshafts) hold separate loot for each player.
   > How: open any generated chest. You get your own roll; your friends still get theirs. Click the checkmark when you've seen one.
   > Tip: on a server, nobody has to race to a dungeon first.
21. **Stack Upgrade**: `sophisticatedbackpacks:stack_upgrade_tier_1` *(after: Iron Backpack)*
   > What: a stack upgrade multiplies how many items each backpack slot holds. Higher tiers multiply more.
   > How: check JEI; each tier is built from the one before it.
   > Tip: stack upgrades make a backpack the best place to carry dust and ingots between machines.
22. **Tool Swapper**: `sophisticatedbackpacks:tool_swapper_upgrade` *(after: Stack Upgrade)*
   > What: swaps the tool in your hand for the best one in your backpack when you start mining a block or hitting a mob.
   > How: check JEI, then put it in an upgrade slot and fill the backpack with tools.
   > Tip: pairs well with a paxel; it switches to shears or a sword only when needed.
23. **Refill Upgrade**: `sophisticatedbackpacks:refill_upgrade` *(after: Stack Upgrade)*
   > What: keeps chosen hotbar items topped up from the backpack: torches, blocks, food.
   > How: check JEI. Set the items to refill in the upgrade's tab.
   > Tip: refill building blocks while bridging and you'll never stop to open your inventory.
24. **Pocket Anvil**: `crafting_on_a_stick:anvil`; `crafting_on_a_stick:grindstone` *(after: A Whole Workshop)*
   > What: pocket versions of the anvil and grindstone, opened with right-click.
   > How: each one is its block plus a stick (check JEI).
   > Tip: the pocket anvil wears down like a placed one, so carry a spare for long trips.
25. **FTB Ultimine**: checkmark *(after: Stone Age)*
   > What: Ultimine breaks every connected matching block at once: a full ore vein, a tree, a strip of stone.
   > How: hold the Ultimine key (default `, the key left of 1) and break a block. Arrow keys up and down change the shape. Click the checkmark when you've tried it.
   > Tip: it can cost extra hunger, so eat first. Shapes include tunnels and flat planes for clearing space.
26. **Form a Team**: checkmark *(after: A Workbench)*
   > What: FTB Teams groups players into a party. Teammates share quest progress and claimed chunks.
   > How: open the Teams screen from the button on your inventory screen (or use /ftbteams) and create a party, then invite friends. Click the checkmark when done.
   > Tip: playing alone? A solo party still lets you set chunk permissions for visitors.
27. **Homes and /back**: checkmark *(after: Form a Team)*
   > What: FTB Essentials adds /sethome and /home (one home by default), /back to return to where you died or last teleported, /spawn, and /tpa to ask to teleport to a friend.
   > How: stand at your base and type /sethome, then try /home from somewhere else. Click the checkmark when done.
   > Tip: each command has a short cooldown. /back after a death walks you straight to your grave.
28. **Just Zoom**: checkmark *(after: A Workbench)*
   > What: Just Zoom lets you zoom in on the distance with a key, no item needed.
   > How: find its key under Options → Controls, and hold it to zoom (its options are in the Mods menu). Click the checkmark when you've tried it.
   > Tip: handy for spotting structures, meteorites and villages from a hilltop.
29. **Ping Wheel**: checkmark *(after: Form a Team)*
   > What: Ping Wheel puts a marker on the spot you look at, visible to the other players nearby.
   > How: find its keys under Options → Controls. Look at something and ping it. Click the checkmark when done.
   > Tip: ping an ore deposit or a dangerous mob instead of typing coordinates.
30. **Chat Heads**: checkmark *(after: Form a Team)*
   > What: Chat Heads shows each player's face next to their messages in chat.
   > How: nothing to set up; it works as soon as someone talks. Click the checkmark when you've seen it.
   > Tip: its options are in the Mods menu if you want to change how heads are shown.
31. **Trade Cycling**: 16x `minecraft:emerald` *(after: Iron)*
   > What: Trade Cycling adds a button to the villager trade screen that rerolls the offers of a villager nobody has traded with yet.
   > How: give a villager a job block, open its trades and press the cycle button until the offer you want appears. Earn 16 emeralds by trading.
   > Tip: once you trade, the offers lock. Cycle a librarian until it sells the enchanted book you need.
32. **Merchant Prince**: 4x `minecraft:emerald_block` *(after: Trade Cycling)*
   > What: four emerald blocks: 36 emeralds. The villagers have noticed you.
   > Chemistry: emerald is beryl, Be₃Al₂Si₆O₁₈, coloured green by traces of chromium.
   > Tip: every Warp Stone needs an emerald, so a good trading hall pays for your travel network.
33. **Tool Belt**: `toolbelt:belt` *(after: Iron)*
   > What: the Tool Belt holds tools outside your hotbar and opens as a radial menu so you can swap fast.
   > How: leather and string (check JEI). Keep it in your inventory; find its key under Options → Controls.
   > Tip: put the wrench, the config card and your paxel on the belt.
34. **Belt Pouches**: 2x `toolbelt:pouch` *(after: Tool Belt)*
   > What: each pouch adds a slot to your tool belt.
   > How: craft pouches, then craft them together with the belt (check JEI).
   > Tip: a full belt keeps your hotbar free for blocks and food.
35. **A Waystone Network**: 4x any of `or(item(waystones:waystone)item(waystones:mossy_waystone)item(waystones:sandy_waystone)item(waystones:deepslate_waystone)item(waystones:blackstone_waystone)item(waystones:end_stone_waystone))` *(after: Waystones)*
   > What: one waystone is a landmark; four make a network. Base, mine, Nether portal and a far-off biome.
   > How: any four waystones of any style (check JEI for the variants), or bring back ones found in villages.
   > Tip: name each one clearly ('Bauxite Mine -40') so the list stays readable.
36. **Warp Stone**: `waystones:warp_stone` *(after: A Waystone Network)*
   > What: a reusable item that opens the waystone menu wherever you stand. It recharges after each use.
   > How: amethyst shards, ender pearls and an emerald (check JEI). It's also in the Demigod Token shop.
   > Tip: with a Warp Stone, warp scrolls become backup only.
37. **Waypoints**: checkmark *(after: Maps)*
   > What: JourneyMap waypoints mark places on the minimap and in the world, with distance shown on screen.
   > How: open the full map (J) and right-click a spot to add a waypoint, or use JourneyMap's waypoint key (see Controls). Click the checkmark when you've made one.
   > Tip: JourneyMap makes a death waypoint for you; share waypoints with teammates in chat.

### Utilitarian

*Small blocks and shortcuts that save a lot of clicks.*

1. **Sticks by the Bundle**: 32x `minecraft:stick` *(after: A Workbench)*
   > You remember never wasting a motion. These recipes feel familiar.
   > What: Utilitarian adds bulk recipes that skip the planks step. Two logs stacked in a column make 16 sticks.
   > How: put two logs of any kind one above the other in the crafting grid.
   > Tip: JEI shows every Utilitarian shortcut: press U on a log to see them all.
2. **Chests and Ladders**: 4x `minecraft:chest`; 24x `minecraft:ladder` *(after: Sticks by the Bundle)*
   > What: logs in a chest ring make 4 chests at once. Seven logs in a ladder shape make 24 ladders.
   > How: the same shapes as the plank recipes, but with logs (check JEI).
   > Tip: three logs in a V make 12 bowls, and logs between iron make a hopper without a chest.
3. **Slabs and Stairs**: 24x any of `item_tag(minecraft:wooden_slabs)`; 16x any of `item_tag(minecraft:wooden_stairs)` *(after: Chests and Ladders)*
   > What: three logs in a row make 24 slabs; six logs in a stair shape make 16 stairs.
   > How: use logs of one wood type in the usual slab or stair shape (check JEI).
   > Tip: two slabs placed diagonally in the grid turn back into a plank, so leftover slabs are never wasted.
4. **Doors and Trapdoors**: 12x any of `item_tag(minecraft:wooden_doors)`; 12x any of `item_tag(minecraft:wooden_trapdoors)` *(after: Slabs and Stairs)*
   > What: six logs in a door shape make 12 doors; six logs in a trapdoor shape make 12 trapdoors.
   > How: one wood type per craft (check JEI). Two logs side by side also make 4 pressure plates.
   > Tip: doors placed side by side open together, so a double door needs only one click.
5. **Re-Dyeing**: 8x any of `item_tag(c:concretes)` *(after: Doors and Trapdoors)*
   > What: Utilitarian lets you re-dye concrete, concrete powder, terracotta, stained glass, glass panes and candles that already have a colour.
   > Chemistry: real pigments are chemicals too: concrete's colours come from metal oxides, like iron oxide (Fe₂O₃) for red and brown.
   > How: eight coloured blocks around one dye re-colours all eight, or one block plus one dye does a single block (check JEI).
   > Tip: it also mixes blue and yellow dye into green, so cactus isn't the only green.
6. **Tiny Coal**: 8x `utilitarian:tiny_coal`; 8x `utilitarian:tiny_charcoal` *(after: Sticks by the Bundle, Coal)*
   > What: one coal or charcoal makes 8 tiny pieces. Each burns for 10 seconds, so you don't waste a whole coal on one item.
   > Chemistry: charcoal is wood heated without air (pyrolysis): water and volatile compounds are driven off, leaving mostly carbon.
   > How: put one coal or charcoal in the crafting grid. Eight tiny pieces craft back into one.
   > Tip: this pack's furnaces cook faster than vanilla, so one tiny coal covers more than one item.
7. **The Trowel**: `utilitarian:trowel` *(after: Sticks by the Bundle, Iron)*
   > What: the Trowel places a random block from your hotbar each time you use it. Great for natural-looking walls and paths.
   > How: two iron ingots and a stick (check JEI). Sneak-use it to switch between hotbar only and your whole inventory.
   > Tip: fill the hotbar with stone, cobblestone, andesite and tuff, and a plain wall looks weathered in seconds.
8. **Angel Block**: 4x `utilitarian:angel_block` *(after: Sticks by the Bundle, Iron)*
   > What: the Angel Block can be placed in empty air, with nothing next to it. It's the start of any sky bridge or floating build.
   > How: two feathers, two gold ingots and obsidian (check JEI). Use it while looking at empty air.
