   > Tip: it's a building block, not flight. Flying in this pack is a chemistry milestone (see the Nether chapter).
9. **Snad**: 4x `utilitarian:snad` *(after: Tiny Coal)*
   > What: cactus, sugar cane, kelp and bamboo grow taller and faster on Snad. Soul Snad does the same for nether wart.
   > Chemistry: sand is mostly quartz grains, SiO₂, the same compound as the Nether quartz and certus you'll meet later.
   > How: two sand make one Snad (red sand makes Red Snad, soul sand makes Soul Snad).
   > Tip: a column of sugar cane on Snad is the fastest paper source for Sophisticated upgrades and Iron Furnace augments.
10. **Block Magnet**: `utilitarian:magnet` *(after: The Trowel)*
   > What: with a redstone signal, the Block Magnet pulls iron blocks, steel blocks and anvils below it up towards it.
   > Chemistry: iron is ferromagnetic: its atoms' tiny magnetic moments line up in domains. The recipe is an electromagnet, an iron core with copper on each side.
   > How: copper ingot, iron block, copper ingot in a row. Place it, put an iron block somewhere below, and power the magnet.
   > Tip: a magnet and a lever make a simple lift for iron blocks: power it to pull them up, cut the power to let them fall.
11. **Fluid Hopper**: `utilitarian:fluid_hopper` *(after: The Trowel, Water)*
   > What: the Fluid Hopper moves fluids the way a hopper moves items: it collects fluid from above and passes it on, the way a hopper does with items (check its JEI page).
   > How: iron ingots around a bucket, with white dye on top (check JEI).
   > Tip: under an infinite water source it fills a tank for your first steam boiler without any pump.
12. **Well Behaved Dropper**: `utilitarian:well_behaved_dropper` *(after: The Trowel)*
   > What: a dropper that drops items neatly in front of itself instead of flinging them in random directions.
   > How: a dropper with a gold nugget on each side (check JEI).
   > Tip: drop items onto a pressure plate or into water streams and they land exactly where you planned.
13. **Redstone Clock**: `utilitarian:redstone_clock` *(after: Snad)*
   > What: the Redstone Clock pulses a redstone signal on its own, with no repeater loop.
   > How: chiseled stone bricks with redstone on four sides (check JEI). Right-click it with an empty hand to change the speed; sneak to change it faster.
   > Tip: a slow clock on a dispenser or a Well Behaved Dropper makes a simple timed feeder.
14. **TPS Meter**: `utilitarian:tps_meter` *(after: Redstone Clock)*
   > What: the TPS Meter gives a redstone signal based on the dimension's ticks per second. A healthy server runs at 20.
   > How: four comparators around obsidian (check JEI).
   > Tip: wire it to a lamp in your factory: if it dims, something you built is lagging the server.
15. **Sound Muffler**: `utilitarian:sound_muffler` *(after: Redstone Clock)*
   > What: the Sound Muffler mutes sounds in a radius around it. Your crushers, pumps and boilers go quiet.
   > Chemistry: real soundproofing works the same way: soft, porous fibres like wool turn sound energy into a tiny amount of heat.
   > How: any wool plus a note block, shapeless.
   > Tip: one in the middle of a machine row is usually enough. Its tooltip shows the radius.
16. **Trader Carpet**: 1x any of `or(item(utilitarian:white_soliciting_carpet)item(utilitarian:orange_soliciting_carpet)item(utilitarian:magenta_soliciting_carpet)item(utilitarian:light_blue_soliciting_carpet)item(utilitarian:yellow_soliciting_carpet)item(utilitarian:lime_soliciting_carpet)item(utilitarian:pink_soliciting_carpet)item(utilitarian:gray_soliciting_carpet)item(utilitarian:light_gray_soliciting_carpet)item(utilitarian:cyan_soliciting_carpet)item(utilitarian:purple_soliciting_carpet)item(utilitarian:blue_soliciting_carpet)item(utilitarian:brown_soliciting_carpet)item(utilitarian:green_soliciting_carpet)item(utilitarian:red_soliciting_carpet)item(utilitarian:black_soliciting_carpet))` *(after: Redstone Clock)*
   > What: a wandering trader arriving nearby appears on a Trader Carpet instead of right in your face.
   > How: any carpet plus a small flower, shapeless. Each colour has its own carpet.
   > Tip: put it in a little market stall by your base. JEI also shows a trapped version, if you're feeling unkind.
17. **No Soliciting**: `utilitarian:no_soliciting_banner` *(after: Trader Carpet)*
   > What: the No Soliciting Banner stops wandering traders from spawning in the chunks around it.
   > How: wool, a lead and a stick, in a banner shape (check JEI).
   > Tip: use either the carpet or the banner: carpet if you like the trader's goods, banner if you don't.
18. **Outpost Claim**: `utilitarian:no_raider_block` *(after: No Soliciting)*
   > What: place the Raider Outpost Claim Block inside a pillager outpost and raiders stop coming back to it.
   > How: the recipe uses an ominous (illager) banner, which a pillager captain drops (check JEI).
   > Tip: claim the outpost before building there; it makes a fine tower base.
19. **Un-Name Tag**: `utilitarian:unname_tag` *(after: No Soliciting)*
   > What: the Un-Name Tag removes a mob's custom name.
   > How: a name tag plus white dye, shapeless. Use it on the named mob.
   > Tip: useful when a pet name typo has been bothering you for weeks.
20. **Slime in a Bucket**: `utilitarian:slime_bucket` *(after: Un-Name Tag)*
   > You remember finding hidden things by feel. The slime remembers too.
   > What: you scooped a small slime into a bucket. It gets visibly excited when you carry it into a slime chunk.
   > How: use an empty bucket on a small slime. Place it again by using the bucket on the ground.
   > Tip: slime chunks spawn slimes underground at any light level, which makes them the best spot for a slime farm.

### The Demigod's Market

*Spend your Demigod Tokens: supplies and comforts, never shortcuts.*

1. **The Market**: checkmark *(after: Demigod Tokens)*
   > You remember offerings left at your altar. Now the world pays you in coins instead.
   > What: Demigod Tokens are the reward currency of this book. You trade them for supplies by crafting, with no shopkeeper and no server plugin.
   > How: every shop trade is a shapeless crafting recipe: put tokens in a crafting grid and the item appears.
   > Tip: this chapter explains the price bands, the exchange and what tokens will never buy.
2. **Where Tokens Come From**: `kubejs:demigod_token` *(after: The Market)*
   > What: almost every quest pays tokens, scaled by tier: 1 per quest at the start, 2 in the steam and Nether chapters, 3 at LV and MV, 4 at HV, 5 at EV, 8 in space.
   > How: milestone quests (the big ones) pay double and also roll that tier's reward table. Optional side quests pay 1 or 2.
   > Tip: on a team, rewards go to each player who claims them, so everyone builds their own purse.
3. **Reading the Price List**: checkmark *(after: Where Tokens Come From)*
   > What: there is no shop screen. The price list is JEI.
   > How: search Demigod Token in JEI and press U (uses). Every trade shows as a crafting recipe. Do the same for the Greater Demigod Token.
   > Tip: bookmark the trades you use most so they stay on screen.
4. **What Tokens Never Buy**: checkmark *(after: Reading the Price List)*
   > What: the shop sells raw supplies and comforts. It never sells machines, circuits, alloys or anything that skips a tier.
   > Chemistry: a bag of raw ore is not a refinery. Raw tin still needs your crusher, raw titanium still needs your blast furnace.
   > How: use tokens to skip boring gathering (sand, gravel, string, torches), never to skip learning.
   > Tip: if a trade seems missing, it was left out on purpose.
5. **Small Band: 1 to 3 Tokens**: checkmark *(after: What Tokens Never Buy)*
   > What: cheap stacks of things you'd otherwise spend an afternoon gathering.
   > How: 1 token: 32 torches, 16 bread, 8 bottles o' enchanting, 32 bone meal, 64 sand, 64 gravel or 32 clay balls.
   > How: 2 tokens: 16 iron ingots, 16 coke, 16 rubber sheets, 16 fire clay dust, 32 salt dust, 16 string, 16 leather or a sleeping bag.
   > How: 3 tokens: 8 osmium ingots, 2 backpack upgrade bases, 16 gunpowder, 16 slime balls, 32 redstone or 16 lapis.
   > Tip: sand and gravel are the best early value: concrete, glass and silicon all start there.
6. **Medium Band: 4 to 8 Tokens**: checkmark *(after: Small Band: 1 to 3 Tokens)*
   > What: raw ores (you still process them yourself) and drops you would otherwise farm.
   > How: 4 tokens: a Warp Stone, 32 raw copper, 16 raw tin, 16 raw lead, 16 bauxite dust, 32 quartz or 32 glowstone dust.
   > How: 5: 16 raw nickel, 8 ender pearls, 4 phantom membranes, 16 amethyst. 6: 8 blaze rods, 4 ghast tears, 16 certus quartz, 8 source gems.
   > How: 7: 8 raw titanium or 2 name tags. 8: 8 raw tungsten or 32 prismarine shards.
   > Tip: raw ore bought here is worth up to 5x once your Mekanism line is running. Buy late, process well.
7. **Large Band: Greater Tokens**: checkmark *(after: Medium Band: 4 to 8 Tokens)*
   > What: the big trades cost Greater Demigod Tokens, not regular ones.
   > How: 1 Greater: 4 golden apples, 4 diamonds, 16 emeralds or a saddle. 2: 2 shulker shells, a heart of the sea or a netherite scrap.
   > How: 3 Greater: a totem of undying or 4 echo shards. 4 Greater: an enchanted golden apple.
   > Tip: shulker shells mean shulker boxes. Many players find that the best trade in the shop.
8. **The Exchange**: `kubejs:greater_demigod_token` *(after: Small Band: 1 to 3 Tokens)*
   > What: a Greater Demigod Token is worth 9 Demigod Tokens, and the trade works both ways.
   > How: 9 tokens in a crafting grid make 1 Greater Token. 1 Greater Token alone in the grid gives the 9 back.
   > Tip: late-tier milestones and boss loot sometimes pay Greater Tokens directly.
9. **Market Day: Early**: 10x `kubejs:demigod_token` *(after: Small Band: 1 to 3 Tokens, Iron)*
   > What: a reward for saving. Hold 10 Demigod Tokens at once and choose one comfort: a sleeping bag, a Warp Stone, backpack upgrade bases or a saddle.
   > How: the tokens are only checked, not taken. Spend them afterwards if you like.
   > Tip: the Warp Stone is the pick if you explore a lot.
10. **Market Day: Industrial**: 30x `kubejs:demigod_token` *(after: Market Day: Early, Medium Band: 4 to 8 Tokens, Analog Circuit)*
   > What: hold 30 tokens and choose a stack of raw feedstock: tin, lead, nickel, bauxite or salt.
   > How: tokens are checked, not taken.
   > Tip: bauxite is the slow one to find, and aluminium is the gate to MV.
11. **Market Day: Divine**: 64x `kubejs:demigod_token` *(after: Market Day: Industrial, Large Band: Greater Tokens, EV: Processing Units)*
   > You remember when worshippers brought you treasures. Now you just keep a full stack.
   > What: hold a full stack of 64 tokens and choose a treasure: a totem, shulker shells, a heart of the sea, an enchanted golden apple or 3 Greater Tokens.
   > How: tokens are checked, not taken. Exchange Greater Tokens back if you need 64 loose ones.
   > Tip: picking the Greater Tokens is the same as taking 27 regular tokens home.
12. **A Dragon's Hoard**: 9x `kubejs:greater_demigod_token` *(after: The Exchange)*
   > What: hold nine Greater Demigod Tokens at once. That's 81 tokens you chose not to spend.
   > Tip: the demigod approves of patience. The demigod also notes that totems exist.

### Completionist

*Collections for the thorough demigod. All optional.*

1. **Sixteen Colours of Wool**: `minecraft:white_wool`; `minecraft:orange_wool`; `minecraft:magenta_wool`; `minecraft:light_blue_wool`; `minecraft:yellow_wool`; `minecraft:lime_wool`; `minecraft:pink_wool`; `minecraft:gray_wool`; `minecraft:light_gray_wool`; `minecraft:cyan_wool`; `minecraft:purple_wool`; `minecraft:blue_wool`; `minecraft:brown_wool`; `minecraft:green_wool`; `minecraft:red_wool`; `minecraft:black_wool` *(after: The Awakening)*
   > You remember making a world with every colour in it. Start small: sixteen.
   > What: a collection page. Nothing here is needed to progress; every quest is a bragging right with a small reward.
   > How: shear sheep and dye white wool in a crafting grid (wool + dye). Sheep can also be dyed before shearing.
   > Tip: dye a sheep once and it grows that colour back forever. A pen of sixteen sheep is a wool farm.
2. **The Dyer's Palette**: `minecraft:white_dye`; `minecraft:orange_dye`; `minecraft:magenta_dye`; `minecraft:light_blue_dye`; `minecraft:yellow_dye`; `minecraft:lime_dye`; `minecraft:pink_dye`; `minecraft:gray_dye`; `minecraft:light_gray_dye`; `minecraft:cyan_dye`; `minecraft:purple_dye`; `minecraft:blue_dye`; `minecraft:brown_dye`; `minecraft:green_dye`; `minecraft:red_dye`; `minecraft:black_dye` *(after: Sixteen Colours of Wool)*
   > What: every dye colour, from flowers, plants, minerals and mixing.
   > Chemistry: real dyes are molecules that absorb some wavelengths of light. Lapis gets its blue from lazurite, a sodium aluminosilicate with sulfur ions trapped in its cage.
   > How: flowers give most primaries; cocoa gives brown, cactus smelts into green, ink sacs give black. Mix the rest (JEI shows every recipe).
   > Tip: bone meal on grass and flowers gives you endless primaries.
3. **Concrete Rainbow**: `minecraft:white_concrete`; `minecraft:orange_concrete`; `minecraft:magenta_concrete`; `minecraft:light_blue_concrete`; `minecraft:yellow_concrete`; `minecraft:lime_concrete`; `minecraft:pink_concrete`; `minecraft:gray_concrete`; `minecraft:light_gray_concrete`; `minecraft:cyan_concrete`; `minecraft:purple_concrete`; `minecraft:blue_concrete`; `minecraft:brown_concrete`; `minecraft:green_concrete`; `minecraft:red_concrete`; `minecraft:black_concrete` *(after: The Dyer's Palette)*
   > What: one block of every concrete colour.
   > Chemistry: real concrete sets by hydration, not drying: water reacts with the calcium silicates in cement to grow a stiff mesh of calcium silicate hydrate.
   > How: craft concrete powder (4 sand, 4 gravel, 1 dye), then place it in water or touch it with water to set it.
   > Tip: place powder in front of a water stream and mine it with a good pickaxe: fast and repeatable.
4. **Every Vanilla Tree**: `minecraft:oak_sapling`; `minecraft:spruce_sapling`; `minecraft:birch_sapling`; `minecraft:jungle_sapling`; `minecraft:acacia_sapling`; `minecraft:dark_oak_sapling`; `minecraft:cherry_sapling`; `minecraft:mangrove_propagule`; `minecraft:azalea`; `minecraft:flowering_azalea` *(after: A Workbench)*
   > What: one sapling from every vanilla tree, including the mangrove propagule and both azaleas.
   > Chemistry: every tree builds itself from air and water: 6CO₂ + 6H₂O → C₆H₁₂O₆ + 6O₂, powered by sunlight.
   > How: break leaves in each biome. Cherry groves sit on mountain meadows; azaleas grow above lush caves; propagules hang under mangrove leaves.
   > Tip: a Nature's Compass finds the biome you need.
5. **Trees of Distant Lands**: `biomesoplenty:redwood_sapling`; `biomesoplenty:jacaranda_sapling`; `biomesoplenty:magic_sapling`; `biomesoplenty:palm_sapling`; `biomesoplenty:snowblossom_sapling`; `regions_unexplored:baobab_sapling`; `regions_unexplored:eucalyptus_sapling`; `regions_unexplored:joshua_sapling`; `regions_unexplored:sky_wisteria_sapling`; `regions_unexplored:kapok_sapling` *(after: Every Vanilla Tree)*
   > What: saplings from Biomes O' Plenty and Regions Unexplored trees.
   > How: find the biome first: redwood and jacaranda groves, mystic groves (magic), tropics (palm), snowblossom groves; baobab savannas, eucalyptus forests, Joshua deserts, wisteria groves and rainforests.
   > Tip: JEI shows each sapling's leaves. Botany Pots grow any of them without a forest.
6. **The Creeper Collection**: `minecraft:music_disc_13`; `minecraft:music_disc_cat`; `minecraft:music_disc_blocks`; `minecraft:music_disc_chirp`; `minecraft:music_disc_far`; `minecraft:music_disc_mall`; `minecraft:music_disc_mellohi`; `minecraft:music_disc_stal`; `minecraft:music_disc_strad`; `minecraft:music_disc_ward`; `minecraft:music_disc_11`; `minecraft:music_disc_wait` *(after: Shield)*
   > What: the twelve classic discs. A creeper drops one when a skeleton (or stray or bogged) kills it.
   > How: lure creepers into a skeleton's line of fire, or build a mob farm that lets skeletons do the shooting. Dungeon chests also hold a few.
   > Tip: a skeleton spawner dungeon is perfect: trap the skeletons, then walk creepers past them.
7. **Treasure Discs**: `minecraft:music_disc_pigstep`; `minecraft:music_disc_otherside`; `minecraft:music_disc_relic`; `minecraft:music_disc_5` *(after: The Creeper Collection, Into the Fire, An Ancient City)*
   > What: four discs that only come from structures.
   > How: Pigstep: bastion remnant chests. otherside: dungeon and stronghold chests (rare). Relic: brush suspicious gravel in trail ruins. 5: craft nine disc fragments found in ancient city chests.
   > Tip: an Explorer's Compass points to the structure you need.
8. **Trial Discs**: `minecraft:music_disc_creator`; `minecraft:music_disc_creator_music_box`; `minecraft:music_disc_precipice` *(after: The Creeper Collection)*
   > What: the discs added with trial chambers.
   > How: Precipice comes from vaults opened with a trial key; Creator from ominous vaults (ominous trial key); Creator (Music Box) from decorated pots in the chambers.
   > Tip: drink an ominous bottle before a trial spawner to turn it ominous. Bring food, a shield and fire resistance.
9. **Overworld Trims**: `minecraft:coast_armor_trim_smithing_template`; `minecraft:dune_armor_trim_smithing_template`; `minecraft:wild_armor_trim_smithing_template`; `minecraft:sentry_armor_trim_smithing_template`; `minecraft:vex_armor_trim_smithing_template`; `minecraft:tide_armor_trim_smithing_template`; `minecraft:eye_armor_trim_smithing_template` *(after: Iron Pickaxe)*
   > What: armour trim templates decorate armour in a smithing table.
   > How: coast: shipwrecks. dune: desert pyramids. wild: jungle temples. sentry: pillager outposts. vex: woodland mansions. tide: elder guardians in ocean monuments. eye: strongholds.
   > Tip: copy a template with 7 diamonds and the block it's made of (check JEI) instead of hunting a second one.
10. **Dug-Up Trims**: `minecraft:wayfinder_armor_trim_smithing_template`; `minecraft:raiser_armor_trim_smithing_template`; `minecraft:shaper_armor_trim_smithing_template`; `minecraft:host_armor_trim_smithing_template` *(after: Overworld Trims)*
   > What: four trims buried in trail ruins.
   > How: brush suspicious gravel in trail ruins with a brush (feather, copper, stick). These are rare drops, so brush a lot.
   > Tip: the same gravel holds pottery sherds and the Relic disc.
11. **Trims of the Deep Dark**: `minecraft:ward_armor_trim_smithing_template`; `minecraft:silence_armor_trim_smithing_template` *(after: Overworld Trims, An Ancient City)*
   > What: the two ancient city trims. Silence is the rarest template in the game.
   > How: loot ancient city chests. Sneak, wool on the floor, and don't let the shriekers call the Warden.
   > Tip: a stack of wool carpets silences your footsteps on sculk sensors.
12. **Trims of Fire and Void**: `minecraft:rib_armor_trim_smithing_template`; `minecraft:snout_armor_trim_smithing_template`; `minecraft:spire_armor_trim_smithing_template` *(after: Overworld Trims, Fortress Found, End City Loot)*
   > What: one trim each from Nether fortresses (rib), bastions (snout) and End cities (spire).
   > How: fortress chests, bastion chests and End city chests. Bring gold armour for the bastion.
   > Tip: bastion treasure rooms also hold the netherite upgrade template.
13. **Trial Trims**: `minecraft:flow_armor_trim_smithing_template`; `minecraft:bolt_armor_trim_smithing_template` *(after: Overworld Trims)*
   > What: the two trial chamber trims.
   > How: bolt comes from regular vaults (trial key); flow from ominous vaults (ominous trial key).
   > Tip: every player gets their own loot from each vault, so bring friends.
14. **Ore Cabinet: Overworld**: 1x any of `or(item(minecraft:coal_ore)item(minecraft:deepslate_coal_ore))`; 1x any of `or(item(minecraft:iron_ore)item(minecraft:deepslate_iron_ore))`; 1x any of `or(item(minecraft:copper_ore)item(minecraft:deepslate_copper_ore))`; 1x any of `or(item(minecraft:gold_ore)item(minecraft:deepslate_gold_ore))`; 1x any of `or(item(minecraft:redstone_ore)item(minecraft:deepslate_redstone_ore))`; 1x any of `or(item(minecraft:lapis_ore)item(minecraft:deepslate_lapis_ore))`; 1x any of `or(item(minecraft:diamond_ore)item(minecraft:deepslate_diamond_ore))`; 1x any of `or(item(minecraft:emerald_ore)item(minecraft:deepslate_emerald_ore))` *(after: Iron Pickaxe)*
   > What: one ore block of each vanilla Overworld ore (stone or deepslate both count).
   > Chemistry: real ores are minerals: iron as hematite (Fe₂O₃), copper as chalcopyrite (CuFeS₂), lapis as lazurite. Diamond is pure carbon.
   > How: you need a Silk Touch pickaxe so the ore drops itself. Emerald only grows under mountains.
   > Tip: place them in item frames as a display: a real geology cabinet.
15. **Ore Cabinet: Nether**: `minecraft:nether_gold_ore`; `minecraft:nether_quartz_ore`; `minecraft:ancient_debris` *(after: Ore Cabinet: Overworld, Ancient Debris)*
   > What: Nether gold, Nether quartz and ancient debris.
   > Chemistry: real quartz is silicon dioxide, SiO₂, the same thing as sand and the silicon you refine later.
   > How: Silk Touch for the gold and quartz ores. Ancient debris always drops itself; mine around Y 15.
   > Tip: beds explode in the Nether. That's a fast (and loud) way to find debris.
16. **Ore Cabinet: Industrial**: 1x any of `or(item(modern_industrialization:tin_ore)item(modern_industrialization:deepslate_tin_ore))`; 1x any of `or(item(modern_industrialization:lead_ore)item(modern_industrialization:deepslate_lead_ore))`; 1x any of `or(item(modern_industrialization:nickel_ore)item(modern_industrialization:deepslate_nickel_ore))`; 1x any of `or(item(modern_industrialization:bauxite_ore)item(modern_industrialization:deepslate_bauxite_ore))`; 1x any of `or(item(modern_industrialization:antimony_ore)item(modern_industrialization:deepslate_antimony_ore))`; 1x any of `or(item(modern_industrialization:salt_ore)item(modern_industrialization:deepslate_salt_ore))`; 1x any of `or(item(modern_industrialization:lignite_coal_ore)item(modern_industrialization:deepslate_lignite_coal_ore))`; 1x any of `or(item(modern_industrialization:monazite_ore)item(modern_industrialization:deepslate_monazite_ore))` *(after: Ore Cabinet: Overworld, Tin)*
   > What: one block of each common MI ore.
   > Chemistry: bauxite is aluminium hydroxide; antimony is mined as stibnite (Sb₂S₃); monazite is a rare-earth phosphate, (Ce,La,Nd)PO₄, often with thorium.
   > How: Silk Touch. Rich deposits sit at metal-specific depths: lead around Y −24 to 16, nickel lower at Y −32 to 8.
   > Tip: Jade shows the ore name as you look at it, so you can tell tin from lead in the dark.
17. **Ore Cabinet: Deep and Rare**: 1x any of `or(item(modern_industrialization:uranium_ore)item(modern_industrialization:deepslate_uranium_ore))`; 1x any of `or(item(modern_industrialization:tungsten_ore)item(modern_industrialization:deepslate_tungsten_ore))`; 1x any of `or(item(mekanism:osmium_ore)item(mekanism:deepslate_osmium_ore))`; 1x any of `or(item(mekanism:fluorite_ore)item(mekanism:deepslate_fluorite_ore))` *(after: Ore Cabinet: Industrial, Osmium)*
   > What: the deep ores, as blocks.
   > Chemistry: uranium ore is uraninite (UO₂); tungsten comes from wolframite and scheelite (CaWO₄); fluorite is calcium fluoride, CaF₂, the source of the hydrofluoric acid you'll need for uranium.
   > How: Silk Touch. Uranium and tungsten deposits sit deep, around Y −56 to −24.
   > Tip: Mekanism's radiation comes from its nuclear fluids and waste later on. Hazmat gear is in Tools and Comfort.
18. **Ore Hoarder**: 256x any of `item_tag(c:ores)` *(after: Ore Cabinet: Industrial)*
   > What: four stacks of unprocessed ore blocks (anything tagged as an ore).
   > How: Silk Touch plus FTB Ultimine makes this quick. Keep them as blocks: Mekanism and MI process ore blocks too.
   > Tip: ore blocks wait patiently. Store them until your 5x line is ready and every block is worth more.
19. **Circuit Collection**: `modern_industrialization:analog_circuit`; `modern_industrialization:electronic_circuit`; `modern_industrialization:digital_circuit`; `modern_industrialization:processing_unit` *(after: EV: Processing Units)*
   > What: one of each MI circuit tier you have built: analog, electronic, digital, processing unit.
   > Chemistry: each tier stands on new chemistry: rubber, then aluminium, then polyethylene, then PVC and pure silicon.
   > How: you made all of these on the main path. Keep one of each.
   > Tip: frame them in order. It's a history of everything you've learned.
20. **Quantum Circuit**: `modern_industrialization:quantum_circuit` *(after: Circuit Collection, Plutonium, The Rare Four)*
   > What: MI's top circuit. Nothing on the main path needs it, which makes it the perfect trophy.
   > How: an Assembler: 4 processing units, 2 cooling cells, 2 qubits and a quantum circuit board. The board needs superconductor cable, plutonium batteries, iridium plates and helium-3 (check JEI for each).
   > Tip: iridium comes from the PGM residue line; plan the iridium before you start.
21. **Basic Factory**: 1x any of `or(item(mekanism:basic_smelting_factory)item(mekanism:basic_enriching_factory)item(mekanism:basic_crushing_factory)item(mekanism:basic_compressing_factory)item(mekanism:basic_combining_factory)item(mekanism:basic_purifying_factory)item(mekanism:basic_injecting_factory)item(mekanism:basic_infusing_factory)item(mekanism:basic_sawing_factory))` *(after: Mekanism Circuits)*
   > What: a Mekanism factory runs several copies of one machine in one block. Basic factories have 3 slots.
   > How: right-click a placed Mekanism machine with a Basic Tier Installer, or craft the factory (check JEI). Any basic factory counts.
   > Tip: a basic smelting factory with speed upgrades already beats a row of furnaces.
22. **Advanced Factory**: 1x any of `or(item(mekanism:advanced_smelting_factory)item(mekanism:advanced_enriching_factory)item(mekanism:advanced_crushing_factory)item(mekanism:advanced_compressing_factory)item(mekanism:advanced_combining_factory)item(mekanism:advanced_purifying_factory)item(mekanism:advanced_injecting_factory)item(mekanism:advanced_infusing_factory)item(mekanism:advanced_sawing_factory))` *(after: Basic Factory, Electronic Circuit)*
   > What: advanced factories have 5 slots.
   > How: an Advanced Tier Installer on a basic factory. Its control circuit needs an MI electronic circuit (MV) and infused alloy.
   > Tip: upgrade in place: the installer keeps the factory's contents and settings.
23. **Elite Factory**: 1x any of `or(item(mekanism:elite_smelting_factory)item(mekanism:elite_enriching_factory)item(mekanism:elite_crushing_factory)item(mekanism:elite_compressing_factory)item(mekanism:elite_combining_factory)item(mekanism:elite_purifying_factory)item(mekanism:elite_injecting_factory)item(mekanism:elite_infusing_factory)item(mekanism:elite_sawing_factory))` *(after: Advanced Factory, HV: Digital Circuits)*
   > What: elite factories have 7 slots.
   > How: an Elite Tier Installer; its circuit needs an MI digital circuit (HV) and reinforced alloy.
   > Tip: factories sort their own input slots if you turn on auto-sort in the GUI.
24. **Ultimate Factory**: 1x any of `or(item(mekanism:ultimate_smelting_factory)item(mekanism:ultimate_enriching_factory)item(mekanism:ultimate_crushing_factory)item(mekanism:ultimate_compressing_factory)item(mekanism:ultimate_combining_factory)item(mekanism:ultimate_purifying_factory)item(mekanism:ultimate_injecting_factory)item(mekanism:ultimate_infusing_factory)item(mekanism:ultimate_sawing_factory))` *(after: Elite Factory, EV: Processing Units)*
   > What: ultimate factories have 9 slots, the most Mekanism offers.
   > How: an Ultimate Tier Installer; its circuit needs an MI processing unit (EV) and atomic alloy.
   > Tip: one ultimate enriching factory can feed a whole 5x ore line's first step.
25. **The Full Line-Up**: `mekanism:ultimate_smelting_factory`; `mekanism:ultimate_enriching_factory`; `mekanism:ultimate_crushing_factory`; `mekanism:ultimate_compressing_factory`; `mekanism:ultimate_combining_factory`; `mekanism:ultimate_purifying_factory`; `mekanism:ultimate_injecting_factory`; `mekanism:ultimate_infusing_factory`; `mekanism:ultimate_sawing_factory` *(after: Ultimate Factory)*
   > What: every factory type at ultimate tier: smelting, enriching, crushing, compressing, combining, purifying, injecting, infusing and sawing.
   > How: build or upgrade one of each. Purifying and injecting are the 4x and 5x ore steps; infusing makes alloys.
   > Tip: this is a lot of atomic alloy. Automate the refined obsidian first.
26. **The s-Block**: `chemlib:hydrogen`; `chemlib:helium`; `chemlib:lithium`; `chemlib:beryllium`; `chemlib:sodium`; `chemlib:magnesium`; `chemlib:potassium`; `chemlib:calcium`; `chemlib:rubidium`; `chemlib:strontium`; `chemlib:cesium`; `chemlib:barium`; `chemlib:francium`; `chemlib:radium` *(after: Element Transmutation)*
   > What: every s-block element: the alkali metals, the alkaline earth metals, hydrogen and helium.
   > Chemistry: their outermost electrons sit in an s orbital. One or two to give away, which makes them reactive metals (helium's full 1s² shell is the exception).
   > How: the Dissolver gives most of the light metals (helium needs fusion: H + H). Rubidium, cesium, francium and radium are rare: build them in the Fusion Chamber by adding proton numbers (check JEI).
   > Tip: francium is so unstable that no one has ever seen a visible amount of it.
27. **The p-Block: Light**: `chemlib:boron`; `chemlib:carbon`; `chemlib:nitrogen`; `chemlib:oxygen`; `chemlib:fluorine`; `chemlib:neon`; `chemlib:aluminum`; `chemlib:silicon`; `chemlib:phosphorus`; `chemlib:sulfur`; `chemlib:chlorine`; `chemlib:argon`; `chemlib:gallium`; `chemlib:germanium`; `chemlib:arsenic`; `chemlib:selenium`; `chemlib:bromine`; `chemlib:krypton` *(after: The s-Block)*
   > What: the lighter p-block elements: the non-metals of life and most of what you've refined so far.
   > Chemistry: these elements fill p orbitals. Across a row they go from metals that lose electrons to halogens that grab them, ending at a noble gas.
   > How: most come from the Dissolver. Neon, argon and krypton need the Fusion Chamber (check JEI).
   > Tip: carbon, nitrogen, oxygen and phosphorus are most of the mass of a living thing besides hydrogen.
28. **The p-Block: Heavy**: `chemlib:indium`; `chemlib:tin`; `chemlib:antimony`; `chemlib:tellurium`; `chemlib:iodine`; `chemlib:xenon`; `chemlib:thallium`; `chemlib:lead`; `chemlib:bismuth`; `chemlib:polonium`; `chemlib:astatine`; `chemlib:radon`; `chemlib:nihonium`; `chemlib:flerovium`; `chemlib:moscovium`; `chemlib:livermorium`; `chemlib:tennessine`; `chemlib:oganesson` *(after: The p-Block: Light)*
   > What: the heavy p-block, including the superheavy elements made only in particle accelerators.
   > Chemistry: past lead, every element is radioactive. Bismuth's half-life is a billion times the age of the universe; oganesson's is under a millisecond.
   > How: the Fusion and Fission Chambers. Pick pairs whose proton numbers add up (check JEI).
   > Tip: astatine is the rarest naturally occurring element: less than a gram exists in Earth's crust at any time.
29. **The d-Block: Period 4**: `chemlib:scandium`; `chemlib:titanium`; `chemlib:vanadium`; `chemlib:chromium`; `chemlib:manganese`; `chemlib:iron`; `chemlib:cobalt`; `chemlib:nickel`; `chemlib:copper`; `chemlib:zinc` *(after: The s-Block)*
   > What: the first row of transition metals, the workhorses of industry.
   > Chemistry: transition metals fill d orbitals, which gives them colourful compounds, several oxidation states and catalytic tricks (iron in Haber-Bosch, platinum in Ostwald).
   > How: dissolve their ingots and dusts; build scandium and vanadium by fusion if you can't find a source (check JEI).
   > Tip: stainless steel is iron, chromium and nickel: three boxes in this row.
30. **The d-Block: Period 5**: `chemlib:yttrium`; `chemlib:zirconium`; `chemlib:niobium`; `chemlib:molybdenum`; `chemlib:technetium`; `chemlib:ruthenium`; `chemlib:rhodium`; `chemlib:palladium`; `chemlib:silver`; `chemlib:cadmium` *(after: The d-Block: Period 4)*
   > What: the second transition row, including three platinum-group metals.
   > Chemistry: technetium (43) was the first element made artificially, in 1937. It has no stable isotope at all.
   > How: ruthenium and rhodium come from your PGM residue; the rest by dissolving or fusion (check JEI).
   > Tip: rhodium is one of the most expensive metals on Earth, mostly used in car catalytic converters.
31. **The d-Block: Period 6**: `chemlib:hafnium`; `chemlib:tantalum`; `chemlib:tungsten`; `chemlib:rhenium`; `chemlib:osmium`; `chemlib:iridium`; `chemlib:platinum`; `chemlib:gold`; `chemlib:mercury` *(after: The d-Block: Period 5)*
   > What: the heaviest stable transition metals: refractory tungsten, dense osmium, noble platinum and gold.
   > Chemistry: osmium is the densest element (22.6 g/cm³). Gold is yellow and mercury is liquid because of relativity: their inner electrons move fast enough to change the orbitals' energies.
   > How: the PGM line gives platinum, iridium and osmium; the rest by dissolving or fusion (check JEI).
   > Tip: tungsten has the highest melting point of any metal, 3422 °C.
32. **The d-Block: Period 7**: `chemlib:rutherfordium`; `chemlib:dubnium`; `chemlib:seaborgium`; `chemlib:bohrium`; `chemlib:hassium`; `chemlib:meitnerium`; `chemlib:darmstadtium`; `chemlib:roentgenium`; `chemlib:copernicium` *(after: The d-Block: Period 6, Superheavy Elements)*
   > What: the superheavy transition metals, 104 to 112.
   > Chemistry: each was made a few atoms at a time by smashing nuclei together. Several are named after scientists: Rutherford, Seaborg, Bohr, Meitner, Röntgen, Copernicus.
   > How: only the Fusion Chamber makes these. Check JEI for pairs that add up.
   > Tip: seaborgium was named while Glenn Seaborg was alive, a rare honour.
33. **The Lanthanides**: `chemlib:lanthanum`; `chemlib:cerium`; `chemlib:praseodymium`; `chemlib:neodymium`; `chemlib:promethium`; `chemlib:samarium`; `chemlib:europium`; `chemlib:gadolinium`; `chemlib:terbium`; `chemlib:dysprosium`; `chemlib:holmium`; `chemlib:erbium`; `chemlib:thulium`; `chemlib:ytterbium`; `chemlib:lutetium` *(after: The s-Block, Rare Earths)*
   > What: the whole lanthanide row, 57 to 71.
   > Chemistry: they fill the 4f orbitals, which sit deep inside the atom, so their chemistry is almost identical. That's why separating them takes hundreds of extraction steps.
   > How: press U on each element in JEI for Dissolver sources; fuse the rest in the Fusion Chamber. Promethium has no stable isotope.
   > Tip: four of them are named after one Swedish village, Ytterby: yttrium, ytterbium, terbium, erbium.
34. **The Actinides**: `chemlib:actinium`; `chemlib:thorium`; `chemlib:protactinium`; `chemlib:uranium`; `chemlib:neptunium`; `chemlib:plutonium`; `chemlib:americium`; `chemlib:curium`; `chemlib:berkelium`; `chemlib:californium`; `chemlib:einsteinium`; `chemlib:fermium`; `chemlib:mendelevium`; `chemlib:nobelium`; `chemlib:lawrencium` *(after: The s-Block, Actinides)*
   > What: the whole actinide row, 89 to 103. Every one is radioactive.
   > Chemistry: only thorium and uranium last long enough to be found in quantity. Everything past uranium was first made in reactors or in the debris of bomb tests.
   > How: dissolve uranium and thorium materials; fuse the heavier ones (check JEI).
   > Tip: americium is in most household smoke detectors.
35. **Every Box Filled**: checkmark *(after: The p-Block: Heavy, The d-Block: Period 7, The Lanthanides, The Actinides)*
   > Every element, in a chest. You remember when this was how you made worlds.
   > What: you've collected every block of the periodic table.
   > How: click to claim. Keep the chest; a periodic table wall of item frames is a fine way to show it off.
   > Tip: the Combiner can now build almost any compound you can name.
36. **Tour: The Frozen North**: find biome `minecraft:snowy_plains`; find biome `minecraft:ice_spikes`; find biome `minecraft:snowy_taiga`; find biome `minecraft:grove`; find biome `minecraft:snowy_slopes`; find biome `minecraft:frozen_peaks`; find biome `minecraft:frozen_river` *(after: Iron Pickaxe)*
   > What: visit seven snowy biomes.
   > Chemistry: ice floats because water expands about 9% when it freezes: hydrogen bonds lock the molecules into an open hexagonal lattice.
   > How: walk in. A Nature's Compass finds each one.
   > Tip: powder snow on frozen peaks swallows players. Leather boots let you walk on it.
37. **Tour: Heat and Rain**: find biome `minecraft:desert`; find biome `minecraft:badlands`; find biome `minecraft:eroded_badlands`; find biome `minecraft:savanna`; find biome `minecraft:jungle`; find biome `minecraft:bamboo_jungle`; find biome `minecraft:mangrove_swamp` *(after: Iron Pickaxe)*
   > What: visit seven warm biomes, from dry desert to jungle.
   > Chemistry: badlands terracotta gets its reds and oranges from iron oxides, the same rust you can make in the Combiner.
   > How: walk in. Badlands have gold at every height, so bring a pickaxe.
   > Tip: jungle temples and desert pyramids hold trim templates for the trim quests.
38. **Tour: Hidden Places**: find biome `minecraft:mushroom_fields`; find biome `minecraft:cherry_grove`; find biome `minecraft:lush_caves`; find biome `minecraft:dripstone_caves`; find biome `minecraft:deep_dark`; find biome `minecraft:old_growth_pine_taiga` *(after: Tour: The Frozen North, Tour: Heat and Rain)*
   > What: the rare and underground biomes.
   > Chemistry: dripstone forms when water with dissolved calcium bicarbonate drips and loses CO₂, leaving calcium carbonate behind: Ca(HCO₃)₂ → CaCO₃ + H₂O + CO₂.
   > How: mushroom fields are islands far out at sea. Lush and dripstone caves sit under the surface; the deep dark sits below Y 0 under mountains.
   > Tip: no hostile mobs spawn on mushroom fields. Safe camping.
39. **Tour: Strange Lands**: find biome `biomesoplenty:redwood_forest`; find biome `biomesoplenty:lavender_field`; find biome `biomesoplenty:volcano`; find biome `biomesoplenty:mystic_grove`; find biome `regions_unexplored:bayou`; find biome `regions_unexplored:redwoods`; find biome `regions_unexplored:prismachasm` *(after: Tour: Hidden Places)*
   > What: seven biomes from Biomes O' Plenty and Regions Unexplored.
   > How: Nature's Compass lists modded biomes too. Prismachasm is a cave biome, so dig.
   > Tip: volcanoes have lava at the top. Bring fire resistance.
40. **Tour: The Five Fires**: find biome `minecraft:nether_wastes`; find biome `minecraft:crimson_forest`; find biome `minecraft:warped_forest`; find biome `minecraft:soul_sand_valley`; find biome `minecraft:basalt_deltas` *(after: Into the Fire)*
   > What: visit all five vanilla Nether biomes.
   > Chemistry: basalt is cooled lava, mostly silicates of iron and magnesium. Nether quartz is SiO₂.
   > How: walk in. Warped forests are the safest place in the Nether: endermen only.
   > Tip: ghasts hunt soul sand valleys. A shield blocks fireballs.
41. **Tour: The Deeper Fires**: find biome `biomesoplenty:crystalline_chasm`; find biome `biomesoplenty:undergrowth`; find biome `biomesoplenty:visceral_heap`; find biome `biomesoplenty:withered_abyss`; find biome `regions_unexplored:infernal_holt`; find biome `regions_unexplored:blackstone_basin` *(after: Tour: The Five Fires)*
   > What: Nether biomes from Biomes O' Plenty and Regions Unexplored.
   > How: Nature's Compass works in the Nether too. Withered abyss is a void: don't fall in.
   > Tip: crystalline chasm's crystals are pretty and brittle. Mine with care.
42. **Tour: The Void**: find biome `minecraft:the_end`; find biome `minecraft:end_highlands`; find biome `minecraft:end_midlands`; find biome `minecraft:small_end_islands`; find biome `minecraft:end_barrens` *(after: The Dragon's Breath)*
   > What: visit the five End biomes, from the central island to the outer islands.
   > How: after the dragon, throw an ender pearl into an end gateway to reach the outer islands.
   > Tip: end cities sit in the highlands. Bring slow falling against shulkers.
43. **Tour: Twilight Lowlands**: find biome `twilightforest:forest`; find biome `twilightforest:dense_forest`; find biome `twilightforest:firefly_forest`; find biome `twilightforest:clearing`; find biome `twilightforest:mushroom_forest`; find biome `twilightforest:enchanted_forest`; find biome `twilightforest:spooky_forest`; find biome `twilightforest:oak_savannah`; find biome `twilightforest:lake` *(after: The Twilight Forest)*
   > What: the Twilight Forest biomes you can walk into from day one.
   > How: walk in. The Magic Map shows biomes as you explore.
   > Tip: enchanted forests hold rainbow oak and the odd quest ram.
44. **Tour: Twilight Frontier**: find biome `twilightforest:swamp`; find biome `twilightforest:fire_swamp`; find biome `twilightforest:dark_forest`; find biome `twilightforest:dark_forest_center`; find biome `twilightforest:snowy_forest`; find biome `twilightforest:glacier`; find biome `twilightforest:highlands`; find biome `twilightforest:thornlands`; find biome `twilightforest:final_plateau` *(after: Tour: Twilight Lowlands, The Ur-Ghast, The Snow Queen)*
   > What: the Twilight biomes that open as you beat its bosses.
   > How: locked biomes hurt or block you until you beat the boss before them. Follow the Twilight boss order in Worlds.
   > Tip: the final plateau is the last place in the Twilight Forest. Enjoy the view.
45. **Tour: The Undergarden**: find biome `undergarden:forgotten_field`; find biome `undergarden:smogstem_forest`; find biome `undergarden:wigglewood_forest`; find biome `undergarden:gronglegrowth`; find biome `undergarden:frostfields`; find biome `undergarden:barren_abyss`; find biome `undergarden:ancient_sea`; find biome `undergarden:indigo_mushroom_bog` *(after: The Undergarden)*
   > What: explore the Undergarden's biomes.
   > How: walk in. Bring light: the Undergarden has no sun.
   > Tip: smog spires and smogstem forests are where you'll find the strangest mobs.
46. **Tour: The Otherside**: find biome `deeperdarker:deeplands`; find biome `deeperdarker:echoing_forest`; find biome `deeperdarker:overcast_columns`; find biome `deeperdarker:blooming_caverns` *(after: The Otherside)*
   > What: the four Otherside biomes from Deeper and Darker.
   > How: walk in. Sculk listens here too: sneak.
   > Tip: echo trees in the echoing forest give wood you can't get anywhere else.
47. **Tour: The Solar System**: find biome `ad_astra:lunar_wastelands`; find biome `ad_astra:martian_wastelands`; find biome `ad_astra:martian_canyon_creek`; find biome `ad_astra:martian_polar_caps`; find biome `ad_astra:venus_wastelands`; find biome `ad_astra:infernal_venus_barrens`; find biome `ad_astra:mercury_deltas`; find biome `ad_astra:glacio_snowy_barrens`; find biome `ad_astra:glacio_ice_peaks` *(after: Glacio, Mercury)*
   > What: stand in every planetary biome Ad Astra adds.
   > Chemistry: Mars is red from iron(III) oxide dust; Venus's clouds are sulfuric acid droplets, the same H₂SO₄ you make in the Contact Process.
   > How: fly to the Moon, Mars, Venus, Mercury and Glacio. Wear the right suit: Venus and Mercury need heat protection.
   > Tip: martian polar caps are frozen CO₂ and water in real life; in game, bring an oxygen supply.
48. **Element 5, Track 5**: `minecraft:music_disc_5`; 5x `chemlib:boron` *(after: The Creeper Collection)*
   > What: disc 5 and five boron. Boron is element 5.
   > Chemistry: boron is in borosilicate glass, which barely expands when heated. That's why lab glassware survives a flame.
   > Tip: the demigod finds this funnier than anyone else will.
49. **An Egg from the Past**: `minecraft:sniffer_egg` *(after: Dug-Up Trims)*
   > What: a sniffer egg, brushed out of warm ocean ruins.
   > How: place it on moss to hatch it faster. Adult sniffers dig up ancient seeds.
   > Tip: torchflowers and pitcher plants only come from sniffers.
50. **The Only One**: `minecraft:dragon_egg` *(after: Tour: The Void)*
   > What: the dragon egg. It teleports when you hit it.
   > How: push it off the portal with a piston, or let it fall onto a torch, then pick it up.
   > Tip: you'll need it for the Seed of a New World. Don't drop it in the void.
